# CATCC: decisions and findings

Running log of decisions made after HANDOFF.md. Where they disagree, this file wins.

## Hosting and environment (2026-11-07)

- **DCS server:** Fox3 Managed Solutions (`dcs.fightingstingrays.com`; Playground at `dcs2.`). We have file access to `Saved Games` (Scripts, Hooks, Mods), so we can install our own Lua hooks. Outbound connections from the host are probably restricted. Inbound mod ports are evidently allowed (Tacview 42674, LotATC 10310 and Olympus 3001 are all reachable).
- **Already on the server:** Tacview real-time export, LotATC, DCS Olympus v2.0.5 (web client on :3001), and DCS-gRPC (in `Scripts/DCS-gRPC`).
- **Credentials live outside the repo:** in `.env.local` / Fly secrets only.

## Seats

- **Typical:** 2 carriers × 2 controllers. The first controller is Departure + Marshal + Approach + Boss ("captain"); the second is the LSO.
- **Growth order:** a mission commander first, then per-boat approach/deck controllers. Up to 3 boats × 2.
- **Design limit:** 10 seats.
- **Model:** a seat is `(carrier, role)`, and one person may hold several roles. Command authority follows the roles:
  - the ship's controller steers;
  - the ship's controller or the mission commander launches tankers.
- **Supersedes the handoff:** this replaces HANDOFF §10's single global DEP/MARSHAL/APPROACH/FINAL row. Carriers are a list (up to 3), not "focus + other".

## Data sources

| Source | What it gives | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **Tacview real-time** (42674, no password) | Every object: position, attitude, heading, type, Pilot/Group/Color. Measured at about 11 KB/s during a 2-carrier cruise. **No fuel, no TAS/GS for other aircraft** (GS, course and VS are derived from position history). | **Phase 1 source.** Documented protocol, read-only, multiple clients are fine. Implemented. |
| **LotATC** (10310, coalition password) | A controller picture. | **Not used.** The protocol is proprietary and undocumented (the server waits for the client to speak first). A client of our own would be reverse-engineering a licensed product. Licences apply to the LotATC *client*, not to the stream, so licences aren't the reason; the protocol is. |
| **Olympus** REST (`/olympus/units`, `mission`, `commands`, etc.) | Every unit including **fuel (0–100 %, integer)**, plus unit control (routes, speed, spawn, tanker/AWACS, refuel). Also has an **SRS audio backend** (browser radios over WebSocket). | **Best candidate for fuel and Phase 2 control.** The auth is HTTP Basic `user:sha256(password)` with an `X-Command-Mode` header. A read-only probe returned 401 with the Blue password while the server was idle; recheck it while a mission is running. |
| **Own Lua hook** | Anything in the mission environment. | Fallback if Olympus doesn't fit. |

### Fuel

Tacview can't supply fuel. ACMI defines `FuelWeight`/`FuelVolume`, but DCS's exporter (DCS2ACMI 1.9.4) doesn't write them. The Week 5 cruise recording (1.3 M lines) has no fuel property at all, not even for the recording player's own jet.

A server-side Lua export **can** supply fuel. `Unit.getFuel()` in the mission scripting environment returns fuel as a fraction of internal capacity (above 1.0 with external tanks). Olympus exports exactly this, as an integer percentage. Converting it to pounds needs an internal-capacity table per type (F/A-18C: 10,860 lb, so 1 % ≈ 109 lb, which is enough for a `7.4` display).

**To verify live:** that `getFuel()` is accurate for *client* (player) aircraft on the server, not only for AI.

### Other facts found in the cruise recording

- `Coalition` is relative to the recorder (blue jets show as "Enemies"). Use `Color` for the side.
- Pilot strings are free text, e.g. `Hawk 4-2/733 QUIZNOS`. `parsePilot` extracts the callsign, a 3-digit board number and the handle. Roster matching should use the board number.
- `Group` matches the DCS flight, so formations come from the feed directly (HANDOFF §5.5).
- Several non-carrier ships report `AircraftCarrier` (e.g. `atconveyor`). Carriers are identified by type name (`CARRIER_HULLS`).

## Room service egress (Fly.io)

- **Ingress is free.** That covers the Tacview stream into the room service.
- **Worst case:** forwarding the raw stream to 10 clients is about 110 KB/s ≈ 0.4 GB/h, so ~1.2 GB for a 3-hour event, which is cents at Fly's per-GB egress rates.
- **Normalised deltas at 1 Hz with permessage-deflate:** roughly 10× less.
- **Conclusion:** telemetry won't affect the bill. Video would (see below).

## PLAT sharing (aspirational, documented per user request)

The capture tool (`Desktop/LSO-Diagnostic`) shares `LSOFRAME` as a D3D11 shared texture on the LSO's own machine. That machine must run DCS with the LSO platform selected. As of 2026-11-07 it has been tested in single player only, and it is pinned to a single DCS build.

1. **Other controllers monitoring the PLAT** (useful, but not on the critical path). The LSO station hardware-encodes the texture (NVENC/AMF/QSV via Media Foundation, zero-copy from D3D11) and publishes it to the room. Viewers receive it over WebRTC, which keeps latency under a second. Controllers then don't need DCS running to watch.
2. **A squadron-wide PLAT stream on a ReadyRoom page.** One publisher per boat pushes RTMP. **Don't relay it through Fly:** 1080p30 at about 4 Mbps is ~1.8 GB per viewer-hour. Push to an unlisted YouTube or Twitch stream, or to Cloudflare Stream, and embed that player in ReadyRoom. Expect 3–10 s of latency, which is fine for spectators.

## Own export hook (2026-11-08)

The user chose to build our own hook rather than reuse Olympus. It reuses LotATC's port 10310 (already open on Fox3; nobody uses LotATC), so the server admin doesn't need to open anything. Install steps are in `catcc-dcs/INSTALL.md`.

- **Design:** a GameGUI hook (`Scripts/Hooks/CATCC-hook.lua` loads `Scripts/CATCC/catcc.lua`) runs a non-blocking LuaSocket server and polls the mission environment once per second.
  - The poll runs `net.dostring_in('server', ...)` calling `CATCC_sample()`, which `sampler.lua` installs and reinstalls after a mission restart.
  - Protocol v1 is newline-delimited JSON. The client sends `AUTH <token>`; the token lives in `Config/CATCC.lua`, and the hook fails closed without it.
- **What it adds over Tacview:**
  - fuel (`getFuel()`);
  - true velocity (groundspeed, course and vertical speed come from DCS, not from position differences);
  - true-north heading, with the grid convergence correction;
  - unit/player/group identity, and the coalition as data;
  - deck, 2,000 ft and 10,000 ft winds plus sea-level temperature and pressure at each carrier;
  - mission date, local time and theatre.
- **Tacview stays configured** as a fallback source and for development.
- **Fuel in lb:** the internal capacities in `src/catcc/core/dcs/aircraft.ts` are taken from `M_fuel_max` in the local DCS install. For example, the Hornet is 4,900 kg.
- **Time:** DCS mission time is map-local. `THEATRE_UTC_OFFSET_H` converts it to UTC. The offsets are from memory, **so verify them per map** (especially Normandy/Channel and Sinai).
- **Testing:** `catcc-dcs/test` runs the real hook under fengari (Lua 5.3) with mocked DCS, LuaSocket and a mission environment on a grid rotated 3° from true north. DCS itself is Lua 5.1: the hook avoids 5.2+ features, and the harness shims `math.atan2`.
- **Not verified live yet:**
  - which `net.dostring_in` state reaches the scripting environment on Fox3 (`server` expected);
  - `getFuel()` for player aircraft;
  - CPU cost per sample with about 100 units.

## Room service (2026-11-08)

- **Hosting:** it runs inside SDOBot for now, at `/catcc/ws` on the existing Fly app, off unless `CATCC_ENABLED=true`, because the user is watching the billing threshold. `catcc-server/room/main.ts` is the standalone server for when it moves to its own app.
  - SDOBot loads a CommonJS bundle (`SDOBot/catcc/room.cjs`, built by `npm run catcc:bundle`). SDOBot's dependencies and its Node 20 image stay unchanged.
  - A room failure is caught and logged; it never takes SDOBot down.
- **Auth:** the first WebSocket message must carry the ReadyRoom Supabase access token. It is verified with `/auth/v1/user` using the service key; the display name and board number come from `user_profiles` → `pilots`. Any signed-in user can view and claim seats for now. **TODO:** add a `manage_catcc`/`operate_catcc` permission before Phase 2 commands.
- **Frames:** a complete picture at 1 Hz, as compact `WireTrack`s, with permessage-deflate. Clients keep their own trails. That's about 11 KB per frame uncompressed for 43 tracks.
- **Seats:** `(carrier hull, role)` with the roles departure, marshal, approach, final, boss, lso, and mission_commander (carrier `*`). One user can hold several. A seat survives a disconnect for 60 s, marked `away`.
- **Dev:** `npm run catcc:room -- --acmi <recording> [--speed 1] [--from 2300]` serves a recording through an in-process fake hook with synthetic fuel, no auth.

## Geometry module (2026-11-08)

`src/catcc/core/geometry/` is ported from the mockup's `radials()` as pure functions returning sampled polylines in scope coordinates.

- **What's in it:**
  - Case III departure paths (10 NM arc or S-turn);
  - left- and right-hand holds, sized for 250 kt or from TAS;
  - CV-1 arrivals (gradual or 30° turn at 20 DME, normal intercept or the 12 DME arc with fillets);
  - radial placement rules;
  - the turn-then-direct `fly()` model, EAT, CPA and NATOPS conflict standards;
  - the AEQD projection.
- **Tests:** 0–355° sweeps check that every path is finite and joins final astern of the ship, on the FB line and flying the FB; that arcs are flown only from wide radials and their points are really at 12 DME; and that the first fillet doesn't overshoot.
- **Still to do from the handoff backlog:**
  - hold-entry modelling in the EAT;
  - stack separation by footprint and altitude band (the 10° stack rule is still the mockup's invented one, flagged in code).

## Radar UI, first pass (2026-11-08)

- **Route:** `/catcc` is a top-level route in `main.tsx` next to `/kneeboard`: full-viewport, no app shell, pinned to `data-theme="dark"`. It is meant to be the page each Electron window or browser popup loads. `?src=`, `?cv=` and `?ws=` (dev room URL) are query overrides.
- **Code:**
  - `src/components/catcc/`: `useCatccRoom` (WebSocket + Supabase token, reconnect, trail history), `scopeModel` (pure picture: projection, formations by DCS group, conflicts, phases, tags; unit-tested), `Scope` (SVG), `CatccApp` (shell).
  - Strips reuse the ui-refactor D02.1 `FlightCard` unchanged.
- **Built:**
  - screen-constant symbology (handoff §3.3);
  - rings and compass, CCAs (focus green, others blue), the coordination line with pill, BRC chevron, FB with 3/6/10 ticks;
  - departure radials with DR flags; marshal stacks (hold, fix hash, arrival, flag); emergency (amber) and helo holds;
  - trails (5 dots at about 6 s), 30 s velocity vectors, tags with leaders (row 3 = `FLT ×n · LOW x.x` for collapsed formations);
  - collapsed formations (click to expand), conflict pairs in red with CPA projections and tag, off-scope edge triangles;
  - zoom about the cursor, drag pan clamped to 100 NM, live fits (50NM/CCA/DEP/APP), cursor BRG/RNG/lat-lon readout, 10 NM scale bar;
  - hooked-aircraft tools:
    - **vector helper:** BRG/RNG/HDG/TRK DIST/ETE, with the wheel setting speed;
    - **marshal assignment:** wheel picks the level, fix = angels + 15, EAT; a click assigns, draws the dim assignment, and fills the stack's levels table;
  - strips grouped Departure/Inbound/Marshal/Approach/Support (heuristic phases);
  - Info tab: BRC/FB/speed, wind over deck and down-the-angle, true wind, altimeter, temperature, 2K/10K winds;
  - Radials panel: add departure radials and stacks through the rule checks, base −/+, remove;
  - notifications (CONFLICT, LOW STATE < 4.0) with go-to and Ack;
  - seat pills with claim and release, VIEW ONLY, feed status, LOCAL/ZULU clocks, and an open-another-window button.
- **Verified in a browser:** against your Week 5 host recording through the fake hook (Playwright, 1700×950). Hook, assign, vector, claim and release all work. **Visual QA of styling is still yours.**
- **Not done yet:**
  - Display-settings dialog: the scale multipliers exist but are fixed at the defaults.
  - Layers panel; tag auto-placement; formation wheel-cycling; BRA ruler and CPA probe tools; Light/Case curve; frequencies popover; Reference popup; PriFly screens; Comms scripts.
  - Strip drag between phases.
  - A `manage_catcc` permission.

## Shared room state (2026-11-08)

- **What's shared:** radials plans (departure radials and stacks, per carrier hull) and marshal assignments now live in the room. They are validated by `cleanPlan`/`cleanAssignment` and broadcast to every subscriber.
- **Edit rights:** anyone signed in can edit until someone holds a seat on that carrier (or MC); from then on, only seat holders can. A seat in its disconnect grace still counts, so a reconnecting controller isn't locked out.
- **Cleanup:** an assignment is dropped when its aircraft leaves the picture.
- **Default stack:** until a carrier's plan is first edited, clients draw the CV-1 default stack locally. The first edit sends the full plan.
- **Persistence:** room state is in memory. It's lost on a room restart, and SDOBot deploys restart it. Persisting plans to Supabase per mission is backlog.

## Radar rebuilt as a faithful mockup port (2026-11-08)

The first radar pass was an approximation. The user compared it with the mockup, and it has been rebuilt from `Main.dc.html` with its exact markup values, colours and symbol geometry.

- **Code:** `src/components/catcc/radar/` (`Scope`, `RadarView`, `InfoPanels`, `Panels`, `types`).
- **Scope layers, in the mockup's order:** background svg, overlay svg, HTML flags/edge markers, traffic svg, HTML helper tags, then floating controls.
- **Now matches the mockup:**
  - **Header:** logo, module nav, Ship select (hull · nickname), CASE badge, seat pills with avatars, feed status, LOCAL/ZULU, Reference, Display settings, Displays.
  - **Floating toolbar:** select, BRA ruler, marshal, CPA probe and vector tools; Layers; Comms; zoom/RNG; fit buttons.
  - **Panels and dialogs:**
    - Frequencies popover, read from the ReadyRoom comms plan of the event nearest to now (`missions.mission_settings.comms_plan`; PRI/AUX columns from `radio`);
    - Layers panel with focus and other-carrier columns;
    - Display settings with live previews, applied on Apply and saved in localStorage;
    - Reference popup (Minimums, Approaches, Helo ops, Documents);
    - Comms scripts dialog, with fields filled from the selected aircraft and its assignment.
  - **Scope frame and overlays:**
    - lat/lon frame with edge labels, and the grid;
    - rings and compass;
    - CCAs, plus the other carrier's rings and CCA;
    - coordination line with the rotated pill.
  - **Per-carrier overlays:** BRC, FB, bolter, helo restrictions, CV-1/2/3, tanker, departures, arrivals and marshal. These are the mockup's SVG rotated by (BRC − 345), with labels kept upright. Other carriers are drawn from their **own** room plans, in blue.
  - **Radials manager:** pennant flags (HTML), on-scope placement with blocked wedges, a bow-on wedge and a candidate preview/flag, Edit/Cancel/status rows, highlight, and the expandable stack levels table.
  - **Traffic:**
    - trails as screen-constant dots and velocity vectors;
    - tags placed exactly as in the mockup, with the leader quadrant chosen away from the velocity vector;
    - formation hover ring, wheel cycling and member tags;
    - conflicts with projections, the CPA segment and label, pinned to the edge when off-scope;
    - off-scope triangles;
    - other-carrier traffic (nearest carrier within 60 NM) drawn in blue.
  - **Info tab:** Ship, Weather, and Light/Case. The sun and moon come from `core/env/astro.ts`, and the chart window is solar noon to solar noon at the ship. The case comes from night plus ceiling and visibility.
  - **Notifications:** CONFLICT, LOW STATE, LATE PUSH, COORD LINE and BRC CHANGE, all derived from live data.
- **Hold entry (backlog item 2, done):** `marshalRoute()` flies a turn-rate-limited path to the fix. The entry is straight-in, direct, teardrop or parallel by the 70° sectors, and the aircraft finishes established inbound at the fix. EAT uses that distance, and the hook tag shows the entry type.
- **Hook `env` message (30 s):** mission weather (cloud preset/base, visibility, fog, precipitation), every live ship group's route (used for "Next PIM turn" and the BRC CHANGE note) and the mission start time. These feed the Weather panel and the case. DCS has no dew point.
- **Seats:** the Mission Commander pill was removed from the radar header so it fits on one row. Seat access is being redesigned around qualifications (see the user's request below).
- **PriFly LSO and Boss:** both screens are ported and fed by live data, but the user has paused work on them. **Unverified:** deck placement of parked jets. In the Week 5 recording, jets on deck report headings of 340/160 while the ship is on 070, and nothing was placed during the check, so the deck-frame transform (`prifly/deck.ts`, `DCS_ORIGIN_X`) needs a look against a live carrier.

## Open requests from the user (not started)

- **Seat access tied to ReadyRoom qualifications**, with a settings UI that maps qualifications to seats. Seniority rules: a senior user can displace a junior one; an equal or junior user can only request a takeover, and the occupant must accept it in a dialog. Waiting on answers about the seniority source, the request timeout and who can view.
- **Marine view:** ship traffic, bathymetry and coastlines (DCS doesn't expose them, so external sources such as GEBCO and OSM/Natural Earth are proposed), PIM and waypoints for every friendly CSG, and a CSG formation manager (needs the Phase 2 command channel).

## Code layout (Phase 1 start)

```
src/catcc/core/            pure TS, no DOM/Node APIs; shared by the scope UI and the room service
  acmi/parser.ts           streaming ACMI line parser (chunk-boundary safe, escapes, continuations)
  acmi/state.ts            object table; T= transforms with reference offsets and partial updates
  tracks/trackStore.ts     radar tracks: kind/side/identity, GS/course/VS from history, 60 s history
  tracks/naming.ts         pilot-string parsing, carrier hull table
  geo.ts                   distance, bearing, destination
catcc-server/              Node 23.6+ runs these .ts files directly
  tacview/realtimeClient   Tacview real-time client (handshake, reconnect)
  tacview/replay.ts        .acmi/.zip.acmi reader and paced replay
  tacview/fakeServer.ts    serves a recording over the real-time protocol (dev without a live mission)
  cli.ts                   prints the picture around a carrier
```

Commands:

```
npm test                                                   unit tests (vitest)
npm run catcc:typecheck
npm run catcc:tacview -- --live dcs.fightingstingrays.com --focus CVN-72
npm run catcc:tacview -- --replay <file.zip.acmi> --speed 0 --until 2400 --summary
npm run catcc:tacview -- --serve <file.zip.acmi> --port 42675 --from 2380   (then --live localhost:42675)
```

## Coordination line (replaces the handoff's ship-to-ship bisector)
- Automatic line: separator between each carrier's protected areas (ship + each active marshal hold, holds scored as whole areas). Margins 5 NM for ships, 2 NM for holds. If a clean band exists the line runs midway across the widest one; otherwise (holds of the two carriers overlap) it minimises the weighted depth it cuts into the margins, ships weighted 25x holds. Every bearing 0-179 deg tried; near-ties lean toward the ships' mean course. The original bisector ran through the other carrier's marshal stack, and the first max-margin version broke down when holds overlapped.
- Manual override per carrier pair, stored in the room plan as `coord[otherHull] = { brg, off }` (off = NM from our ship toward the other). Set numerically or placed on the scope (line perpendicular to the bearing from our carrier to the cursor, at the cursor range; one click sets; Esc/right-click cancels) from the Coordination section of the airspace panel; Auto clears it. If only the other carrier set one, it is reframed into ours.

## Realistic radar update rate (option, off by default)
- Display settings → Radar. Hits repaint only when the antenna sweep passes their bearing from the reference carrier: AN/SPN-43 (the CATCC marshal/approach search radar on Nimitz-class), 15 RPM, 4 s scan. Own ship always current; new contacts appear on first paint; lost contacts drop when the sweep passes their last position; history dots are past paints (one per scan).
- Driven by the wall clock (performance.now) so the painter and the sweep line agree. The sweep line is a Layers toggle (Reference → Radar sweep, off by default), independent of the update-rate option: one thin line at range-ring weight.
- Telemetry arrives at 1 Hz, so a paint shows the newest frame (up to 1 s old), not an interpolated position.

## Stack names carry the hull number
- Stack A on CVN-71 is "71A" (E and H likewise: 71E, 71H) on the scope, panel and assignment tags. Stored ids stay single letters. New-stack ids skip E and H (they were colliding with the helo stack).

## Marine layer (coastline + bathymetry)
- Layers → Marine: Coastline (land shaded, shoreline stroked) and Bathymetry (m). Shared, not per carrier.
- Water shading: each contour level fills the region shallower than it with a faint blue, so the water lightens toward the shore and the deepest water is the scope background. Water shallower than 15 m (a CVN draws ~12 m) gets an extra light-blue tint as not navigable.
- Contour lines shade from light (shallow) to dark (deep) blue; depth labels (metres) every ~30 NM along lines over 10 NM, kept upright, knocked out of the line.
- Data built offline by `scripts/catcc-marine.mjs <GSHHS_f_L1.shp> <cache-dir>` into `public/catcc/marine/<Theatre>.json` (~5.9 MB in all; only the current theatre loads). Sources: GSHHG 2.3.7 full-resolution shoreline (LGPL, Wessel & Smith) simplified to ~0.07 NM; NOAA ETOPO 2022 15" relief (public domain) via the CoastWatch ERDDAP at 30" (1' for the big open-ocean theatres). Contours by marching squares on the grid (and on a deep-padded copy for the closed fills), one Chaikin pass, Douglas-Peucker; short deep-water speckle dropped.
- Theatre from the source; if absent or the carrier is outside that theatre's box, inferred from position (`core/dcs/theatres.ts`). Nevada and Afghanistan have no file.
- Paths are projected about an origin near our carrier and re-projected only after it moves 10 NM.

## Tacview replay clock
- The replay fake hook used to report theatre "Normandy" (UTC+0) so its clock passed as UTC, which made every local time read as Zulu. It now infers the theatre from unit positions and sends local time like DCS does.

## Light chart
- Elevation axis is linear (one scale fitted to the day's extremes) so the sun path is the true curve; the old power-law mapping had infinite slope at the horizon and clamped the night.

## Marine layer fixes + Geography layer
- Contour lines are now the depth-fill rings minus their run along the data border (one geometry), so shading and lines coincide; the shoreline is cut from the simplified land ring itself (no stray stubs).
- Depth labels: open lines every 40 NM; closed loops by perimeter (one per 120 NM, at least one; none under 10 NM), evenly spaced.
- Layers → Geography: Place names (Natural Earth 10m populated places) and Airports / airbases (OurAirports: large and medium airports, plus military fields by name). Aerodrome symbol (ring + four ticks; military gold with a filled centre), ICAO id and short name. Decluttered by view width (major only beyond 250 NM, all within 60 NM). Both sources public domain; stored in the same per-theatre file (format v3, `--places`/`--airports` generator options).

## Bullseye, runways, island names, carrier lock
- Bullseye: hook env carries `bulls` (DCS `coalition.getMainRefPoint`, blue and red); the Tacview replay hook reads the Navaid Bullseye objects. Layers → Reference → Bullseye (on). Cursor readout adds `BE brg/rng` from the blue bullseye.
- Airports drawn sectional-style from OurAirports runways.csv: the runway layout at true orientation, in a ring unless the field has a runway ≥ 8069 ft; enlarged to symbol size, true scale once larger. Fields without runway data keep the ring-and-ticks symbol.
- Island names from Wikidata (CC0; island, island group, atoll with ≥ 8 sitelinks; ranked by sitelinks), italic chart blue. Place/airport label sizes raised; they follow the "Other scope text" scale.
- Shoreline simplification tightened to ~30 m (it was ~130 m and looked blocky at full zoom). Data is now ~15 MB across theatres (Falklands 4.6 MB, Kola 3.5 MB); only the current theatre loads.
- Bathymetry slightly more transparent (fills, shallow tint, lines, labels).
- Centre-on-carrier toolbar button. Range presets and the button lock the view to the carrier; panning unlocks it (the view centre is then pinned to a lat/lon). Wheel zoom keeps the lock and zooms about the carrier view centre while locked.
- Light chart plots sin(elevation): exactly a sinusoid in hour angle. Degrees of elevation reverse sharply where the sun passes near the nadir (low latitudes), which drew a V at midnight.

## Round of fixes (bullseye, lock toggle, alerts, hatch, sweep, datalink, tags)
- Bullseye on replays: Tacview's reference point is a property of global object 0 ("0,ReferenceLongitude=…"), not a header line; the replay hook now reads it there (it was placing bullseyes at their raw offsets). Test added.
- Centre-on-carrier is a toggle: click while following to pin the view where it is; panning also unlocks.
- Low-state alerts only for carrier-capable types (`isCarrierCapable`: Hornets, Growler, Tomcat, Viking, Hawkeye, Greyhound, F-35C, Rafale M, Skyhawk, Goshawk, deck Seahawks). Conflict alerts unchanged.
- Conflict notification "go to" zooms to fit both aircraft now and at CPA (min 8 NM across).
- Hooked strip outline drawn over the card's own 1 px border (offset −1, radius 6): no dark sliver by the squadron bar.
- Unnavigable water (< 15 m) gets a screen-constant diagonal hatch over the tint.
- Sweep line is a CSS animation (compositor thread), phased to sweepAngle(); the JS-driven rotation hitched whenever the main thread was busy redrawing.
- Ship datalink (Display settings → Radar, needs realistic updates): every friendly carrier's SPN-43 (own phase) plus escorts within 30 NM of a friendly carrier with a known air-search radar (Aegis SPY-1 ~1 s revisit; Perry SPS-49 6 RPM) paint hits.
- Tag layout: covering any part of another aircraft costs far more than a longer leader; stickiness only for a still-clear placement; a 4th leader length.
- Bathymetry at the full ETOPO 2022 15" grid (fetched in 2° tiles), line tolerance ~0.07 NM. Data ~18 MB over all theatres; only the current theatre loads.

## Map performance, unnavigable outline, territorial limit
- Measured: panning with bathymetry on ran ~25 fps (40 steps in 2.2 s) vs ~160 fps off; zoom similar. The depth layers (7 stacked translucent full-map fills, lines, hatch) were re-rasterised on every view change and every carrier update.
- The marine layer (depth, land, places, airports, territorial limit) now renders in its own memoised SVG in the layer's origin frame with a 35 % overscan margin, snapshotted with its scale-dependent props. Pans and carrier drift move it with a compositor transform; wheel zoom scales the snapshot and redraws ~250 ms after the last step (or beyond 2.5x). Re-snapshot also when the view leaves the margin. Pan and zoom now match the marine-off frame rate (~165 fps in the test).
- Land fill opaque (the hatch showed through at 0.92).
- Unnavigable (< 15 m) areas outlined in light blue as well as hatched.
- Geography → 12 NM territorial limit (off by default): dashed violet line 12 NM from the shoreline, from a ~0.5 NM land raster and an exact distance transform. Approximate: the legal limit is measured from baselines (closing bays, straight baselines along island chains), which no open dataset provides.
- LSO pattern view: viewBox now includes the upper turn (it started below the arc's top).
- Zoom: redraws after a zoom took ~100–130 ms each (rasterising the depth layer). Depth fills are now non-overlapping bands (each level's region with the next-shallower one cut out, one even-odd path; opacity matches the old stacking), overscan reduced to 20 %, mid-zoom redraw only past 4x, and each snapshot builds level-of-detail paths: rings outside the snapshot dropped, points closer than ~0.5 px skipped. Worst frame in the zoom test 67 ms (one), others < 30 ms.
- Territorial limit v2: 12 NM from a baseline approximation (land closed by 12 NM: grow then shrink, bridging bays and island gaps up to 24 NM, the UNCLOS bay closing limit) instead of from the raw shoreline, so island groups get straight runs instead of overlapping-circle cusps. Simplified (~0.3 NM) rather than smoothed, like a charted limit; "12 NM" labels along it (perimeter-limited on closed loops).

## PIM / waypoints overlay
- Source: the hook `env` routes (every live ship group's mission route, already used for "Next PIM turn"); carrier groups on the focus carrier's side with at least 2 points.
- Layers → Ship → PIM / waypoints, own and other-carrier columns like the other ship layers (own on, others off by default). Radar sweep moved to the Ship group too (own ship only).
- Drawing: completed legs dim dotted, remaining legs dashed, drawn under the BRC line; waypoints numbered as in the ME (WP0 = start) with local ETA and the outbound course; PIM is a diamond interpolated along the leg whose ETAs bracket mission time (`pimAt` in core/env/weather.ts, tested), labelled with leg course/speed (speed from distance over ETA difference) and the carrier's distance off PIM, with a dotted line from the carrier to it. The diamond is not drawn while it sits under the ship symbol; a waypoint label next to the PIM is hidden.
- The replay hook's synthetic routes restart at the carrier every 30 s, so PIM sits on the ship there; real DCS missions use the planned ETAs.

## BRC / FB
- BRC starts at the bow of the (screen-constant) ship symbol, so it touches the ship at every zoom; FB's final approach ends at the IFLOLS, 3/4 SM astern on the final bearing (LSO handover), drawn as the ball with datum bars perpendicular to the FB.
- BRC and FB labels are radial flags like departure radials and marshal stacks (BRC outbound at 9 NM, FB inbound at 12 NM), for both carriers; the arrowhead and loose text labels are gone.

## Marshal entries (rework)
- Bugs found with a property sweep (`marshalSweep.test.ts`: 6 ranges × 12 bearings × 8 headings × 3 speeds × 3 hold sizes × 2 hands = 10,368 routes; every route must end at the fix, be continuous, bounded, go out on the correct side for its entry, and be established before the fix): 65 % of routes failed before the rework.
  - Transit: pure pursuit orbited a fix inside the turn circle, then flew straight for up to 1,000 NM (the long magenta lines). Now `direct()`: both turn senses simulated, shorter kept; a sense whose circle contains the fix flies straight until it doesn't; each try capped at distance + two orbits.
  - Join: the 3-try intercept gave up and jumped to the fix. Now `intercept()`: a capture law that holds a 45° cut to the lead point then flies an arc of 1.15 R onto the course (finite settling, no overshoot).
  - Teardrop and parallel angles: the textbook 30° / 45° assume standard-rate turns. With the CV 6-minute pattern (2-minute 180s, R = 2L/π) a 30° teardrop needs a 10+ NM leg to roll out on course. The offset is now θ = 2·atan(R / leg) (~35° standard rate, ~65° for the CV pattern), used for the teardrop leg and the parallel entry's intercept.
  - Parallel: the short turn to outbound could carry the aircraft onto the holding side; three ways onto the outbound leg (short turn, long turn, 30° cut) are flown and the shortest that stays on the non-holding side is kept.
  - Established at least 1.5 NM before the fix (0.5 NM after a parallel's intercept); otherwise the leg is lengthened, then a go-around out the inbound course (never needed in the sweep).
- Holding speed: aircraft slow to their max-endurance speed before the hold (`maxEnduranceKias`, approximate: Hornet 230, Tomcat 220, F-35C 250, Rafale 230, A-4 220, T-45 200, S-3 180, E-2 160, C-2 170, H-60 70, others 250). The entry is flown at that speed's TAS at the fix altitude with 1-minute legs and 2-minute 180s (`holdSpecForTas`), and the hooked aircraft's hold is drawn at that size. Fix time = transit at current speed with a 2 kt/s slow-down to holding speed (`marshalTimeS`) + entry at holding speed; the hook tag shows `→ HOLD 230 KIAS`.
- Cost: 0.27 ms per route on average (1.3 ms worst).

## Marshal entries: fly-by first turn
- Only the final inbound crossing of the fix is a fly-over. The entry's first turn at the fix is a fly-by (a fillet of the hold's radius begun R·tan(D/2) before the fix, rolling out on the line through it) when it is at most 150° and the entry's turn direction is the short way; otherwise the fix is flown over. Parallel entries go outbound on the radial (the first-turn sense is picked by side; the leg is lengthened if it does not establish).
- Direct and teardrop outbound headings are solved (θ near 0° / the 2·atan(R/L) offset) so the turn inbound rolls out on the course for the actual arrival, rejecting solutions with the leg on the wrong side; this removed the S-bend after the reversal (reported: a teardrop rolling out 1.2 NM short, then a 36° jog).
- Capture law is linear within 0.05 NM of the line (no chatter); a near-duplicate last point is replaced when snapping onto the fix.
- Sweep: correctness checks hold for all 10,368 routes; shape checks (outbound side, no S-bends > 10°) hold for every start ≥ 10 NM from the fix, with < 1 % slight misses closer in (29 routes, 2–7 NM starts, ≤ 1.2 NM to the wrong side or one 26° correction).

## Assigned marshal routes on hover
- After assignment, an aircraft's marshal route, fix hash and EAT tag show only while it is hovered on the scope (or its formation is), or its strip is hovered in the Strips list. Several at once (a flight) stack their tags in EAT order instead of overlapping.

## Datalink sources / Radar blindspots (sensors layers)
- Donors: friendly (focus carrier's coalition) ships with an air-search radar and airborne AEW (E-2, E-3, A-50, KJ-2000). AEW on deck / below 500 ft doesn't count. Ground radars are not included: the DCS hook exports aircraft and ships only.
- Ranges are open-source detection ranges vs a fighter-sized target (SPS-48E 160, SPY-1 175, APS-145 200, APY-2 215 NM ...), table in src/catcc/core/sensors/radar.ts.
- Coverage vs a target at altitude h = min(range, 1.23(√h_antenna + √h_target)) NM (4/3 earth). Ship antenna heights from the table; AEW uses its altitude. No terrain masking, no cone of silence.
- Blind spots: everything outside the union of coverage circles for the chosen target altitude (100 ft – 25K, picked under the layer row; default 1000 ft), tinted with the union edge dashed.

## Case I port hold (layer "Case I port hold", Approach group, off by default)
- Left-hand circle 5 NM across on the port side; position 1 (its 3 o'clock) is offset 0.5 NM to starboard of the ship so the deck is down the pilot's left side crossing 1 (offset from the squadron Case I diagram, not CV NATOPS; adjust OFF in portHold).
- Positions numbered in the direction of flight from overhead: 1 overhead heading BRC, 2 ahead, 3 abeam to port (reciprocal), 4 astern. Commence drawn from 3, descending through 4 to the initial (3 NM astern on the BRC, 800 ft). Numbering and commence point are assumptions pending the user's confirmation.

## Locations and seats (phase 1 of the seats / CDC / Bridge / Final work)
- Locations: CDC, CATCC, PriFly, Bridge, LSO. Seats are functions within a location (src/components/catcc/seats.ts).
- Layouts (Options dialog, per browser, default condensed): condensed = Strike / CATCC / Air Boss / OOD / LSO; expanded = AW, Strike, SUW / Air Ops, Departure, Marshal, Approach, Final / Air Boss, Mini Boss / OOD / Controlling LSO, Backup LSO, Writer.
- URL ?seat=<role>; old ?m=radar|lso|boss links map to CATCC / LSO / Air Boss. A seat not in the current layout resolves to its location's equivalent.
- Radar seats each have a preset (default layers for own/other carriers, zoom, tool) and their own saved layers (catcc.ov.<seat>); Layers → Reset restores the seat's preset. CDC seats open at the new THTR zoom (200 NM; max zoom-out raised to 440 NM wide).
- Seat roles added to the room protocol (room server must be restarted / rebundled). Carrier plans are editable by anyone until a CATCC seat is held for that carrier, then only by CATCC seat holders.
- Header: location tabs, seat tabs (expanded) with a holder dot, claim pill for the current seat only.
- Interfaces not yet built (Strike tasking, Bridge, Final/Approach displays) show the radar with the seat's preset for now.

## Seat interfaces (toolbars, sidebars, header)
- Each radar seat has a SeatUi (seats.ts): scope tools, range presets, comms scripts, the N-UP/T-UP switch, left tabs (info/strips) and right sections (dep, marshal, coord, deck, donors, helm, notes).
  - CATCC / Air Ops: everything. Departure: dep + coord. Marshal: marshal + coord. Approach: marshal + deck. Final: deck. CDC: radar donors (no comms scripts, THTR/50/CCA fits). Bridge (OOD): Info only on the left, Helm on the right, select/BRA/CPA tools, N-UP/T-UP; no strips, radials, stacks, coordination or notifications.
- Header holds only what every seat shares (location/seat tabs, source, ship, case, seat pill, feed, clocks, Reference, Displays, Options). Display settings moved to the scope's top-right buttons. PriFly/LSO status (deck clear/foul, LA/cat chips, live/sand-table, WOD) moved to a status bar under the header. Deck stats left the Info panel; a Deck section is in the CATCC, Approach and Final sidebars.
- Track-up (Bridge): the picture turns about the carrier so its heading is up. Map coordinates stay north-up; rp()/rpInv() convert to the turned frame used by the viewBox, HTML overlays and the mouse. Text is turned back upright with the CSS `rotate` property (.nr groups — tags, BRA, CPA labels, along-line labels — handle it in their transforms). Border scale hidden while turned; the marine layer re-snapshots when the turn changes. North pointer under the toolbar.
- Helm: ordered course/speed stored in the shared carrier plan (plan.helm); "Into the wind" fills 30 kt down the angled deck (course = wind + 9°). Orders aren't sent to DCS yet: the hook is read-only (needs a command channel in the Lua hook).
- Plan editing seats (protocol PLAN_ROLES): CATCC seats, OOD and mission commander, same rule on client and room server.

## Handoff 2 implementation (night of 2026-11-09)
Source: carrier-ops-handoff-2/HANDOFF-2.md and mockups (that file wins over this log where they differ).
- **Ownership and handoffs.** Every flight (keyed by DCS group, else track id) has an owner seat. Until a handoff completes, the owner follows the standard chain by phase and range (Strike > 50 NM → Marshal → Approach → Final ≤ 6 NM → LSO ≤ ¾ NM; Departure → Strike past 50 NM; pattern traffic is Approach/Final), mapped onto the staffing layout (condensed owners are locations). The owning seat offers a handoff, the receiver accepts, the sender can recall until then. Once handed off the owner sticks; when the flight reaches the next standard point the strip's Handoff button turns amber ("due"). Room: `handoff` / `accept` messages and an `owners` map; a user acts for a seat they hold or one nobody holds (roll-up). Strips: owner chip + handoff row; notifications: HANDOFF with Accept.
- **Shared ops state** per carrier (`ops` message, validated in src/catcc/core/room/ops.ts): PriFly's deck call, ICLS/ACLS + per-aircraft mode, lights, catapult status and queues, the CSG formation. Editable by anyone holding a seat on that carrier (or anyone while none are held). None of it is sent to DCS yet (the hook is read-only).
- **Deck status** is PriFly's call (CLEAR / FOUL + reason) or AUTO (foul while an aircraft is in the landing area). Shown on PriFly's deck bar, the Approach/Final deck panel and the LSO status bar.
- **Approach / Final** (CATCC seats: Radar | Approach/Final switch; Approach and Final open on it, Final at 12 NM, others 24): elevation and azimuth displays per the mockup; arrivals = aircraft inbound within 2,500 ft of the Case III profile; bolters = downwind (reciprocal FB ±40°, 0.5–5 NM abeam, below 3,000). Bolter slot: tBall(X) = (X−R0)/Vdw + Tturn + (X−¾)/Vfinal with real relative speeds (aircraft minus ship), Tturn from a 25° bank turn, Vfinal = 135 kt − ship speed; window keeps 60 s to both neighbours, options NOW/4/6/8 green/amber/red. ACLS lock-on inside 8 NM within 3°, acquiring to 10 NM. Upwind → downwind timing not built yet (open item 1).
- **PriFly**: deck bar (call, next arrival, wires set weight from type empty weight + fuel + 2,000 lb, barricade, lens, WOD), right sidebar PLAT placeholder + Launch (per-cat status READY/HOLD/SUSPEND/DOWN, queues of flights on deck, spotted aircraft, weight board/JBD, interleaved sequence) / Lights (DCS lights panel) / Air plan tabs. Mini Boss opens on the air plan.
- **Bridge CSG formation**: escorts = friendly ships within 30 NM nearer this carrier than any other, cruisers first (CG, DDG-1, DDG-2 …). Presets Flight ops / Transit / Combat from the mockup's placeholders; extra escorts start free. Station keeping ON STA / OFF x NM (point stations ±0.5 NM). CSG stations layer on the Bridge radar. Bridge lights (nav 1/2, room) live on the Bridge.
- **Dev**: `npm run catcc:replay:demo` adds three synthetic Hornets around CVN-71 (6 NM final, bolter downwind 2 NM left, Case III inbound), looping every 200 s.

## PriFly deck management: taxi network (night of 2026-11-11)
Asked for by the user: click an aircraft, hover a catapult or parking spot to see the route, click to send it; routes follow a fixed road system, not free-form paths; conflicts flagged on the route.
- **Road system** (`src/components/catcc/prifly/taxiNet.ts`, `ROADS` and `SPOT`): roads are plain polylines and are joined wherever they touch or cross, so retuning is editing coordinates. Modelled on the Supercarrier deck-crew routes but straightened and shared.
  - Main artery: one straight line from in front of EL 3 to the bow (deck-crew routes 9/10/14), bent 10° at its aft end to run along the patio clear of the parked noses. It fans out at x = 22.9 m to cat 1, the box and cat 2.
  - Waist crossing: leaves the artery where the EL 3 forward feeder meets it, crosses the landing area at 75° to the cat 3 line and on to the cat 4 line. Patio and EL 3 traffic share it. EL 4 and the finger feed the cat 3 line direct; EL 4 forward also sits on cat 4's line.
  - Catapult approaches are each track's own line extended aft, so aircraft arrive lined up.
  - Landing-area roll-out along the centreline, with a turn-round to starboard at x = 4 m that crosses the artery and carries on as the EL 1 aft feeder; straight ahead it leads to the crotch.
- **Destinations**: the four catapults and 20 spots. Elevator, Corral and Patio spots are the DCS spawn spots 5-16 (where players start); Point 1/2 are DCS recovery spots 11/12; Junk Yard 1/2, Box, Crotch 1/2 and Finger are placed by me in the user's traced areas. **No Six-pack / Street spots**: the artery runs through them (DCS spawn spots 1-4 and recovery spots 7-9 sit on it), so anything parked there blocks stern-to-bow traffic and is flagged. Open question for the user.
- **Solver**: cheapest path = distance + 0.12 m per degree turned, no turn over 125°; corners filleted at 8 m radius (less where two corners are close). An aircraft within 5 m of a spot or junction starts from it; otherwise it joins the best road within 60 m at a point ahead of abeam (off-road distance costs 1.5×, the leg must stay on deck and clear of the island). An aircraft may leave in any direction (it can pivot), with a small cost for the swing.
- **Conflicts**: an aircraft in the way is its fuselage line (±8.5 m); a route is blocked where its centreline comes within 5 m of one. Instructions are worked in the order given: an aircraft with an earlier instruction counts where it will end up (its destination, or holding 5 m short of what blocks it), everyone else where they are now. So the second jet sent to a catapult is clear to behind the JBD, and a jet parked across the route blocks it until it is given an earlier instruction. No rerouting round a blocker: the route is always the network's route.
- **Drawing**: clear part thick solid green, the rest thinner dashed amber from an amber dot; for the aircraft in hand also a dashed outline where it ends up (or holds) and a ring where the blocker is or will be. Destination tag over the board number. Road system shows while an aircraft is selected, or always with the new Taxi Network layer.
- **State**: `ops.taxi` (track id → `{ to, at }`), shared and persisted with the rest of the ops state; validated in `ops.ts`. **The room must be restarted / SDOBot redeployed (bundle rebuilt) before taxi instructions are accepted.** Sending to a catapult also queues the flight on it; removing it from the queue in the Launch tab drops the instruction; a flight queued from the Launch tab without an instruction taxis to that catapult after the explicit ones.
- Replaces the Taxi-to CAT buttons and `taxiToCat`. The free-hand Draw path tool is still there. Planning only: DCS can't be told to taxi an aircraft.
- Verified in a throwaway harness (BossView on a faked deck, headless Chromium), not against a live carrier.

## Pilot's deck kneeboard page (2026-11-11)
- **Where:** page 3 ("Flight Deck") of the ReadyRoom kneeboard (`/kneeboard`, OpenKneeboard). `src/components/kneeboard/DeckKneeboard.tsx`, model in `src/components/catcc/prifly/kneeboardDeck.ts`. It joins the CATCC room only while the page is shown, as a view-only client (no seat).
- **Pages:** OpenKneeboard keeps the page list it was first given; KneeboardLayout now re-sends the list when the GUIDs it has differ from the current ones, so new pages appear for existing users.
- **Whose aircraft:** the one on a carrier deck carrying the pilot's board number (ReadyRoom pilot record, else the room's lookup), a player's before an AI's; otherwise the pilot picks it from the player aircraft on deck (kept for the browser session). The carrier drawn is the one the pilot is aboard.
- **Drawing:** PriFly's deck drawing turned bow up (`DeckDrawing bowUp`: level labels turned back, scale bar left out), equipment layer only. The pilot's jet is magenta, the rest of their flight (same DCS group) grey with the tail (stabilators across the aft fuselage and the fins: `TAIL` in deck.ts) in cyan, everyone else dark grey. Board numbers level, on the jets.
- **Clearance:** the same `planTaxi` as PriFly, so the kneeboard and the Boss always agree. Magenta as far as clear, dashed amber beyond, a magenta hold bar ahead of the nose where the pilot stops, a dashed amber ring where the aircraft in the way is (or will be). Other aircraft's instructions in light grey, their destination under their board number.
- **Text:** TAXI CAT 1 (magenta) / "Hold short of 311 · clear for 155 of 168 m" (amber) or "Clear all the way"; for a catapult, "#2 FOR CAT 1 · behind 311" and the cat's line (spotted aircraft first, then in the order sent); the pilot's flight with each aircraft's instruction.
- **Not done:** no light theme for the deck itself (it is always the dark PriFly drawing); no audio or flashing when the clearance changes.

## Supercarrier taxi directors: test (2026-11-12)
- **Where the data is** (`CoreMods/tech/USS_Nimitz/scripts`): `GT.TaxiForTORoutes` (16 spawn spot -> catapult) and `GT.TaxiRoutes` (16 landing -> parking) in `USS_Nimitz_RunwaysAndRoutes.lua`; recorded jet paths in `trajectories_f18.lua` / `trajectories_f14_to.lua` (take-off) and `trajectories_f18_park.lua` / `trajectories.lua` / `trajectories_f14_taxi.lua` (parking); the yellow shirts in `crew.lua` `takeoff_crew` (members = positions; `takeoff_tasks` and `parking_tasks` per route = `{member_id, route_id, progress}` hand-overs, so directors handle both taxi-out and parking after landing); their behaviour tree `takeoff_logic.xml` is a single `Move` (loaded by name from C++).
- **Test** `catcc-dcs/deck-test/director-test.ps1` (one command per variant, `-Revert`; touches only lines/blocks marked CATCC-DIRTEST, refuses if another CATCC patch is in takeoff_logic.xml, e.g. a chock gate, or deck-test.ps1 is applied):
  - `-Hold Pause`: message + 20 s pause before every director task. Does the tree run on the client, when do tasks start (taxi out, parking), what does a held director do.
  - `-Hold Gate`: director waits while the pilot's draw arg 997 > 0.5 (launch gate arg; the room sets it for every player jet on deck while Air Boss/Shooter is seated). Take/leave the AIR BOSS seat to hold/release.
  - `-Reroute Traj|Routes|Both`: every spawn route bent 5 m left of travel halfway (sin^2 taper, `director-reroute.lua`); shows which data the directors steer by.
- Bend checked under fengari on the installed DCS files (`catcc-dcs/test/director-test.test.ts`); script dry-run on copies (apply, switch, revert = identical files). Not yet run in DCS.
- **Result 2026-11-14, hold:** `takeoff_logic.xml` does not drive the yellow shirts. With a 600 s Pause and a message before its Move, the hand-overs on taxi-out happened exactly as stock, no message ever showed. The taxi crew is compiled C++ (`wTakeoffCrew` in Flight.dll: task state, member locks, distance remaining, all synced between clients by network messages); it reads only the data tables in `crew.lua` (`takeoff_crew.takeoff_tasks` / `parking_tasks`). No behaviour-tree or script hook found for it, so no "hold position" through the deck crew. Reroute (data only) still to test.
- **Result 2026-11-14, reroute Traj:** bending `trajectories_f18.lua` / `trajectories_f14_to.lua` changed nothing. Tacview of the run (spot 1 → cat 1): the track stays within 1.05 m on average (max 2.4) of the STOCK trajectory 1, while the bent one was up to 6.4 m away. No sim binary names those files (only ModelViewer2 / the mission generator mention "trajectories"); they look like recorded reference paths, not live data. The same track also follows stock `GT.TaxiForTORoutes` route 1 to ~1 m at its vertices, so `-Reroute Routes` is next; its bend on route 1's 7 points is ~3–3.5 m at the S-turn, judged from Tacview rather than by eye.
- **Result 2026-11-14, reroute Routes: inconclusive.** The two runs from spot 1 differed by up to 15 m sideways at the S-turn (the Routes run swung wider to port, the bend's side, but far more than the bend), so a pilot's track can't show a 3–5 m change. Replaced by **deck route lines** (`catcc-dcs/deck-test/route-lines/`): each GT.TaxiForTORoutes route drawn on the deck as a flat strip with a pad on every route point, white = stock, red = the test bend (computed by running DCS's file plus `director-reroute.lua` under fengari, `routes.mjs`), built headless in Blender with ED's EDM exporter (`build_route_lines.py`; exporter writes Blender (x,y,z) as DCS (x,z,−y)), declared as non-colliding deck equipment (mod CATCC-RouteLines), placed as statics linked to the carrier at each route's spawn spot from F10 > CATCC route lines (`Scripts/CATCC/routelines.lua`, loaded by the hook when `Config/CATCC-test.lua` has `routeLines = true`; the hook's chock-test loader became a generic `testScript`). Install: `deck-test/route-lines/install.ps1` (Saved Games only, `-Uninstall`).
- **Result 2026-11-14, route lines in DCS:** work (height 20.15 as expected). Cold "Takeoff from ramp" spawned on spot 1, hot on spot 5 (lift2 p1, also cat 1); both within 0.5 m of their route's first point. Hand-offs went yellow shirt 1 → 2 → 3 (4 next). Tacview of the hot run: on the first forward leg (~25 m) the jet stayed within 0.3 m of the RED (bent) line, ~3 m off the white: the directors look to follow `GT.TaxiForTORoutes` (user to confirm by eye). The user got stuck once: 70 s stationary at ship (1–3, 3) on the centreline, 2.5 m from the red strip; the strips have no collision geometry, cause unknown.
- **How the catapult is chosen (data):** each spawn spot n has exactly one route n (its first point IS the spawn spot: no other fixed-wing spawn table in the file) ending at one catapult (`RunwayIdx`), and `crew.lua takeoff_tasks[n]` lists the yellow shirts for route n with the fraction of the route where each takes over. Cat 1: spots 1, 5, 9, 11, 14; cat 2: 2, 6, 10, 12, 13; cat 3: 3, 7, 15; cat 4: 4, 8, 16. Nothing to choose between at the salute, so blocking other cats can't redirect; the tables are read by compiled code at load, no runtime hook. Open: whether a second route from the same spot would be an alternative or just a 17th spawn spot.
- **Own deck crew, feasibility (2026-11-14, read from export tables only, nothing run):** the user's idea: native deck crew off, our own marshallers walking and signalling along PriFly's routes. Not a mission-script job (scripts place fixed objects: no walking, no animation). What the sim exposes to a native module (the method `pilot/native/catcc_chocks.cpp` already used: run-time lookup of exported functions, SEH-guarded, fail-open, carrier found through the object registry):
  - `animator_logic.dll` (walking + crowd avoidance, ORCA): `CharacterEnvironmentManager::instance()` (static) → `get_environments()` → `CharacterEnvironment::get_registered_objects()`; `AnimatedCharacterBase::get_name()`, `set_animation(name, name)` / `set_animation(int)`, `set_override_animation(name)`, `set_idle`, `SetPosition(wPosition3<float>)`, `show_attachment`, `get_animation_manager()`; `CharacterMovable::move_command_world(wPosition3<float>)` (walk to a point), `move_command(speed, pos, pos, callbacks, route points)`, `stop_move`, plus its own net sync (`net_send_start_move`, `send_pos_sync`); `PathFinder`; `CharacterEnvironment::add_static_obstacle`, `debug_draw`, `gui`, `load_debug_config`.
  - `animator.dll` (character engine): `AnimationManager::get_animation_names()` (the animation list: the files themselves are packed in `animations/*.edce`), IK (`set_effector`, `solve_ik`), `CharacterModelCookie::set_animation / push_animation / set_pose / add_gpu_attachment`.
  - `Flight.dll`: `wCarrierCrew::create_crew(Lua::Config&)` (crews are built from the Lua tables), `taxi(pos, bool) -> TaxiSignState`, `set_LA`, `gui`, `debug_draw`; `wAirbase::procCrewMoveMember`, `procSyncCrewMembersPos`, `procMsgCrewConfigHash`, `procMsgTakeoffCrewDebugStateEnable`. `Transport.dll`: `woShip::populate_carrier_crew`, `requestDeckCrewPermission`, `Get_TaxisForTO`.
  - Data side: `GroundCrew.lua` has a commented-out `DebugDraw` block (draw_goal, show_nav_grid, route_height…) whose keys are still in the shipped `animator_logic.dll`; crew members are data (`model_name`, `unit_type`, `livery`, `position`); the deck-crew switch is per PC (`options.lua` plugins.Supercarrier.deck_crew). Node names of the behaviour trees and the animation names are not readable as plain strings in the binaries.
  - Make-or-break unknowns: do the yellow-shirt characters exist with deck crew off (else: create them, or keep it on with empty task lists); does the native logic override commands given to its members; does a commanded move show on other PCs (ED's sync, or our own room → pilot hook channel); struct layouts (`wPosition3`, `ed::string`) and telling characters apart (exported vftables). Staged experiments proposed: 0 DebugDraw + look with deck crew off; 1 read-only native listing (characters, animation names); 2 one character plays an animation / walks to our point; 3 two PCs; 4 our marshalling logic in the room / pilot hook.
- **Crew test built (2026-11-14), awaiting the first DCS run** (`catcc-dcs/deck-test/crew-test/`; the user: pack every test that needs only one PC into the first experiment):
  - Native module `native/catcc_crew.cpp` → `bin/catcc_crew.dll` (run-time lookup of 21 exported DCS functions, SEH-guarded, fail-open). Finds characters three ways: the crew environments (`CharacterEnvironmentManager::instance` → `get_environments` → `get_registered_objects`), the object registry (kind 0x20 as the chock module; other kinds on request), and a scan of the process's memory for objects whose RTTI class derives from `AnimatorLogic::AnimatedCharacterBase` (whole-object check: every base's virtual table must agree). Everything it knows about an object comes from MSVC RTTI (class, bases, offsets); calls go only to exported non-virtual members with `this` at the right base, plus the virtual `GetPosition` through the object's own table once its slot is found by the exported address. Checked at run time, not assumed: `ed::string` = std::string layout (names must read back), `wPosition3<float>` layout (12 floats or 4×16 bytes, by orthonormal axes), ship frame vs world (a walk is refused if our ship coordinates put the character > 5 m from where DCS says he is). Commands: `set_animation(int)` (index cross-checked with `get_animation_index` by name), `set_override_animation`, `show_attachment`, `move_command_world`, `stop_move`; reads: name, id, state, state name, positions, animation list, own memory (hex).
  - Verified without DCS: `native/catcc_crew_selftest.cpp` (55 checks on stand-in classes carrying DCS's class names, same compiler: RTTI walk, base offsets, strings both ways, values returned by value, virtual by slot, the three scans, handles, walk maths; build.cmd builds the DLL only if it passes), `native/smoke.py` (the built DLL under DCS's own lua.dll: every function, errors as nil + message, stack level), `test/crewtest.test.ts` (every looked-up name is in the installed DLLs' export tables; the data patch on DCS's own GroundCrew.lua; menu + hook end to end with a stand-in module).
  - Data patch `groundcrew-patch.lua` via `director-test.ps1 -Crew Draw|People|Full` (appended to GroundCrew.lua, CATCC-DIRTEST markers, revert byte-identical): `environment_options.DebugDraw` on; one added take-off crew member "catcc-00" (green shirt, 14 fwd / 27 stbd, declared like the cat crews' green shirts, same four wand attachments, in no hand-off list); `takeoff_tasks[1]` (spawn spot 1) emptied of its yellow shirts, brown kept, spot 5 stock. The script now reads/writes DCS's files as Latin-1 (byte-transparent) and puts a file with nothing of ours left back to its backup byte for byte.
  - Hook `Scripts/CATCC/crewtest_hook.lua` (own loader in Scripts/Hooks, on with `crewTest = true`) + F10 menu `Scripts/CATCC/crewtest.lua`: list people (falls back to the memory scan), target (nearest / nearest yellow / ours), play next signal, walk to 16 m ahead of the nose, stop, watch, arm pose, light wands, memory scan, registry scan. Lists the people once per mission by itself; a recorder logs every state change / half-metre move of everyone found and the jet's position at 1 Hz. Log: `Saved Games\DCS\Logs\catcc-crewtest.log` (flushed per line; native writes before and after each call into DCS).
  - Install: `crew-test/install.ps1` (Saved Games, no admin) + `director-test.ps1 -Crew Full -Spawn Shift` (admin).
- **Result 2026-11-14, crew test run 1: DCS crashed on the first simulation step, caused by our data patch.** The patch emptied spawn spot 1's hand-off list (`takeoff_tasks[1]`); the player's jet spawned on spot 1; read of address 0 in Flight.dll inside the take-off crew's update (stack: `wGroundCrewOwner::update_carrier_crews` → wTakeoffCrew code). From the crash dump: the crew keeps one 32-byte record per route = { vector of hand-offs (24 B), int brown_id, int brown_route_id }; route 1's record read {0, 0, 0, 14, 0} (the emptied list next to its own brown_id 14), route 2's was intact (2 hand-offs, 15 / 1). The faulting code takes the first hand-off unchecked (hand-off record 12 B: member_id byte @0, route_id int @8; member looked up in the crew's member vector at +0x80). So **every route must keep at least one hand-off: native directing can't be switched off for a spot by emptying its list**, and the list is used at once (not at the salute). The emptying is gone from `groundcrew-patch.lua`; a test now asserts no variant touches a hand-off list. Which spot the player gets is not ours to choose (spot 1 here, 5 the run before): no per-spot destructive changes.
  - Also learned from that run: **the spawn shift works for spawning** (jet at 20.90 fwd / 5.60 stbd, nose −0.68 / −0.73 = spot 1 moved along its first leg); **the added person was created** (the crew's member vector held 27 pointers = 26 + ours; not yet seen on deck); the native module ran in live DCS (all 21 functions found; RTTI read real objects: woAIPlane, woShip, wHumanAircraft with their bases; 2 crew environments, empty while the mission sat paused before "Fly"; registry kind 0x20 = aircraft and ships only); **Flight.dll is packed on disk** (.text has no raw data, sections UPX0/1/2: no static disassembly, which is also why its strings were invisible) so crash dumps are the way to see inside (`deck-test/tools/minidump.py`; `pe_exports.py` for export tables); the test mission **forces the deck crew option** (`mission.forcedOptions.Supercarrier` deck_crew = 1), so a "deck crew off" run needs that changed in the mission.
  - Changed after it: the hook lists people only once the simulation has run 8 s (model time) and goes on to the memory scan by itself if the quick scan finds nobody; the memory scan reads only pages that are resident (QueryWorkingSetEx), so it neither creates untouched pages nor pages anything in.
- **Result 2026-11-14, crew test run 2 (35 min, ~100 native commands, no crash).** What the log (`catcc-crewtest.log`) and the user's eyes showed:
  - **Finding people:** once the simulation runs, `CharacterEnvironmentManager` lists them all (2 environments = the mission's 2 carriers, 51 characters each: 27 `woCarrierTakeoffTechnician` = 14 yellow + 12 brown + ours, 24 `woCarrierTechnician` = the four cat crews; both classes: `woCarrierTechnicianMovable` → `AnimatorLogic::CharacterMovable` → `AnimatedCharacterBase`, + viObjectNode/viObject/Registered @0, `Graphics::ModelInstance` @48, `ISceneObject` @120, `AnimatorLogic::IEnvObject` @256, `IAnimatedEntity` @1168). The object registry holds them as **kind 0x10** (102 objects, all characters); other kinds: 0x1 static structures, 0x2 lLandVehicle, 0x20 aircraft + ships, 0x100 pylons, 0x2000 lights. Memory scan: 7.6 s, 6.8 GB read, nothing new. `wPosition3<float>` = four 16-byte vectors; ed::string = std::string layout (all 102 names read).
  - **Added by data: works.** "catcc-00" stood on deck (the user saw the lone green shirt between elevators 1 and 2), on both carriers, and took every command. Not touched by native logic afterwards (stayed where we walked him).
  - **Animations: work.** `set_animation(index)` plays on any take-off crew member (the user saw a brown and a yellow shirt do each signal). 330 animations + 16 blend spaces (`bs_sign_taxi_fwd/left/right`, `bs_sign_stop`, `bs_pass_control`, `bs_turn`, `bs_move*`, and `night_bs_*`); names are lower case; the name list (346, alphabetical, blend spaces included) is NOT in index order: `get_animation_index(name)` is the index to use. Director vocabulary present: `sign_taxifwd`, `_l`, `_r` (+ body-angle variants `_0`, `_45l`, `_45r`), `sign_taxifwdloop_0/_l45/_r45`, `sign_setbrakes` (+ `_0`, `_l45`, `_r45`, idle variants), `sign_releasebrakes`, `sign_emergstoploop`, `sign_passcontrol_l/r` (+ `_l0/45/90/135`, `_r…`, `passcontrol2_l/r`), `sign_pointl/r`, `sign_attention_taxi`, `sign_foldwings`, `sign_unfoldwings(_short)`, `sign_lowerlaunchbar`, `sign_raiselaunchbar`, `sign_runupengine_l/r`, `sign_catapultlaunch_l/r`, `sign_launch_*`, `sign_ok_l/r`, `sign_salut`, `sign_handsup`, `sign_dontknow*`; night versions (`night_sign_*`) and `night_stick_armpose(_l/_r)`, `holdbar_armpose_l/r`; walk / run / slow-walk / turn / crouch / idle sets. Full list in the log.
  - **Light wands: work** (`show_attachment` RightStick / LeftStick / RightLight / LeftLight on, off: seen).
  - **Walking: works, exactly.** `move_command_world` takes true world coordinates; the green shirt walked 33.4 m in a straight line at ~1.75 m/s and stopped 0.0 m from the target; yellow-02 the same over 16.7 m. No deck limit: a man told to go to a point off his ship walks there over the water (see next point).
  - **Two carriers, one bug of ours:** each carrier has its own 51 people at the same ship coordinates, and the hook picked "the nearest" by ship coordinates across both. So several orders went to the OTHER carrier's men (unseen), whose walk target was a world point on the player's carrier: three of them walked off their ship toward it (yellow-01 was 1.9 km out after 18 min). This is why the user saw nothing from the first yellow-shirt orders, and why "the marshalling director did not comply": that order went to the other ship's yellow-00. **The override test on a busy director has therefore NOT been done.** What was seen of native logic: an idle director we had moved (yellow-01, first hand-off of route 1) was walked back to his own position by DCS about 80 s later; directors walk to their positions as the jet advances (yellow-00 → 35.6 / −11.9, yellow-02 → 47.3 / 11.5 for route 1); the brown shirt walks back to his station after the salute; a parking task for a landing AI jet moved exactly the `parking_tasks` members.
  - **Spawn shift: works end to end.** Spawned on the moved spot 1 (20.9 / 5.6), brown shirt came, native yellow shirts marshalled and handed off.
  - **Deck crew option off** (it is forced by the mission): only the 48 cat-crew characters exist; no yellow or brown shirts and not our added person (he is in the take-off crew). No order was sent to a cat crew member in that run (the target mode was still "our added person" / "yellow": our menu, not DCS).
  - **Debug draw: nothing shows** in the release build. **Arm pose: not tried** (our lookup was case-sensitive, the names are lower case). `get_current_state_name` only says "idle"; `get_state` is 0 standing / 1 moving. `GetPosition` could not be found by address in the technicians' tables (overridden): world position to be taken through `Graphics::ModelInstance`'s table instead, as the chock module does.
- **Crew test, second version (2026-11-14, built; run the same day, results in the next entry):**
  - Ships told apart: every scan gives each man his own world position (slot 3 of his `Graphics::ModelInstance` table, a true virtual call) and the world position of what he is attached to (`get_parent`, read the same way: his ship); the hook keeps to the people whose ship is where the player's ship is (fallbacks: their own position, then everyone, said on screen). A walk order to a man whose world position is not where our ship coordinates put him is refused by the module (this is what would have stopped the men who walked into the sea).
  - `play` takes an animation NAME (index from `get_animation_index`); `walk` takes a facing (ship coordinates) for the end of the walk; arm pose names lower case; the target starts at "nearest" in every mission, steps nearest > yellow > green shirt > cat crew and skips kinds nobody on the ship matches.
  - F10 > CATCC crew test: list; target change; submenu of ten named signals (come ahead loop / come ahead / turn left / turn right / stop / emergency stop loop / release brakes / pass control / fold / unfold wings); play next signal; walk to 16 m ahead of the nose and face the jet; stop; arm pose; light wands; **demo: our green shirt marshals me** (walks to 25 m ahead of the nose, faces the jet, then come ahead / turn left / turn right by where he is off the nose (> 8°) and stop at 13 m, all worked out in the hook four times a second; turn and stop signals re-sent every 3 / 5 s, the loop once); demo stop.
  - Still to learn from the run: does a director who is busy marshalling obey a signal / a walk order, and for how long; do signals run on or play once; which turn signal is the pilot's left; does the man face the jet after a walk (is his x axis "forward"); arm pose; orders to a catapult crew member; the demo as a whole.
  - Idea parked until then: keep the deck crew option on and get DCS's own directing out of the way either by never letting the salute through (the pilot mod already holds it for the chock hold; the native sequence starts on it) or by giving every route a single hand-off to a hidden dummy member (inside the island footprint, x −64…−37 / z 22…37 in the deck polygon) with a one-point walking route. Not built: it changes hand-off lists (the kind of change that crashed run 1) and the override result decides whether it is needed.
- **Crew test, run 3 (second version, 2026-11-14 12:16–12:31, two missions, deck crew on; the user's report + `catcc-crewtest.log`):**
  - Ships told apart correctly this time: 102 characters, 51 on the player's ship; every order went to a man on the player's ship (frame check 0.00–0.01 m on every walk).
  - **Signals play once** when sent with the character's own `set_animation(int)` (the user; the "loop"-named ones not judged by eye). **Left / right are the director's own:** `sign_taxifwd_l` is the PILOT'S RIGHT turn, `sign_taxifwd_r` the pilot's left (the user).
  - **Walk and face: works.** brown-0-11 6.5 m and yellow-01 4.6 m to 16 m ahead of the nose, each stopped 0.0 m from the point and faced the jet (the character's x axis is "forward").
  - **Demo: followable.** catcc-00 walked 41.0 m (27 s) to 25 m ahead of the nose, stopped 0.0 m from the point; "come ahead" sent once at 12:25:11, the user taxied 11.5 m to him from 12:25:45 and got the stop at 13.0 m. No turn was needed (he stayed within 1° of the nose). The demo ran with DCS's own sequence running beside it (the user saluted to get rolling): yellow-00 walked to his own station and brown-0-11 walked off, as on any native launch.
  - **A director who is marshalling obeys.** Second mission: salute at ~12:27:24 (yellow-00 to his station, brown-0-11 off), the jet left standing on its spot, so yellow-01 (18.7 m ahead of the nose; `takeoff_tasks[1]` lists yellow-01, yellow-00, yellow-02 in that order, hand-offs at progress 0.181 / 0.314 / 0.578) was the route's director on duty. 12:28:25 `sign_emergstoploop`: he played it (arms crossed), once. 12:28:49 walk order: he walked 4.6 m, exact, faced the jet, and stayed there until the mission ended 2 min 43 s later. Both men we moved stayed where we put them until the native sequence next had an order for them: in mission 1 brown-0-11 stood where we had sent him for 72 s, until the native launch sequence began (12:25:32: walked back to his own start, then off on his route, with yellow-00 going to his station; the user had saluted to get rolling for the demo). So, as far as seen, the native logic gives orders at its events (the salute, hand-offs) and does not hold a man in place between them. (The "would not break out of his loop" of run 2 was the two-carrier bug.)
  - **A catapult crew member obeys a walk:** cat 3's shooter walked 27.0 m to the point, 0.0 m off, and stayed.
  - **Arm pose: call returned, nothing seen** (`night_stick_armpose_r` on that shooter, daylight, no wand shown). It is the arm held for a light wand at night (GroundCrew.lua: `Night_Stick_ArmPose_L/R`, `Holdbar_ArmPose_L/R` as `override_animation`); `CharacterModelCookie::set_override_animation` takes it only if the animation is declared as an override (else it clears the override). Left for night work; removed from the menu.
- **How DCS's character animation works (read from animator_logic.dll / animator.dll 2.9.31 with `dumpbin /disasm`, 2026-11-14; neither is packed, Flight.dll is):**
  - Each character has an `Animator::CharacterModelCookie` (pointer in a member at +0x70 of `AnimatedCharacterBase`; no exported getter; `set_override_animation(string)` begins `mov rcx,[rcx+70h]`). It derives from `model::ModelCookie` and `Animator::PropertyHolderImpl` (named float parameters) and holds a stack of `ShaderAnimation` records (0x44 bytes: id, +4 start, +8 duration, +0xC end, +0x10..0x18 loop, +0x1C/+0x20 blend ratio, +0x24/+0x28 transition, +0x30/+0x34 override).
  - `AnimatedCharacterBase::set_animation(int i)` = `cookie->set_animation(i, (float)Animator::get_model_time(), false, true)`. The third argument is the **loop flag**: the engine's own walking loop (`bs_move`) is applied with it on (transition word 0x0101), its one-shot walk start (`bs_move_start`) with it off (0x0100). That is why every signal we sent played once.
  - `set_animation(string name, string override)`: index by name (`get_animation_index` lower-cases its argument), played with the flag off, then the override. `set_idle(float, string)`: the idle the character falls back to (a generated timeline of idle variants). `AnimationController::change_animation_state(float, string, bool)` and the `AnimationStateMachine` exist (the animation graph: states, transitions with `loop`, `transition_duration`, `next_animation_mode`; the F/A-18 pilot's graph is readable at CoreMods/aircraft/FA-18C/animations/animations/typeV/graph/fa18c_anim_graph.lua, the deck crew's is inside `animations.edce`).
  - Every animation is an `Animator::Animation` ("blend space"): 330 plain ones plus 16 real blend spaces with named axes (`get_x_axis_name`, bounds, defaults), sampled at the value of the cookie's parameter of that name. `AnimationManager::get_animation_duration(name)` gives a plain animation's length.
  - The engine's debug inspector (`AnimatedCharacterBase::gui`) reads what a character plays with `cookie->get_animation(t)` → `manager->get_animation(record)` → `get_id()/get_name()`: the same calls give us a live readout.
  - The catapult crew's director does his come-ahead / left / right by replaying one-shot `Sign_TaxiFwd / _L / _R` from the behaviour tree (common.xml, `start_time_shift="-0.88333"`); the take-off crew's directors are driven from Flight.dll (packed), presumably through the `bs_sign_*` blend spaces: to be recorded, not guessed.
  - Also exported and not used yet: `CharacterMovable::net_send_start_move / net_send_start_stop / send_pos_sync / sync_pos / get_net_state / set_net_phantom` (the characters' own network sync), and constructors `CharacterMovable(viObjectNode*, const char* model, wPosition3<double> const&, float)` (a character made in code, no data list needed: the fallback if people cannot be added by data with the deck crew option off).
  - GroundCrew.lua has `static_crew = crews.static_crew`; crew.lua ships no such list; ModelViewer2.exe reads `static_crew.members` with the take-off crew's member loader. Catapult crews are `crews.crew[1..4]`, six members each, behaviour tree common.xml addressing members by name.
- **Crew test, third version (2026-11-14, built; run the same day, results in the next entries):** module `crewtest-3` (38 DCS functions looked up; self-test 81 checks; `catcc-dcs/test` 125 tests).
  - Module: `play(h, name, mode)` with mode 2 = through the cookie with the loop flag on (blend spaces playable too); `anim(h)` = what he plays this instant (name, start, length, loop values, blend coordinates); `animinfo(h)` = every animation's length and every blend space's axes; `prop(h, name [, v])` = a blend parameter of his cookie; `time()`. The cookie's offset is read out of `set_override_animation`'s first instruction at run time (8- or 32-bit form, must be followed by `test rcx,rcx`), and what the pointer leads to must say by its own type information that it is a `CharacterModelCookie`; otherwise the new functions refuse and the rest works as before.
  - Hook: the recorder now also logs, twice a second, every signal animation anyone on the player's ship starts or ends, with its length, loop values and blend coordinates (the native directors' vocabulary, for one native-marshalled taxi); animation lengths and blend-space axes logged once a mission.
  - **Signal test** (F10, automatic, ~75 s): our green shirt comes to 14 m ahead of the nose and is given, in turn: come ahead once (as before); come ahead / pilot's left / pilot's right / set brakes / emergency stop with the loop flag; `bs_sign_taxi_fwd` with its x axis swept across its range through the cookie parameter of that name; `bs_sign_stop`. What he is seen playing (the readout) decides each verdict (ran on / ended by itself / not played), in the log and in one closing message.
  - **Demo** corrected: pilot's left = `sign_taxifwd_r`, right = `sign_taxifwd_l`; every signal sent held (loop flag), and sent again when the readout shows it has ended anyway (by its length if the readout is not available), so it is continuous either way; back to idle at the end. Signals submenu: held come ahead / left / right / stop / emergency stop, one-shots, back to idle. Arm pose item removed.
  - Data: `-Crew Extra` (not applied; the last, optional test) = Full + "catcc-c0" as a seventh member of catapult crew 1 (all nine fields a catapult crew member has, no routes, as crew 4's white2) + "catcc-s0" in a `static_crew` list, at 14 fwd / 29 and 31 stbd. Question: can people of ours exist with the deck crew option OFF. Untried lists: may upset DCS at mission load.
  - To learn from the run: which way of holding a signal works; whether the blend parameter steers a blend space; **whether the catapult crew takes a jet that taxied there without a salute with the deck crew option on** (then DCS's directors never start, and its 26 take-off crew people stand idle and take our orders); what the native directors play (one saluted, native-marshalled taxi); with `-Crew Extra` and the option off, who of ours exists.
- **Crew test, run 4 (third version, 2026-11-14 13:15–13:26, deck crew on; the user's report + log):**
  - **The loop flag works.** Every signal sent through the cookie with the flag on ran until replaced (readout: loop value 1; one-shots 0). The user: all looked right except (a) the turn signals `sign_taxifwd_l/_r` looped as whole clips: the pointing arm drops after three waves and comes up again (the clip is 3.17 s with a raise and a lowering at its ends), (b) the blend spaces played but the torso did not turn.
  - Why (b): a blend space reads its parameter when it is applied and when `CharacterModelCookie::update_properties(t)` is called; we wrote "torso_angle" 48 times without that call, the record stayed at 0, and the NEXT blend space started at the last value written (−45). (A one-shot's record also stays current after it has ended: "is he still playing it" must look at the loop value / end time, not the name.)
  - **Blend-space axes** (read from the game): `bs_sign_taxi_fwd / _left / _right`, `bs_sign_stop` and their `night_` versions: x "torso_angle" −45..45; `bs_pass_control`: x "angle" −135..135; `bs_turn`: "turn_angle" ±180; `bs_move`: "curvature" −1..1, "speed" 0.43..3.81; `bs_move_start`: "start_heading", "speed"; `bs_move_stop_ls/rs`: "final_heading", "speed". Signal lengths are in the log (e.g. `sign_taxifwdloop_0` 0.83 s, `sign_taxifwd_l_0` 1.33 s, `sign_attention_taxi` 1.33 s, `sign_taxifwd_l` 3.17 s, `sign_taxifwd` 5.33 s, `sign_setbrakes` 4.67 s).
