  - **F8 > Wheel chocks > Remove is refused with the deck crew on** (the user; same as the cat-hold tests of 11-13): with the option on, the salute is the only way to get rolling, and the salute starts DCS's directors. So "deck crew on, never salute" is not a way to keep the native directors out.
  - **What DCS's own people play, recorded on the saluted taxi from spot 1 to catapult 1** (the recorder; this is the vocabulary to copy):
    - Salute: the brown shirt plays `bs_pass_control` once (2.0 s, angle 71: towards the first director), then `idle`, and walks off.
    - Each deck director, on taking the jet: `sign_attention_taxi` once (1.33 s; the next signal starts 1.3–1.4 s after it), then `bs_sign_taxi_fwd` / `bs_sign_taxi_left` (loop value 1) with the torso angle changing all the while (−15 … +33 as the jet came round) and the record's start moving on by one length every cycle (that is `update_properties` keeping the phase, i.e. they call it every tick). Handing over: `bs_pass_control` once (2.0 s; angles 55.8, 74.0, 172.8), then `idle` (loop value 1); the next director's attention begins when the 2 s are over.
    - Through the whole right-hand turn out of spot 1 the directors played `bs_sign_taxi_left`: DCS's "left" is the director's left = the PILOT'S RIGHT (as the user saw for `sign_taxifwd_l`).
    - Angles, fitted on yellow-01 and yellow-00 (constant to within 2°): angle = the way he faces − the bearing from him to what he looks at (bearings from the bow towards starboard; positive = it is to his left). The torso looks at a point 4 m ahead of the jet's own position along its nose (the pilot); pass control looks at the next director (yellow-01 → yellow-00: 55.7 worked out, 55.8 played).
    - The catapult crew's director (behaviour tree) uses the plain clips with a **loop range**: `sign_taxifwd_l` / `_r` loop 0.833–2.17 s of 3.17, `sign_taxifwd` 0.833–4.17 of 5.33, `sign_unfoldwings` 1.83–2.83, `sign_lowerlaunchbar` 2.5–4.17, shooter `sign_launch_0001_01_p1` 6.17–8.17, `…_p2` 6.83–7.67, white shirts `crouchu_signok2` 2.67–4.33: the waving part only. (That is the engine's own answer to (a); the range is the second and third "loop" values of the record. Not built: it needs `Animation::apply` called with a transition record of our own.)
- **Crew test, bonus run (2026-11-14 13:28–13:32, `-Crew Extra`, deck crew option OFF):**
  - The mission loaded. 25 people on the ship: the 24 catapult crew and **catcc-c0**, the man added to catapult crew 1's member list (class `woCarrierTechnician`, "1: catcc-c0", no routes). catcc-s0 (the `static_crew` list) was not created: dropped.
  - catcc-c0 walked 35.4 m to 16 m ahead of the nose, took the light wands and the whole signal test (all eight steps played as sent).
  - The user: with the wands shown he does not hold them properly, and guesses there are two sets of signals. There are: every director signal and blend space has a `night_` version, and the arm that carries a wand between signals is the override animation `night_stick_armpose` (`_l`, `_r`).
  - **So people of ours can exist with the deck crew option off: as extra members of a catapult crew's list.** Not seen yet: catapult 1's crew launching a jet with extra members in its list.
- **Crew test, run 5 (fourth version, 2026-11-14 14:07–14:16, `-Crew Extra` with four people, deck crew option OFF; the user: "no issues"):**
  - 28 people on the ship: the 24 catapult crew and catcc-c0 … c3. Four extra members in catapult crew 1's list load.
  - Signal test: 15 of 15 steps played as sent. The torso follows: swung through the parameter, the playing record's angle went −45.0 … 44.8 (77 writes). Turns as blend spaces: seamless (the user). Pass control to either side: played at ±90. Night: `night_stick_armpose` with a wand in each hand looked right (the user noticed nothing out of place); the night signals played.
  - Demo with a hand-over: catcc-c0 (first director, 12 m ahead / 6 m right, torso −10.3 at the start) took the jet, passed control at 20.9 m from the point (angle −128.7, towards the last director), catcc-c3 took it 2 s later and stopped it; the user: the hand-off looked correct.
  - **Catapult 1 hooked the jet up and launched it normally** with our four extra members in its crew's list (the user; the log has the crew's whole sequence).
  - So, on one PC with the deck crew option off: people of ours exist (extra members of a catapult crew's list), walk to exact points, play DCS's own director signals with the torso on the pilot, hand over, work at night with wands, and the catapult crews are unaffected. Not known: what other players see in multiplayer (each PC makes its own crew characters).
- **Crew test, fourth version (2026-11-14, built; run the same day, results in the entry above):** module `crewtest-4` (39 DCS functions; `catcc-dcs/test` 126 tests).
  - Module: writing a blend parameter now also calls `CharacterModelCookie::update_properties(model time)` (only for a man who stands: a walking man has the walk's own blend space on his stack), so a playing blend space follows its parameter; `override(h, "")` takes the arm pose off; `state` also returns the way he faces.
  - Hook: the directors' signals are DCS's own (`sign_attention_taxi`; `bs_sign_taxi_fwd`; pilot's left = `bs_sign_taxi_right`, right = `bs_sign_taxi_left`; `bs_sign_stop`; `bs_pass_control`), the torso angle worked out ten times a second from where he stands, the way he faces and where the pilot is; one-shots return to idle when over; a held signal the readout shows replaced is sent again. **Night mode** for the target: wands shown, `night_stick_armpose` held, the `night_` set. Signal test (15 steps, 75 s): attention, come ahead with the torso on the pilot, torso swung across its range, both turns, stop, pass control to either side, then night: the three arm poses standing idle, night come ahead / turn / stop. Demo: one person of ours as before, or with two a first director 12 m ahead and 6 m to the pilot's right who takes the jet and passes control to the last one at 25 m, who stops it.
  - Data: `-Crew Extra` is now Full + **four** people of ours in catapult crew 1's list (catcc-c0 … c3 at 14 / 29, 31, 33 and 16 / 29); the static crew is gone. Needs the patch command run again (the installed patch still has the one man).
  - To learn: are the turns seamless and does the torso follow; which arm pose is right with a wand in each hand; the hand-over; **does catapult 1's crew still hook up and launch a jet with four extra members in its list**; do four extra members load.
- **Marshalling along a PriFly taxi route, first version (2026-11-14, built and installed, awaiting a run):** F10 > CATCC crew test > "Marshal me to" > Catapult 1–4. Our people bring the jet from the spawn spot it stands on to the catapult picked, along the route PriFly's own taxi network gives, up to the catapult's start box, where the catapult's own crew takes over as it always does. Single PC, meant for the deck crew option off.
  - **Routes** come from `src/components/catcc/prifly/taxiNet.ts` (`taxiRoute`, the solver behind PriFly's click-to-send), run offline by `catcc-dcs/deck-test/crew-test/routes.gen.ts` (`node …/routes.gen.ts`) from each of the 16 DCS spawn spots to each catapult and written to `Scripts/CATCC/crewtest_routes.lua` (64 routes, a point about every 2 m, ship coordinates). This file stands in for the link "PriFly's room sends the route to the pilot's PC"; the follower takes any polyline. A test fails if the file is not what the network gives now.
  - **Spots:** 5–16 (elevators, corral, patio) are spots of PriFly's network and their routes leave along the way the jet is parked. **The six-pack spots 1–4 are not in the network** (the open question of 11-11): the solver joins the nearest road, and to the bow catapults that route starts 85–145° off the jet's nose. The hook refuses a route that starts more than 60° off the nose and says why. The jet may be up to 15 m along its route already (a rolled jet, or a spot moved by the spawn test) and 3 m off it.
  - **Directors:** the part before the start box is cut into equal stretches of at most 30 m, one director each (as many as there are people of ours, at most). A director stands 8 m beyond the end of his stretch and 8 m to the side: outside of the bend there, the pilot's left where it is straight; if that is not on the deck (DCS's own deck outline from GroundCrew.lua, island cut out, 2 m to spare) the other side, then 5 m. He faces back along the route. Whoever of ours is nearest takes each station. Checked on all 63 routes that need directors: every station is on the deck. (EL 4 forward to catapult 4 starts inside the start box: no directors.)
  - **Signals:** the route point 10 m ahead of the jet's place on the route, seen from the jet: more than 10° off the nose = turn that way, back to come ahead under 5°. Attention on taking the jet; at the end of a stretch pass control pointing at the next director, who takes the jet when the pass is over (2 s) if he is at his station; in the start box the last one passes control towards the catapult's director; then everyone walks back to where he stood.
  - **Not in it yet** (by choice, to see the basics first): no stop / hold-short; stretch ends fall where the arithmetic puts them (on EL 2 forward → catapult 1 the first hand-over comes in the middle of the turn onto the artery, as DCS's own did out of spot 1); one jet at a time; the route is the precomputed one, not PriFly's live `ops.taxi` instruction; night set only by the manual toggle; other catapults' crews still react when the jet crosses their start boxes (seen 14:11: catapult 2's director signalled a jet bound for catapult 1).
  - Tests (`catcc-dcs/test`, 132): the follower on a made-up route and end to end on the real EL 2 forward → catapult 1 route (three directors: ahead, right, hand-over, ahead, hand-over, ahead, right, ahead, pass to the catapult crew), refusals, stations on deck for every route, the hook's deck outline equal to DCS's.
  - To fly it: deck crew off, a slot on an elevator spot (the hot start: spawn spot 5, EL 2 forward; the cold-start slot is six-pack 1).
- **Marshalling, run 6 (2026-11-14, the user):** successful as a demonstration of the concept. The path and where the directors stand need tuning: at catapult 4 the path did not bring the jet to where the catapult's crew picks it up, and the user had to loop round. Hand-overs hard to judge with a keyboard and no head tracking; to be judged in the sim rig. (The hook ends at the first metre of the route inside `start_box`; what the catapult crew needs to take a jet is evidently more than that: to be read from a run's log.)
- **Catapult crews, collision avoidance, own animations and models: research (2026-11-14; the user: high priority).** From DCS's files, export tables and animator_logic.dll's code; nothing below has been run in the sim yet.
  - **Dynamic placement:** yes. Every walk order of these tests took a point worked out at run time. PriFly already has the deck model (`planTaxi`, `firstBlock`: jets' poses, routes clear up to the first aircraft in the way); what is missing is the link room → pilot's PC and the multiplayer question (each PC makes its own crew characters; Flight.dll does have its own crew sync messages: `wAirbase::procCrewMoveMember`, `procCrewMoveMemberFinalPos`, `procCrewMoveMemberStopAnim`, `procSyncCrewMembersPos`, `procSyncCrewState`, `procMsgCrewNetEvent`).
  - **The catapult crews have orders of their own, per catapult.** Their behaviour tree (USS_Nimitz/scripts/common.xml, one per crew, `crew[n].behavior`) loops: with no aircraft assigned, **`GC_ORDER_FREE_CATAPULT`** makes the six walk their `free_cat` routes, after which the crew is not `READY_TO_ASSIGN_PLANE` (the catapult is out of use) until **`GC_ORDER_TAKE_CATAPULT`**, when they walk their `occupy_cat` routes back; with an aircraft in the box, they wait while **`GC_ORDER_OPERATION_ALLOWED`** is false (the "Cat 4 blocking start. Please wait" message; DCS uses it between catapults 3 and 4 through `BlockMasterCatapult`, `crew[4].master_catapult = 3`). Flight.dll exports the setters: `wCarrierCrew::request_free_catapult(bool)`, `set_operation_allowed(bool)`, `reset_orders()`, reached through `wGroundCrewOwner::get_carrier_crew(int)` (a carrier is a `wGroundCrewOwner`). Also exported: `get_start_box()`, `set_LA` / `reset_LA` / `get_LA_ptr` (the assigned aircraft), `check_LA_in_start_box`, `taxi()`, `woATC::crew_assign_plane`, `getFoulDeck`, `deck_crew_notify_land`, `getFreeCatapultsNum`. Flight.dll's code is packed on disk (its export table is not), so what DCS's own logic does with the orders meanwhile (it frees catapults for landing aircraft) can only be seen by trying.
  - **Where the crews go is data** (crew.lua, each member's `routes`): bow crews' `free_cat` spots are a few metres aft of their stations, between the tracks (x 50–74, z 4–9). Catapult 3's crew stands IN the landing area (x −14…−26, z −16…−21) and its free route runs aft along the area's port side (via −61/−22 and −80/−27) to the port edge at x −79…−86, z −35; catapult 4's crew frees to the port balcony (−56…−60, −36…−39). Other spots and other ways there are a data change; members also take our own walk orders (seen: catapult 3's shooter).
  - **Walking round things:** the crew environment has two switches with exported getters and setters, `avoid collisions` and `path avoid collisions` (plain flags, no config key: set by code), a nav grid (`query_obstacles_map_cell_size`, `update_nav_grid`, `PathFinder::find_path / can_see / add_collision`, one `PathFinder*` per walker), ORCA avoidance against agents, static obstacles (`deck_obstacle_data`, `add_static_obstacle`) and scene objects' contours (`query_scene_objects`, `update_contours`). `move_command_world` goes through `move_command` (which takes a path). Whether the switches are on with the deck crew option off, and what a walker does with a jet in his way, is for the run.
  - **Own animations:** DCS's modules mount character content in their entry.lua (`mount_vfs_animations_path`, `mount_vfs_model_path`, textures, liveries). An animations path has a folder per skeleton; clips are one FBX each (read through Assimp), with `graph/*.lua` for states, transitions, loop ranges (frames / 30), blend spaces, overrides and masks (the F/A-18C pilot's is readable: CoreMods/aircraft/FA-18C/animations/animations/typeV/graph/fa18c_anim_graph.lua). **The deck crew's animation list contains the pilot figure's clips** (`f18_0_init`, `f18_520_salut_l`, …), which exist only in the F/A-18C module's plain `typeV` folder: the deck crew is on skeleton `typeV`, and clips mounted by another module reach it. So a mod of ours with `animations/typeV/<clip>.fbx` should add clips the deck crew can play, without touching the encrypted `animations.edce`. Authoring: the typeV skeleton can be taken from the pilot figure's plain FBX files (model and clips) into Blender.
  - **Own models:** a character model is a small Lua descriptor (`.chanimgpu`: mesh FBX, skeleton, default animation, LODs, connectors such as `__conn_hand_R` for attachments) plus textures and liveries; a crew member names his in `model_name`. The pilot figure, the gloves and the cow are plain examples. The deck crew's own meshes are in `models.edce` (encrypted): to be replaced, not edited. A model of ours on `typeV` keeps all 330 stock animations; a skeleton of our own needs every animation made.
- **Crew test, fifth version (2026-11-14, built and installed, awaiting a run):** module `crewtest-5` (51 DCS functions, 12 new: 7 in Flight.dll, 5 in animator_logic.dll; `catcc-dcs/test` 136 tests). Menu regrouped (Target: signal / Target: walk / Our crew / Catapult crews / Collision avoidance).
  - Catapult crews: report (crews matched to catapults by their start boxes; the two avoidance switches; path finder present); per catapult "free it" / "take it" (`request_free_catapult` true / false); "operation allowed off / on" for the nearest catapult. The recorder logs where each crew member walks.
  - Collision avoidance: each switch on / off; "Target: walk > To the far side of my jet": the man is sent to the point beyond the jet on the straight line through it, and the closing message says how near he came to its middle and how far he walked.
  - Own animation: `crew-test/Mods/tech/CATCC-Crew` (installed to Saved Games\Mods\tech by crew-test/install.ps1) mounts an animations path with `typeV/catcc_test_clip.fbx` (a copy, under our name, of the pilot figure's salute from this PC's DCS). The mission-start message says whether the deck crew has it; "Target: signal > Our own clip" plays it.
  - Own model: the mod also has `models/catcc_test_pilot.chanimgpu` (the pilot figure's mesh, skeleton typeV, deck crew idle); `director-test.ps1 -Crew Model` = Extra + `catcc-m0` in catapult crew 1 with that model, at 18 / 30. Untried: may upset DCS at mission load; last.
- **Crew test, run 7 (2026-11-14, the user, v5; deck crew option off; log read afterwards):**
  - **Own animation: works.** `catcc_test_clip` (a clip mounted by our own mod under `animations/typeV`) was in the deck crew's list and played on a green shirt of ours (the seated salute). Adding clips needs no change to DCS's encrypted pack.
  - **Own model: works.** `-Crew Model`: DCS loaded the mission and the pilot figure (`catcc-m0`, our own `.chanimgpu` on skeleton typeV) stood among the green shirts. Not yet tried: giving him signals.
  - **Free order: obeyed, then undone.** Three times `request_free_catapult(true)` on catapult 3: the crew walked its free route to the port edge (about 55 s), and on arriving walked straight back, with no take order from us (a take order sent mid-walk changed nothing: they finished the walk out first). Something sets the take order again by itself.
  - **DCS frees the waist catapult for every landing aircraft, by itself, with the deck crew option off.** From 9.5 min into the mission catapult 4's crew walked off and back four times, 48 s apart: the mission's AI four-ship recovering. (Catapult 3's crew had the player's jet and stayed.) This is the walking through the landing area during recoveries the user wants stopped.
  - **Operation allowed = false: holds a catapult.** Set before the jet entered the start box (35 s before): the crew did not take the jet and DCS showed its "blocking start" message. Set after the crew had taken the jet: no effect (the tree is past the check). DCS's own code writes this order only from the tree's `BlockMasterCatapult` (catapult 4's launches, for catapult 3).
  - **My report missed catapult 4:** DCS numbers its crews from 1 (count 4; number 0 nothing; 1..3 = catapults 1..3) and I asked for 0..3.
  - **Collision avoidance: the two environment switches alone do nothing.** Found off; with both on the walker still went straight through the jet (0.0 to 0.4 m from its middle, three walks). Cause, from animator_logic.dll's code: `MovePathTracker::find_path_to_target` asks the walker's `PathFinder` only when byte 3 of the exported data `AnimatorLogic::debug_settings` (struct CharacterDebugSettings, 4 bytes) is set; otherwise the path is the straight line. Byte 2: `CharacterMovable`'s update takes the velocity its environment object worked out (the avoidance's answer) instead of the one it wanted. Bytes 0, 1: logging. Nothing in animator_logic.dll writes them (exported data: Flight.dll's side). The environment's `path avoid collisions` switch makes `update_nav_grid` run (on a timer): it clears the path finder's collisions and adds one per world object (through `RegisterManager`). A walk order does go through the path finder's tracker (`move_command_world` > `move_command` > `MoveGoalTracker::start_tracking` > `update` > `find_path_to_target`).
  - **Where a catapult crew takes a jet:** exports `wCarrierCrew::check_LA_in_start_box`, `get_la_gear_in_box`, `_get_gear_positions_local`: it is the gear that has to be in the start box, not the jet's reference point (catapult 4's box is 8.5 m wide). The marshalling ends on the reference point's first metre in the box: why the user had to loop round at catapult 4.
- **Crew test, sixth version (2026-11-14, built and installed, awaiting a run):** module `crewtest-6` (52 names: the new one is the data symbol `debug_settings`; `catcc-dcs/test` 140 tests).
  - Catapult crews asked for by DCS's numbers 0..count: all four found.
  - **Free it and HOLD:** the free order given again every frame until "take it".
  - **Order read-back:** Flight.dll exports no getter, so the bytes a setter changes 0 <-> 1 are found by copying the crew object (4 KB) and the memory it points to (two levels) before the call and comparing after it; the places are kept as places in the class and read for all four crews every frame. A change since the frame before is DCS's own: logged (at most 6 lines a second per catapult). "Report" finds the places first by switching each order and switching it straight back on catapult 1's crew inside one call. So the log will show what DCS itself does to the orders at each trap, and whether the hold wins.
  - Per catapult: operation allowed off / on; **send its crew away / back by our own walk orders** (six spots 2 m apart: abeam cat 1 to starboard; the port bow edge; starboard forward of the island's aft end; starboard aft; a man below the flight deck, catapult 4's white2 on the catwalk, is left).
  - Collision avoidance: "Path finding: ON" (environment path switch + debug_settings 3), "Avoidance velocity: ON" (environment avoid switch + debug_settings 2), "All off", and the walk through the jet.
  - Target kinds gain "our model" (catcc-m0) so he can be given signals.
- **The spotting plan's 40 deck spots (2026-11-14; the user: "the new spawn locations I want to implement, with taxi routes to/from all cats from each"; built, not yet run in DCS).** Source: `carrier-ops-handoff-2/deck-spots.csv` (the user's `scripts/catcc/nimitz-spots.py`, from the Aircraft Spotting layer of Nimitz.svg): 40 F/A-18C spots, position = the jet's DCS reference point, heading as parked.
  - **PriFly:** `deckSpots.gen.ts` (by `scripts/catcc/deck-spots.gen.ts`; ids = area letters + number: BS1-5 bow stbd, BP1-3 bow port, CR1-3 crotch, PT1-4 point, E11/E12 ... E41/E42 elevators, SP1-6 six-pack, CO1-2 corral, PA1-2 patio, ST1-2 stern stbd, PO1-5 port). They REPLACE the taxi network's 20 old spots (E1F, COR, JY1, BOX, FGR ... are gone). Each spot's feeder runs straight ahead of the parked jet to the road named for it in `FEED` (crossing, and so joining, every road on the way). New roads: the artery carried forward between the bow catapult tracks to x 150 (the **bow lane**, where bow, crotch and point jets pull out to); the catapult 1 and 2 lines carried aft to the turn-round road (so a jet coming aft down the bow lane turns back up to a bow catapult: two turns about 12 m apart); the landing-area road and the cat 3 line carried aft for the stern spots; port-side feeders cross the waist catapult lines and the landing area to the artery; EL 4's spots can also pull out 6 m and swing onto the cat 4 line.
  - **Solver rules added:** bound for a catapult, no turn over 30 degrees within 8 m of the shuttle (a feeder that crosses a catapult line 2 m short of the shuttle is no way onto it); no turn over 30 degrees in the first 5 m out of a spot (dropped if that leaves no route). `smoothRoute` no longer repeats the first point when a fillet starts on it.
  - **Result:** all 160 spot-to-catapult routes exist, leave along the nose, stay on the deck, arrive lined up (tests). Every spot reaches every other spot and catapult; 25 of 1,720 spot-to-spot routes still kink where they run up another spot's feeder the wrong way (most end at Crotch 3).
  - **DCS:** `director-test.ps1 -Spawn Spots` appends `director-spots.lua` (by `deck-test/spots.gen.ts`) to USS_Nimitz_RunwaysAndRoutes.lua: `GT.TaxiForTORoutes` replaced by 40 routes in the CSV's order: point 1 the spot (terminal size 20), point 2 straight ahead (the heading), then the corners of PriFly's route to the area's catapult (bow stbd, point, EL 1, corral, EL 2 > 1; bow port, crotch, six-pack > 2; EL 3, patio, stern stbd > 3; port, EL 4 > 4), ending 5 m short of the shuttle. GroundCrew.lua gets `director-spots-crew.lua`: hand-off lists 17..40 copied from the stock 16 in rotation, only so the deck crew option ON does not crash DCS (a route without a list did, 2026-11-14); the spots are for the option OFF. `-Revert` restores both files byte for byte (test on copies).
  - **Marshalling routes** (`crewtest_routes.lua`): now from the 40 deck spots (160 routes; every director station on the deck). On DCS's stock spawn spots the hook finds no route.
  - **To be seen in DCS:** whether DCS takes 40 spawn routes (mission load, the mission editor's count of deck slots, which slot gets which spot); `GT.TaxiRoutes` (where AI park after landing: 16 stock places) is untouched. **Four spots stand in or within 5 m of a catapult's start box** (Sixpack 1 and 2: catapult 2; Port 1 and El 4 1: catapult 4): DCS's catapult crew may take a jet that spawns there as one that taxied in. The bow spots stand on the bow catapult tracks. A route FROM a catapult shuttle backs down the track's line (no road leads forward off a catapult yet). The F10 route-lines tool still draws the stock routes.
- **Crew test, run 8 (2026-11-14, the user, v6 with `-Spawn Spots -Crew Model`; deck crew option off; log read afterwards):**
  - **The 40 spawn routes work.** DCS loaded the mission and the player spawned on the bow, on Bow stbd 1 (151.21 fwd, 8.85 stbd, nose aft: the plan's position and heading to the centimetre), where stock DCS has no spawn spot. Not yet looked at: the mission editor's count of deck slots; which slot gets which spot.
  - **Path finding works.** With the environment's "path avoids collisions" and `debug_settings[3]` on, both walks through the jet went round it (30.7 m walked for a 28.0 m straight line; nearest 4.8 m from the jet's middle). The walk ordered before the switch went straight through. (The user's "works from one submenu, not the other" was before / after the switch: both menu items run the same command.) No ill effects in the 10 minutes it stayed on.
  - **The held free order does not hold.** Given every frame, the crew walks off, stands a moment, walks back, turns round and walks off again, for ever (catapults 3 and 4, 18 minutes). The order read-back shows why: `request_free_catapult(true)` sets one order (free); `request_free_catapult(false)` clears it and sets a second (take). DCS's own code gives the take order whenever a crew is clear and nothing is landing, and clears our free order in between. "Take it" ended the loop.
  - **The AI four-ship never landed** in this run (it held to port for the whole 28 minutes; in run 7 it landed at 9.5 minutes). Not known why: the crews of catapults 3 and 4 were walking in and out the whole time (DCS may wait for them to be clear), or something in the 40-route table.
  - **The order read-back's places are per crew**, not per class: a place found on catapult 1's crew read rubbish on the others (the orders hang in a tree of their own per crew).
  - **A catapult crew walked away by our own orders: works** (all six of catapult 3 across the deck, catapult 4's five, catapult 1's six; catapult 4's white2 on the catwalk left). **Ordered back while still walking, three of catapult 1's six turned round and walked on the spot.**
  - **Marshalling refused** because the jet had taxied off its spot (routes started only on spots).
- **Crew test, seventh version (2026-11-14, built and installed, awaiting a run):** module `crewtest-7`; `catcc-dcs/test` 145 tests.
  - **Catapult crew gate** (`director-test.ps1 -CatGate`): one condition added to common.xml's "Occupy catapult" sequence, before the take order is asked for: a cleared crew walks back only while `GC_ORDER_OPERATION_ALLOWED` is true. DCS writes that order only from the tree's own `BlockMasterCatapult` (catapult 4's launches, on catapult 3), so it is the one channel that is ours. Menu per catapult: **Close it** (operation allowed off + the free order every frame) / **Open it** (operation allowed on + take). With the gate a closed crew should stay off through landings; a jet in its start box is left alone ("blocking start" message). Known wrinkle: catapult 4 finishing a launch sets catapult 3's operation allowed true again.
  - **Walk orders are queued:** a man who is moving is told to stop first and gets the new order when he stands (asked 4 times a second, given anyway after 6 s). Used for every walk order of ours.
  - **Marshalling from anywhere:** off the spots, the route to that catapult that suits best is joined where it passes nearest (within 40 m, leading on within 110 degrees of the nose; cost = metres off + 0.15 per degree of turn + 0.05 per metre left to taxi). And it ends when the gear is in the start box (the points 5.5 m ahead of and 1 m behind the jet's position both in it), or 8 m past where the route enters the box, instead of on the first metre.
  - **Path finding is switched on at mission start** (when the people are first found).
  - Order read-back kept per crew.
- **Crew test, run 9 (2026-11-14, the user, v7 with `-Spawn Spots -Crew Model -CatGate`; log read afterwards):**
  - **The catapult crew gate works.** "Close it" on catapults 3 and 4: both crews walked off and stayed off for the 7.5 minutes they were closed; "Open it": both walked back. So a catapult can be closed and opened from outside, per catapult, with one added line in common.xml and two exported calls.
  - **Marshalling from off the spot worked** (from the bow to catapult 3: 276 m, five of ours; the catapult's crew took the jet).
  - **The AI four-ship again never left the port holding pattern** (as in run 8), with the waist catapults closed and after they were opened. In the six minutes after opening, with the deck in its normal state, DCS made no move to clear a catapult for a landing (no crew walked by itself): no approach was ever begun. What runs 8 and 9 have in common against run 7 (where catapult 4's crew cleared four times, 48 s apart, at 9.5 minutes) is the 40-route spawn table. DCS's stock table has 16 parking routes (`GT.TaxiRoutes`: from where the roll-out ends, 13 fwd / 19 port, to a parking place), independent of the 16 spawn routes by number, but ten of the parking places ARE spawn spots (the same point). An AI that has no parking place to go to holds: that is the working explanation, untested.
  - **The crew sent back did not go: my bug.** A walk order waiting for its man to stand kept the ship's world position from when it was asked for; the carrier (30 kn) had sailed 60 m by the time it was given and the module refused it ("ship coordinates don't match"). The men's remembered places were then dropped, the next "away" took the away spots for their stations, and walk orders of no length made them jump about.
  - **The mission editor shows every carrier aircraft on catapult 1: that is the editor, not the table.** `MissionEditor/modules/me_parking.lua` builds a ship's list of stands from `RunWays` only (`getStandListForShip`: one stand per catapult, at its start) and `GT.numParking = 4` for the carriers; it carries `TaxiForTORoutes` along but reads it nowhere. Which spot an aircraft gets is settled when the mission runs.
- **After run 9 (2026-11-14, built and installed, awaiting a run):**
  - **Parking routes for the plan's spots** (`spots.gen.ts`, in the same `-Spawn Spots` block): `GT.TaxiRoutes` replaced by 31 routes, each from the stock first point via the turn-round road's bend and PriFly's taxi route to a spot. 31 of the 40: the port side (Port 1-5, El 4) and the stern (Stbd 1-2) are in or beside the landing area and get none. Order = DCS's own, place for place: 1-2 patio, 3 corral 2, 4-5 EL 3, 6-9 four of the six-pack (DCS's `BlockerTerminals = {6,7,8,9}`, left as it is), 10-11 EL 2, 12 corral 1, 13-14 point, 15-16 EL 1, then the rest of the point, six-pack, bow and crotch. An aircraft arrives up the spot's feeder, so it parks pointing the opposite way to a jet spawned there. Elevator spots keep DCS's 180 s wait.
  - **Walk queue:** the waiting order keeps only the place on the ship; the ship's position is asked for when the order is given. A man standing where he is sent gets no order. A crew member's remembered place is kept for the mission.
- **DCS crash of 2026-11-15 00:04 (the run after run 9): a graphics driver fault, not the crew test.** Ten seconds after the mission started the NVIDIA driver logged `Graphics FECS Exception: UCODE Fatal Error` (Windows System log, nvlddmkm id 13), DCS lost its graphics device (`DX device removed. Reason: 0x887A0006`), and the PC blue-screened (bugcheck 0x133) and restarted. The crew test had made no call into DCS that mission (its first scan comes 8 s of simulation in; nothing after "menu installed" in its log). The same driver fault is in the System log on 2026-11-10 06:29, 2026-11-14 07:28, 2026-11-14 14:58 and 2026-11-15 00:04, each within seconds of a mission start; three of the four restarted the PC. In the two with a DCS log, the last add-on line before the first graphics error is ILARTS starting to share (`sharing: started`, `keep PLAT: installed`: 0.4 s and 1.3 s before); ILARTS starts the same way in the runs that did not fail. (The 2026-11-14 10:33 crash is a different one: Flight.dll, the emptied hand-off list.)
- **Crew test, run 10 (2026-11-15, the user; `-Spawn Spots` with the 31 parking routes, `-Crew Model -CatGate`; a 7.5-minute run; log read afterwards):**
  - **The AI four-ship was still orbiting** when the run ended, with parking routes that end on the plan's spots. So the parking routes alone are not the answer, or 7.5 minutes was too short to tell. **Correction to run 7:** catapult 4's crew clearing four times there started the very second the player's jet arrived on catapult 3 (20:06:59 both), so it shows DCS clearing catapult 4 for a jet on catapult 3, not landings. **There is no run on record, stock table or ours, in which this mission's AI came aboard.** The mission gives them a plain Landing waypoint on the carrier (no orbit task). Still to do: the same mission on DCS's own files (`director-test.ps1 -Revert`), untouched for 15 minutes.
  - **DCS's stop order does not end a walk.** Five men told to stop (`CharacterMovable::stop_move`) walked on at the same pace to where they were going. Its code stops the animation controller's move and nothing else: the goal stays, and the walk resumes.
  - **A walk order given to a man who is still walking jumps him or sticks him.** Given 6 s after the stop that did not stop: two men were at their new places the next second (30 m away: jumped), and catapult 1's shooter stayed where he was, "moving", for good (later orders did not free him). The crew that came back by itself last run and "warped" was this.
- **After run 10 (2026-11-15, built and installed, awaiting a run):** module `crewtest-8` (54 names); 173 tests.
  - **Walk orders:** no stop order any more. A man who is moving gets a new walk order only when he stands, however long that takes (logged if he has not stood after two minutes).
  - **The catapult control bubbles can be commanded.** DCS has its own command, exported and readable (Transport.dll): `woShip::setBubbleCommand(bubble, command)` (bubble 0..1; command 0..2, kept per bubble, and sent to the clients when called on a server) and `woShip::getBubbleState(bubble)`. Every frame the ship moves each bubble's model argument (`GT.animation_arguments.bubbles = {138, 139}`): command 2 takes it up to 1, any other command down to 0. Taken until a run says otherwise: bubble 1 is the bow's, argument 1 = lowered flush. Menu: Catapult crews > Bubbles (each bubble down / up, report). **Rule built in:** with catapults 1 and 2 both closed ("Close it") the bow bubble is told to go down; when either is opened, up.
- **The AI lands once no hot-start jet sits on deck (2026-11-15, the user; old spots, `-Spawn Shift -Crew Model -CatGate`).** The AI four-ship held to port while the user sat in a hot-start jet; 58 s after he left that slot for a cold-start one (00:29:51), DCS itself cleared catapults 3 and 4 for the recovery (00:30:49, both crews walking their free routes). So the hold was never the 40-spot table or the parking routes: a running jet on deck that has not launched keeps the carrier from starting a recovery (the user's reading: the carrier stays in its launch state until those aircraft are airborne). The carrier's ATC exports its own answers (`woATC::canStartLanding`, `canStartTakeoff`, `getFoulDeck`, `isRunWayOpen`, `getFreeCatapultsNum`, `getRecoveryCASE`; reached through `wAirbase::getATC`): from crewtest-9 they are read once a second and logged when they change, so the next run shows exactly what flips.
- **The vision (the user, 2026-11-15) against what is known.** PriFly configures launch and recovery events; the deck crew reacts.
  - *A plane captain goes out to a spawned jet, gives engine-start signals, clears it to taxi after PriFly's release, hands it to the directors:* built as a first version in the crew test (below). The crew's own animations have `sign_runupengine_l/_r`, `sign_setbrakes`, `sign_releasebrakes`, `sign_unfoldwings`, `sign_foldwings`, `sign_ok_l/_r`, `sign_salut`, `sign_handsup`, `sign_pointl/_r`; there is no separate "start engine" signal (the run-up one stands in), and none for chocks.
  - *Simultaneous launch and recovery: bow catapults open, catapult 3 and 4 crews out of the landing area; recovery only: bow catapults closed, aircraft parked on the bow:* a catapult can be closed and opened (crew off and staying off) with the gate line in common.xml and `set_operation_allowed` + `request_free_catapult`; the bow bubble can be lowered (`woShip::setBubbleCommand`); the bow has spawn and parking spots. Open: what makes DCS's ATC start a recovery while aircraft wait to launch (see above).
  - *Crew walk round obstacles:* path finding works once switched on (run 8). A man must not be given a walk order while he walks (run 10).
  - *Crew marshal aircraft to parking spots after recovery, or to the next catapult for carrier qualification:* to a catapult from anywhere a route passes within 40 m (run 9); to a parking spot: built (below), not yet run.
  - *Animated tugs respot aircraft:* the BetteraircraftCarrier mod moves the PLAYER'S OWN aircraft with the keyboard from its cockpit device DLL, and its "tug" is an object placed at the nose; it does not move other aircraft. For any aircraft DCS exports `woATC::teleport_aircraft(wPosition3<double>, unsigned int)` (a jump, not a tow) and `woShip::SetWheelchocksFlag(int, bool)`. An animated tow of an AI or parked aircraft has no known hook yet.
  - *Damaged or surplus aircraft to the hangar:* the elevators can be commanded (`woShip::setElevatorCommand(elevator 0..3, command 0..2)`, `getElevatorState`; the BetteraircraftCarrier mod calls exactly this, and it is sent to clients when called on a server). DCS also exports `woATC::return_object_from_terminal_to_warehouse_and_free(wsBasicTerminal &, IwWarehouse *)` and `getElevatorByTerminal`, `getTerminalAtElevatorNum`, `getTerminalList`. Its own AI already ride an elevator down after parking 180 s on an elevator place.
  - Other exports seen that bear on events: `woATC::getClients` (every aircraft's ClientData), `setClientStatus(ClientData &, ClientStatus, wMsg)`, `getMarshalClients`, `getGlideSlopeOccupants`, `canClientShipTaxi`, `setRadioSilentMode`, `setIFLOSCutAndWaveOffLights`. Flight.dll's code is packed: names and signatures only.
- **Built overnight (2026-11-15, installed, awaiting a run):** module `crewtest-9` (63 names); 177 tests.
  - **Deck state log** (above), also in "Catapult crews > Report".
  - **Elevators by hand:** Catapult crews > Elevators (each elevator, command 1 / 2, report).
  - **Plane captain** (menu "Plane captain"): the nearest of ours walks to 11 m ahead of the nose and 3.5 m to the pilot's left, facing the jet, and signals hold brakes; "Engine start signals" = run-up right 6 s, thumbs-up, run-up left 6 s, thumbs-up; "Release me" = unfold wings, release brakes, then either his salute or, with a catapult named, the marshalling starts (he is left out of its team) and he passes control to its first director; then he walks home.
  - **Marshalling to a parking spot** (Our crew > Marshal me to a parking spot > deck area): the parking route of the area's first spot (the same 31 routes as DCS's table, filleted; in `crewtest_routes.lua` under `parking`), joined where it passes within 40 m; the last director gives the stop signal on the spot.
- **Run 11 (2026-11-15 morning, the user; `-Spawn Spots -Crew Model -CatGate`, crewtest-9): what it showed.**
  - *Spawned on Bow stbd 1 hot; the AI held to port the whole time and commenced when the jet was being hooked up on catapult 1.* The ATC state log did not run: `wAirbase::getATC` called on the wAirbase part of the ship gave nothing usable. The ship has its own `woShip::getATC` (Transport.dll, a virtual): from crewtest-10 it is found in the ship's own virtual tables and called on the part of the ship its slot belongs to.
  - *Plane captain:* he had 123 m to walk; after 60 s he was taken to be "in place" 24 m short and still walking, and the hold-brakes signal slid him the rest of the way in that pose. Now nobody counts as in place while he walks, however long. The engine signals were the wrong way round for the pilot (the animations are named for the signaller's side: `_l` is the pilot's right engine). "Unfold wings" is not his (the shooter's, on the line-up): dropped from the release.
  - *The bow bubble's collision shape stops a jet:* the route from Bow stbd 1 to catapult 1 runs over it; the jet stood against it at 71 m forward, 3.4 m to starboard (nose aft) for 50 s and climbed over on high thrust. So the bubble is at about 64 m forward, 3 m starboard, and **a raised bubble works as a chock** (worth keeping in mind).
  - *Bubbles:* both arguments stood at 1 with both bubbles up: argument 1 = raised, and command 2 raises (the ship's code: command 2 takes the argument to 1, command 0 or 1 to 0). The first build had the directions inverted, which explains the run: its "down" sent "raise" to raised bubbles (nothing), its "up" sent "lower". DCS commands the bubbles itself (the waist bubble went down for a landing aircraft and up when it left the landing area). By the eye, bubble 1 (index 0, model argument 138) is the waist's; so the bow's is taken to be index 1 (argument 139) until the command log confirms.
  - *Elevators:* command 1 lowers, command 2 raises (and stops a lowering). From the ship's code: lowering raises the deck-edge fence, takes the platform down, and at the bottom opens the hangar door (`GT.animation_arguments.elevators_doors`, part of the same sequence) and lowers the bottom fence; raising is the same backwards. Given once, the platform went down and came straight back up: something of DCS's (the ATC's elevator control, `woATC::controlElevators`) had commanded it up again.
  - *Parking:* the marshalling to El 1 2 ended with the jet on the spot. *Crew recall mid-walk:* worked; but catapult 1's director, still in a DCS marshalling pose from the hook-up, slid across the deck in that pose when sent off.
- **Built after run 11 (crewtest-10, 65 names; installed, awaiting a run; 182 tests):**
  - **The cockpit camera follows whoever signals** (asked for): while the jet is marshalled, the view turns to the director who has it (or the next one standing at his station between a pass and a take), to the plane captain while he stands at the nose, and to the catapult's own director for the hook-up (two minutes after the hand-over, or until the jet leaves the deck). Left / right only; only in the cockpit view (camera within 9 m of the jet); on by itself; "Our crew > Camera follows the director: on / off". Done with DCS's "absolute camera horizontal view" axis (command 2007 through the export interface's LoSetCommand). How many degrees a unit of that axis is, and its sign, is measured from where the camera goes (LoGetCameraPosition), not assumed; if the view does not answer, it says so and stops.
  - **Plane captain:** signals only once he stands; right engine first; release = release brakes only; "My catapult: N" sets the jet's catapult (stand-in for PriFly's launch plan), and with one set the directors walk out to their stations when the engine signals begin and wait; on release the captain passes control and the first director takes the jet.
  - **A man in a pose is put back to idle before any walk order.**
  - **Bubbles the right way round; a bubble we want down is kept down** (the command the ship holds is read four times a second out of the place the setter's own code names, and given again if DCS changed it); every command DCS itself gives a bubble or an elevator is logged with its time. The bow bubble is down while catapults 1 and 2 are both closed AND while a marshalling route of ours crosses it (lowered at the start, raised when the jet is 14 m past; also if the marshalling is stopped).
  - **Elevators:** menu is now down / up; down is kept down the same way.
  - **Put square on the parking spot (experimental):** when the last director has stopped the jet on a parking spot and it has stood still 2 s, the jet is put on the spot's own place and spawn heading through `woATC::teleport_aircraft(wPosition3<double>, unsigned int)` (world position and axes, the aircraft's object id); also "Our crew > Put me square on the nearest spot (teleport)". What the call does to a player's jet is not known yet: two seconds later the hook says how far from the spot the jet is. (`wPosition3<double>`: four 32-byte vectors, from how Transport.dll reads one. Also exported: `wTakeoffCrew::teleport_player()`, `woATC::getShipFreeTerminalForTeleport`, `MovingObject::ForcePosition(wPosition3<double> const&)` in WorldGeneral.dll: untried.)
  - Deck spots in the routes file now carry their spawn heading (`fx`, `fz`).
- **The start-up and release in the real procedure (for the plane captain's sequence; CV NATOPS NAVAIR 00-80T-105, 4.2.1 and 6.x, the copy in carrier-ops-handoff-2).** "Engines shall be started only on signal and under the positive control of PriFly." "The plane directors shall monitor the aircraft throughout the warmup and checkout period. Control is assumed by the plane captain for preflight checks only." After the post-start checks the pilot signals the aircraft's status to the director, who relays it. Before chocks and tiedowns come off, the director calls for brakes and gets them confirmed; a director does not leave an aircraft once they are off. A Navy training manual's post-start routine (T-45, tpub P-1212): pilot thumbs-up; the plane captain signals in turn wipe out the controls, flaps down, speed brakes out, launch bar out, hook down; the pilot holds his hands above the canopy rail while the plane captain checks for leaks; then the retract signals. Open design question (the user's): whether the captain reacts to the pilot's inputs or the pilot waits for (or is held to) the captain's signal, triggered by time or by PriFly. What can be read on the pilot's PC for either: engine RPM (export interface), and control surface, hook, probe, speed brake and launch bar positions (the aircraft's own model arguments).
- **The hangar door opens when the elevator is told to go down (2026-11-15, the user's call; crewtest-11, 66 names, installed, awaiting a run).** DCS's own sequence for "lower" is fence up, platform down, then door open, then lower fence: the door stays shut until the platform is at the bottom. The user wants it opening from the command. What is known about the real ship: no public source found that spells out a door sequence; the CV NATOPS (darken ship measures, section 2) lists "closure of hangar bay doors" and "positioning of aircraft elevators at the flight deck level" as two separate measures, so the doors are not tied to where the platform is. How it is done: the door is one of the ship's model arguments (`GT.animation_arguments.elevators_doors = {47, 48, 53, 54}`, the same on CVN-71..75), and DCS's sequence leaves it alone while the platform moves; the hook moves it itself every frame at DCS's own door speed (1/6 a second: open in 6 s) through the ship's own argument list (`woShip::GetDrawArguments`, a virtual found in the ship's tables; `native.shiparg`). At the bottom DCS finds the door open and goes on. Going up is DCS's (door shut first, then the platform). Limits: what is drawn on this PC only (not sent to other players: each PC running the hook does its own); whether the door's collision follows a directly written argument is not known.
- **Run 12 (2026-11-15 07:18, the user; stopped after 2 minutes): the camera follow swung the view round behind the jet.** The first build took view command 2007's value for an angle to go to and sent it twenty times a second (-0.14 for a plane captain 26 degrees left of the nose): the camera went to 172 degrees left (its stop) and stayed. So the value is an amount to turn BY, as mouse movement is. Rebuilt (installed, awaiting a run): what the command does is found out first with a value too small to notice (sent once, then a zero, then the value again: a turn or an angle; the other direction is tried too, for a camera standing against its stop), and from then on the camera is steered by the angle between where it looks and where the signaller is: a third of it a tick, at most 8 degrees in one go, nothing sent within 0.6 degrees. Also from that run: the plane captain stood on his point before signalling (73 s walk, 0.0 m off); the ATC state log works (first reading: can start landing YES, can start take-off YES, foul deck no, runway open yes, recovery case 0; free catapults not readable, -1); the ship's bubble commands read 2 2 with both bubbles up, as the corrected directions have it.
- **Run 13 (2026-11-15 07:29 to 07:56, the user; crewtest-11): what it showed.**
  - *Camera:* the view command turns the camera 1804 degrees a unit; following worked and aimed right, but the pan stepped (20 times a second, a third of the error each, stop and go at 0.6 degrees) and was hard to look at in turns.
  - *Plane captain, engine order, directors walking out on the engine signals:* all as intended.
  - *Bubbles:* index 0 (model argument 138) is the BOW's, index 1 (139) the WAIST's: the build had them swapped, so the route over the bow bubble lowered the waist's. DCS gives the waist bubble its command again every frame (up while launching, down for a landing); the bow's it leaves alone.
  - *AI recovery:* the jet reached catapult 1 at 07:34:54; the first AI aircraft was in the landing area at 07:38:38. `woATC::canStartLanding` read YES from mission start to end, hot jet on deck or not: that getter does not show what holds a recovery. `canStartTakeoff` and `getFoulDeck` follow each landing; `getFreeCatapultsNum` cannot be read (-1).
  - *A jet already on a catapult cannot be closed out* by the free / operation-allowed orders.
  - *Parking:* the directors' signals were right; the jet stopped 2 m short of the spot's own point (nose wheel 4.6 m from the edge), tail outside the elevator's outline. *Teleport:* `woATC::teleport_aircraft` returned and the jet had not moved.
  - *Elevator:* lowered and stayed down (DCS's own control commands it up again every tick: ours is re-given); the hangar door opened in 6 s from the command. People of ours standing on the platform stay at deck height (they float).
  - *Crew recall:* worked; each man finishes his current walk first. Catapult 1's blast deflector stayed up after its crew was walked away mid hook-up.
- **Decided / asked for (the user, 2026-11-15):** the first director's job after a landing is to get the jet out of the landing area at once (a turn perpendicular to it until clear), then the route; a parked jet's nose wheel stops 6 ft from the deck edge; spawn spots have the main wheels 4 ft from the edge; the hangar doors need not shut before an elevator rises; a manual way to break a catapult hook-up; walkers interrupted at once if a smooth way is found; crew not floating over a lowered elevator; hangar parking routes (from a map of the hangar, or the BetteraircraftCarrier mod's).
- **Built after run 13 (crewtest-12, 70 names; installed, awaiting a run; 185 tests):**
  - **Camera:** steered every frame, the angle to the signaller worked out in ship coordinates only (no mixing of two readings of the ship's world position), closing with a 0.2 s time constant and at most 80 degrees a second, nothing sent within 0.05 degrees.
  - **Bubbles the right way round** (bow = index 0), the menu says "Bow bubble" / "Waist bubble"; kept commands are re-given quietly and shown with a star in the command log.
  - **Parking stop:** the parking route goes on straight to the deck edge (the outline) and the jet is stopped with its nose wheel 6 ft from it (nose wheel 2.99 m ahead of the jet's position, FA-18C_hornet.lua).
  - **Edge spots moved** (scripts/catcc/deck-spots.gen.ts, from the CSV's hand-placed positions): a spot whose tail points at the edge (edge within 9 m straight behind) is moved along its heading until the nearer main wheel (2.44 m behind the position, 1.55 m either side) is 4 ft from the edge. 29 of the 40 moved, by 0.1 to 2.6 m, most of them outboard. The edge used is DCS's deck crew outline (GroundCrew.lua deck()): if that is not the physical edge, the figures are off by the difference. PriFly's spots, the routes file and DCS's spawn table (director-spots.lua) are regenerated; DCS's own files need `director-test.ps1 -Spawn Spots -Crew Model -CatGate` again to take the new spawn places.
  - **Teleport, second way:** if the ATC's teleport leaves the jet where it is, the aircraft's own `MovingObject::ForcePosition` (WorldGeneral.dll's virtual, called through the aircraft's own table; the object found by its id in the world's register) is tried with the same position, and its result said. Untried in DCS.
  - **Elevator up with the door open:** the door is written back to open every frame (DCS's raise sequence then stops at its door step) and the hook moves the lower fence, the platform (17 s) and the deck-edge fence itself; "Elevators > Shut the hangar doors" lets DCS finish. Model arguments are now written through the ship's own setter (`woShip::setDrawArgumentValue`, the call the BetteraircraftCarrier mod makes).
  - **"Abort the hook-up"** per catapult: `wCarrierCrew::reset_LA` (the crew's own "no aircraft"). Untried: what the crew, the holdback bar and the blast deflector then do is to be seen.
- **The BetteraircraftCarrier mod, read (its DLLs' import tables and strings, its scripts):** it resolves by name `woShip::setElevatorCommand`, `SetWheelchocksFlag`, `setDrawArgumentValue`, `getDrawArgumentsValue`, `viObjectManager::GetBoxList`, `viObject::getRadius`; it moves the player's jet (cold start in the hangar, RCtrl + WASD to slide) with no position function imported, so by the object's own memory or tables (not followed further). It has no hangar taxi ROUTES: only seven hangar parking positions for the Nimitz class (ship coordinates, hangar floor at 8.71 m): (-110, -8), (-110, 9), (-75, -9.5), (-47.25, -9.5), (-19.5, -9.5), (8.25, -8), (36, -9.5).
- **Not done yet:** the exit-the-landing-area-first leg for parking; people of ours cleared off (or riding) a lowering elevator; hangar routes; interrupting a walker at once (`CharacterMovable::stop_move` does not end a walk; `CharacterMovable::reset` and the net stop calls are untried).
- **Run 14 (2026-11-15 09:08 to 09:26, the user; crewtest-12): what it showed.**
  - *Camera:* smooth ("perfect"). *Bow bubble:* lowered for the route to catapult 1 and raised again once the jet was past.
  - *Teleport:* `woATC::teleport_aircraft` again left the jet where it was; the aircraft's own `MovingObject::ForcePosition` (through the object's own table) put it on the spot, 0.0 m and 0 degrees off, and it stayed there. **ForcePosition moves a player's jet.**
  - *Parking stop 6 ft from the edge, elevator down / up with the door left open, "Shut the hangar doors":* as intended.
  - *"Abort the hook-up" (`wCarrierCrew::reset_LA`):* nothing to be seen with the jet in the shuttle; the crew stayed in their launch places and the shooter went on giving the run-up signal. (The blast deflector came down at some point, after a re-slot.) `reset_LA` alone is not an abort.
- **Decided / asked for (the user, 2026-11-15, after run 14):** crew on an elevator get off it before it goes down; the plane captain is a brown shirt (not the pilot figure); the flight directors are yellow shirts; towing "like the BetteraircraftCarrier mod" but automated (no steering by keys), with the tractor and its driver, and the joints tractor / tow bar and tow bar / aircraft articulated if possible; first test: lowered into the hangar on elevator 1, the tractor hooks up and pulls the jet into the hangar.
- **Built after run 14 (crewtest-13, 73 names; installed, awaiting a run; 188 tests):**
  - **Shirts** (`groundcrew-patch.lua`, `-Crew Model`): catcc-c0..c3 yellow (flight directors); catcc-m0 the plane captain, DCS's own crew figure in the brown livery, with wands; a new member catcc-t0, the tow tractor's driver, green (DCS's crew liveries are yellow, brown, white, green and the shooter's own: no blue). All of ours now stand 12 to 14 m forward, off elevator 1's platform. The hook gives jobs by name: the yellow shirts direct (the brown shirt only if there is no yellow shirt), catcc-m0 is the plane captain wherever he stands, catcc-t0 does neither. Needs `director-test.ps1 -Spawn Spots -Crew Model -CatGate` again.
  - **Out of the landing area first:** a jet to be parked that stands in the landing area (DCS's own landing strip of the ship, `GT.RunWays[1]`: through 45 m aft / 10.49 m to port, 9.14 degrees to port of the bow, 25 m wide) gets a route that goes 4 m straight on, then square off the landing area to starboard until the jet's own position is 10 m beyond the strip's edge, and only then joins the way to the spot (its nearest point from there, onwards). The route's points are logged (`marshal: route ...`).
  - **Nobody of ours rides an elevator down:** "Elevator N: down" first walks anyone of ours standing on the platform off it, inboard (to 15 m from the centreline), and lowers it when they are clear (or after 40 s). The platforms' outlines are taken from the deck outline and the spots on them (27 m by 16 m, with a margin): `ELEV_BOX` in the hook.
  - **"Abort the hook-up"** is now three things: the crew told to let go (`reset_LA`), the catapult closed and kept closed (operation allowed off, the free order held), and the jet moved 20 m straight back off the shuttle by ForcePosition. Untried: what a jet held by the shuttle does when moved.
  - **Tow test** (F10 > Tow): see the next entry.
- **Towing: how it is built (crewtest-13; nothing of it has run in DCS yet).**
  - *How the BetteraircraftCarrier mod shows its hangar tug (its scripts, read):* a mission script makes a static object (`AS32-31A`, category `ADEquipment`) NOT linked to the ship (a linked static stands on the flight deck, an unlinked one lies on the sea bed), and the mod's DLL puts it in front of the jet's nose every frame. It has no tow bar and no driver; the jet is moved by keys.
  - *A static's class:* `lLandVehicle` (Terrain.dll) : `lLandObject` (edObjects.dll) : `viObjectNode` : `viObjectShape` : `viObject`; NOT a `MovingObject`, so ForcePosition does not apply. `lLandObject::SetPos(wPosition3<float>)` is exported and virtual: `native.staticpos(id, 12 world numbers[, sx, sy, sz])` finds the object by its id (the scripting object's `id_`) in the world's register under whatever kind it is filed, reads SetPos's slot from `lLandObject`'s exported virtual table and calls the object's own. The three scale numbers stretch the axes of the position given.
  - *The tractor:* DCS's `AS32-31A` static (its model has wheels and steering knuckles as animated nodes, no driver, no tow bar). *The tow bar:* DCS has no tow bar model; a static type of our own (`CATCC towbar`, declared in our mod's entry.lua the way the Supercarrier declares its tractors) is drawn with the Supercarrier's holdback bar model (a plain bar 1.016 m long along its x axis, 0.1 m thick) and stretched to 6.1 m (20 ft: the jet's nose reaches 5.6 m ahead of its nose wheel) by the position's axes. **Untried: whether DCS draws a model stretched that way; whether the type declaration is taken.** *The driver:* catcc-t0 put on the seat every frame by `AnimatedCharacterBase::set_origin` (`native.place(h, x, y, z, fx, fz)` in ship coordinates: the pose his animation is played from; heights are free, so a man can be put on the hangar deck), in the pilot figure's seated pose `f18_0_init`, which is in the deck crew's animation list. **Untried: whether a placed man stays; where the seat is** (F10 > Tow > Driver seat moves him 10 cm at a time and logs the offsets). *The jet:* ForcePosition every frame.
  - *Character positions (animator_logic.dll, read):* a character keeps its world pose at +0x200, its pose relative to what it is attached to at +0x240 (`local_position()`), and an "origin" at +0x2D8 (`set_origin` / `get_origin`; `stop_move` reads its heading). `SetPosition` writes the world pose. Crew members' `model_attachment` (a plain EDM on a connector, with a 4x4 transform) is still read by the shipped DLL next to `gpu_attachment` and `light_attachment`: another way to hang a model on a person, not used yet.
  - *Geometry (ship coordinates):* the tractor follows a polyline by pure pursuit (3 m look-ahead, turning circle 4 m radius, 1.4 m/s, 0.5 m/s2); the bar pivots on the tractor's rear hitch (1.7 m behind its position) and on the jet's nose wheel; the jet follows as a trailer, pivoting about its main wheels (nose wheel 2.985 m ahead of its position, main wheels 2.438 m behind). Heights: the floor is taken as the jet's height less 1.82 m.
  - *Sequence:* the tractor waits at 36 m forward / 9.5 m to port (the mod's forward hangar place); drives to beside the jet's nose line, U-turns onto it, backs the bar up to the nose wheel (0.7 m/s), pauses 2 s, pulls the jet straight ahead to the hangar's port lane (8 m to port) and aft along it; the jet ends at about 8.25 m forward / 8 m to port, nose aft (the mod's sixth hangar place); "Unhook" sends the tractor 8 m on. In the test (4 frames a second) the jet ends within 1.5 m of the place.
  - *Known limits:* drawn on this PC only; the two statics lie on the sea bed until the first tow and are then placed every frame for the rest of the mission; the hangar's walls and the door's width are not known to the hook (the lane and the places are the mod's, whose author checked them for a Hornet); the tractor's wheels do not turn or steer; started only on the hangar deck (the jet below 14 m above the ship's origin).
- **Not done yet:** interrupting a walker at once (looked at: `sync_final_pos` only writes the network's "final position" record; `CharacterMovable::reset` posts to a queue and is not understood; nothing tried); hangar taxi routes in general (the tow's one lane only: a hangar map would give the rest); towing on the flight deck; other players seeing the tractor.
- **Our own tow bar model (2026-11-15, asked for by the user with photographs of the ALBAR universal tow bar; built and installed, not yet seen in DCS).**
  - *No exporter:* there is no Blender on this PC, and ED's own Blender EDM exporter download (files.eagle.ru) has an expired TLS certificate: not fetched. So the .edm is written by a writer of our own (`crew-test/model/edm.py`), from the community's format notes (Blender_ioEDM's EDM_Specification.rst, read as text only) checked against DCS's files: it reads DCS's version 10 models and writes them back **byte for byte** (Holdback_Bar, Light_Stick, Catapult_Runner, both AS32 tractors).
  - *Version 10 as DCS has it (differs from the notes):* the two type-count indexes are empty; the RootNode has no unknown byte before its bounding box; its last two numbers are 0 and the model's argument count (highest argument + 1: 16 for the holdback bar's argument 15, 0 with none). Vertex format of DCS's own props: [4,3,3,3,2] (position + parent index, normal, tangent = dP/du, bitangent = dP/dv, uv), stride 15; faces counter-clockwise seen from outside; textures 0 diffuse, 1 normal, 2 roughmet.
  - *The model* (`crew-test/model/towbar.py` → `Mods/tech/CATCC-Crew/Shapes/catcc_towbar.edm` + three 256 px uncompressed DDS textures in `textures/`): Model 8, 108 in; red lunette ring at the origin (on the tractor's pintle), white yoke with red plate and bolts, two white 2.5 in square arms spreading to red heads 0.54 m apart, dark grey jaw plates and pin knobs, small wheels, yellow reflective strips; 1627 vertices, 1264 triangles; 2.72 m long, the lug pins 2.637 m from the ring centre. One def_material on a flat-colour atlas (normal map flat, normalMapValue 0). Also written for a 3D artist: `model/out/catcc_towbar.obj/.mtl/_diff.png`, and a preview picture.
  - *In the tow:* the bar is no longer stretched: rigid, its ring on the tractor's hitch (taken 3.05 m behind the AS32-31A's position, the rear of its collision shell, 0.45 m up) and pitched down to the nose wheel's lugs (0.30 m up); 2.633 m on the flat. The tractor's rear therefore stands under the radome when hooked up, as in the photographs. Driver seat first guess 1.2 m behind the tractor's position, 0.3 m to its right, 0.35 m up (adjustable from F10).
  - *Untried:* whether DCS accepts the static type and finds the model and textures (ModelViewer2 can open the .edm to check outside a mission); where the AS32-31A's real pintle is.
- **Tow bar joints (the user, 2026-11-15):** the bar's only yaw joint is the ring on the tractor's pintle; the jaws clamp the nose gear's lugs, so on the aircraft the bar is rigid in yaw and roll and free only in pitch: the bar's heading is the nose wheel's (castoring, nose wheel steering off). Built (crewtest-14): the bar is pinned to the nose gear first with its ring resting on the deck (0.07 m up) straight ahead; the tractor backs its pintle onto the ring, the ring is lifted onto it over the 2 s pause; the nose wheel rolls along the bar (it follows the hitch as a trailer's axle at the bar's length: the path is as before), the jet follows it with its mains dragged; the steering angle (bar against nose) is written to the jet's own model argument 2 at 75 degrees a unit (both ASSUMED) every frame by a new native call, `objarg(h, objectId, n[, v])`: the ship's exported GetDrawArguments override gives the slot and base (Graphics::ModelInstance), and the object's own function in that slot gives its argument list. The value is read back each frame: if the aircraft's own code overwrites it, the log says. The largest angle of the tow is said at the end. Unhook: the ring is set down, the bar stays on the nose gear as the nose wheel is turned.
- **Tow bar jaws and the hook-up sequence (asked for by the user, 2026-11-15; built, installed, not yet run):** the model's jaws (each head's jaw plate, hook lip and an axle pin pointing inboard) are moving parts: an `ArgPositionNode` per jaw on model argument 10 (0 open, 6 cm outboard; 1 closed), the key offsets added to the node's base position (identity matrix and quaternions; render node vertices about the pivot), as DCS's AS32-32A model does it. The root's argument count is 11. The bar's attach point is now the axle pins, 2.595 m from the ring. Pitch about the axle needs no argument: the hook places the whole bar. A script-made static keeps its own argument list (lLandVehicle's GetDrawArguments, ModelInstance slot 4, returns this+0x1D0 of that part, read in Terrain.dll), so `native.objarg` sets the jaws; UNTRIED whether that list is long enough for argument 10 (the log says). Sequence: the tractor arrives with the bar on its pintle trailing on its own wheels (axle end 0.115 m up), U-turns onto the jet's nose line clear of the radome and stops facing away; the ring is set down; the bar is taken to the nose gear (0.8 m/s) and lifted onto the axle; the jaws close (1.5 s); the ring end is lifted to the hitch's height (2 s); the tractor backs onto the ring; 1.5 s; the pull. The tractor backs to connect (it faces away from the jet). ModelViewer2 mounts a `textures` folder next to the model it opens (once per session): the textures are now in Shapes/textures, mounted by our entry.lua too.
- **Tow bar arms (the user, 2026-11-15, correcting the first jaw build):** on the ALBAR the jaws open by the ARMS splaying: each arm pivots on a vertical pin in the yoke at the ring end, so the heads swing in and out to fit nose gear axles of different widths. Argument 10 now turns each whole arm (tube, strips, plug, head, jaw, axle pin, wheel, bolts) about its pivot (0.27 m from the ring, 0.045 m either side): an `ArgRotationNode` per arm, key quaternions about +y (x, y, z, w), 1 closed as drawn, 0 open (+-1.47 degrees: the axle pins 6 cm outboard). Pivot pins drawn on the yoke. The hook is unchanged (it writes argument 10).
- **Tow bar geometry from the user's orthographic sheet (ALBAR 15 model kit, 2026-11-15):** red ring head 0.2 m long (0.05 to 0.25 m from the ring's centre) carrying the flat lunette ring; the arms leave it side by side, touching (pivot pins at 0.21 m, 0.034 m either side), and spread to heads 0.30 m apart centre to centre (they were 0.54); six adjustment holes on each upper arm; yellow patches near the ring head, half way and near the heads; a safety chain stowed along the right arm; jaws at the heads' tips, the axle pins pointing inboard (tips 0.15 m apart), a wheel on each head's outside. Length kept at 108 in (the sheet gives none). The axle pins are now 2.637 m from the ring (the hook's barLen); arms splay +-1.42 degrees at argument 0. Whether 0.15 m between the pin tips fits the F/A-18C's nose gear is not known: argument 10 between 0 and 1 is where the fit would be set.
- **Tow bar rebuilt from the user's references, and the axle finder (2026-11-15; crewtest-15; built and installed, not yet seen in DCS; 194 tests).**
  - *Textures:* ModelViewer2's session after the textures were moved beside the model logged no texture error: DCS takes the uncompressed 32-bit DDS files. They now carry their mipmaps.
  - *What the references show that the earlier builds had wrong (ALBAR Model 8, 108 in: gloria and Res/Kit renders, a Galaxy Model photograph, the ALBAR 15 sheet):* the AXLE PINS are on the INBOARD sides of the heads (one each, pointing at each other): they go into the two ends of a hollow nose wheel axle. The dark plates at the heads' tips are TOW HOOKS, for aircraft with tow lugs (not the F/A-18's way). The chain is the TENSIONING CHAIN: from a bushing through one arm across to a bushing through the other and on to a star knob; tightening it pulls the arms together and holds the pins in the axle. The arms pivot on the two rear bolts of the ring head (a white bushing round each, showing at the arm's open end); closed, the arms lie almost together (pin tips a couple of centimetres apart); to go on an F/A-18 they must open to about half a metre.
  - *The model* (`crew-test/model/towbar.py`, rewritten; 7,806 vertices, 6,730 triangles, 22 parts): lunette ring (3 in eye) on a red neck; red top and bottom plates (an eight-sided outline) with three bolts on washers, nuts below; the pivot bushings; a foot under the ring head; two hollow white 2.5 in tubes with holes (three near the ring head, the row of five on the left arm), yellow patches on top and outboard near each end, stencils on the outboard sides ("GPNB", "D9K108": a made-up serial, drawn into the texture with a 5 x 7 font), two bolts above and below and four in each side round the tube's end; the chain bushing as a boss on both sides of each arm 0.24 m from the tube's end, the star knob on the right arm, the chain's end pin on the left; red heads set into the tubes, tapering to the tip (top down, bottom up, outboard side in), the tip's end dark; the axle pin (25 mm, 50 mm long, chamfered, a hexagon collar) 0.12 m along the head on its inboard side; the tow hook plate (five holes, three teeth, a hook) at the tip on the inboard side; a wheel (80 mm) on a lug on the outboard side at the tube's end; the chain as 19 links.
  - *One model argument (10) for what is done with the bar:* 0 closed (stowed); 0 to 0.5 the arms open, to 7.5 degrees each at 0.5 (pin tips 0.62 m apart), the chain slack (it drops to the deck and lies in a loop, coming straight as the arms open); 0.5 to 1 the arms close again WITH THE CHAIN TAUT at every width (the knob has taken it up: the links beyond the right arm's bushing are inside the arm): the clamped states, whatever the aircraft's axle width. Each arm is an `ArgRotationNode` about its pivot (keys at 0, 0.5, 1); each chain link is an `ArgAnimationNode` (position and rotation keys at 21 values, from a hanging-chain calculation with a floor). The hook's `Tow.armArg(theta, clamped)` gives the value; `Tow.thetaFor(half)` the opening that puts the pins' tips `half` from the bar's line. The model prints its pin geometry for the hook (`hook: pivotX=... pinU=... a0=... thetaMax=...`) and a test keeps the hook's copy the same.
  - *Our .edm writer, checked wider:* 537 of 701 stock models (Bazar/World/Shapes and the Supercarrier's, under 1.2 MB) read and write back byte for byte, `ArgAnimationNode`, `ArgScaleNode`, bones and skins among them; the other 163 have node kinds it does not read (segments, lights, fake lights, a DECAL material key). Texture coordinates: v = 0 is the texture's top row.
  - *The tow with the new bar:* arrive (bar closed on the pintle, trailing on its wheels) > unhitch (ring set on its foot) > OPEN (arms swung wide) > carry to the nose gear, an arm either side of the nose wheels > CLAMP (the chain tightened: the arms close until the pins are in the axle's ends) > lift > the tractor backs onto the ring > pull. The axle as the hook takes it: `TOW.noseGear` 2.985 m ahead of the jet's own position, `TOW.axleDown` 1.555 m below it (FA-18C_hornet.lua: nose wheel diameter 0.5385 m; the jet's position 1.82 m above the deck = 2.221 - 0.4), `TOW.axleLen` 0.46 m end to end (A GUESS), a pin 4 cm into each end.
  - **The axle finder** (asked for by the user, "as the chock tool before": F10 > Tow > Axle finder): three statics of types of our own (a magenta rod with a yellow disc at its middle and a green flag; a cyan disc with a cross for each axle end) put on the player's nose gear every frame at exactly `TOW.noseGear` / `TOW.axleDown` / `TOW.axleLen`, and moved 10 cm, 1 cm or 1 mm at a time: fore / aft, up / down, longer / shorter. Every move is logged (`command axle ... noseGear= axleDown= axleLen=`) and the tow uses the numbers as they stand (for the session: the found values then go into the hook's source). "Flag test" sets the rod's model argument 1: the test of whether a script-made static takes a model argument at all (the bar's arms depend on it).
  - *How a static is placed (edObjects.dll, read):* `lLandObject::SetPos` does NOTHING when any of the bits 0x1D000009 of the object's word at +0x18 is set; otherwise it copies the pose (16 floats, rows of 4) to +0x158 and calls the object's ChangePos. `native.staticpos` now also returns whether the object afterwards says it is where it was put, and that flag word; the hook logs both for the tractor and for the marker.
  - *A limit to expect:* a static keeps its place as single-precision WORLD numbers. At this mission's place (z about -183,600 m) they step by 1.6 cm along that axis, so on a ship under way a static placed every frame shimmers by up to that against the jet (which is placed in double precision): the marker and the bar both. Nearer the map's origin the step is smaller. Sub-centimetre moves of the marker still shift its average place.
  - *Untried, all of it:* whether DCS takes the three static types; whether a script-made static takes its place (the log will say) and its model argument (the flag); the arms' direction of opening and the chain in DCS (ModelViewer2: argument 10).
- **Our static types never existed until 2026-11-15 13:00 (two bugs in the mod's entry.lua, found from dcs.log after the axle finder said "its type is missing"):** DCS runs a mod's entry.lua WITHOUT pcall ("attempt to call global 'pcall' (a nil value)": the declarations stopped at the first one), and the loop variable was named `_`, hiding DCS's translation function `_()` used for the display names. So no tow bar was ever drawn by earlier builds. Both fixed; a test now runs entry.lua with only what a mod's entry gets (no pcall) and checks the three types are declared and plugin_done is reached.
- **Spawn test** `director-test.ps1 -Spawn Shift` (`director-spawn.lua`): every spawn spot moved 8 m (≤ 60% of the leg) along its own first leg, same heading; route lines show it blue with a yellow X on the stock spot (48 models now). `Clean-Lua` now strips every CATCC-DIRTEST block (a bend and a shift can both be in the routes file).
- **The axle finder redone the chock tool's way (2026-11-15 afternoon; installed, not yet seen in DCS; 171 catcc-dcs tests).** After the entry.lua fixes and a declaration copied from the chock mod, DCS still answered "unknown static shape_name, category , type: CATCC axle marker" (run of 18:26 UTC). The user: "our chocks worked fine before"; sizing the axle is not needed, positioning is. So every remaining difference from the chock and route-line mods (which DCS made) was removed at once, rather than found one by one:
  - *Types:* named as their model files are, no spaces (`CATCC_AxleMarker_H15` ... `_H40`, `CATCC_Towbar`, `CATCC_AxleProbe`), declared by the chock's `deck_static()` in `Mods/tech/CATCC-Crew/Database/db_catcc_crew.lua`, run from entry.lua by `dofile`.
  - *Marker models:* Blender + ED's exporter (`crew-test/model/build_axle_marker.py`, as `chock-model/build_chock.py`), not our own .edm writer: a 0.9 m rod in 5 cm magenta / cyan bands across the jet, a yellow cube on each end (seen from the side it goes on the hub's middle), a yellow disc at the middle, a white stem to the deck.
  - *Spawn:* LINKED to the ship (`linkUnit`, `linkOffset`, `offsets`, `canCargo = false`), by the mission script's `CATCC_CR.marker`, destroyed and made anew (a new name each time) at every move. A linked static stands on the flight deck, so the height is in the model: one model per centimetre, 0.15 to 0.40 m; "Up / down" (10 cm, 1 cm) picks the model. Fore / aft by 10 cm, 1 cm, 1 mm. No per-frame placement, no native module: the marker does not follow the jet ("Report" or any move stands it under the jet again). Length and flag test dropped.
  - *A probe, once a mission at the first "Show", for the log only* (`CATCC_CR.probe`, line "axle finder: what DCS makes of our static types"): each of `CATCC_AxleMarker_H27` unlinked, `CATCC_AxleProbe` (our writer's model) linked and unlinked, `CATCC_Towbar` linked and unlinked, `AS32-31A` unlinked is made for an instant and removed. It answers what the tow needs to know: whether DCS takes a model from our own writer as a static, and whether an UNLINKED static of a type of ours can be made (the tow's tractor and bar are unlinked and placed by `native.staticpos`).
  - The mission-start "static types DCS does not know" check is gone: `DCS.getUnitTypeAttribute` knows no static at all (it called even AS32-31A unknown).
- **The axle finder worked in DCS, and the nose wheel axle is found (2026-11-15, run of 13:46 local; 171 catcc-dcs tests).**
