  - *The marker appeared and moved as asked* (ten markers made, one at a time). So a static type of ours works when it is named as its model file is, with no spaces, and declared in a Database file.
  - *The probe's line:* `CATCC_AxleMarker_H27 not linked: made; CATCC_AxleProbe linked: made; CATCC_AxleProbe not linked: made; CATCC_Towbar linked: made; CATCC_Towbar not linked: made; AS32-31A not linked: made`. So DCS also makes statics from models written by OUR OWN .edm writer, and makes them UNLINKED: neither was the cause of "unknown static". What was left as the cause: the type names ("CATCC axle marker": spaces, and a name other than the model file's) or their being declared in entry.lua itself (not told apart). The tow's tractor and bar (unlinked) can be made; whether they take their places (`native.staticpos`) and the bar its argument is still untried.
  - *The axle (F/A-18C, the user's eye, jet on the flight deck at 151 m forward):* its middle is **3.005 m ahead** of the jet's own position (was 2.985, from FA-18C_hornet.lua) and **0.257 m above the deck** (the marker at 0.26 m; the user: "down about 2 mm", then "maybe down 3 mm": there are no 1 mm height steps, the height being a model per centimetre). The jet's own position stood 21.975 m above the ship's origin; the flight deck is 20.149 m (DCS's route data): **1.826 m** above the deck (was 1.82). In the hook: `TOW.noseGear = 3.005`, `TOW.gearUp = 1.826`, `TOW.axleDown = 1.569`. The axle's length was not measured (the user: not needed); `TOW.axleLen` 0.46 m stays a guess.
- **The tow ran end to end in DCS (2026-11-15, run of 13:54 local), and what the user's four screenshots showed was put right (crewtest-16; installed, not yet seen; 171 catcc-dcs tests).** Proven in that run: an unlinked static of ours takes its place every frame (`native.staticpos`: "it says it is there", flag word 2) for the tractor and the bar; the bar's arms move on its model argument 10 (`native.objarg` on a static); the jet's argument 2 turns the nose wheels on screen although the aircraft writes it back to 0 every frame; the jet is pulled as a trailer to the hangar place.
  - *The bar's heads sat inside the nose wheels* (the user: "splay not wide enough; could also be scale"). Scale is right (Model 8, 108 in). The axle's length was a guess (0.46 m). Measured from DCS's own model (`FA-18C_Collision.edm`, its two `WHEEL_F` shells): each nose tyre is 0.173 m wide, 0.115 to 0.289 m either side of the centre line: 0.578 m over all. The heads belong OUTSIDE the tyres with the pins inwards: `TOW.axleLen = 0.58`: pin tips 0.25 m either side, the heads' inboard faces 0.30 m. The arms' widest opening went from 7.5 to 9 degrees (tips 0.74 m apart) so the open bar passes the tyres with 8 cm either side.
  - *The ring hung above and short of the tractor's hook.* The hitch was guessed (3.05 m behind the tractor's position, 0.45 m up). Measured from `as32-31a.edm`: the pintle hook is 2.85 to 3.00 m behind, 0.22 to 0.39 m up, on the centre line: `TOW.hitch = 2.93`, `TOW.hitchUp = 0.30`.
  - *The nose wheels turned the wrong way:* `TOW.steerSign = -1` (argument 2's sense). Its scale (75 degrees a unit) is still assumed.
  - *No driver on the tractor.* He was there in the code: catcc-t0 played the seated pose and `native.place` never failed, but it went through `AnimatedCharacterBase::set_origin` alone, which only copies the pose to +0x2D8; nothing reads that for a man who stands, and the recorder shows he never left the flight deck. From crewtest-16 `place` also writes his own pose on the ship (the 16 floats `local_position()` returns, +0x240) and says which it did ("origin + pose"). UNTRIED: whether DCS leaves that pose alone between frames, and where the seated pose's origin is on the man. The seat as the tractor's model has it: the steering wheel on the left (0.40 to 0.76 m off the centre line, 0.10 to 0.35 m ahead of the tractor's position), the cushion 0.65 m up from 0.15 to 0.55 m behind the position: `TOW.seat = { fwd = -0.40, left = 0.58, up = 0.65 }` (before: -1.2 / -0.3 / 0.35, inside the air start unit). F10 > Tow > Driver seat moves him 10 cm at a time.
  - *Textures* (the user: more wear, the tractor's colours): the paints are now the tractor's own, sampled from `as32-31a.zip` (31A_DIFF / 31A_DIFF_ROUGHMET, DXT3): white 188 186 185 (its panels: 189 186 186 clean, 163 162 162 dirty; roughness 0.46 to 0.55), red 148 32 30 (its hitch parts 146 32 32; roughness 0.65), yellow 182 130 20 (its reflective patches 180 128 18; roughness 0.38). Wear drawn into the atlas (`towbar.py` `atlas_layers`): every face's edges chipped to bare steel or rust, scratches (scuffs and to the metal), paint worn thin to the primer in patches, grime, rust stains on the bare metal parts, dust on the rubber, the stencilled letters partly worn off; roughness and metalness follow. Each of the eight sides of the two arms has its own strip of the atlas (512 x 16 pixels, 4 mm a pixel) so no two look alike, dirtier at the ring end and towards the heads.
  - *How the models were measured:* `edm.py` reads `as32-31a.edm` whole; the Hornet's collision model needs one more node kind (`model::SegmentsNode`: a u32, a count, 6 floats each), read with a copy of the reader. World places = each node's matrix x translation x rotation, arguments at 0.
- **The tow bar was the wrong bar: it is the 15 ft ALBAR 15, not the 9 ft one (2026-11-15; the user, with five photographs).** I had written "scale is right (Model 8, 108 in)": that was an assumption, never checked against anything. The user's photographs of F/A-18s under tow all show the bar's tractor end about 2 ft short of the aircraft's nose and a much heavier section than the model's; his orthographic sheet is headed "ALBAR 15 Universal Towbar". The ALBAR comes in 9, 15 and 20 ft; an F/A-18C's nose is 5.6 m ahead of its nose wheel axle, so the 9 ft bar's ring came 2.5 m ahead of the axle (what he saw: under the forward fuselage), a 15 ft bar's 4.2 m (his mark on the screenshot: about 4.5 m).
  - *What was done:* `towbar.py` still draws the bar as before, 108 in long, and writes it out `SCALE` = 180 / 108 times larger in every direction: 4.572 m long, its arms 4.2 in (0.106 m) square, the heads, plates, bolts and wheels with them (the proportions the user had approved). What has a size of its own stays real: the lunette ring (86 mm eye, 162 mm over all), the axle pins (60 mm long, 32 mm thick), the chain's links (48 x 24 mm, 8 mm wire; 21 of them now). The arms' widest opening is no longer a fixed angle but whatever puts the pins' tips 0.37 m off the bar's line (5.25 degrees).
  - *The hook's bar numbers* (`TOW.bar`, from the model's `hook:` line): pivotX 0.3333, armZ0 0.05458, pinU 3.8577, pinW 0.1042, a0 0.017170, thetaMax 0.091651, wheelUp 0.1250, ringRest 0.1529. Hooked up, the tractor's position is now 7.1 m ahead of the axle.
  - *Not known:* the real ALBAR 15's tube section (4.2 in comes from scaling the drawing; a close-up photograph of the bar beside an F/A-18's nose tyre, 6.8 in wide, agrees with about 4 in).
- **The wheels turn while towing (2026-11-15; the arguments from the user; installed, not yet seen; 171 catcc-dcs tests).** Tractor: 8 every wheel, 9 steering (+ to the left), 20 pitch about the rear axle. Hornet: 101 nose wheels, 103 left main, 102 right main. Their ranges and senses, read from DCS's models (`as32-31a.edm`, `FA-18C_Collision.edm`): a wheel argument is one turn a unit and rising = rolling forward (tractor and the jet's nose wheel; the mains taken to be the same); tractor steering +1 = 31 degrees left; pitch +1 = nose up 1 cm; the jet's nose wheel steering (argument 2) is 90 degrees at 1 (75 had been assumed: `TOW.steerDeg = 90`) and + is to the left, which is the sense the first run got wrong.
  - *How (`Tow.wheelsShow`, every frame, from what moved since the frame before):* the tractor's travel along its own heading turns its wheels (rear wheel radius 0.385 m; the front ones, 0.266 m, share the argument and so turn too slowly for their size: the model's limit); its turn for that travel gives the steering a tractor of its wheelbase (1.685 m) needs, eased over a quarter second and kept while standing; its change of speed gives the pitch. The jet's nose wheel point along the bar turns argument 101 (rolling radius 0.257 m, the axle height found); each main wheel's own travel along the jet's nose turns 103 / 102 (0.37 m, 1.56 m either side), so the outer wheel turns further in a turn.
- **The shimmer of the tractor and the bar, and its cure: they are now the ship's children (2026-11-15; crewtest-17; installed, not yet seen; 171 catcc-dcs tests).** The user, after the 15 ft bar and the turning wheels worked: "the only issues I see are the jittering". The cause as expected: a static made by itself holds its pose as single-precision WORLD numbers (1.6 cm steps where the mission is), given again every frame beside a ship doing 30 knots. What pointed to the cure: the axle finder's marker, a static LINKED to the ship, stood steady to the millimetre on the same ship.
  - *What a linked static is (edObjects.dll, read):* `viObjectNode` has a parent (`Parent()` = the pointer at +0xE0; `ChangeParent()`, exported). `lLandObject::GetPosition()` gives, when there is a parent, the world pose worked out through the parents; its own pose at +0x158 is then in the PARENT'S frame. `lLandObject::SetPos(p)` copies p to +0x158 and calls `ChangePos(GetPosition())`: so for a child, p is a pose in the parent's frame. A parent that moves tells each child whose `getPositionUsingMode()` is 1 (lLandObject's) through `OnParentChangePos()`.
  - *So:* the tow's tractor and bar are made LINKED (`CATCC_CR.statics(spec, fwd, stbd)`, at the tractor's waiting place), the native module says whether each has a parent (`native.staticinfo`: class, register kind, parent's class or false, flag word; `staticpos` returns the parent's class as a sixth value), and the hook then gives `staticpos` the pose in the SHIP'S OWN FRAME (`Tow.OWN`: metres forward, up, to starboard: any height, so the hangar deck too) in place of world numbers. No parent (an older module, or linking refused): placed in the world as before, and the log says so. The `took` check for a child compares against +0x158.
  - *Untried:* whether `SetPos` goes through for a linked static (its flag word; the log's "staticpos:" line says), and whether anything of the ship's puts a linked static back on the flight deck. If the tractor stays on the flight deck above where it waits, that is this.
  - *The tractor's front hitch* (the user: "the tug has attachment points on its front and rear... keep that in mind"): the same hook at the front, 1.65 to 1.80 m ahead of the tractor's position, 0.22 to 0.39 m up (`TOW.hitchFront = 1.73`, not used yet). For pushing a jet (back into a spot, nose out) the tractor would face the jet with the bar on its front hook.
- **On-screen messages can be hidden, for filming (2026-11-15; asked for by the user for a demo reel; 172 catcc-dcs tests).** F10 > CATCC crew test > Tow > "Messages on screen: hide / show (for filming)" (the top level is full: ten items). Going off: one 4-second notice that also clears what is on screen (`outText`'s third argument), then the crew test puts nothing more on screen; every message still goes to the log as "SAY (not shown) ...". On again from the same item, and with every new mission. It hides the crew test's own messages only (everything of the tow, the crew, the catapults); the other CATCC scripts' messages (PriFly's pilot hook, the chock and route-line tools) are not touched.
- **Unhook: the bar comes off the jet and goes back on the tractor (2026-11-15; the user: "after unhooking, the towbar should be disconnected and reattached to the tug"; 172 catcc-dcs tests; installed, not yet seen).** Before, the ring came off the pintle and the bar was left clamped on the nose gear. Now (F10 > Tow > "Unhook (the bar goes back on the tractor)"), with the ring staying on the pintle throughout: the chain is let go and the arms open wide, the pins out of the axle (`release`); the head end is set down on the bar's own wheels (`setdown`); the tractor draws the open bar 1.2 m forward, its heads clear of the nose wheels they stood either side of (`clear`, `TOW.clearBar`); the arms close (`close`); the tractor moves 8 m on with the bar trailing on its pintle and parks (`leave`, `parked`). The jet is left where it stands. Unhooking under way does the same from where the tractor stops. Broken off before the bar reached the jet: still on the tractor, it leaves with it; broken off while the bar was being put on the jet: the bar is left where it is, as before.
- **Run of 2026-11-16 (the user) and what was built after it (crewtest-17 unchanged; 174 catcc-dcs tests; installed, not yet seen).**
  - *The shimmer's cure, from the log (the user's note on it was left empty):* the tractor and the bar were made linked, both are children of `.?AVwoShip@@`, their flag word is 2, and `SetPos` with a pose in the ship's own frame went through ("it says it is there") in every tow of the day. So a linked static CAN be moved anywhere on the ship, at any height, through its own `SetPos`. Whether the shimmer is gone to the eye: not said yet.
  - *Good as they are (the user):* the driver on his seat; the shirts; parking from the landing area ("seems better now"); the bar's section; the tractor's front wheels; the textures ("amazing").
  - *Unhook, in the user's order* ("disconnect from tug. Disconnect from aircraft. Connect to tug. Drive away."): OFF THE TRACTOR (`ringoff`: the ring lifted off the pintle and set down on its foot; `moveup`: the tractor moves 1.2 m up to make room), OFF THE AIRCRAFT (`release`: the chain let go, the arms open, the pins out of the axle; `setdown`: the head end onto the bar's own wheels; `roll`: the bar rolled forward clear of the nose wheels, up to the tractor; `close`: its arms closed), BACK ON THE TRACTOR (`ringon`: the ring lifted onto the pintle), AWAY (`leave`, `parked`). The build of the evening before had kept the ring on the pintle throughout.
  - *Nobody walks across a lowered elevator* (the user: the elevator waits for them to leave, but "sometimes they walk back over a lowered elevator and float"). Every walk order of ours (`walkOrder`) is now planned (`Walk.plan`): a straight walk that would cross a platform we hold down, are about to lower, or are raising goes round its inboard side, corner to corner, 1.2 m clear; a place ON such a platform becomes the nearest place at its inboard side. The legs after the first are kept (`Walk.legs`) and given one at a time, each when the man stands at the end of the one before (planned again from where he stands if he stops elsewhere for 6 s). Not covered: a man already walking when the elevator is told to go down (a walking man takes no new order), and an elevator DCS lowers by itself.
  - *The deck spotting tool* (asked for: "spawn a full deck of aircraft for me to review... menu items to lock on to the nearest aircraft and adjust its position in large and small increments, including rotation, so I can spot the entire deck accurately and you can record the exact locations"): a menu of its own, F10 > CATCC deck spots (the crew test's top level is full). "Park a Hornet on every deck spot": a static F/A-18C, LINKED to the ship, on every spot of `crewtest_routes.lua` (the spotting plan's), made by the mission script (`CATCC_CR.deck`; category "Planes"); none within 14 m of a live aircraft (the player's own: static aircraft collide). "Lock on the one the camera looks at": where the camera's line of sight meets the deck (looking level or up: the one nearest the camera), so from any outside view; also "the next / the one before". Moves along the aircraft's OWN nose and wings by 1 m / 10 cm / 1 cm, turns by 5 / 1 / 0.1 degrees: the static is made anew each time. Add an aircraft where the camera looks; remove the locked one; put it back on its built-in spot; start over. Wings folded / spread for all: the statics' model argument 8 through `native.objarg` (untried on a static aircraft; if it cannot be set the tool says so once and leaves them as DCS draws them). **The record:** every change rewrites `Saved Games\DCS\Logs\catcc-deck-spots.lua` (for each spot its place now, the built-in place it started from, and moved / added / removed), and the next "Park" takes that file up again, so the work carries over between missions. When the user is done, that file is what goes back into the spotting plan (`carrier-ops-handoff-2/deck-spots.csv` > `deckSpots.gen.ts`).
  - *A Lua limit met:* a chunk takes 200 local names and `crewtest_hook.lua` has them: new things hang on a table (`Walk`, `Tow.Deck`).
  - *Still untested:* "Abort the hook-up" (the user was in the hangar).

## Approach radar and the Hornet's ICLS / ACLS (2026-11-22)

- **The Hornet's own ICLS / ACLS indications: partly readable (2026-11-22; research, nothing built; `catcc-handoff/research/hornet-acls.md`).** From the DCS install's F/A-18C cockpit scripts: the ICLS localizer / glideslope deviation is readable as numbers only through the standby attitude indicator's ICLS pointers, cockpit draw arguments 211 (vertical) and 212 (horizontal), -1..1 (the SAI device is fed by the ICLS receiver, device 52); the HUD and DDI ADI needles and the ACLS tadpole are indicator elements placed by C++ controllers, so at most their presence can be read; the ACLS status cues are text (HUD windows 5 / 6 through `list_indication(1)`: 10 SEC, TILT, ACL RDY, CPLD P/R; the DDI ACL page's ACL RDY / CMD CNT / LND CHK / MODE 1 / MODE 2 only while a DDI shows that page). The Hornet has no ACLS lamps. ICLS channel 352, UFC/MAN 353. Readable on each client in the export environment (`GetDevice(0):get_argument_value`, `list_indication`), which the server export settings do not gate as far as the sources say; `LoGetGlideDeviation` / `LoGetSideDeviation` are ownship-gated and probably not the Hornet's ICLS. The GUI's `Export` table lists `GetDevice`, so the pilot hook may read the SAI itself: unverified. Mission needs Activate ICLS (numbers), plus Activate Link 4 and Activate ACLS on a carrier with those attributes for the ACLS cues. Proposed transport: the pilot hook's existing TCP line to the server hook, a new `ICLS` line relayed as `{"type":"icls",...}`; if the GUI cannot read the cockpit, a one-line Export.lua addition sends UDP to the pilot hook on 127.0.0.1. Next: the user flies the unshipped probe (`research/hornet-acls/probe-export.lua`), which settles the pointers' scale, sign and rest value and which environment can read them.
- **The approach radar: AN/SPN-46 ACLS tracks aircraft on final continuously (2026-11-22; asked: does the carrier have a second, faster radar for the approach / final, and if so should what it covers refresh faster).** Research verdict: yes, in substance. The SPN-43 (15 RPM, 4 s) is the marshal / approach-control search radar; the final approach has its own precision radar, the AN/SPN-46(V) Precision Approach Landing System (the ACLS radar; the AN/SPN-35 PAR on older ships, replaced by the SPN-46 on Nimitz-class by the mid 1990s). It is not a faster-turning search radar but a tracking radar: two Ka/X-band monopulse antennas, each locking one aircraft and following it continuously in range, azimuth and elevation to touchdown or waveoff, so two aircraft at once ("leapfrog": as one lands the next is acquired). Its data go to the CATCC operator consoles (radar display) and aircraft-control indicators, and as commands over Link 4A to the aircraft; the final controller compares the pilot's "say needles" with "the readout on the approach radar scope".
  - *Figures found:* range 8 NM (FAS) / 10 NM (Wikipedia; Navy approval in 1987 of "full automatic control from aircraft acquisition at ten nautical miles to touchdown"); azimuth 25 degrees, PRF 2,000 pps, precision 2 ft (Wikipedia infobox); antenna travel left 30 / right 60 degrees, +30 / -15 degrees ((V)3 FCC filing); lock-on "sometime between 8 DME and 4 DME" (CNATRA P-816). The SPN-42 (its predecessor) "locks on aircraft when the aircraft enters the acquisition gate ... then tracks the aircraft ... until touchdown or waveoff" (NAVEDTRA). No public source gives the SPN-46's track data rate in updates per second; a monopulse tracker measures every pulse, far faster than anything we can show. The SPN-41 (ICLS, "bullseye") is not a radar: two Ku-band transmitters (azimuth at the stern, elevation aft of the island) the aircraft receives one way; it sees nothing. The SPN-44 is a range-rate radar measuring approach speed for the LSO, not position.
  - *Sources:* FAS man.fas.org/dod-101/sys/ship/weaps/an-spn-46.htm, an-spn-41.htm, an-spn-44.htm, an-spn-35.htm; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AN/SPN-46 and /AN/SPN-35; electronicstechnician.tpub.com/14089/css/Significant-Interfaces-34.htm and An-Spn-46-V-Pals-33.htm (Electronics Technician vol. 4, radar systems); navyaviation.tpub.com/14030/css/Landing-Control-Central-System-An-Spn-42-206.htm; CNATRA P-816 (01-14) (murphysys.com/dcs/P-816.pdf), case III / ACLS lock-on; fcc.report/ELS/Sierra-Nevada-Corporation/0107-EX-PL-2014/145575.pdf (SPN-46(V)3 antenna limits).
  - *Modelled (`src/components/catcc/radar/spn46.ts`):* coverage 10 NM astern, 12.5 degrees either side of the final bearing (BRC less 9), elevation to the antenna's 30 degrees, airborne aircraft of our side. A free channel locks the nearest aircraft in the acquisition gate, inside 8 NM and 3 degrees of the centreline (the gate the Final view's LOCK-ON readout already uses); a lock holds while the aircraft stays in coverage and drops at touchdown, past the ship or out of the sector; two channels. The SPN-46 is a paint source of the focus carrier beside the SPN-43 sweep, looking at its locks only (`Radar.only`): a locked aircraft repaints on every painter step that brings a new frame. Everything else keeps the SPN-43's 4 s.
  - *Effective rate:* the data, not the radar, set it. The DCS hook samples at `rate_hz` (1 Hz in the shipped config), so a locked aircraft moves once a second (4 times the SPN-43 rate); raise `rate_hz` and it follows, up to the painter's 10 steps a second. The painter no longer repaints a track twice from one frame.
  - *Only while the ACLS is up* (Final view's Landing aids: ACLS UP / DOWN, `aids.acls`, up when unset as there), and *only on the Approach / Final display* (a CATCC seat's FINAL view): the SPN-46's data go to the final controller's displays, not the SPN-43 scope, so the RADAR view of the same seat and every other seat keep the 4 s picture for everyone. With "realistic radar updates" off nothing changes (the live picture at the data rate).
  - *Not modelled:* the lock being broken and re-acquired by the controller (bad "needles"), side-lobe locks, the beacon (APN-202 / 245) an aircraft needs for a Mode I / II lock (any aircraft in the gate locks), and the antennas' place on the ship (measured from the ship's reference point).

## PriFly: the Sand table mode is the Ouija Board (2026-11-22)

- The user asked for it: PriFly's manual mode (the mockup's "Sand table", HANDOFF.md's "Sand table (drag the pieces)") is labelled **Ouija Board**, the carrier's own name for the deck-handling table. Mode toggle "Ouija Board"; deck title "Ouija Board (manual chits)". The internal mode key stays `sand`.

## Approach / Final: AZ/EL display (2026-11-22)

- **A third display on the Approach / Final seat: AZ/EL, one square scope on the glidepath** (the user: elevation and azimuth "in single scope... centered on the ACLS/ICLS glidepath"). Azimuth (localizer) deviation runs across, elevation (glideslope) deviation up, both in degrees. It sits beside the Azimuth display, as tall as it and as wide as it is tall (square to start with; the user will tweak it). `AzElScope.tsx`; the geometry in `finalModel.ts` (`glideDeviation`, `azElPicture`).
- **Origin:** the glidepath origin the Elevation and Azimuth displays already use: the touchdown point (along 0, lateral 0 in the final frame, which is the carrier's reported position; 0 ft), on the final bearing, 3.5° glidepath (`GS_DEG`). ICLS and ACLS share that path in this app. Azimuth = atan2(lateral, along); elevation = atan2(altitude, ground range from the origin) − 3.5°. Right of course (the pilot's right, inbound) is right on the scope, high is up, as the pilot's needles would show it.
- **Field of regard:** ±6° azimuth, ±3° about the glideslope (elevation angles 0.5° to 6.5°), out to 6 NM ground range from the origin; nothing at or past the origin (along ≤ 0.05 NM). The two axes have their own scales (6° across, 3° up, over the same width), so the glideslope reads twice as fine as the localizer. Crosshair through the centre, hash marks every 0.5° (longer at whole degrees), whole degrees labelled, L / R and HI / LO at the ends; the edge of the field drawn faintly; the ±0.5° glideslope lines follow the Final layers' "Glideslope ±0.5°" switch, as on the Elevation display.
- **Contacts:** the nearest aircraft in the field (ground range from the origin) is the contact: the main radar's track colour (red in a conflict), its trail where the trail was in the field, and a tag with its label and range (NM). Every other aircraft in the field is a ghost: a dim square, no tag, drawn behind the contact.
- **Sidebar in two columns** (the user's sketch: "SIDE 1", "SIDE 2" beside the Elevation display, above the AZ/EL display): side 1 = Deck and the landing aids (ICLS / ACLS); side 2 = Bolter slot, Upwind turn and Sequence. Each column scrolls by itself. The column pair is as wide as the AZ/EL display, so the Elevation and Azimuth displays give up the difference from the old 344 px sidebar. In the narrower columns the slot options' gap text wraps under the DME / time.
## CATCC UI kit and Figma plugin (2026-11-22)

- **A CATCC UI kit, and a Figma plugin made from it (2026-11-22; the user: "create another UI kit for the CATCC interface ... something I can import into figma that will allow me to quickly 'sketch' out the interface for new features using existing CATCC UI components").**
  - *The kit:* `/catcc/kit` (signed in, like `/catcc`; in the CATCC shell, `.cc-root` with `catcc.css`, dark only). Sections: Tokens, Typography, Header & seats, Controls, Panels, Sidebar panels (the live components), Strips & rows, Scope, Contacts & tags, Overlays & dialogs, Icons. Source: `src/components/catcc/kit/`. `tokens.ts` holds the colours, fonts, text sizes and radii gathered from `catcc.css` and the components' inline styles. `primitives.tsx` and `scopeKit.tsx` hold kit primitives. `specimens.tsx` is the list the page renders. `demo.ts` is a demo picture, run through the radar's own `buildPicture`.
  - *Real components, not pictures:* where a component exists it is imported and rendered with demo data: InfoPanels, DeckPanel, InterceptPanel, DonorsPanel, LightsPanel, Notifications, DeckBar, LayersPanel, FreqPanel, OptionsDialog, HandoffRow, the flight strip (FlightCard via `flightOf`) and the icons. Where a look exists only as inline styles in a view (FinalView's `segBtn` / `kv` / deck status, CatccApp's header pieces, Scope's hits / trails / vectors / `renderTag` / rings, BossView's `btn` ...), the kit primitive copies that call site's values exactly, and its comment names the call site. No view was changed: moving the views onto the primitives is a follow-up.
  - *Scope elements at the default sizes:* the scope draws in map units scaled per zoom. The kit draws hits, tags and trails at the default display settings (UI_DEF, step 2 = ×1.3: one unit = 860/97 × 1.3 = 11.53 px), with the scope's own classes (`.scope`, `.zs`, `.tg`), so a sketch matches the live scope at its defaults. `ScopeFrame` draws the rings / compass / grid for any size and range with the scope's formulas.
  - *The Figma plugin is generated from the kit:* `node scripts/catcc/figma-kit.gen.ts` serves the kit page from its own Vite server on a free port (no `.env`; the Supabase client gets a dead address), opens it in headless Chromium and reads every `data-figma` specimen as the browser laid it out (`scripts/catcc/figma-kit/serialize.js`): boxes, fills, borders, radii, shadows, text runs with their fonts, and inline SVG with computed styles inlined. Non-scaling strokes are converted to the SVG's units, trail dots become squares, and SVG lettering is lifted out as real text. Flex boxes and block stacks become auto layout where the browser's positions can be reproduced (margins become padded wrapper frames). Otherwise the frame keeps absolute positions. The plugin (`figma/catcc-kit/plugin.cjs`, assembled with the data into `code.js`) makes the `CATCC` colour variables, `CATCC/…` text styles, and a "CATCC Kit" page of Components / Component Sets (variants from `data-variant`, e.g. `state=on`). It binds matching fills to variables and matching text to styles.
  - *Why a plugin rather than an SVG / .fig file:* only the plugin API makes real Components with variants, Variables and text styles. A pasted SVG gives flat vectors. The plugin needs no network (`networkAccess: none`), uses `documentAccess: dynamic-page` and async APIs throughout, and loads every font before use. Claude can't run Figma. The plugin is tested against a stand-in for the API (enums, auto layout children, fonts loaded before text, unique variant names) in `src/components/catcc/kit/figmaKit.test.ts`. That test also checks `kit.json` against the kit's specimen list and tokens, and checks that `code.js` is current.
  - *FinalView is being redesigned (new az/el scope, two-column sidebar):* the kit's `Scope/Precision display`, `Panel/Deck status`, `Panel/Landing aids` and `Panel/Sequence card` copy FinalView as of this entry. Update them, and regenerate, once the redesign lands.

## Room access: wing members only (2026-11-22)

- **Before:** any signed-in ReadyRoom user got into the room. `/catcc` was behind `ProtectedRoute` only (any Supabase session), and the room's `supabaseVerifier` accepted any valid Supabase token; its profile/pilot lookup was for the display name and never refused. Sign-in itself isn't restricted (Discord OAuth with `identify guilds` scopes; the `discord_guilds` list is stored but checked nowhere), so anyone with a Discord account could open the link.
- **Now the room refuses non-members** (`catcc-server/room/membership.ts`, `auth.ts`). Member = the pilot whose `pilots.discord_id` is the Discord id of the Supabase Auth Discord identity, with a current `pilot_assignments` row (started by today, no end or an end after today) in a squadron with no past `deactivated_date`, and whose current `pilot_statuses` row (latest start) is a status with `statuses.isActive` true. Or: an active `manual_override` rule for `access_developer_settings` on the user's profile (ReadyRoom admin).
  - *Why the Discord identity, not `user_profiles.pilot_id`:* `user_profiles` RLS lets a user update their own row with no column check, so `pilot_id` can be pointed at anyone's pilot. `pilots.discord_id`, assignments and statuses are written by roster managers only, and the Auth identity comes from Discord's OAuth. On 2026-11-22 all 28 linked profiles had `pilots.discord_id` = their Discord identity.
  - *Statuses:* the roster's own `isActive` flag (Settings > Roster). Today Active is the only one; On Leave, Retired, AWOL and Removed are refused. Standing (Provisional, Nugget...) doesn't matter. One wing, four squadrons (operational and training), so any squadron counts. Dry run against the data: 25 members let in; refused: 1 Retired, 1 Removed, 1 with no current assignment, 2 with no roster pilot.
  - *No migration.* The alternative, a new `access_catcc` permission granted by rules, needs an additive migration, the full permission checklist (TypeScript, calculator init and switch), rules for every basis, and the room reading `user_permission_cache`, which the client computes and which expires; not done.
- **Wire:** a valid sign-in that is refused is closed with **4403** and a short reason (`CLOSE_FORBIDDEN` in `protocol.ts`); an invalid token stays 4003. Members are cached 5 min per token, refusals 1 min, failed lookups not at all (closed 4003 and retried). A connection already open is not re-checked.
- **Client:** `useCatccRoom` gets conn `forbidden` and `refused` (the reason) and doesn't retry. `/catcc` shows the app's `EmptyState` (lock, "Access denied", the reason) instead of the interface; the deck kneeboard shows "Access denied · reason" in its feed line. `CATCC_DEV_NOAUTH` unchanged; the DCS hook's token is separate and unchanged.
- **CATCC kit brought up to the merged Approach / Final redesign (2026-11-22, follow-up to the kit entry above).**
  - The kit now renders the live FinalView (`Layout/Approach Final`, 1520 × 860, on the demo picture with two arrivals on final added). Its Elevation / Azimuth displays, approach toolbar, two-column sidebar and bolter slot become Figma components by being picked out of it (`from` / `pick` on a specimen; `data-figma-from` / `data-figma-pick` for the generator). So they are FinalView's own markup, not copies. The hand-made `ParFrame` (which approximated the grid and ramp) is gone. `ParBlip` / `ParLabel` stay because they are exact.
  - The AZ/EL display is the real `AzElScope` (334 × 334, FinalView's fallback size), in three variants: contact + ghost, conflict, empty.
  - The Final-derived panel specimens (Deck status, Landing aids, Sequence card) are now as wide as a sidebar column in that layout (180 px). Deck status gained `called=no` (the "PriFly hasn't called it" line). Landing aids gained the ACLS lock rows (`AclsRow`). 227 specimens.
- **Approach / Final: slot timeline labels, ACLS list held to the SPN-46's two channels, deck word CLEAR / FOUL only (2026-11-22).**
  - *Slot timeline (Bolter slot and Upwind turn panels):* with the sidebar in two ~180 px columns the option keys (NOW / 4 / 6 / 8) ran into the sub-line and into each other, and the arrivals' callsigns ran together ("HAWK2032"). The timeline (`SlotTimeline` in `FinalView.tsx`) now reserves two label rows above the line and two below at all times, 70 px in all, so its height never changes with the picture; the sub-line sits in its own row above it. Placement is a pure function, `final/timelineLabels.ts` (tests beside it). Widths come from the mono advance (0.6 em of 10.5 px).
  - *Placement rule:* labels in time order. Every box stays inside the panel and over its own tick (clamped at the ends). Near row first, then the far row, whose label gets a vertical leader from the tick through a column kept clear of the near row. To make room, the row's previous box may slide left (still over its tick), or the near row's last label moves up to the far row so the new one takes the near row. So leaders are never slanted and nothing crosses text.
  - *Overflow:* a label that fits in neither row is folded into a grey "+n" chip where it would have gone, or into the chip just before it. If even the chip has no room, the latest label placed is folded in too. The earliest marks keep their own labels. Ticks are always drawn. In practice it takes ticks a few px apart (several arrivals within ~10 s on a 180 px column) to make a chip. The option rows beneath now wrap their margin text instead of running past the panel edge.
  - *ACLS list:* the Landing aids list showed LOCK-ON for every arrival in the 8 NM / 3° gate, three at once, while the SPN-46 painter (`spn46.ts`) holds two. CatccApp now keeps the SPN-46's holds with the same `spn46Locks` over the same live tracks as the painter, whether or not the realistic sweep is on, and passes them to FinalView (`aclsLocks`). LOCK-ON is shown only for those (two at most). The other arrivals on final inside 10 NM and 6° show ACQUIRING (approaching the gate or waiting for a free channel). Without the held set (the kit), the nearest two in the gate are shown as locked. The sweep and CatccApp keep their own copies of the holds: they agree except right after the sweep restarts with three aircraft in the gate, when the painter may pick a different two for a moment.
  - *Deck status panel:* the big word is CLEAR or FOUL only. Who or what fouls the deck is PriFly's business, and "FOUL · <CALLSIGN> IN THE LANDING AREA" wrapped and changed the panel's height. The "From the picture · PriFly hasn't called it" line is removed. The header's next arrival stays on one line: it now reads "201 3/4 in 1:12" (the Sequence card's wording, which fits a 180 px column), and a long callsign gives way (ellipsis) before the time. PriFly's own deck displays are unchanged. Kit: `DeckStatusPanel` matches, and its `called=no` variant is gone (226 specimens, figma/catcc-kit regenerated).

## Conflict alerts by relative motion, arrivals cut off at touchdown (2026-11-22)

- **The report:** Approach / Final showed many conflicts, e.g. a Case III sequence (FB 061, ship ~22 kt) with 712 at ~1.2 NM / 300 ft / 128 kt and 205 behind it at ~3-3.5 NM / 1,000 ft / ~150 kt, both red. That is a normal in-trail sequence: 712 traps in ~40 s, when 205 is still ~2 NM back.
- **Cause:** the red tags (and the radar's) come from `predictConflict` (`src/catcc/core/geometry/maneuver.ts`, via `findConflicts`). It projected every aircraft in a straight line for the whole 120 s look-ahead. So the leader on final flew on through the ship and the faster trailer "caught" it 1-1.5 min later, after the leader had already trapped. A pair inside the bubble that was opening or steady was a conflict too (CPA clamped to now). The separation bubbles were coloured by a second test, `sepViolated` in `finalModel.ts` (another aircraft inside the bubble now, no motion at all), so bubbles and tags could disagree.
- **Research.**
  - CV NATOPS lateral minima (positive radar control): 5 NM at 50 NM or more from the antenna, 3 NM inside 50, 2 NM on a designated approach or established downwind inside 12 NM, 1-1/2 NM established on final within 5 NM of the ship ([Navy Air Traffic Controller training manual, CATCC separation criteria, Table 11-6](https://navyaviation.tpub.com/14342A/Table-11-6-Lateral-Separation-381.html)). These match `lateralStandardNm` and `sepStd`. Separation is lateral or vertical: a loss needs both lost (the same table's vertical counterpart, Table 11-7, and FAA JO 7110.65 ch. 5 §5, radar separation, which measures between target centres and applies the reduced in-trail minimum only on the same final approach course: [FAA JO 7110.65 ch. 5 §9 Radar Arrivals](https://www.faa.gov/air_traffic/publications/atpubs/atc_html/chap5_section_9.html)).
  - Approach control "establishes the interval for aircraft on final approach" and is responsible for separation until hand-off to final / PriFly ([same manual, Approach Control](https://navyaviation.tpub.com/14342A/Approach-Control-408.html)). Case III pushes are a minute apart, which is the interval at the ramp; a leader still in the landing area when the trailer reaches the ramp is a foul-deck waveoff for the trailer (LSO / PriFly), not a radar loss of separation. No source found gives a radar rule for a pair whose leader lands first: the standard simply stops applying once the leader is on deck.
  - Pairs the standard applies to: every pair under control. The 1-1/2 NM figure is only for aircraft both established on final within 5 NM (in trail on the same course); crossing or opposite-direction traffic near the final takes the ordinary standard (here, in the carrier pattern, the existing 1 NM `PATTERN_LAT_NM`, see the pattern-traffic entry).
- **New rule (`predictConflict`):**
  - Each aircraft flies its course and groundspeed (climb / descent for 30 s, not below its marshal floor), sampled every 1 s plus the lateral CPA. The worst moment is where max(lateral / lateral std, vertical / 1,000 ft) is least; under 1 is a loss.
  - **Conflict** = a loss within the look-ahead that the pair is heading into: the worst moment is now or ahead. A pair inside the bubble that is opening or holding steady is not a conflict. A pass stays alerted for 6 s after its closest point (`CONFLICT_HOLD_S`) so it doesn't flicker at the abeam. A pair already inside and only creeping closer (under 5% of the standard: track noise) is not news.
  - **Landing cut-off:** an aircraft established on final (`onFinalOf`: astern of the ship within 12 NM, inside 6° of the centreline, course within 20° of the final bearing, closing on the ship at 40 kt or more, closure net of the ship's own course and speed) is predicted only up to its touchdown. A trailer that hasn't closed to the standard by then is clear. The ramp interval is shown by the slot timeline already.
  - **Standards:** both on final = in trail: 1-1/2 NM when the trailer is within 5 NM of the ship, else 2 NM (the old rule used the nearer aircraft's range and only inside 6 NM). Otherwise both in the pattern (inside 10 NM, below 2,500 ft): 1 NM. Otherwise `lateralStandardNm` by the nearer aircraft's range. Vertical 1,000 ft throughout (unchanged).
  - **Bolter / waveoff:** one climbing out ahead of the next arrival is opening and isn't a conflict. One on the downwind passing an arrival the other way is a conflict only if it will pass inside 1 NM with less than 1,000 ft (unchanged from the pattern-traffic entry).
- **Display:** the separation bubbles on the Elevation / Azimuth displays keep the standard's geometry (`sepStd`). They turn red when the aircraft is in one of the picture's conflicts (`sepViolated(a, pic.conflicts)`), so bubbles and tags always agree. The radar seats use the same `findConflicts`, so their alerts follow the same rule. No amber "compression" caution: the slot timeline already shows each arrival's time to the ball, and a new colour would be a new state the mockups don't have.
- **AZ/EL display: track table and primary track (2026-11-22).**
  - *Table:* top right of the scope, one row per aircraft the SPN-46 holds (the Landing aids LOCK-ON list, so two at most), nearest first: board number (else callsign), type as people write it (`typeName` in `scopeModel.ts`: F/A-18C, not the tag's F18C), ground range from the glidepath origin as the scope's tag gives it. Green dot on the primary row; the other row keeps the dot's space empty so the text lines up. Two rows and a flag line are always reserved and the table is absolutely placed, so nothing moves as tracks come and go. It is 136 px wide in the scope-label size (10.5 px mono) so it clears the HI and top "3" scale labels.
  - *Picking:* click a row, or a held track on the scope (24 px hit square), to make it primary. The primary is the bright contact with trail and tag; everything else in the field, held or not, is a ghost. The pick is this browser's (`localStorage` `catcc.final.azelPrimary`) and follows the aircraft id. When the picked aircraft, having been held, drops out of the SPN-46's holds, the pick is cleared and the nearest held track is primary again, so the next pass doesn't come back to an old pick. After a reload the stored pick waits until the room has that aircraft held again. A held track beyond the scope's 6 NM (the SPN-46 locks out to 8) is listed with its range but not drawn; if it is the primary, the scope has no bright contact until it comes inside.
  - *Primary not the nearest held track:* the primary row turns amber (`#E7B04F`, the app's attention colour) and an amber NOT NEAREST flag shows under the rows. The words say why the row is amber, and the reserved line keeps the table's size fixed. The other option the user named, an amber box around the row, would show the same state with no words. "Nearest" means the nearest of the held tracks.
  - *Nothing held (ACLS off in Landing aids, or nobody in the gate):* the table is empty and the scope works as before: the nearest aircraft in the field is the contact, the rest are ghosts.
  - Logic: `azElSelect` in `final/finalModel.ts` (tests beside it). Kit: the AZ/EL specimens pass the two demo arrivals as held, and the new `contact=not-nearest` variant picks the farther one (227 specimens, figma/catcc-kit regenerated).
  - *Seen from the ship (user call):* the AZ/EL is drawn as the controller sees it, looking out from the ship along the approach. Elevation is unchanged (high is up) and azimuth is mirrored: an aircraft high and right of course from the pilot's view shows high and left, and the controller reverses the direction mentally. `glideDeviation` keeps its sign (az + = pilot's right) and the mirroring is in the screen mapping (`azElX` / `azElY` in `finalModel.ts`, tested). As on the Azimuth display, the end labels name the side of course an aircraft drawn there is on, so R is now at the left end and L at the right. The contact, ghosts, trail, click targets and tag all go through the same mapping, and the tag still flips left in the right 30% so it stays inside the scope. The track table stays top right, where it now sits over high and left-of-course aircraft.

## Conflict check: 800 ft inside 10 NM (2026-11-22)

- The user: "Separation standard should be 800 ft inside 10 NM for conflict check and bubbles." The conflict prediction (`predictConflict`) now takes its vertical standard from `verticalStandardFt` by the nearer aircraft's range from the ship: 800 ft inside 10 NM, 1,000 ft beyond (CV NATOPS 3.3). The Final display's bubbles (`sepStd`) use the same function, so tags and bubbles agree.

## CATCC kit for Figma's free plan: SVG export (2026-11-22)

- **The report:** the Figma development plugin (`figma/catcc-kit`) didn't work on the user's free (Starter) account.
- **Facts (Figma help centre):** development plugins are listed for all plans in the desktop app. A Starter seat runs plugins in Figma Design but not in Dev Mode. Starter allows 3 design files per team, unlimited drafts and no team libraries. SVG import is on all plans and makes vector layers. Sources are in `figma/catcc-kit/README.md` (*Plans*). The README also lists the likely blockers (Dev Mode, browser, the 3-file limit, publishing a library) and says to try the plugin in a draft. Either way, a route that needs no plugin is the dependable one.
- **Built:** `figma/catcc-kit/svg/`, generated from `kit.json` by a pure conversion (`scripts/catcc/figma-kit/svg.ts`; `node scripts/catcc/figma-kit-svg.ts`, also run by `figma-kit.gen.ts`). `CATCC Kit.svg` is the whole kit by section (plus colour swatches and type samples). `parts/` has one SVG per specimen (227). Layers are `<g id>` named for the components (`Control/Tool button`), and variants are named `prop=value`, so Figma's *Combine as variants* reads them as properties. Text is `<text>`/`<tspan>` in the form Figma's own SVG export uses (font family, weight, px size, letter spacing in em, fill + fill-opacity), on the baseline the browser measured. Wrapped text keeps the browser's line breaks, and truncated text is cut as the browser shows it.
- **kit.json gained** per text run `by` (baseline below y), `gx` (first glyph right of x), `lines` (wrapped text) and `vis` (ellipsis-cut text), plus `clip` on inline SVGs the browser clips. The plugin ignores them.
- **Not carried by SVG:** auto layout, variant properties (made in Figma in two clicks), Variables / text styles, shadows. Checked: valid SVG, and rendered in Chromium it matches the kit page. Not checked: Figma's import itself.

## Profiles can no longer claim another pilot (2026-11-22)

- Migration `pilot_id_lock` (applied 2026-11-22; private repo `supabase/migrations/user-profiles/pilot_id_lock.sql`, rollback beside it): a BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE trigger on `user_profiles` pins `discord_id` to the caller's Discord identity and allows `pilot_id` only as NULL or the pilot whose `pilots.discord_id` is that identity, for writes by app users (service role and SECURITY DEFINER functions pass). A trigger on `pilots` relinks profiles when a roster manager changes a pilot's `discord_id`. The sign-in code no longer links by Discord username ("### Callsign"). The note above under room access ("`pilot_id` can be pointed at anyone's pilot") no longer holds; the room still reads the Discord identity, which stays correct.
- Still open: `user_permission_cache` is writable by its own user, so a user can grant themselves permissions. Fixing it means computing the cache server-side.

## Approach / Final: 30 s conflict look-ahead; replay groundspeed spikes (2026-11-22)

- **The user:** "On the main radar we're using 2 minutes as the threshold for conflict alerts. Is that the case for the Approach/Final view as well? If so it needs to be reduced to 30s." It was: the Final display coloured tags and bubbles from the picture's conflicts, at the radar's look-ahead.
- **Now:** `buildPicture` also returns `finalConflicts`, the same `findConflicts` at `FINAL_CONFLICT_LOOKAHEAD_S` = 30 s (`scopeModel.ts`). The Final display's red tags and red bubbles (`sepViolated`) both use it. The radar seats keep `conflicts` at the Display settings look-ahead (60 to 240 s, default 120). That option doesn't change the Final display's 30 s. Everything else in `predictConflict` applies unchanged to both sets: the standards, the 6 s hold past the closest point (`CONFLICT_HOLD_S`) and the cut-off at touchdown for aircraft established on final.
- **Why the screenshots were red (120 s):** 203 (demo inbound, a constant 275 kt) running down HAWK 1-2 (192 kt) from 2.8 to 5 NM behind is a real overtake: the loss is 80 to 110 s out. A bolter downwind 211 passing HAWK 1-2 head-on about 1 NM abeam also counts. HAWK is outside the 10 NM pattern area now, so the pair takes 2 NM / 800 ft, and the window ends before they pass, about 1.4 NM apart. Neither pair is within 30 s at those moments. On Final the overtake turns red about 30 s before HAWK and 203 close to the 2 NM in-trail standard, and the bolter pass doesn't turn red.
- **Groundspeeds like 8,155 / 9,841 / 16,336 kt (Replay room):** the dev `--demo-pattern` traffic (205, 211, 203) looped every 200 s by moving the same object back to its start, 7 to 14 NM in one step. The Tacview track store (`TrackStore`, groundspeed from a 4 s position difference) and the realistic sweep's tracker (`trackerVelocity`, at least 3 s between paints) both differenced across that jump for a few seconds. The display then showed 6,000 to 16,000 kt, the vectors and predictions used it, and everything near those aircraft went red.
  - *Root cause fixed:* each demo loop flies new objects (`d205.<loop>`). The previous ones are removed, and the aircraft on final is removed at the ramp instead of flying through the ship.
  - *Guards for any source* (`implausibleMove` / `MAX_PLAUSIBLE_GS_KT` = 1,500 kt in `core/geo.ts`; intervals under 1 s count as 1 s): `TrackStore` resyncs a track that jumps further than that or whose time runs backwards. Its history restarts at the new sample, with no groundspeed, course or vertical speed until a new window builds (under a second). The sweep painter and the client trail history restart a jumped track's paints and trail. `trackerVelocity` refuses a difference above the cap. `HookTrackStore` drops a DCS groundspeed above it, and `findConflicts` never predicts from one.

## Permissions computed by the database (2026-11-22)

- The user: "I do want the permissions re-work done so it can't be 'hacked' by the user." Closes the open item above: a signed-in user could write any `permissions` JSON into their own `user_permission_cache` row, and the RLS helpers and the frontend trust that row.
- **Chosen:** the database computes the cache. `compute_user_permissions(auth uid)` (SECURITY DEFINER, not callable by clients) is a port of the old `permissionCalculator` computation; which database permission fills which cache key, and how (`boolean`, `scoped`, `training_scope`), is now data: new columns `app_permissions.cache_key` and `cache_kind`. A BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE trigger on `user_permission_cache` replaces whatever a writer sends with the computed permissions, `bases_hash`, `calculated_at` and a one-hour `expires_at`. Clients call `refresh_my_permissions()` (own row only) and read their row; the frontend no longer calculates or writes permissions (`permissionCache.ts`, `permissionDefaults.ts`; `permissionCalculator.ts` keeps only the checks).
- **Fresh:** rows are recomputed in place, not deleted, when a rule, `app_permissions`, a squadron's wing, a profile's pilot, or the pilot's standings, qualifications, billets, assignments or teams change. Before, the rule-change trigger ran as the editing admin, so RLS let it clear only the admin's own row, and team changes had no trigger.
- **Rejected:** computing in SDOBot (sign-in would depend on the bot being up, and the calculator would need a bundled copy kept in step with the frontend); a check trigger that only rejects forged JSON (it would still need the full computation to know what is forged).
- **Differences from the old calculator, by design:** ended squadron assignments no longer count as a basis (no rules use that basis today); `canBulkEditRoster` gets the own-wing-includes-own-squadron entry like every other scoped permission (grants nothing new). Unchanged: `delegate_debriefs` stays empty because the database permission is named `delegate_debrief`, and nine database permissions with no cache key (`manage_teams`, `assign_team_members`, `set_flight_status`, ...) are still ignored.
- **Ship order:** step 1 migration (`permission_cache_server`) first; the old frontend keeps working because its upserts are recomputed and the server hash matches the old client hash. Then the frontend. Step 2 (`permission_cache_lock`) after old tabs have reloaded: drops the client INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies and grants.
- Still open, outside this change: `pilot_teams` accepts inserts from any signed-in user and updates/deletes from anyone, so a user can join a team and get its permissions; a separate migration (`pilot_teams_write_lock`) gives it the `pilot_roles` write rule and waits on the user's decision.

## AZ/EL: one scale on both axes (2026-11-22)

- The user asked for the same scale on both axes and chose to keep the scope square: azimuth is now ±3° (was ±6°), the same as elevation (±3° about the glideslope). Ticks every 0.5°, whole degrees labelled, on both axes. An aircraft more than 3° off course is outside the field.
- The track table now sits inset in the plot's top-right corner (inside the frame, just below the HI label, sized to its rows) instead of the pane's corner (the user).

## Projection vectors: a Vector button on every radar display (2026-11-22)

- The user asked for a toolbar button on all radar displays that cycles the projection (velocity) vector time 1 → 2 → 5 → 10 → 30 → 1 s. The radar scope (every seat that shows it: CATCC, CDC, Bridge) defaults to 30 s; the Approach / Final displays (Elevation and Azimuth) to 2 s. The AZ/EL display draws no vectors, so it has no button.
- The button sits in each display's centred toolbar: on the radar after the zoom group (Zoom out · RNG · Zoom in | Vector |), on Approach / Final after the 6 / 12 / 24 NM range buttons. It is styled as an unlit range button (h 32, IBM Plex Mono 11.5) and shows the time, e.g. "30s"; label and title "Vector". It is not the Vector / heading line tool (IcVec), which keeps its icon.
- The vector is now exactly the chosen time of travel at groundspeed along the course, with no length cap: handoff §5.2's 2.5 NM cap is dropped, since a capped vector would no longer mean the seconds the button shows (at 30 s it only bit above 300 kt). Still no vector under 20 kt. The Elevation display's vector projects the climb or descent over the same time.
- The setting is kept per display type, not per seat: one value for the radar scope and one for Approach / Final, saved in this browser (`catcc.vec.radar`, `catcc.vec.final`), matching the user's defaults, which are given per display type.
- Tag placement copes with any length: the radar's layout keeps tags off the vector's direction, and Approach / Final keeps tags off the drawn vector itself.
- The Approach / Final displays (Elevation, Azimuth, AZ/EL) now work in the moving ship's frame (the user: a track flying the centreline perfectly appeared to drift, because the landing area moves sideways to the final course at about ship speed × sin 9°). Each trail dot is placed about where the ship was at that dot's time, on its final bearing of that time: the pictures' tracks now carry the time of each trail dot and of their latest update (`trailT`, `pT`); the ship's position then is interpolated between its own radar updates (its position and trail dots), or dead-reckoned along its course and speed past either end (the radar's own ship with the sweep on has no history: dead-reckoned only). A turning ship's final bearing then follows the turn rate its last trail chord shows against its present course. An aircraft's present position is also taken about the ship as it was at that aircraft's update.
- Projection vectors on the Final displays use the aircraft's velocity relative to the ship (ground velocity less the ship's) and its climb or descent, so a track on the moving centreline has no lateral vector. On the Elevation display the horizontal part of the vector is now the closure on the ship (shorter than over the ground); on the Azimuth display a centreline track's vector lies on the centreline instead of angling off it.
- The AZ/EL display draws a projection vector for its contact (the radar's vector style), from its deviation now to its deviation after the Approach / Final Vector time: on centreline and glidepath it has no length (straight into the scope); right of course it points left (the display is mirrored).
- The main radar scope stays earth-stabilized (ground-track trails and vectors, unchanged): it is a picture of the airspace over the ground, where the ship moves like any other contact.
- The scale-end labels read RIGHT / LEFT / HIGH / LOW (were R / L / HI / LO; the user). RIGHT is still at the left end: each names the side of course an aircraft drawn there is on.

Straight lines, not arcs: range is measured from the origin, so a mark is the same at every azimuth. From `profileAlt`, so they follow the profile. ## Radar scope: Follow (2026-11-22)

- A Follow button on the primary radar scope's toolbar (Scope.tsx, so every seat's Radar view: CDC, CATCC, Bridge and the rest; not Approach / Final), just left of Centre on carrier (the user). Its icon is not in the mockup set: `IcFollow` in icons.tsx, drawn in that set's style (a hit square in the corners of a tracking gate). On, it shows like the other on tool buttons (rgba(241,88,34,.18), #FFB08F).
- While on, the view centre is the hooked contact's position every update, at the current range (`followStep` in radar/follow.ts). The carrier lock is off meanwhile.
- With nothing hooked the button is disabled (#5E6A78, as the altitude filter's reset); it can always be turned off.
- Hooking another contact while following follows the new one.
- Follow stops, leaving the view where it last was, when: the hook is dropped (Escape, a click on empty map, clicking the hooked contact again), the hooked contact leaves the picture, the scope is panned (dragged), the Follow button is pressed again, the map is double-clicked off any contact, a range preset or Centre on carrier is pressed, or a go-to (a panel's go-to button) moves the view.
- Double-click (the user): on a contact it hooks the contact and turns Follow on; on empty map it stops following and centres the view on that point at the current range. Only with the Select tool and nothing being placed: the other tools keep their own clicks (a marshal / vector / intercept click commits, BRA / CPA / waypoint clicks set points), so a double-click there is still just two of their clicks (`dblClickAction`). A double-click's two clicks hook then unhook the contact first (single clicks toggle the hook as before); the double-click hooks it again.
- Zooming while following keeps following (the user): the toolbar zoom buttons and the mouse wheel (a trackpad pinch arrives as the wheel) zoom about the followed contact, not the cursor, and are not a pan (`zoomCentre`). The scope has no keyboard zoom.
- The range rings, compass rose, sweep and off-scope pointers stay on the carrier (the radar's position), as they already did for a panned view: while following, the user sees the rings and sweep origin off centre, or off the scope, with the followed contact in the middle. Data tags, the BRA ruler and the off-scope pointers already work from the view centre, so nothing else changes.
- Double-click follows / centres under every tool, not only Select (the user: it did nothing with the marshal tool selected). Its two clicks still do what the tool does with a click (marshal, vector and intercept clicks on a contact hook it, as Select does; BRA / CPA / waypoint clicks set points). Not while a radial or coordinate is being placed.

- A seat on another carrier is labelled with its hull ("CVN-72 APP") on the owner chip, the pending lines and the handoff notification (`seatLabel`).
- Strips and handoff notifications follow the room's owner over geography (`onCarrier`): a flight offered to another carrier shows on both carriers' lists until accepted, then only on the receiving carrier's; a flight with no room owner stays with the nearest carrier, as before. The room already accepted a handoff to any carrier's seat; room.test.ts now covers one.

## Display size measured before the first paint

- Switching views or seats flashed the radar's labels oversized for one frame in Firefox (the user). The scope sizes its SVG text from its own width (`z` from `box.w`), and that width started as a guessed 900 × 675 until the ResizeObserver's first report, which Firefox can deliver after the first paint. The scope, the Approach / Final displays (their tags and the AZ/EL's size) and the slot timeline now read their size in a layout effect before the paint (`useBoxSize`), then follow it with the ResizeObserver as before. A hidden (zero-size) display keeps its last size.

## Taxi preview: off-network aircraft join the nearest road (2026-11-22)

- An aircraft on no spot or junction joins a road no more than 6 m further off than the nearest one (`NEAR_SLACK`), instead of any road within 60 m: the old rule let a loose jet beside EL 2 run one straight leg across the landing area onto the cat 4 track (the user). Farther roads are tried only when the near ones lead nowhere. Same rule in the hook's port (`crewtest_hook.lua` Tow.Net.seeds), not for tractor tows.
- A route that ends on a catapult shows the parked outline on the shuttle lined up with the track, not along the last leg.

## PriFly in the handoff chain (2026-11-22)

- The user: departures go PriFly (Air Boss) → Departure (CATCC when condensed) → Strike, and arrivals end LSO → PriFly. Both PriFly steps are automatic: a flight on deck is the Boss's (`autoRole` deck), whoever had it airborne, and the room drops a handed-off owner once every aircraft of the flight is on deck. Airborne again, it is Departure's by the chain (opening inside 50 NM) with no handoff. Read "off the deck" for arrivals as touchdown.
- On-deck strips show the owner chip (BOSS). Boss and Mini Boss sit at the head of the chain, so a flight never shows as due back to them.

## Assume control (2026-11-23)

- The user: the hand-off popover offers "Assume Control" at the top, for a seat to take a flight without a handoff. The hand button now shows on every strip whose flight this seat could take (any seat the user may act for, not only the owner's), not amber; for a flight the seat does not own the popover holds Assume Control alone (only the owner hands a flight on). It cancels any pending handoff.
- The room records the seat it was taken from (`Ownership.took`) until the next handoff. That seat's holder gets a CONTROL notification on the radar with Reclaim (assume it back) and Ack, and, because Approach / Final has no notifications, the strip says TAKEN with Reclaim for two minutes.
- The owner chip of a flight this seat owns has white text (#E6EDF3) and a light grey outline (#93A1B0, the secondary text grey; others #374250), not orange: orange / red stay for warnings and amber for cautions (the user).

## PriFly flight deck: Layers panel, spot labels, catapult queue ghosts (2026-11-23)

- **Layers (the user).** The deck card's row of layer buttons is one Layers button (the radar displays' button: `.tbtn`, `IcLayers`), right of Clear paths, opening a panel under it in the Approach / Final display's single-column layers panel markup (`DeckLayersButton.tsx`, list in `deckLayers.ts`). Deck: Catapults, Elevators, Zones (was "Areas"), Helo spots, Equipment. Taxi: Taxi Network, AI Catapult Routes, AI Parking Routes. Reset (the old reset icon) is in the panel's header. Names only: the old buttons' tooltips are gone.
  - *Catapults* (new, default on) switches the four tracks with their end blocks, shuttle slots and edges, the jet blast deflectors (raised or not) and the CAT labels. The catapult foul lines stay: they are one set of lines with the landing area's. *Elevators* (new, default on) switches each elevator's edge and safety lines and its EL label. The weapons elevators stay under Equipment. The other layers keep their defaults (Zones and Equipment on).
  - Kept in this browser under the same key, `catcc.deck.layers`, now as `{ on: { layer: boolean } }`. A choice saved by the old buttons (`{ l: [layers on] }`) is read as it was, with Catapults and Elevators on. The kneeboard's deck page is unchanged (`DeckDrawing` hides the two only when told to: `hide`).
  - While an aircraft is being sent somewhere a hidden catapult is still a target (its track lights under the pointer).
- **Taxi network labels (the user).** With Taxi Network on, every destination (the 40 spots and the four catapult shuttles) carries its short id ("BS1", "E22", "C2") in the marker used while an aircraft is being sent (`DestMark` in `TaxiOverlay.tsx`, one component for both); the small unlabelled road-coloured discs are gone. While an aircraft is selected the same markers are the buttons, as before.
- **Spot names in the deck card's text (the user).** A spot's number follows a hyphen: "TAXI TO EL 2 - 2", "BOW STBD - 1" (`destName` in `catQueue.ts`). Used in the line under the deck and in the destination markers' hover title and accessible name. Spot labels and the generated data are unchanged; the kneeboard still shows the label as generated.
- **Catapult queue ghosts (the user).** Each aircraft bound for a catapult has a ghost aircraft where it stands in that catapult's queue: the deck's spread planform, outline only, dashed (1 / 0.7), in the deck's amber (`TAXI_COL.blocked`, #E7B04F) at 40% opacity; 90% while the pointer is over that aircraft. Nothing else changes on hover. Drawn over the taxi routes and under the aircraft, in live and Ouija Board modes; not a layer.
  - *Who is queued, in what order:* the aircraft whose taxi instruction ends at that catapult (`planTaxi`'s plans, so both PriFly's own instructions and aircraft of flights queued from the Launch tab), in the order the instructions are worked: the time each was given, then Launch-tab flights in queue order. It is the order the routes and holds are already worked out in; the Launch tab's queue is by flight and can differ when a flight's aircraft were sent at different times. An aircraft standing on its catapult is first whenever it was sent. An aircraft on a catapult with no instruction to it (an AI jet DCS spotted there) is not in the queue.
  - *Where:* the first on the shuttle, heading up the catapult; the rest straight behind it on the catapult's own line, 19 m apart centre to centre (a Hornet is 17.07 m), for as long as an aircraft fits (nose, middle and tail on the deck and off the island): 5 on cat 1, 10 and more on cat 2, 7 on cat 3, 3 on cat 4. A longer queue carries on back down the taxi route onto that catapult from the aft end of the main artery, each at the first place 19 m or more from every ghost before it. The deck has room for at least 7 per catapult; beyond the artery's aft end no ghost is drawn. Long queues on two catapults can put ghosts of both on the same stretch of artery.
  - *Advance:* an aircraft that launches is no longer on deck, so it has no plan and every ghost behind moves up one; the same when its instruction is cancelled or changed.
- Both PriFly seats that show the deck (Air Boss, Mini Boss) are one view, `BossView`. The LSO station has no deck drawing.

## PriFly flight deck: Layers in the frame, AI routes gone, ghost outline, occupied catapults (2026-11-23)

The user's review of the entry above; where they differ, this one holds.

- **Layers button inside the deck frame (the user).** It sits in the frame's top right corner over the sea (12 px in, as the radar's layers button sits on the scope), and its panel opens under it, over the drawing. The panel lists Catapults, Elevators, Zones, Helo spots, Equipment, without group headings.
- **Taxi Network is its own button again (the user)**, in the row left of Draw path, in the row's button style (orange when on). Kept in the same saved layers (`catcc.deck.layers`).
- **AI Catapult Routes and AI Parking Routes removed (the user: they are to be replaced for the new taxi network).** Both switches and their drawing (the routes and their numbered spots) are gone. A saved choice that still names them is read without them. The DCS deck-crew routes themselves (`NIMITZ_TAXI` in the generated `nimitzDeck.gen.ts`, `TAXI_ROUTES` and `routeAhead` in deck.ts) stay: the deck still uses them for the orange dashed "route ahead" of an aircraft taxiing with no instruction.
- **Ghost outline (the user).** One dashed line round the aircraft's perimeter, no edges between the fuselage, wing and stabilator polygons. Each polygon is stroked at twice the width (0.6 m) through one SVG mask that blacks out the inside of every ghost, so only the outer half of the perimeter strokes shows (0.3 m). No fill; hover brightening unchanged.
- **Occupied catapults (the user).** A catapult is occupied while any aircraft stands on it (`onCat`, sent there or not: an AI jet DCS spotted there counts). The occupant has no ghost and is not in the queue. The first queued aircraft's ghost then waits behind that catapult's jet blast deflector: on the catapult's line, heading up it, its nose 2 m aft of the deflector's aft edge (`behindJbd`: about 29 to 32 m aft of the shuttle). The rest line up behind it 19 m apart, then down the taxi route as before, each 19 m or more from the occupant and every ghost ahead. On a free catapult the first ghost is on the shuttle as before. When the occupant launches, the first ghost moves to the shuttle; when that aircraft taxis on, it becomes the occupant and the next ghost waits behind the deflector.
- **Kneeboard.** The pilot's clearance names spots in the same form ("TAXI EL 2 - 2", "AT CORRAL - 1"). Its line for a catapult ("Cat 2 line", "#2 FOR CAT 2") now comes from the same queue as PriFly's ghosts (occupant first, then the queue in instruction order), so the two always agree.

## Data tags: callsign in CDC, MODEX in CATCC, toolbar switch (2026-11-23)

- **Default per seat (the user).** The radar's data tags name aircraft by tactical callsign ("VOLT 3-1") at the CDC seats (Strike, AW, SUW, Mission Commander) and by MODEX at the CATCC seats (CATCC, Air Ops, Departure, Marshal, Approach, Final). The Bridge (OOD) uses MODEX like CATCC. A track without the chosen field shows the other one, then the pilot, then the track id (`tagLabel` in scopeModel.ts).
- **Toolbar switch (the user).** Every radar seat's toolbar has a Tags button after the conflict-alerts switch: a hit square with its leader and a number sign (MODEX) or a letter A (callsign) in the tag, titled "Tags: MODEX" / "Tags: callsign". It is lit (the toolbar's orange) while it overrides the seat's default. The override is kept per seat in this browser (`catcc.tag.<seat>`); switching back to the default removes it, so a later change of default reaches that seat.
- **Where it applies.** All the scope's aircraft labels: the data tags (a collapsed formation's tag too; an expanded formation's third row names the member by the other field), the hook tag (marshal and intercept, the intercept target too) and the marshal assignment tags. The Approach / Final display's data tags follow the same seat's choice (read when the view opens); its sidebar lists, slot timeline and AZ/EL labels stay MODEX, as do strips, notifications and the PriFly and LSO pages.

## PriFly flight deck: deck orders per aircraft, queue ghosts on the taxi routes (2026-11-23)

The user's review of the two entries above; where they differ, this one holds.

- **Deck orders are per aircraft (the user).** An aircraft sent to a catapult from the deck brings only itself: its flightmates, and a flightmate that lands later, get no order from it. A flight queued on a catapult from the Launch tab still sends the whole flight (every aircraft of it without an instruction of its own follows the queue, flightmates landing later included).
  - *State (`ops.launch`, per catapult, both optional, so older state reads as before: the whole flight):* `fromDeck`, the flight keys on that queue that were put there by sending one of their aircraft from the deck (only the aircraft with an instruction there go); `skip`, the aircraft (track ids) of a Launch-tab flight that no longer follow it. Validated in `cleanOpsPatch` (`fromDeck` up to 12, `skip` up to 40, track or flight keys; empty lists are dropped). The ops room must be rebuilt (`room.cjs`) and restarted before the marks are kept: until then the room strips them and every queue entry acts as a Launch-tab one.
  - *Cancel taxi* removes the aircraft's order, the implied one included: an aircraft following its flight's Launch-tab queue goes on `skip` and stays there (a later flightmate still follows the flight). Sending it from the deck again takes it off `skip`. A flight none of whose aircraft is still bound for its catapult comes off that queue; a flight queued from the deck whose aircraft is cancelled or sent to park moves to the catapult another of its aircraft is sent to, else off the queues.
  - *One aircraft of a Launch-tab flight sent to another catapult or a parking spot* goes alone; the flight stays queued where it is for the rest of it (a flight is on one catapult's queue at a time, so the Launch tab does not list that aircraft on the other catapult; its ghost and the kneeboard line do show it there). A Launch-tab flight with nobody left following it moves to the catapult its aircraft was sent to, as queued from the deck.
  - *Launch tab edits* keep each flight's marks while it stays on the same queue; a flight added there is a Launch-tab entry (the whole flight). Taking a flight off a catapult's queue drops its aircraft's instructions to that catapult only (before, any instruction to a catapult the flight was not queued on was dropped, which would have cancelled a split aircraft's order on any edit).
  - The kneeboard and the ghosts read the same orders (`taxiOrders`, then `planTaxi` and `catQueues`), so they follow the same rules.
- **Queue ghosts stand on the taxi routes, wings folded (the user: they were in a straight line behind cat 2 with the wings spread).** Each ghost stands on its own aircraft's taxi route into the catapult (its plan: the clear part and the rest), facing along the route, drawn with the folded planform like the taxi preview's outline. The first stands on the shuttle when the catapult is free; when it is occupied, at the first place back along its route where its nose (8.5 m ahead, the way it is going) is 2 m aft of the jet blast deflector, measured along the catapult. The routes join the catapult lines at a slant (cat 2's only about 9 m short of the shuttle), so this place is often beside the catapult line rather than on it. Each later ghost stands 19 m further back along its own route than the one ahead, and further back still where that is closer than 19 m to a ghost ahead or the occupant (routes joining from either side). An aircraft already nearer the catapult than its place in the queue waits where it is: its ghost is on it. This replaces the straight line down the catapult's line and the lane from the main artery (`queueSpots`, `queueLane` removed). Ghosts of queues on two catapults can still overlap where their routes share a stretch.

## Strips: AI mark and filters (2026-11-23)

- **AI mark (the user).** A strip shows a small robot icon (muted grey, titled "AI") right after its callsign when the flight is AI. A flight is AI when none of its members is a player (DCS's player flag, `ip`); a mixed flight counts as a player flight. A source that doesn't send the flag (Tacview) never marks a flight AI, so the AI filter hides nothing there. The icon (`IcRobot`) is drawn in the set's stroke style; it is not in the mockups. The mark is on every CATCC strip: the radar's strip bay and PriFly's Arrivals and Departures boards (`StripCard.tsx`).
- **Filters (the user).** Every strip list has a filter button (a funnel, `IcFilter`, in the list's header; lit orange while the list's filter isn't the default) opening a popover with three switches: "AI aircraft" (show AI flights), "Other agencies" (show flights another seat controls) and "CVW only" (show only this carrier's air wing). Defaults: AI and other agencies shown, CVW only off. Each list keeps its own setting per seat in this browser (`catcc.strips.filter.<seat>.<list>`, lists `radar`, `arrivals`, `departures`). The filters hide strips only: the radar still paints and tags every track. The counts on each list (the Strips tab, each phase header, each PriFly board header) count the strips shown; hidden strips aren't counted anywhere (no mockup shows a hidden count). PriFly's boards keep each flight's place in the whole sequence: a hidden flight still holds NEXT or its number, so the first strip shown may be numbered 2.
- **Other agencies.** A flight is another agency's when the room holds an explicit owner for it (a handoff accepted, or control assumed) and that owner isn't this screen's seat (compared under the staffing layout, so the expanded Marshal is CATCC under the condensed layout). Flights this seat has, flights nobody has taken (owner derived from the handoff chain) and a handoff offered to this seat stay shown. On the deck PriFly has every flight (as `ownerOf` already says), so deck strips are never another agency's.
- **CVW membership: the spawn carrier (the user).** A flight belongs to the carrier it departed from. The room notes each aircraft (by track id) the first time it appears in the picture: on deck (DCS's in-air flag false; without the flag, below 200 ft) within 0.5 NM of a carrier, it belongs to that carrier (the nearest, if two are that close); anywhere else (an air start, a land field, or already flying when the room started or the source connected) it belongs to none and "CVW only" hides it. The answer is fixed: a later recovery, on this carrier or another, adds or changes nothing. A frame without any carrier decides nothing (a feed still filling in). A flight's carrier is its lead's, else the first member's that has one (`flightSpawn`). The room keeps the memory while the source runs, also for aircraft that leave the picture for a while, saves it with the mission's state so a room restart keeps it (a restored carrier wins over "none" noted for a jet already flying when the room came back), and clears it on a new mission or when the mission stops. It reaches clients as a new `spawns` message (track id -> hull; aircraft with no carrier aren't listed), sent on subscribe and whenever an aircraft is given a carrier (`core/room/spawns.ts`). No DCS change.
