## PriFly Launch tab: a queued flight is spread over the catapults (2026-11-23)

The user's review of the entry above ("a flight would not be assigned to a single catapult; each flight member would be sent to a unique catapult so that they can launch at shorter intervals"). Where they differ, this one holds; the per-aircraft deck orders stand.

- **Spread.** Queuing a flight on catapult k from the Launch tab assigns its aircraft without an instruction, lead first, one per catapult: the lead to k, the next to k's pair (1 and 2 on the bow, 3 and 4 in the waist) if the pair is usable, the rest to the other usable catapults, shortest queue first (ties: lower number). A catapult is usable unless it is DOWN or SUSPENDED (HOLD counts as usable; the waist catapults start DOWN). Catapult k takes the lead even if it is not usable, because the user picked it. A catapult takes a second aircraft of the flight only when the flight has more aircraft than there are usable catapults (the assignment wraps round in the same order). Lead order: the element number at the end of the callsign ("Volt 3-2" is 2, the same rule as the radar's `leadOf`), then deck order.
- **State.** A flight can now be on several queues at once, one entry each. Each catapult's `ops.launch` entry carries `jets`: for each flight on its queue, the aircraft the Launch tab assigned there. A flight on a queue only because one of its aircraft was sent there from the deck has an empty list: its instruction sends it. A flight stays on a queue while one of its aircraft is bound there (assigned and without an instruction of its own, or sent there); an aircraft sent from the deck to a catapult its flight is not on puts the flight at the back of that queue. Validated in `cleanOpsPatch` (up to 12 flights, 12 aircraft each).
- **Older state** (an entry with no list; `fromDeck` and `skip` from the entry above) is read as it was meant: the whole flight, flightmates landing later included, less `skip`, none for `fromDeck`. It stays that way until the queues next change, when it is written out as lists (no `fromDeck`/`skip` written any more; still accepted).
- **Cancel / deck orders.** An aircraft given an instruction from the deck loses its Launch-tab assignment. Cancelled, it is left with no instruction, and the flight's other aircraft keep theirs.
- **Removing the flight from one catapult** (the x on that catapult's entry) removes only the assignments and taxi instructions of its aircraft for that catapult. Those aircraft are left unassigned, not moved to another catapult (the coordinator's preference). The flight stays on its other queues.
- **Flightmates landing later** are not assigned automatically (the spread is fixed when the flight is queued). The flight then appears in the "+ queue" list again: the list shows every flight with an aircraft that has no instruction, including one already on that catapult. Queuing it spreads only those aircraft.
- **Display.** Each catapult's entry, and the launch sequence, show the flight by the aircraft of it bound for that catapult ("401 F/A-18C"; several when a catapult took two). The departures board lists each catapult the flight is on ("CAT 1 · #1 / CAT 2 · #1"). An aircraft's deck label shows the catapult that aircraft is bound for, not its flight's. The kneeboard and the queue ghosts follow each aircraft's own order, as before.

## PriFly Launch tab: sections on catapults that can be fired together (2026-11-23)

The user's review of the spread above: "the pairing needs to consider which catapults can launch simultaneously. Dash 2 needs to be able to rejoin quickly with Lead, Dash 4 with Dash 3 ... Cats 1 and 2 can't launch simultaneously. Neither can Cats 3 and 4 ... Only catapults with diverging headings can be fired together, all others must be staggered." This replaces the "pair = 1/2 and 3/4" rule; the rest of the entry above stands.

- **Which catapults can be fired together (from the deck geometry, `CATS` in nimitzDeck.gen.ts).** The launch headings, along each catapult track (degrees to port of the bow), are: cat 1 6.20, cat 2 2.40, cat 3 5.54, cat 4 0.32. Each track is carried on straight beyond the bow. The distance ahead of the forward end of the pair at which their paths cross (`pathsCross`) is:
  - 2+3: never (they diverge).
  - 1+3: 3.87 km (they converge by 0.66 deg).
  - 2+4: 686 m.
  - 1+4: 388 m.
  - 1+2: 228 m.
  - 3+4: 33 m.
- **Rule.** A pair whose paths do not cross within 1 NM (1852 m) of the bow can be fired together (`SIMULTANEOUS_CLEAR_M`). This gives 1+3 and 2+3, which agree with the user's ground truth, and excludes 1+2 and 3+4, as the user said.
- **Cat 4 has no simultaneous partner.** Its path converges on every other catapult's within a mile (on cat 2's at 0.69 km, on cat 1's at 0.39 km), so a section led from cat 4 is always staggered. If the user wants cat 4 paired with a bow catapult anyway, 2 is the least converging; it is one change to the table and its test.
- **Partners table.** Each catapult's partners, best first, are ranked by how far ahead the paths cross (`CAT_PARTNERS`): 1: 3, 4, 2. 2: 3, 4, 1. 3: 2, 1, 4. 4: 2, 1, 3. The simultaneous partners (`SIMULTANEOUS`) are: 1: 3. 2: 3. 3: 2, 1. 4: none. The table is written into launch.ts, and a unit test derives it from `CATS` so it cannot drift from the deck.
- **Assignment (`flightCats`).** A flight queued from the Launch tab is worked in sections: (lead, -2), (-3, -4), (-5, -6), and so on, in element-number order.
  - **Section 1.** The lead goes to the catapult the user picked. -2 goes to the best usable catapult that can be fired together with the lead's. With none usable, -2 goes to the best usable one fired after it (staggered, by the same ranking), else behind the lead on the same catapult.
  - **Later sections** take the best usable simultaneous pair: fewest catapults already used by the flight, then the shortest queues (aircraft already queued plus the flight's own), then the leader on the less used catapult. Once every simultaneous pair is used, the sections' pairs are repeated in the same order, so the sections launch as pairs one after the other. With no simultaneous pair usable, the section leader takes the least used, shortest-queue catapult and its wingman is chosen as for -2.
  - **An odd last aircraft** goes to the usable catapult with the shortest queue (the one that frees soonest).
  - **Usable** is not DOWN or SUSPENDED, as before (HOLD counts; the waist cats start DOWN). With nothing usable, the whole flight goes to the picked catapult.
- **Examples:**
  - All open: 2-ship on 1 is 1/3; on 3 is 3/2; on 4 is 4/2 (staggered). 3-ship on 1 is 1/3/2. 4-ship on 1 is 1/3, 2/3. 5-ship adds 4. 6-ship is 1/3, 2/3, 1/3.
  - Bow only (the default, waist down): 1/2, 1/2, 1, all staggered.
  - Waist only: 3/4, 3/4, 3.
  - Cat 3 closed: 1/4, 2/4, 1.
  - One catapult: everyone behind the lead.
- **Staggered fallback ranking.** For a staggered wingman the ranking by crossing distance prefers cat 4 over the other bow catapult for a cat 1 or cat 2 lead when cat 3 is closed and cat 4 is open (1/4 rather than 1/2). The user's example, "only the bow cats open: Lead Cat 1, Dash 2 Cat 2", comes out as asked, because cat 4 is closed then. If the user would rather keep a staggered wingman on the same end of the ship (1/2 with cat 3 closed), that is a change to the fallback order only.
- **Launch sequence display.** The Launch tab's launch sequence shows a flight's aircraft that stand at the same place on catapults that can be fired together as one chip: "1. 401, 402 F/A-18C · C1+3". This uses the existing chip markup. Staggered pairs stay separate chips.

## Replay playback controls (2026-11-23)

- **Controls (the user).** A replay source (`npm run catcc:replay`) shows playback controls right after the "Replay feed" indicator in the header: Rewind, Play / Pause, Fast forward, Restart, Skip ahead, then the skip interval as a button showing its value ("1 min" with a chevron) that opens a popover (the hand-off popover's shell: presets 30 s, 1 min, 2 min, 5 min, 10 min, and a Seconds field, 1 to 3600). The interval is kept per browser (`catcc.replaySkip`, default 1 min). The buttons are the header's own button style (`hdrBtn`, icon only; Rewind and Fast forward lit while they are in force); the icons are new ones in the set's stroke style (`IcRewind`, `IcPlay`, `IcPause`, `IcFastForward`, `IcRestart`: a bar and a triangle, `IcSkipAhead`: a clockwise arrow round a chevron). After them the recording's time (h:mm:ss, Tacview's time from the file start) and the rate in orange when it isn't 1x ("4×", "−2×"). The indicator reads "Replay feed · paused" while paused. A live DCS source shows none of this.
- **Rates.** Fast forward: 2x on the first press, then 4x and 8x (it stays at 8x). Rewind mirrors it: −2x first, then −4x, −8x (there is no −1x; a first press at −1x would be a one-line change in `nextRate`). Pressing the other direction starts it again at 2x. The play / pause button shows Pause only while playing at 1x; at any other rate, or paused, it shows Play, which goes back to 1x forward. Pause forgets the rate. Restart goes to the replay's start point (`--from`, else the file's first frame) playing at 1x. Skip ahead keeps the rate and pause state; a skip past the end lands on the last frame. Rewinding to the file's first frame pauses there. At the end it starts again from `--from` as before (`--once`: pauses on the last frame). `--speed` now takes 2, 4 or 8 (the fast-forward rates); the old free speed and `0` (as fast as possible) are gone from the room's replay (`cli.ts --replay` keeps them).
- **Consistent picture when seeking or rewinding.** The recording is parsed once and indexed with keyframes: a TrackStore snapshot every 30 s of recording (no position history, so small: the 2.2-hour Fall Cruise recording, 1.3 M events, indexes in about 2.5 s into 267 keyframes). A seek restores the last keyframe at least 10 s before the target and plays the events from there to it, so the picture is exactly what playing forward shows: groundspeed, course and vertical speed come from forward-ordered samples over the TrackStore's 4 s window, never from differencing a jump or time going backwards. A short forward move (up to 40 s) just plays the events in between. Rewinding is a seek on every tick (twice a second; a few ms each). The demo pattern (`--demo-pattern`) follows seeks: its traffic is a function of the recording's time, and after a keyframe is restored it places its aircraft afresh. Bullseyes now come from the store's object table, so ones placed before a keyframe stay.
- **Trails while rewinding.** The scope's trails (the client's track history in useCatccRoom and the sweep's paints) drop the points later than the frame's time when time runs backwards, instead of clearing only after a 5 s step back: a rewind keeps the trail up to the new time.
- **Who may steer (the user's brief).** Any member let into the room may use the controls; no seat is needed. It is one shared picture, so every viewer's controls, rate and time agree: the room passes the command to the replay (`{ t:'replay', src, cmd, s? }`, checked by `cleanReplayCmd`), and the replay's new clock goes to every subscriber (`{ t:'replayState', src, playing, rate, mt, start, end }`, also sent on subscribe). The replay announces itself in the hook protocol's hello (`replay: true`, shown as the source status's `replay`) and takes the order as `CMD {"op":"replay"}`; the room refuses the command for any other source ("this source is not a replay"), so a live DCS server can't be steered by it.

## CATCC follow-ups: AZ/EL locks, tags, strips, PriFly deck tools (2026-11-23)

- Each ACLS lock-on on the AZ/EL scope is bracketed by a horizontal and a vertical line out to the plot's edges, clear of its hit by 11 px: the primary in its hit's colour (green, red in conflict), the other lock in the faded green of its hit. The locked aircraft in the top-right table are boxed: the primary in the hit green, the other in a faded green (the user).
- A third tag state (the user): "both" puts the tactical callsign on a line of its own above the MODEX line, the rest of the tag unchanged (a track without both fields gets no extra line; an expanded formation's member line then just gives its place, "2/4"). The Tags button steps MODEX → callsign → both; one-line labels (hook, marshal tags) go by MODEX under both. Its icon is a tag chip reading "123", "ABC", or "ABC" over "123".
- Replays mark AI aircraft too (the user: JEFF GROUP 1 showed as players): an aircraft whose Tacview Pilot is just its DCS unit name (the callsign alone, the callsign plus a unit number, or the group's name: `isUnitName`) has no player; before, what parsePilot left over ("2" of "Jeff Group 1-1-2", all of "Harrier Group 1") read as a player's name.
- "Other agencies" off hides every flight whose owner chip names another seat, handed off or by the automatic chain (the user: deck strips showing BOSS stayed). Only flights this seat owns, and a handoff offered to it, stay.
- Queue ghosts never stand on a jet blast deflector, any catapult's (the user: the second jet for a free Cat 2 was drawn on its JBD). A waiting aircraft's folded footprint (17 m by 4.2 m) is checked against every JBD; where it would touch one it moves further back along its route until clear (`onJbd`).
- The Launch tab's "+ queue" lists flights by tactical callsign ("HAWK 2 · 2× F18C") and, below, every aircraft without an instruction by MODEX (the user). A flight gets the group instructions (spread over the catapults); a single aircraft gets an individual taxi instruction to that catapult, as from the deck, even a flight or section lead.
- AZ/EL: the lock-on boxes in the track table stand 3 px apart (the user).
- Strip filters: every switch off by default in every view (the user): AI aircraft and Other agencies off (those flights hidden), CVW only off. Saved settings moved to a new key (`catcc.strips.filter2.*`) so earlier choices made under the old defaults do not carry over.
- PriFly: the outline of the selected aircraft (hovering a catapult, or its instruction in hand) stands where the queue puts it (queueGhosts: behind the aircraft ahead, clear of the JBDs), as the committed ghosts do, not at the route planner's stop (the user: the hover outline overlapped a JBD or the aircraft ahead).
- PriFly: "Cancel all taxi" beside the deck's other buttons (the user) cancels every taxi instruction: PriFly's own (deck orders, single aircraft from the Launch tab) and the Launch tab's (every flight comes off every catapult queue). Catapult statuses stay. Disabled while there are none; not shown read-only.
- PriFly deck: a toolbar centred at the top inside the deck frame, as on the radar (the user), with icon buttons: Taxi Network (lit while the layer is on), Draw path / Clear paths grouped (Draw lit while drawing; Clear disabled with no paths), Cancel all taxi (disabled with none; not shown read-only). The text buttons above the frame are gone. The frame's top padding is 70 px to keep the deck clear of the toolbar.
- PriFly deck toolbar: "Selective cancel" grouped with "Cancel all taxi" (the user). While it is on (lit), clicking an aircraft cancels its taxi order (one at a time, as Cancel on the deck card); aircraft without one show a not-allowed pointer; nothing gets selected or dragged. Esc, the button again, or Draw path turns it off. Icons: Cancel all is three routes under one no-entry sign, Selective is a pointer with a small no-entry sign.
- PriFly: the deck's name moved inside the frame, top-left, as "Flight deck" alone (the user); the "live from DCS" / "Ouija Board (manual chits)" part is gone (the Live positions / Ouija Board switch above already says which).
- PriFly: only catapults take a queue (the user). While an aircraft is being sent somewhere, a parking spot another aircraft is already sent to is drawn amber and is not a destination: no hover preview, a click does nothing (not-allowed pointer). An aircraft's own spot stays pickable.

- **Deck crew in multiplayer: statics become client aircraft (the user, 2026-11-18; recorded, not started).** His words: "When we want to make this work for multiplayer, we'll need to replace those statics with client aircraft once they are spotted. I'm thinking we'll need a new Coalition and Slot UI when the player joins the server that shows them the deck spotting plan and status of their aircraft. Once the aircraft is spotted they can spawn into it and the static gets replaced with a client aircraft in that same location with the correct livery and MODEX already set. We don't have to fix this now but it needs to be recorded for future." What is known that bears on it: the aircraft, tractors and bars the deck crew moves are statics moved by our native module on the machine it runs on: nothing we do now sends those movements to other players; DCS's own syncing of statics linked to a ship has a history of faults in forum reports (not tested by us); our hook reaches the mission script through `net.dostring_in`, which ED's API notes call obsolete since 2.9.18. Open: how a slot is tied to a spot (dynamic spawn / a client aircraft placed at the static's place and heading), who owns the spotting plan (the server), how the plan and each aircraft's state are shown in a slot screen of our own, livery and board number on the client aircraft.
- **Planning off DCS's main thread, and shared between machines (the user, 2026-11-18; researched, nothing built).** See the round-4 entry at the end of this file for what was found. Open: move the planners (way-finding, tow plans) into a second Lua state on a worker thread of catcc_crew.dll, results handed back a frame later; plans sent as data (a way and a start time) so that every machine with the mod plays the same movement.
- **Hangar configurations with other aircraft types (the user, 2026-11-18; recorded, not started).** His words: "At a later time, we may add hangar deck configurations that include other aircraft types (e.g. F-14 Tomcats, S-3B Vikings, E-2C Hawkeyes, A-6E Intruders, and helicopters (M60) but that's a problem for later." What it will touch: the hangar's solver and planners take one aircraft shape, one wheelbase and one tow bar geometry (the F/A-18C's) for everything. Also from him, the same day, on refilling: "When the hangar is refilled, spots will be filled in reverse order as unpacking so that no new conflicts are created" (the solved tows driven backwards, last out first in; not built).
- **An in-game panel (2026-11-16, asked for by the user: "using the radio menu is getting cumbersome"; built and installed, NOT YET SEEN IN DCS; 175 catcc-dcs tests).** His flow: F11 view, open the interface, pick the deck spotting tool, put the cursor on an aircraft, click to select it, nudge and turn it with controls, a key to save.
  - *Whether it can be done (checked on this PC, nothing assumed):* yes. The crew test hook already runs in DCS's GUI Lua state, and that state has DCS's own window toolkit (`dxgui`: `DialogLoader.spawnDialogFromFile`, `Button.new`, `Static.new`, a window's `addHotKeyCallback`): the Scratchpad mod installed here builds its window with exactly those calls. For the click: `dxgui.AddMouseCallback('down', fn(x, y, button))` (DCS's own multiplayer dialogs use it), `Export.LoGetCameraPosition()` (the camera's place and axes), `DCS.getCurrentFOV()` (DCS's options page reads it), `dxgui.GetScreenSize()`. No export function gives the field of view to the native module; none is needed.
  - *What was built:* `Scripts/CATCC/crewtest_ui.lua` (+ `crewtest_ui.dlg`, the window; `crewtest_ui_mark.dlg`, a 16-pixel yellow mark), loaded by the hook (`CATCC_UI`), its window built at the first frame of a mission. **Ctrl+Shift+F10** shows / hides it (hidden the Scratchpad way: a hidden skin, since an invisible window loses its hot keys). Two pages. *Deck spots:* park / take away / wings; **a click anywhere in the world locks on the aircraft nearest the line of sight through the cursor** (within 12 m of it), and the yellow mark follows the locked one on screen; move step 1 m / 10 cm / 1 cm with Forward / Aft / Left / Right (the aircraft's own axes), turn step 5 / 1 / 0.1 degrees with Turn left / right; previous / next; add where I look, remove, back to built-in, start over; Save (also **Ctrl+Shift+S**; every change is saved anyway). *Crew test menu:* the F10 menu's whole tree as buttons (asked from the mission script: `CATCC_CR.tree()`), a submenu opening in place with "< Back", the panel staying put after a click. The panel's last line shows the hook's latest message, also when on-screen messages are hidden. Every button gives the same command string the F10 item does (`run`): nothing is done twice.
  - *Not known until seen:* (1) whether `DCS.getCurrentFOV()` is the horizontal or the vertical field of view: taken as horizontal; the panel's "FOV: H / V" button switches it, and the yellow mark tells (it sits on the locked aircraft when right). (2) The mouse in an outside view: it turns the camera unless DCS's cursor mode is on (LAlt+C). (3) Whether Ctrl+Shift+F10 / Ctrl+Shift+S clash with a DCS binding. (4) How the skins look (buttons borrowed from DCS's "buttonSkinAwacs", white text on a dark window).
  - *If the panel fails:* everything in it runs guarded; a failure to build or an error while running switches the panel off, says so in the log ("panel: ..."), and the F10 menu goes on as before.
- **The deck spotting tool works on the carrier the CAMERA is at (2026-11-16, the user: "based on camera position, not aircraft position... if I slot into an aircraft 100 km away, but have the camera overhead the carrier I want to spot it should still work"; 175 catcc-dcs tests).** Before, every command needed the player's jet on a carrier (within 250 m) and took that one. Now, for the deck tool and the panel's click and mark only: the Supercarrier-class ship (CVN_71, CVN_72, CVN_73, CVN_75, Stennis) nearest the camera within 5 km (`CATCC_CR.ctx(px, pz)` with the camera's place, `Tow.Deck.ctx`); chosen when the aircraft are parked and then kept BY NAME until they are taken away, so a camera wandering to another carrier does not carry the work over. The statics are linked to that ship (`CATCC_CR.deck(..., shipName)`). The player's jet need not be on it; the height a line of sight is measured against is a parked jet's (21.975 m above the ship's origin: `Tow.Deck.jy`), no longer the player's own. No camera to read: the jet's carrier, as before. Everything else (marshalling, the tow, the crews) still goes by the carrier the jet stands on. All five hulls load the same routes file and crew file (`USS_CVN_7x.lua`), so the spots and our people are the same on each; each hull has its own model, so its model arguments (the hangar doors) are only known from CVN-72.
- **The panel's first run (2026-11-16 21:06 to 21:25, the user; log read afterwards) and two faults put right (175 catcc-dcs tests).**
  - *It worked:* built and shown (Ctrl+Shift+F10), "Park Hornets" parked 40 aircraft, the buttons gave their commands once each. **A click locks on the right aircraft:** four picks in a row along the starboard bow (spots 4, 4, 3, 2) were 1.8 to 5.0 m from the line of sight, with the field of view taken as HORIZONTAL (78.0): that reading is right. **The wings fold on static aircraft:** model argument 8 through `native.objarg` took on all 40 ("wings folded (model argument 8) on 40 aircraft").
  - *Fault 1, the user: "after starting to spot aircraft I can no longer use the mouse to look around".* The panel's window was given a cursor whenever it was shown (`setHasCursor(true)`, as the Scratchpad mod does), and while a window keeps DCS's cursor up the mouse does not turn the camera. (The log's eleven clicks "no aircraft within 12 m" in the last half minute are him trying to drag the view.) Now the panel shown leaves the mouse with the camera; **Ctrl+Shift+M** gives it to the panel (cursor: click aircraft and buttons) and back; the panel's "Look" button gives it back too; the window's title says which it is. DCS's own LAlt+C is the same switch.
  - *Fault 2: "the UI also remains visible after the mission ends".* The hook's frame stops with the mission, and nothing hid the window. Now a mission's start and its end put the panel and its mark away (`UI.reset`: hidden, then really invisible, the cursor released); the first frame of the next mission brings the hidden window back with its hot keys.
- **The deck as the user respotted it in DCS is the plan now (2026-11-16 evening; 200 tests in catcc-dcs/test and prifly; installed).** He parked a Hornet on every spot with the panel and moved all 40 ("I moved some aircraft from the bow to the stern, so you'll need to update the names of all the parking locations").
  - *The record:* the tool's file (`Saved Games\DCS\Logs\catcc-deck-spots.lua`, 22:25) became `carrier-ops-handoff-2/deck-spots-dcs.csv` (spot number, name, x forward, z to starboard, heading; `was` where the name changed). The Logs file is kept beside where it was as `catcc-deck-spots.applied-2026-11-16.lua`, so the next "Park Hornets" starts from the new built-in spots, which ARE his places. The drawing's export (`deck-spots.csv`, from Nimitz.svg) is untouched; `scripts/catcc/deck-spots.gen.ts` takes a spot from the DCS file where it has a row (all 40 now) and leaves it exactly there: the "main wheels 4 ft from the edge" rule only moves a spot the DCS file does not have. (Every one of his 40 has its main wheels more than a metre inside the deck outline.)
  - *The names* (areas keep theirs; within an area the numbers run from the bow aft): the starboard bow row has SIX: Bow stbd 1, 2, then the old "Bow port 1" as **Bow stbd 3**, the old 3 / 4 / 5 as **4 / 5 / 6**. The port bow row has four: old "Bow port 3" > **Bow port 1**, old "Crotch 1 / 2 / 3" > **Bow port 2, Crotch 1, Crotch 2**. The patio has three: Patio 1, the old "Bow port 2" (now at the stern) as **Patio 2**, the old "Patio 2" as **Patio 3**. Short ids follow (BS1..6, BP1..2, CR1..2, PA1..3). A spot keeps its NUMBER (its row in the drawing's file and its place in DCS's spawn table): spot 6 is "Bow stbd 3", spot 7 "Patio 2".
  - *The taxi network (`taxiNet.ts`) for the new places:* the six-pack's forward spot now meets the artery forward of the fan: it runs on to the cat 1 feeder (`bow1`). Port 1 stands at the cat 4 approach's aft end with its nose across the deck: besides its feeder it has a pivot onto the approach, as EL 4's spots have. The patio road bends where Patio 1's own line meets the artery (it met it 2 m short of the old bend and every route past kinked). Tests: a first leg may be 1.5 degrees off the nose (two feeders sharing a junction: Sixpack 5 and EL 2's forward spot); Port 1 to cat 3 may turn at 32 degrees a metre (a 97-degree turn onto the cat 3 line 2 m from the waist crossing's junction); 3% of spot-to-spot routes may kink (was 2%: the stern spots now point more along the patio road).
  - *Regenerated:* `deckSpots.gen.ts`, `crewtest_routes.lua` (160 routes from 40 starts), `director-spots.lua` (DCS's spawn table and 31 parking routes; the parking order's patio places are PA3, PA2, and PA1 among the rest; catapults per spawn spot 16 / 10 / 7 / 7). **DCS's own files re-patched** (`director-test.ps1 -Spawn Spots -Crew Model -CatGate`, 22:49): players spawn on the new places.
- **The panel and the deck tool after the user's long session (same evening).**
  - *Messages:* a line for every nudge, 20 s each, stacked up the screen ("a huge long stack that blocks the screen"). The deck tool's messages now REPLACE what is on screen (`outText`'s third argument) and stay 8 s (`Tow.Deck.say`).
  - *"Hide" left the panel up and dead.* The hidden-skin trick (as Scratchpad's) does not hide this window. Now the panel is really invisible when put away (`setVisible(false)`), and its hot keys live on a one-pixel see-through window of their own (`crewtest_ui_keys.dlg`) that is always there during a mission.
  - *Too narrow on the menu page:* the window is 620 wide (was 396); the menu page is one column of ten full-width buttons (an F10 level holds ten); the deck page is laid out over the full width.
  - *The axle's width* (asked for: "a tool for gauging the width of the hornet axles"): F10 > Tow > Axle finder > "Width (the end plates)": an upright cyan plate either side of the nose gear (`CATCC_AxleEnd`, Blender-made, one model: it stands on the deck), `TOW.axleLen` apart, moved wider / narrower by 10 cm, 1 cm, 1 mm (the whole width changes by the step) until each touches its nose tyre's outer face; the tow bar's heads then come 2 cm outside them. It starts at 0.58 m (the tyres' outer faces in DCS's collision model). The number it ends with is logged (`command axle len ...: axleLen=`) and the tow uses it for the session; it goes into the hook's source afterwards.
- **The tow tractor on the flight deck (2026-11-16 night, the user: "make the deck tug useable on the flight deck. For now, make it so I can request a tow to any other parking location on the deck, or to a catapult"; 205 tests in catcc-dcs/test and prifly; installed, NOT YET SEEN IN DCS).**
  - *The menu:* F10 > CATCC crew test > Tow > **Tow me to a catapult** > 1..4 (`tow|cat|N`) and **Tow me to a parking spot** > five groups, bow to stern, of at most ten spots > the spot (`tow|spot|<number>`). The groups come from the routes file (the hook hands the list to the menu script in `CATCC_CR_SPOTS`: the mission's Lua state cannot read files), so a respotted deck needs nothing done here. The panel's menu page shows the same tree. "Tow me into the hangar" is as it was (it now refuses on the flight deck, as the new two refuse on the hangar deck).
  - *The tractor's place on the flight deck* (`TOW.deckHome`): 74 m aft, 27 m to starboard: the pocket between the island's aft end and elevator 3, clear of the artery, the island, the radar mast and every spot. **What DCS itself stands there has not been looked at.** It comes from there along the taxi roads (`Tow.Net.solo`: roads where it can, a leg off them at 1.6 times its length, never through the island, round the jet it is fetching), turns round ahead of the nose and hooks up exactly as in the hangar (the same phases). After "Unhook" it drives back there (called again on its way, it turns round and comes).
  - *PriFly's taxi solver is in the hook now* (`Tow.Net`: `taxiNet.ts`'s seeds, solver and fillets in Lua, on the graph `crewtest_routes.lua` now carries: 131 junctions, their joins, the junction of every destination, PriFly's deck outline, the island and the mast). A tow starts wherever the jet stands and may end on any spot: too many routes to write out. A test holds the two together point for point (332 ways: every spot to every catapult, spot to spot, starts off the roads; one tie, a loop round the fan that can be driven either way). For a tow the solver is asked a little differently (`opts.tow`): the jet is PULLED nose first, so it leaves a junction it stands on within 125 degrees of its nose and joins a road by a leg within 100 degrees of it.
  - *To a catapult:* pulled along the taxi route and on up the track; the nose wheel is left **2 m short of the shuttle** (`TOW.catShort`: assumed, not asked), the jet within 1 degree of the track. The tractor, bar and jet are 13 m from end to end and a taxi route may join a catapult's line 8 m short of its shuttle (from elevator 2 to catapult 2 the jet would stop 7.6 degrees across the track): then it is pulled 12 m (or 20, 28) past the place and pushed straight back into line.
  - *To a spot:* jets stand tail to the deck's edge, so the jet goes in TAIL FIRST: pulled along the taxi route past the spot's front, then pushed back round into it.
  - *The push (what was tried first, and why it was dropped):* first the way OUT of the spot was worked out, recorded every 10 cm, and played backwards (a pushed trailer cannot be steered stably from the tractor's end). For the pull's end and the record's start to meet, tractor, bar and jet had to stand straight there, which takes about 25 m of straight deck after every bend: of 43 tows from elevator 2, 5 could be planned. **Now the push is worked out from the JET's end** (`Tow.Fd.back`): the middle of the main wheels is steered backwards along a way into the spot as a car is backed; where the nose wheel, the bar and the tractor must then be follows exactly from that (the bar lies the way the nose wheel moves; the tractor's own point trails its hitch). Nothing in it is unstable, it starts from the nose wheel as it is turned, and it is worked out again from where the pull actually ended. From five spots (elevator 2, bow, six-pack, patio, port) 214 of 215 tows can be planned; from elevator 2 all 43. In the test's run from elevator 2 to Bow stbd 4 the jet ends within a centimetre and a tenth of a degree of the spot.
  - *Ways tried for a spot, in turn* (`Tow.Fd.planSpot`): past the spot's corner along the taxi road itself and back round that corner (bend 3 to 6 m at the main wheels); else a line across the spot's front 18 m ahead of where the main wheels will stand (clear of the neighbours' noses and the towed jet's wing), at 90 / 60 / 120 degrees, the side the jet comes from first. The first that can be driven with the tractor itself turning no harder than 30 degrees a metre is taken (its tightest real turn, 2.8 m, is 21); else the gentlest. Port side spots, whose corner on the road is 50 m across the deck, try the lines first (else 55 m of pushing).
  - *Every tow is driven in the head before it is driven:* the same `Tow.drive` and the same trailing as the real thing. A plan that takes the tractor or a wheel of the jet off the deck or across a lowered elevator, turns the nose wheel beyond 75 degrees, folds the bar against the tractor, or does not bring the jet square onto the spot is not driven, and the menu says why (the log has every way tried).
  - *Numbers chosen, none asked:* with a jet on the bar the tractor turns no tighter than 7.5 m (the nose wheel then stays near 57 degrees at most); in the push the nose wheel is steered to 62 degrees at most and its turn changes by 0.12 (curvature a metre: 0.35 threw the tractor's tail about, at 0.06 the bend no longer fits the tighter spots); backing pace 0.7 m/s, towing 1.4.
  - *NOT looked at:* other aircraft. The tractor and the tow go where the roads go whoever stands there (only the jet in tow is driven round; a spot one of the deck tool's Hornets stands on is refused). The tractor's FRONT hitch is not used: it pushes backing up, the driver facing away. A jet that stands nose to the deck's edge, or on a catapult facing the bow, has no way out nose first: refused.
- **The panel's hot key (same night; the user: "Ctrl Shift F10 did not bring it up").** The log: every press gave "panel: shown" and "panel: hidden" in the same second (five in one second with the key held): since the keys moved to their own window, DCS calls a hot key's function more than once a press. Now a call counts only when that key has been quiet for 0.6 s, and every call starts the quiet again (a held key acts once). And a way without a key: **F10 > "CATCC panel: show / hide"** on the radio menu's own top level (`panel`), which also hands the panel the mouse.
- **The axle's width: 0.605 m** (the user, with the end plates: "I've updated the width. It was already pretty close"; 0.58 before). `TOW.axleLen`; the bar's pins now go 0.2625 m either side.
- **The tow tractor's driver is a blue shirt (the user, 2026-11-17: "blue shirts drive the deck tractors IRL"; a picture of blue shirts for the shades).** DCS has a blue livery for this figure already, so no texture of ours was made. What is known: `crew.lua` dresses the animated crews in yellow, brown, white, green and the shooter's default only (hence the earlier "no blue", which was wrong: it was read from that file alone); the mission editor's static of the same figure (type `us carrier shooter`, shape `carrier_shooter`: the animated members carry that very `unit_type`) is given the liveries **blue, purple and red** as well in ED's own missions (229 missions on this PC read: brown 360, yellow 117, green 82, purple 62, red 49, white 26, blue 22; `us carrier tech` has the same seven). `groundcrew-patch.lua`: catcc-t0 `livery = 'blue'` (green before); applied to DCS's files (`director-test.ps1 -Spawn Spots -Crew Model -CatGate`, the set that was in). The hook now logs, once a mission, the liveries DCS lists for `us carrier shooter` and `carrier_shooter` (`DCS.getObjectLiveriesNames`, as the mission editor asks). NOT YET SEEN: blue on an animated crew member; what DCS does with a livery name it does not have.
  - *If the blue is not the wanted shade:* the deck crew's textures, liveries and models are in the module's encrypted packs (`CoreMods/tech/USS_Nimitz/animations/*.edce`), which are not opened: no copy of a shirt's texture can be taken from them to recolour. A livery of ours could only be painted from nothing, and neither the model's material names nor its texture layout are known from outside the pack.
- **Blue shirt, seen in the log (2026-11-17 10:05):** DCS lists for `us carrier shooter` the liveries blue, brown, green, purple, red, white, yellow (and none under the model's name `carrier_shooter`: the livery entry is the unit type). Whether the driver showed blue: not yet said by the user.
- **The panel does not come up (the user, 2026-11-17, third report: "I'm still not able to bring up the interface anymore. I'm trying to do it in F9 view, does that matter?").** The view is not it. The log of that session: "menu: panel", "panel: shown", then the hot key "hidden" and "shown" again, 9 seconds apart: the key and the F10 item both reached the hook, every show ran without an error, and nothing was on the screen. Read back over the three builds:
  - *Build 1 (2026-11-16, the one that worked):* the window made visible once for good; put away = a see-through skin, 10 x 10, its buttons not visible. Not to be seen at start; shown by its hot key (one call a press); the whole deck was spotted with it. Its "Hide" button, which put it away inside the button's own click, left it on the screen, dead.
  - *Build 2 (the night of 2026-11-16):* put away = `window:setVisible(false)`, the hot keys on a second, one-pixel window. NEVER SEEN AGAIN in any session (every "panel: shown" since is from this build); on the one-pixel window DCS called a key's function several times a press. My diagnosis that night (the several calls) was the lesser half: with one call a press it still never appeared. The SRS mod's overlay notes of this toolkit: "if you make the window invisible, its destroyed".
  - *Build 3 (now):* build 1's way again: the window is NEVER made invisible (nor the mark: one pixel in the corner with nothing to mark), the hot keys are on the panel's own window, the key window is gone. And NOTHING is done to the window or its buttons inside a key's or a button's own call: a call only notes what is wanted (`UI.want`, `UI.wantMouse`, the page), the hook's next frame does it, the way the building did (the one context in which putting away worked). Every show / hide is logged with what the window says of itself (visible, place, size). The keys do nothing outside a mission. NOT YET SEEN in DCS.
  - *Not known:* why "Hide" failed in build 1 (inside the click is my reading; the same calls from the frame loop worked at start).
- **A parked aircraft is towed (the user, 2026-11-17: "send a tow crew to any aircraft spotted on the deck by using the UI to click on an aircraft like we would for adjusting the deck spotting, and then issuing a tow instruction through the UI"). Built, installed, 207 tests; not yet seen in DCS.**
  - *In the panel:* a third page, **Tow** (tabs: Deck spots / Tow / Crew test menu). A click in the world selects an aircraft there as on the deck page (the deck tool's lock; the yellow mark follows it); "Cat 1..4"; the deck's areas (the F10 menu's five groups) and the chosen area's spots (one that is taken says so); "Hold / go on", "Unhook now", "Report". The same from the radio menu: F10 > CATCC crew test > Tow > Tow the locked deck aircraft (the lock by "Lock on the one the camera looks at").
  - *What is towed:* one of the deck tool's static Hornets (`Tow.begin(mode, arg, 'lock')`, commands `tow|cat|N|lock`, `tow|spot|n|lock`), on the deck tool's carrier (the camera's: the player's jet need not be on it; the tractor and the bar are then made on that carrier and stay its for the mission). The player's jet is moved by ForcePosition; a static is moved as the tractor is: `native.staticpos`, in the ship's own frame if it is the ship's child (`native.staticinfo`). Its class in DCS is `lLandPlane` (the log, from the wing fold); UNTRIED: that `staticpos` takes an lLandPlane (it asks for an lLandObject; the tow is refused with DCS's answer if not). Its nose wheel steering and wheels are written on its own model (arguments 2, 101 to 103: UNTRIED on a static aircraft; a failure there no longer stops the tractor's wheels). Its own height above the ship's origin is read from DCS (the mission script: `CATCC_CR.where`), a parked jet's 21.975 failing that.
  - *Nobody is in it:* the tow's messages say "it" (`Tow.words`), and at the place the tractor unhooks by itself (3 s) and drives home.
  - *A spot and its aircraft are two things now:* a deck entry is the spot (what the file records: unchanged by a tow); `e.at` is where its aircraft stands once a tow has taken it elsewhere (`Tow.Deck.pose`). The click, the lock by camera, the mark, "is this spot taken" and the tractor's ways all go by where each aircraft stands NOW. Parked anew by the deck tool (a nudge, Park Hornets), an aircraft stands on its spot again. A spot is refused if one of the deck tool's aircraft stands on it, or the player's own jet (within 8 m), or a mission aircraft stood there when the deck was filled.
  - *The other aircraft on the deck:* the tractor's own ways (to the aircraft, and home) are first looked for clear of every other parked aircraft (their fuselage lines with 5.5 m to spare), and, where the roads do not allow that, taken regardless (logged). From its waiting place, deck full: every one of the 39 parked aircraft can be reached and taken to every catapult (156 tows planned in the head; 152 of the tractor's ways to them clear of the others, the rest regardless). THE PULL AND THE PUSH are not checked against other aircraft (as before): PriFly's taxi routes are what they follow.
  - *Found on the way, and mended: a second tow was refused.* The tractor starts from where the last tow left it, facing the way it came to a stop (at its waiting place: the way it drove in, e.g. 0.69 / 0.72, towards the deck's edge): from there it could not turn onto its way ("the tractor would leave the deck on its way"). This was so for the player's own tows too, and no test had a second tow get that far. Now it backs STRAIGHT out first, 4, 8 or 12 m, the least that works (phase `backout`; the bar goes with it); if nothing works from where it stands, it is put back on its waiting place and starts from there (said, and logged).
  - *Known and left:* from the elevator 2 area the tractor's way home past the island's inboard forward corner is not driven in the head ("would leave the deck" at 41 m aft, 22 m to starboard: the hook's deck outline has the island's face there at 22.7 m, PriFly's at 24.3 m; which is right is not known) and it then waits 8 m on from where it unhooked; the next tow starts from there. A tow is not planned from a catapult for an aircraft facing the bow (no way out nose first). `tow|trycat|N` / `tow|tryspot|n` (not on the menu) plan a tow without starting it: the tests' sweep.
- **First run of the panel's third build and of a parked aircraft's tow (2026-11-17 11:12 to 11:22; the user: "My initial test was mostly successful").** Seen in the log: the panel comes up by the F10 item and by its key and is used (build 3's way works: the window never made invisible, nothing done inside a call); the Hornet of Crotch 2 (class `lLandPlane`, the ship's child) was towed into El 1 1 (its Hornet taken away first): `native.staticpos` takes an lLandPlane, its nose wheel steering is written on its model (argument 2), its height read from DCS (22.005, not a parked jet's 21.975), it ended 0.0 m from the spot, the tractor unhooked by itself and found its way home. The blue livery is listed by DCS. The user's five points, and what was done (installed; 209 tests; not yet seen in DCS):
  - *"Accelerating time in the mission does not make the tugs move any faster."* The tow ran on real time. Now on MODEL time (`Tow.now`: stands still in a pause, runs faster under time acceleration), a frame's worth driven in steps of 0.1 s at most with the bar and the jet trailing after each (`Tow.follow`), up to 6 s a frame: the same way driven at any frame rate and any acceleration (a test: four times the model time a frame, a quarter of the frames, the same stop to 15 cm).
  - *"While connecting, the tug warped into its position and did a rapid/unrealistic turn."* The log: it stopped in front of the aircraft at 81.4 / -0.8 heading 0.56 / -0.83, where it was meant to be at 85.0 / 0.0 heading -0.63 / 0.78: 3.7 m off the nose line and FACING the aircraft; "backing onto the ring" then slid it 10 m and spun it half round in 15 s. Cause: its turn in front of the aircraft was three square corners 8 m apart, which a tractor turning on 4 m cuts, and the way was only held to the deck, not to how it ended. Now (`Tow.Fd.arrival`, `TOW.arr`): the turn is drawn as it is driven, a half circle of 5 m from a line 10 m to the side (or, failing that, a quarter circle in from the side), then 3 m straight out along the nose line; the way is taken only if, driven in the head, the tractor ends within 0.3 m of the nose line and 6 degrees of it. It stops 11 m beyond the hook-up place (8 before) and backs straight onto the ring.
  - *"While pushing the aircraft into its spot on El 1, the tug pushed the nose of the towed aircraft through the nose of an aircraft parked on the six pack."* The pull and the push were not held against other aircraft at all (said so before the run). Now every plan is (`Tow.Fd.shape`, `check`, `limits`): an F/A-18C as four rounded bars (nose, body, wing folded or spread as it stands, tailplane), the towed one against every other on the deck, 0.1 m to spare (less where it starts or ends nearer a neighbour than that); the tractor against the others' wheels (it goes under a nose or a wing). The user's own case, planned again in a test: the way that was driven (pushed in along the road) is dropped ("the aircraft in tow would run into the Hornet of Sixpack 1"), the next (a line across the spot's front) is clear and taken.
  - *WHAT THIS BROUGHT OUT: THE TAXI ROADS RUN THROUGH PARKED AIRCRAFT.* With every spot filled, of the 152 tows from a parked aircraft to a catapult only 32 are clear; 120 go into another aircraft, up to 3.1 m: the six-pack's aft noses reach 12.2 m to starboard (Sixpack 6: 3.66 + 8.6) where the road runs at about 11 m (the lane between them and El 2's noses, 23.5 m, is 11.3 m wide and its middle is at 17.9 m); the run onto catapult 4's track goes over the spot Port 1. The roads are PriFly's (taxiNet.ts), drawn for the hand-off's spotting plan and NOT moved when the user re-spotted the deck in DCS. So a plan clear of the others is PREFERRED, not demanded: where none is found the tow is planned once more with a contact allowed (`Tow.Fd.soft`), takes the way that goes least far into another aircraft, and SAYS whom it goes through ("NOT CLEAR: the aircraft in tow goes 2.9 m into the Hornet of Port 1 ..."). To be decided by the user: move the roads to the re-spotted deck (PriFly's network: the web page's too), or have a filled spot close the road past it (a tow is then refused until the aircraft in the way is moved).
  - *The shapes are mine, from memory of the published figures* (17.1 m long, 12.3 m span, 8.4 m folded, tailplane 6.6 m; where the aircraft's own position lies along it from the nose wheel found with the axle finder): not checked against the model.
  - *"A persistent stutter throughout the run" (ILARTS closed, still there).* Nothing in the log says where from. Done on suspicion: what the hook did in bursts is spread out (the crew recorder asked all 30 people in one frame every second, the animation recorder every half second, the parked aircraft's wings were written for all 40 every second: now four people a frame, one aircraft every tenth of a second); the panel tells DCS a button is shown or hidden only when that changes (it did so for every button every frame); the deck tool's carrier and the tow's are asked for once a frame, not twice. And the log now has, every 20 s, what the hook costs a frame, by part ("frame cost: ..."): the next run says whether it is the hook at all. NOT KNOWN: whether any of this was the stutter (40 static aircraft are also 40 aircraft for DCS to draw).
  - *"The tug could probably drive a bit faster when transiting a major artery."* On the flight deck the tractor now goes by how its way bends (`Tow.speeds`): 1.4 m/s in a turn as before, up to 3 m/s by itself and 2 m/s with an aircraft on the bar on a straight, braking for the bend ahead. My numbers (6 and 4 knots); the hangar as before.
- **After the second run (the user, 2026-11-17): "It's working pretty well and was much smoother that time."** (The time-acceleration, the turn in front of the aircraft, the clearance checks, the spread-out bursts and the faster straights of the entry above.) Decided by the user:
  - *The taxi network is to adjust to the parked aircraft:* "Yes the network needs to adjust based on parked aircraft. Not every frame, but often enough to keep movements looking realistic. The network structure should remain the same. Main arteries branching to individual parking locations and catapults." NOT BUILT YET.
  - *The street past the six-pack:* "I positioned all of the aircraft such that there is sufficient width to tow an aircraft through the street with all aircraft spotted in the sixpack and El 1/Corral/El 2 areas." (As measured here: 11.3 m between the six-pack's noses and El 2's, a folded Hornet 8.4 m: there is the width; it is the road, at about 11 m to starboard where the lane's middle is at 17.9 m, that is in the wrong place. The adjusting network is to put it in the middle.)
- **The hangar deck, mapped from the models (asked for by the user, 2026-11-17: "What's the best way to get the available space in the hangar? ... maybe there is a faster way from reading the model geometry itself? ... Whatever method ... you think is the best, implement it now"; then: "add it as a layer to the Nimitz.svg file as well").**
  - *The way taken:* read DCS's own model files. They are plain .edm files, `crew-test/model/edm.py` reads them whole, and their coordinates are the ship's own (x forward, y up, z to starboard, from the ship's origin: checked: the collision model's elevator 1 comes out at 16.7 to 42.6 m forward, 21.4 to 37.2 m to starboard, its top at 20.25 m, where the hook's elevator box and the flight deck are; the four elevators within about a metre of the ones traced on the deck drawing). No camera, no clicking, no screenshot. The tool: `catcc-dcs/deck-test/hangar/hangar_map.py` (python -I, no libraries; nothing of DCS's is run or changed).
  - *Read:* `Nimitz_CVN_71_Collision.EDM` (the ship's collision shells: the hangar as the physics has it, the elevators and how far they travel), `Nimitz_Hangar.EDM` (the Supercarrier's drawn hangar, 522,848 triangles), `Hang_Floor.edm` (the plain one). For the second, `edm.py` learnt the material key DECAL (one byte) and the node model::LightNode (the node it hangs on, a byte, its properties, a byte): the file reads whole and writes back byte for byte, as the other two do. A node's 16 numbers are a matrix in column order; an animated node at rest is its base matrix x a move to its base position x its base turn x its scale; a render node's vertices carry as their fourth number which parent they hang on.
  - *Found:* the hangar floor is 8.711 m above the ship's origin, the roof of the physics' shell 7.94 m above it. TWO FLOORS: the floor as DRAWN is 8,572 m2 (129.7 m aft to 74.4 m forward, 21.5 m to port to 22.5 m to starboard at the doorways; the walls at 21.4 m either side); the floor the PHYSICS has is 6,658 m2: the shell `Col_Hangar` (6,194 m2: 127.1 m aft to 53.3 m forward, 17.85 m to port, 16.81 m to starboard: 4 m short of either wall, 20 m short of the forward bulkhead) plus a sill in each of the four elevator doorways (level faces of the hull's shells CENTER_R_01 / _02, BACK_R, BACK_L, out to 22.5 m). On the physics' floor 6,374 m2 are free from 0.15 to 4.8 m above it (a Hornet is 4.7 m to the top of its fins), 26 m2 have something below 1.2 m only, 262 m2 something higher; of the drawn floor outside it another 1,468 m2 are free. The elevators (model arguments 57 to 60) come down 11.43 m, their tops then 0.11 m above the hangar floor.
  - *Written (in `catcc-dcs/deck-test/hangar/`):* `hangar-plan.png` (from straight above, everything over 4.8 m taken away, 10 cm a pixel, bow right, starboard down: white free physics floor, blue-grey free floor that is only drawn, orange low things, red high things, purple parts that move, blue the physics floor's edge, a 10 m grid); `hangar-plan.svg` (the same as a drawing with the ship's coordinates written on the grid: both floors, the cuts through the drawn hangar at 0.3, 1.5 and 3.0 m joined into lines, the elevators' platforms); `hangar-map.json` (the floors as polygons, the elevators, a grid of 20 cm cells with its legend, the areas). And the layer **"Hangar Deck"** in `carrier-ops-handoff-2/Nimitz.svg`, in that drawing's own millimetres by its fit to the ship (read from the top of nimitzDeck.gen.ts): "Floor as drawn", "Floor of the physics", "Low fittings (cut 0.3 m)", "Walls and fittings (cut 1.5 m)"; everything else in the file byte for byte as it was (checked), and `scripts/catcc/nimitz-deck.py` gives the same output with the layer as without. `--nimitz <file>` rewrites the layer. Tests: `catcc-dcs/test/hangar.test.ts`.
  - *For the 40 aircraft (not worked out yet):* a folded Hornet's own rectangle is 17.1 x 8.4 m = 144 m2, forty of them 5,750 m2, against 6,374 m2 of free physics floor (7,842 m2 with the floor that is only drawn): not as plain rectangles side by side with any room to move; whether forty fit nested is for the parking plan to show. An aircraft on its own wheels needs the physics' floor; one we place (a static) only has to clear what is drawn.
  - *Came up on the way, NOT the layer's doing:* running `scripts/catcc/nimitz-deck.py` (to check the layer does not disturb it) rewrote `src/components/catcc/prifly/nimitzDeck.gen.ts` with one difference from the committed file: helicopter spot "1" is gone (7 spots committed, 6 from the drawing as committed: the same with the layer and without it). The committed file was older than the drawing. Left as the generator wrote it (the git guard does not let a checkout of the path through, rightly); the user to say which is wanted.
- **The deck spotting demo (asked for by the user, 2026-11-17, built while he was away: "Spot a maximum of 40 aircraft on the flight deck ... as many aircraft in the hangar as possible, bearing in mind that all aircraft need to be accessible by a tug ... Tugs may not ride on elevators ... outside in ... 4 tugs in the hangar, and 4 on the flight deck, ensure they don't interfere with each other at any point ... tow bars already attached ... CVW-14 liveries in groups of 4 aircraft per flight ... tugs should park themselves when finished ... catapult crews should move somewhere out of the way ... the bow bubble should be retracted ... a shortcut key").** NOT YET RUN IN DCS: everything below is from the test rig. Installed (`crew-test\install.ps1`, 15:01).
  - *Start / stop:* **Ctrl+Shift+F9** (the panel's window holds the key; works with the panel put away, and with no player on the deck), or F10 > "CATCC demo: spot the deck: start / stop", or the two buttons on the panel's Tow page. "CATCC demo: report" says where it is. Pressed again it takes everything away and brings the elevators up. It runs on the carrier the camera is at.
  - *Files:* `Scripts/CATCC/crewtest_demo.lua` (the demo: its own file, loaded by the hook and handed what it needs of it), `Scripts/CATCC/crewtest_hangar.lua` (the hangar's parking plan, generated by `deck-test/hangar/hangar_plan.py` from the hangar's map), `CR.demo` in the mission script (all 61 statics made in one call, each linked to the ship; an aircraft's `livery_id`), the hook (`Tow.tickOne`: one tow stepped for any tractor; `w.ids`, `w.env`, `w.keepBar`, `w.arc`, `w.onFree`; `Tow.Aux`: crews away, bubbles, an elevator through someone on the camera's ship; `Tow.Fd.finder`, `Tow.Fd.log`), the panel (the key, two buttons), `groundcrew-patch.lua` (seven more drivers).
  - *THIRTEEN IN THE HANGAR, 27 ON THE DECK FROM THE START (not 14: first plan).* The hangar is 33.4 m wide between its inner walls and a Hornet 17.1 m long: one row (tails to the starboard wall, noses to port, wing tips 0.4 m apart) and the port side as the lane; a second row would shut the first in. The row has thirteen places between the fittings (56 m forward to 113 m aft). The first plan had a fourteenth and fifteenth idea: two at elevator 4's own door on the port side, noses to starboard. In the rig they and elevator 3's pair stood nose past nose right across the hangar and no tractor could reach the bar of any of the four: dropped. Elevator 4's two now stand in the row and are drawn along the lane past its doorway and backed through it (9 m from the lane to the door: it fits).
  - *Which thirteen:* the eight places on the four elevators (they cannot have aircraft from the start: the platforms go down) and five of the six-pack. The 27 that stand on the deck from the start are the outer spots, which nothing is towed past, and Sixpack 4.
  - *The trips:* elevator 1: the two at its door, then the one from 12 m forward, then two from forward of it (they stay: El 1 1 / El 1 2); elevator 2: the two at its door, then two from aft of it (stay); elevator 3: the two at its door (stay); elevator 4: two from the row (stay). Two aircraft a trip, side by side, pushed onto the platform tail first; all four elevators work at once.
  - *Found in the rig and mended, in the order they came:* (1) a wing tip exactly on the doorway's edge: the doorway was measured at one place (20.8 m between its posts); at its sill the door housings leave 19.0 to 19.6 m at wing height. (2) The housings of the two dividing doors stand on the port side at 2 to 6 m forward and at 61 to 65 m aft, to 12.5 m from the centreline: the lane is now at 8.2 m to port (wing tip 0.1 m inboard of them), and the row's aircraft next to one can only be turned AWAY from it: the one at 12 m goes forward to elevator 1, the one at 68 m aft goes aft to elevator 4. (3) A tractor stands 4 to 6 m short of the lane as it pulls an aircraft out of the row: it may swing up to 4 m wide of the lane and come back onto it (tried in the head; the first that passes is taken). (4) Between the hangar's sill and a lowered platform the map has a 0.37 m strip of "no floor" (two models meeting): taken as floor.
  - *The platforms' places are the user's own elevator spots, as nearly as the doorways allow.* The two spots on a platform are 11.5 to 12.5 m apart (19.9 to 20.9 m over the wing tips); an aircraft is pushed straight through a doorway 19.0 to 19.6 m wide. Each place has its spot's own distance from the centreline; along the ship it is moved only as far as keeps its outer wing tip 0.6 m from the doorway's side. Result: El 2 2 and El 3 2 exact, El 4 2 0.21 m, El 1 2 0.31 m; the outer one of each pair inboard of its spot: El 1 1 1.15 m, El 4 1 1.90 m, El 2 1 2.59 m, El 3 1 2.69 m. (To put those four on their spots a deck tractor would have to shift them after the elevator is up: not done.)
  - *The six-pack: which aircraft to which spot.* A tow turns into a spot from the side it comes from; with a neighbour already on both sides it cannot keep the planner's 0.1 m from both. All 432 ways of sharing the six spots between elevator 1's three, elevator 2's two and the one that stands there from the start were run from start to finish: none has every tow clear; 28 have a single tow that comes 0.1 m inside the margin and nothing worse. Taken: Sixpack 4 from the start; elevator 1's to Sixpack 2, 1, 3; elevator 2's to Sixpack 6, 5 (27 minutes of model time; the tow into Sixpack 5 comes within the margin of Corral 2's nose by 0.1 m: a brush by the padded shapes, not an overlap of the drawn aircraft as far as the shapes can tell). Off an elevator the aircraft whose tow is clear goes first.
  - *NO TWO TRACTORS IN ONE PLACE.* Before a tractor starts, everything it will sweep (its way to the aircraft, the tow, the push, the spot) is laid out as circles and held against what every other tractor on that deck holds and against every idle tractor; it starts only when nothing touches, and holds it until the aircraft is delivered; then it holds only where it stands and its way back. Measured over the whole run: the least distance between two tractors while one of them moves is 5.6 m (the two that wait side by side aft of the island; everywhere else 8 m and more). An elevator is not lowered while a tractor's way lies over it; a platform that is not up is a hole to every plan; no tractor's way crosses a platform's doorway except to push an aircraft onto it, and it is off the platform before it moves ("up as soon as the tractor is inside the hull").
  - *A deck tractor's own way: across the open deck, not along the taxi roads.* The hook's way-finder for a tractor alone keeps to the roads and 5.5 m off every aircraft's whole length: on the full deck it sent a tractor 318 m round the ship to a place 45 m away, 2.1 m past another tractor. The demo has its own (`findWay`, given to the hook's planners as `Tow.Fd.finder`): the deck in cells of 1.5 m, shut where there is no deck, within 3.4 m of an aircraft's wheels (it goes under noses and wings), on a platform that is not up, in what another tractor holds, near an idle tractor; shortest way (A*), corners cut, then driven in the head by the hook before it is taken. It comes back into its waiting place along a fixed line (into the pockets by the island from inboard).
  - *Nothing is planned again until something has changed* (a tow started, an aircraft delivered, a tractor back, an elevator arrived): the first whole run planned again every 5 s while it waited, 60 s of computing for a 30 minute demo in the rig and a hitch every 5 s to come in DCS; now 3 s for the same run.
  - *Tractors:* four in the hangar (waiting forward of elevator 1, two in the bay in front of the big starboard fittings, one in the row's gap forward of elevator 3's doorway), four on the flight deck (the landing area abeam elevator 1, the pocket forward of the island, two in the pocket aft of it). On the deck the tractor that has done least goes: all eight had work in the rig (hangar 5 / 3 / 1 / 4 tows, deck 1 / 2 / 1 / 1). Each parks where it waits when it is done; the rig holds every one within 0.5 m of its place at the end.
  - *Liveries:* by the user's two lists, a flight's four on neighbouring spots, the lead's first with "-FL": VFA-44 `KING - LineJet[-FL] - <board>` (20 boards; 556 not used), VFA-161 `STING - LineJet[-FL] - <board>` (20 boards; 717 and 635, retired / removed, not used). No board twice. Squadrons are told apart by those names only.
  - *The player's own jet:* its spot gets no aircraft (the deck's: a live aircraft within 6 m; first build 14 m, which also emptied the spot beside it). If it stands on an elevator, that elevator is left alone and its aircraft are not made; if on or beside a six-pack spot, those are not brought up. It is in every plan's way as a parked aircraft is (wings taken as spread). OTHER LIVE AIRCRAFT (AI, other players) are not seen by the plans.
  - *Wings:* folded on all forty (the deck tool's model argument 8, written again in turn).
  - *Catapult crews and bubbles:* the four crews are walked to the port deck edge forward of the landing area (20 to 45 m forward, 34 to 38 m to port), both bubbles lowered and kept down; at the stop the bubbles are let go.
  - *DRIVERS: ONE UNTIL THE CREW PATCH IS APPLIED AGAIN.* The demo seats our people named catcc-t0 .. t7, the deck's tractors first. DCS has catcc-t0 only; `groundcrew-patch.lua` now adds catcc-t1 .. t7 (blue shirts, in catapult crews 2, 3 and 4, three / two / two, so that no crew is larger than the twelve crew 1 is seen to work with). That needs `director-test.ps1 -Spawn Spots -Crew Model -CatGate` again (admin: not run, the user was away). Until then seven tractors drive with nobody on the seat, and the demo's first message says "(1 of 8 tractors have a driver.)". Whether DCS takes crews of 9 / 8 / 8 is NOT SEEN.
  - *The tractor drivers are kept out of the crew recorder* (eight of them moving wrote two thirds of the rig's log).
  - *Tests:* `crewtest.test.ts` "the deck spotting demo": the hangar's plan; the start and the stop (fengari); the key; and THE WHOLE DEMO from start to finish, twice (the player's jet on Bow stbd 4: 39 aircraft; no player: 40), under DCS's own Lua 5.1 through `deck-test/crew-test/luarun.py` (new: the test harness run with bin-mt/lua.dll, some fifty times as fast as fengari; skipped where DCS or python is missing): every aircraft on its spot, 13 through the elevators, the five six-pack tows end 0.00 m from their spots, every tractor back, the least distance between tractors, at most one tow inside the margin. 218 tests pass. (vitest's "Timeout calling onTaskUpdate" notice at the end of the long file was there before this work.)
  - *NOT KNOWN UNTIL IT RUNS IN DCS:* a static aircraft's livery by `livery_id` on a ship-linked static; forty aircraft statics moved every frame by the native module (one at a time was seen); the platform's model argument as the reading of where an elevator is (the demo waits for 0.99 / 0.01 and says so if it takes over a minute); DCS's own elevator sequence with two statics standing on the platform; what the 61 statics and the planning cost a frame (the hook's "frame cost" line has a part "demo"); whether the stand-in's elevator timing is DCS's.
  - *Known and left:* the stay pairs' four outer aircraft 1.2 to 2.7 m inboard of the user's spots (above); the one tow that brushes the margin; a tractor's bar stays on every aircraft that came up from the hangar; the taxi network still does not adjust to the parked aircraft (the user's decision of this morning: not built; the demo's tows use the planner as it is, its tractors their own way-finder).
- **The deck spotting demo: its first run in DCS (2026-11-17, 22:09 local, stopped by the user after 8 minutes: "Just let the demo run partially. I'm impressed."), and what he asked for after it.** Installed 2026-11-17 22:58 (native module crewtest-18, hook, demo); NOT YET RUN IN DCS in this form: what follows the run's own facts is from the test rig.
  - *What that run showed (read from `Logs/catcc-crewtest.log`):* 39 aircraft made and kept in place ("put through its own SetPos ... a child of woShip ... it says it is there" for every one: forty linked aircraft statics can be placed); the platforms' model arguments read as the demo expects (each elevator "is down (21 s)", "is up (19 s)" with its two aircraft); seven tows to the elevators and two to the six-pack each ended "0.0 m from the place"; once the elevators were down DCS's own control gave each "up" three times in three seconds and the hook's "down" was given again each time. Whether the liveries showed is not in the log (dcs.log has no complaint about them).
  - *THE FRAME RATE FELL TO 7 TO 13 A SECOND WHILE IT RAN (143 before it).* The hook's own "frame cost" line: "demo" 75 to 100 ms a frame with three tows running, "doors" 44 ms more once three elevators had come up. The user did not mention it. *Cause (the native module):* every model argument written (`objarg`, `shiparg`) and every static placed looked up a virtual function in the object's tables from scratch (`findVirtual`: every slot of every base's table asked of Windows with `VirtualQuery`), about 4 ms a call: a tow makes some six such calls a frame (20 to 25 ms a tow: the single tows of the days before show the same in their own frame-cost lines), an elevator held with its door open four. (2) *Drawn out nose first, then pushed back*: its nose towards the doorway (1 2 9 11 19 20 21 22 27 29): out along its own line, across to the starboard side for the longest run, round onto the line through its place on the platform, across the hangar until the tractor is 2.6 m from the far wall, and pushed straight back (the push eased in: its first 6 m the way the aircraft then points: without that "the bar would fold against the tractor" for every one that came from forward). It may be up to 15 degrees round at the end of the swing: it straightens as it backs. (b) **THE FRONT HITCH** ("its other connection point"; the tractor's model has the same pintle at its front, 1.73 m ahead of its position): where there is no getting past a nose (18: its neighbour's wheels on one side, the starboard wall's big fittings on the other) the tractor comes down the nose line head on, stops 1.5 m short, drives onto the ring, and pushes or draws the aircraft facing it. In the hook this is one flag (`w.frontHitch` / `w.front`, `Tow.real`): the tow goes on working with the tractor as if it stood the other way round on the same ground (the two take up the same ground to the centimetre: 2.93 - 1.73 = 1.2 m); only the model, the driver and the wheels' turning are the real one's. Unknown until tried: whether aircraft against aircraft is tested on those parts at all, what it does to weapons hitting a parked aircraft, and it would switch collisions off on any ground, not only the carrier. Lift the tugs' zone limitations and allow them to assist with aircraft on any catapult, but make them prefer jobs that are closer, i.e. add a distance penalty ... (e.g. 30 seconds) ... Instead of parking in front of the elevator and potentially blocking taxi routes for longer than necessary, tugs that are anticipating an aircraft arriving on an elevator should wait at a staging area (either the junkyard, pocket between el 2 and the island, or any empty aircraft parking space that's nearby) and time their travel to the elevator such that they arrive just before the elevator reaches the flight deck level ... Sometimes it looks like a tug is waiting for another tug to finish an operation before it moves to its next job. I want tugs to do their work in parallel and deconflict with each other logically." ("Catapults" read as elevators: his own example names elevators 4 and 1.)
  - *From his run's log (the third build in DCS, 08:41):* about 130 frames a second before the demo, 21 to 31 during it; the hook 0.5 to 6.5 ms a frame on average, its worst frames 100 to 430 ms (planning). In the session after it (08:56) the spotting tool parked 38 static Hornets and nothing moved: the hook took 0.06 ms a frame and the frame rate over those 20 s was 39 (134 in the 20 s before). So the drop in frame rate comes with the static aircraft being there, not with our script's time; our script's own cost shows as the hitches.
  - *NO SECTIONS (`assign`):* every aircraft on its way up is a task with the time until its platform is up; a tractor's cost for it is the later of that and "the tractor free, its drive there, and 30 s"; the cheapest pairs are made every half second; a pair is kept unless another is 12 s cheaper, and fixed once the tractor is on its way to the elevator. So a tractor waits for a near aircraft rather than cross the deck for a far one unless the far one is more than 30 s the better (his rule).
  - *STAGING AND TIMED ARRIVAL (`stagePlace`, `nextPlace`, `travelTime`):* a tractor waits in its place in the junkyard, in the pocket between the island and elevator 2, or on a deck spot that is still empty (its aircraft still in the hangar, no aircraft on its way up bound for a spot within 11 m). It sets off for the front of its elevator when the time until the platform is up has come down to its drive there (driven in the head) and 6 s. In the rig tractors stood in front of an elevator for 22 to 179 s in all a run (730 s before); in the base run 0 to 57 s before the platform was up, half of them 7 s or less.
  - *WORKING AT THE SAME TIME (`circles` with times, `free`, `giveWay`):* what a deck job holds is held for the time it needs it: two jobs are in each other's way only where circles touch at overlapping times. A job may start BEHIND another: it stops short of what they share (the hook's `d.hold`: it brakes on its way) and goes on when the other has passed; a job that falls behind takes its times with it (`job.lag`). A job that stands waiting for a tractor counts for that tractor as what stands there (an aircraft and a tractor), so a tractor that has delivered into a spot gets out past a tow waiting in the street. The hangar still holds a tow's whole way from start to delivery (it is packed; not changed).
  - *What it bought in the rig:* aircraft stood on a platform that was up for 366 to 783 s in all a run before their tow began, in the runs with all twelve tows (1432 s in the build before); the twenty are up in 26 to 31 minutes (the hangar sets that pace: 31.6 before). Nearest pass between two tractors with one moving 4.4 m, between an aircraft in tow and a tractor not its own 8 m, in all nine variants (1, 2, 4 and 8 times speed, 1 / 6 / 30 planning steps a frame, the player's jet on Bow stbd 4 / El 1 1 / Sixpack 4 / none). With the player's jet on Sixpack 4 one tractor found no way to a staging place for 90 s (it moved 8 m on and stood).
  - *Bugs the rig found on the way:* a hangar tractor's job ending after the deck's tow of the same aircraft had begun put the chocks back in under that tow and took "in tow" off it (the deck tractor is now there the moment the platform is up); two jobs each behind the other (one stopped in the other's path: 0.8 m); a tow held 109 s for a tractor that had long passed (only one that has still to come to what the two contest can give way). Bash heredocs mangled backslashes in patch scripts three more times: patch scripts are written with the Write tool.
  - *RESEARCH: can the planning run off DCS's main thread, in another process, or on the players' machines? (asked by the user; nothing built.)* Found: (1) Our hook and the mission script are Lua states that DCS runs on its main thread; DCS's own calls (placing people, statics, model arguments) must stay there. (2) A native module loaded by a hook may start threads of its own and may make Lua states of its own: DCS's lua.dll exports what is needed (our test rig drives the whole demo through that same lua.dll outside DCS: 31 minutes of demo in 12 s). The planners are pure calculation on data, so they can run in a second Lua state on a worker thread of catcc_crew.dll, the hook asking each frame whether an answer is ready. That takes the hitches (100 to 430 ms frames) out of DCS's frame; it does not change the average (0.5 to 6.5 ms). (3) Other mods work this way on servers: DCS-gRPC (a Rust DLL loaded by a hook, a server inside DCS's process, calls into the mission's Lua limited to a set rate) and DCS Olympus (a C++ DLL inside DCS plus a separate web server process); both are run on dedicated servers. So a DLL, and a second process beside the server, are established practice. (4) The dedicated server: ED's multithreading FAQ (2023) called it single-threaded with a multithreaded server planned; a later forum reply says the 2.9 server is as multithreaded as the client but game logic still runs on the main thread; no ED statement found that settles it. Either way a worker thread of our own does not depend on it. (5) Sharing the work between connected players: nothing in DCS does this for us; it would need our own messages between machines (hooks can open sockets with the LuaSocket DCS ships; chat messages are the only built-in channel found) and a server that checks what comes back. A fact that weighs more: what our native module moves is moved only on the machine it runs on, so in multiplayer every machine with the mod has to play the movement anyway; a plan as data (a way and a start time), made once on the server and sent out, serves both. Not found: any DCS mod that shares planning between clients; whether rented servers allow DLLs (it differs by host).
  - *NOT KNOWN UNTIL IT RUNS IN DCS:* how a tractor braking to give way and going on looks; whether backing into an empty deck spot between parked aircraft looks right; what the frame costs now (giving way is checked twice a second; a deck tow is asked for again every 4 s while it waits).
- **Active pause holds the tractors too (2026-11-18; built and tested in the rig, NOT INSTALLED: DCS was running; 219 tests).** The user: "Pause/Active Pause. When I pressed active pause in my last session, the tugs continued to move aircraft. They should pause too."
  - *Why they went on:* the tow's clock is the mission's (model time). Under DCS's pause that stands still, and the tows with it (tested before). Under ACTIVE pause DCS holds the player's own aircraft only: the mission's clock runs on.
  - *How it is known:* DCS's own `Scripts/Export.lua` lists `LoSimulationOnActivePause()`: "true if simulation is in active pause"; the hook has the Export table (it reads the camera from it). ASSUMED, NOT SEEN IN DCS: that the call is there in the hook's Lua state and answers true under active pause (the log says "active pause ON" / "OFF after N s" when it does).
  - *What was done (`Tow.now`):* the model time that passes while active pause is on is taken out of the tow's clock (`Tow.ap.off`), asked every frame. Everything of ours that moves a tractor or an aircraft runs on that clock: a tow asked for by hand, the whole deck spotting demo, and an elevator the demo raises (the hook's `elevRise` now takes the tow's clock). What DCS itself moves goes on under active pause: its deck crew, an elevator on its way down; our own people's walks are DCS's too.
  - *Rig:* a tow by hand: tractor and jet 0.000 m in 5 s (20 s at four times speed) of the mission under active pause, on again after it; the demo with a minute of active pause at five different moments: nothing of its 57 to 67 statics moves (a millimetre of rounding at most), and it finishes as ever.
  - *Found on the way:* the tow bar of an aircraft that had come up on an elevator stood 11.4 m below it (on the hangar's level) until the hangar tractor that had brought it was back in its place: the hangar tow went on placing the bar. The bar is the aircraft's from the moment it is delivered onto the platform.
