- **The hangar solved: a tow for every aircraft in the user's own order, the tractors' ways planned offline, the whole demo finishing in the rig (2026-11-18; 221 tests; installed 17:53; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS).** The user: "I'm not even going to run the demo anymore unless we have paths for all aircraft. I've described what I believe is the correct unloading sequence to guide you. See if you can make it work." His sequence (EL1 1, EL1 2, EL1 3, EL2 1, EL2 2, EL1 4, EL2 3, EL2 4, EL1 5/6, EL2 5/6, EL3 1, EL4 1, EL4 2, EL3 2, EL3 3, EL3 4, EL4 3, EL4 4, EL3 5/6, EL4 5/6), the side of the platform he gave each ("left side ... looking from starboard to port" = aft = the plan's place 1; right = forward = place 2), his rule change ("Tugs may connect to aircraft with the nosewheel turned initially if required. In these cases, the nosewheel and towbar should be rotated together after the connection is made, then connected to the tug") and his layout change (EL1 4 moved aft in the drawing). Later the same day: "the hangar environment is essentially static ... tugs don't need to account for dynamic obstacles the way they do on the deck." WHAT WAS BUILT. (1) `catcc-dcs/deck-test/hangar/hangar_solve.lua` (run by `hangar_solve.py` in the test rig, some 15 minutes; needs DCS's lua.dll): for each aircraft in his order, with everything later in the order still parked (for elevators 3 and 4 also every aircraft of 1 and 2, whether gone or not: it was never needed to drop that), a search over tows driven in the head by the hook's own Tow.Fd.sim / Tow.Fd.back: pushed only, or pulled (straight, one bend, two bends) then pushed; one reversal at most; the nose wheel turned 0 to 70 degrees at the hook-up. All 24 solved: 13 pushed only, 11 pulled first; three hooked on with the nose wheel turned (EL2 3 20 degrees, EL3 4 30, EL4 5 10). Least room to another aircraft by the planners' shapes: EL2 4 0.36 m, EL4 6 0.76 m (EL3 1 starts 0.87 m INSIDE EL4 1 by those shapes: the two overlap as drawn, said before). (2) The tractors' own ways are planned there too, as a tractor drives (forward only, turning on 4.6 m and more: a search over place and heading), with 1.5 m of floor and 0.8 m to wheels where that can be had (else 1.15 / 0.35), each held against a tractor standing 12 cm and 2.5 degrees off: from every waiting place ("post") to each aircraft's hook-up place, from where each tow leaves the tractor back to every post, and from post to post. Posts: 8 that connect both ways with the hangar packed (the solver's POSTWAY count; which way a tractor stands at each is the solver's): homes 48.5 / -2.2, 42 / 14.5, -77 / 14.5, -52 / -14.5; spare -1 / -14.5, 27 / -14.5, -10 / -14.5, -93.5 / -3. Ways to an aircraft exist from 6 to 8 posts for 23 of the 24, from 2 for EL4 2 (the aftmost). (3) All of it goes into `crewtest_hangar.lua` (`tows`, `hops`; 283 KB) through `hangar-tows.lua` and `hangar_plan.py` (which takes it only if the drawing and the posts are still the ones it was solved for). (4) The demo (`crewtest_demo.lua`): the solved tow is tried first (`towOf`); a tractor that stands at a post goes by its solved way, and back by one to the nearest free post, where it is set square on the post; one that stands where no way starts is brought by a post-to-post way (`hangarFetch`), one that stands idle in a tow's way and cannot take it moves off (`hangarShift`); a hangar job gives up what it swept once the aircraft is delivered and what its own way held behind it; an elevator waits five minutes (not 40 s) for a second aircraft that is only held up. The older on-the-fly ways are what is left when a solved one cannot be driven. (5) The hook: phase `turn` (the bar and the nose wheel turned together onto the tractor's line before the ring is lifted); a tow that ends its pull too bent to begin the push is drawn 5 m straight on and asked again (twice at most). IN THE RIG (four variants: with and without the player's jet, with a minute of active pause, at four times speed): finished; 24 of 24 up, always in pairs; 16 towed on to their deck spots, each 0.00 m from the spot; the last two of each elevator stay on it; 20 or 21 of the 24 hangar tows by a solved tractor way; chocks, covers, bars right; no two tractors nearer than 4.4 m; nothing moves under active pause; some 48 minutes of mission time (the hangar side alone: 39). ON THE FLIGHT DECK, two things the user must know: (a) PORT 3 IS LEFT WITHOUT AN AIRCRAFT (the demo's LEFT_EMPTY: 38 aircraft with a player on the deck, 39 without): with an aircraft on Port 3 from the start the tows to Port 4 and Port 5 go 2 to 3 m into it, and once Port 4 and 5 are filled the hook's deck planner has no way onto Port 3 ("no clean way onto the line across the spot"). (b) One tow (the last of elevator 2's, into Sixpack 5) is driven with its padded shape 0.1 m into that of the aircraft on Corral 2; of ten orders tried for elevator 2's four spots it is the least. The deck destinations (DEST) were reordered for pairs: the aircraft whose way off the platform leads past its partner goes second. NOT DONE: seen in DCS; refilling the hangar (the solved tows backwards); other aircraft types (both recorded under open requests).
- **After the user's first whole run of the solved demo in DCS (2026-11-18, 18:16 to 19:01; rig result of the changes; installed 21:41; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS).** HIS RUN: finished in 45 minutes, all 24 up, 16 parked 0.00 m from their spots; his words: "Overall, the run looked good (no unrealistic behaviours, collisions, or clipping, but we need to bring the overall run time down by parallelizing tow tasks and improving efficiency in a realistic manner." Performance as logged (he was also recording six ILARTS viewports and the screen): fps 21.3 average, 12 to 24 by the second; frame time 46.9 ms average, 8.0 to 227.3; 1% low 72.0 ms, 0.1% low 145.8 ms; the hook 1.16 ms a frame on average, its slowest 1% 38.6 ms, 0.1% 135.1 ms; 31 frames left out as test-only. WHAT HE ASKED AND WHAT WAS DONE. (1) "Tugs should be able to drive 1.5x faster with aircraft in tow whenever they are not turning": TOW.fastTow 2.1 m/s on both decks (it was 2.0, flight deck only); a push 1.5 times its pace where neither aircraft nor tractor turns over the next 4 m (TOW.straight). (2) "up to 3.0x faster with no aircraft in tow if not in a congested area (i.e. driving under an aircraft wing)": TOW.fastSolo 4.2 m/s, kept to 1.4 within 2.5 m of an aircraft's outline (d.cap; the demo's M.capFor), in the hangar too. (3) "The very last tow took forever": it was a 117 m push at a push's pace after 5 minutes of tractors standing in one another's way. The solver now takes the tow that takes the least time (pull where it can); that aircraft (EL4 6) stands nose forward 100 m forward of its elevator and can only be pushed (133 s). The standing-in-the-way is (5) and (9). (4) "I'm not sure what the issue with Port 3 is. If it blocks the aircraft on the stern, just spot it last, no?": Port 3 has its aircraft again, spotted after Port 4 and Port 5, by a new deck way (the demo's axisTow: off the platform, across onto the spot's own line ahead of the spot, pushed straight back, the push eased in), since the hook's own ways have none onto Port 3; Port 2 after it. The same way is tried whenever the hook's planner has no way that is clear. (5) "I see a lot of tugs wasting time making half turns ... when they could have just pulled straight in and used the hitch on the other side": the solver keeps, from each post, the shorter of the two ways (head on for the front hitch, or round to back onto the ring); only a tow that begins with a pull of more than 15 m keeps the rear hitch unless that way is half as long again and 10 m. (6) "Hangar doors remain open ... Some also seem to jitter constantly": the hook no longer writes a door open while it raises a platform (that write fought DCS's own raise every frame): DCS shuts the door while the platform rises. (7) "parallelizing tow tasks": the solver says for every pair of tows whether they keep 0.6 m from each other all along (193 of 276 do: the plan's `clear`): such a tow in hand no longer holds the other up; what a tow holds against a tractor's way is its own swept shapes, not a 5 m band; a waiting tow is looked at again when a tractor has driven by. A second aircraft on a platform sets off when the first is in tow and 16 m away, not when it is parked ("there's no reason a tug couldn't pull an aircraft off ... before the path is fully clear"). (8) A DECK FAULT FOUND ON THE WAY: a tow that gives way to a tractor STANDING in its path was held against that tractor's planning by its whole way instead of where it stands (standsFor compared the tractor's name with "<name>, which stands there"): the two then waited for each other for good. Seen in the rig once aircraft came up in pairs; fixed (yield.who). A tractor's solved way back is now held against the other jobs too (two had come within a metre); an idle tractor beside another's way is moved off; a tractor is not sent to wait at a post a tow still to come passes through (the plan's postClash). (9) "Tugs in the hangar sometimes pull off to the side to stage instead of parking at their next aircraft" and tugs idle: BUILT BUT OFF (CATCC_DEMO_OWN): one tractor for each elevator, which starts at the nose of its first aircraft and goes from each tow straight to the nose of the next (the plan's start, nextWay, early). Hangar alone: an elevator's two aircraft 1.5 minutes apart, the hangar empty in 37 minutes for 39. With the flight deck running: 55 to 63 minutes for 41 to 42 without: the elevators' waiting rules are not yet matched to it. NOT DONE: aircraft pulled into six-pack spots nose first from behind through the landing area; deck tractors going back to a staging place between jobs (the timed arrival he asked for on 2026-11-18 morning); a tractor pulling an aircraft to a holding place to free a platform. RESULT IN THE RIG (eight variants: with / without the player's jet, active pause, paces 1, 2, 4, 8, one planning step a frame, the player's jet on an elevator): all finish; 24 of 24 up, 16 parked 0.00 m; tractors 3.2 m apart at the least; 41, 42 and 45 minutes of mission time at the rig's paces 2, 4 and 8, 54 at pace 1 (48 at pace 2 before, where his run in DCS took 45): how much shorter it is in DCS is not known; 0 to 2 tows a run are still driven although not clear (the one to Stbd 1, 1.1 m into the padded outline of its partner on the platform). THE SOLVE now takes some 15 minutes (hangar_solve.py) and the plan is 326 KB.
- **After the user's second run of the solved demo (2026-11-18, 22:23 to 22:28, at several times speed, stopped by him) and his three messages with two annotated pictures (rig results; installed the same evening; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS).** HIS WORDS: "I'm still seeing a lot of tugs doing half turns to connect, particularly on the flight deck. Also seeing a lot of idling tractors on the flight deck while there are aircraft on elevators that could be pulled off in order to send the elevators back down. THe hangar doors are worse now. They mostly stay closed the entire time, only jittering open slightly over and over, but never fully opening." Then, with a picture of the deck as elevators 1 and 3 came up: "one tug has gone to each elevator, but two tugs are just sitting doing nothing when they could be waiting at El 1 and El 3 as well. Even if they couldn't take their aircraft to its spot immediately because of the other tug, they could at least pull the aircraft off the elevator so it can go back down ... it has the whole landing area to use as a holding point with its aircraft. Work on the parellelization and unecessary idling, but also add two additional tugs on both decks to help with parellelization." Then, with a drawing of the after elevators: "The aftmost aircraft on the elevator gets taken off first and sent directly to the parking spot (purple), remaining on its side of the LA as much as possible to avoid conflict with the opposite elevator. At the same time, tugs take the forewardmost aircraft into the landing area (green line) and push their aircraft back (orange line) on their side of the LA as soon as the other tug is clear. The purple tugs take one of the pink routes after dropping their aircraft in order to stay out of the way of the green/orange tugs. In this fashion it should be possible for 4 tugs to work the rear elevators with little idle time." WHAT THE LOG OF HIS RUN SHOWED. (a) Doors: each door was open 6 s after the FIRST lowering of its elevator and never again in the 8 later lowerings; the platforms came up in 10 to 12 s where the hook's own raise takes 21 to 23. So with the door no longer held open, DCS's own raise runs to its end and moves the platform too (added to ours), and from then on DCS puts the door back every frame while the elevator is lowered. (b) Idle tractors: the second aircraft of a platform was planned against its partner while that one was being hooked up beside it ("the aircraft in tow goes 2.3 m into 744"), put off for 180 s, and given to the tractor busy with the first. WHAT WAS DONE. (1) DOORS (hook): the door of an elevator that is kept down is opened on the hook's own count (6 s, on the mission's clock for the demo) and written every frame in which the model has it anywhere else, for as long as the elevator is kept down; let go when the elevator is told up (DCS shuts it). The raise writes a part only where the model is behind the hook's own count, so it no longer adds to DCS's. What DCS does to a door is counted in the log ("DCS moved it back in N of M frames"): NOT KNOWN YET whether a held door will be seen to jitter. (2) THE SECOND AIRCRAFT OF A PLATFORM: an aircraft in tow on the flight deck is left out of the next plan where it now stands (its job holds what it sweeps); a put-off is dropped when the way is clear. (3) NO DETOUR BY A STAGING PLACE: a tractor that is for an aircraft which is up stays where its last tow ended; one already on its way to a place is called back (it brakes to a stand, only where it is in nobody's way) and takes the tow from there. A tow that has delivered no longer overlooks the tractors it was planned past (two had passed 0.4 m apart in the rig). (4) HEAD ON, FRONT HITCH, ON THE FLIGHT DECK (M.deckFront; the hook's w.frontHitch as in the hangar): taken when it can be driven and is no more than 10 m longer than the way round onto the nose line. A FACT THAT COMES WITH IT: the tractor then FACES the aircraft for the whole tow: it backs along the pull and drives forward on the push; after the tow it backs 7 m before driving off. CATCC_DEMO_NO_FRONT switches it off. Used for 5 to 9 of the 16 deck tows in the rig; the others begin from a tractor that waited on the nose line, backed in. (5) A TRACTOR FOR EACH AIRCRAFT OF A PLATFORM waits in front of the elevator, there when it is up; the two are TAKEN OFF AT THE SAME TIME where their tows keep clear of each other (between the two tows of one platform what each sweeps is held as the aircraft's own width, 4.6 m circles, not the 8 to 9.5 m bands held against everything else: M.tightNow). In the rig elevator 1's two aircraft were taken 1 s apart and the platform went down 2 min 45 s after it came up (it was 4 to 8 min). (6) SIX TRACTORS ON THE FLIGHT DECK: the two more stand at the port deck edge forward of elevator 4 (-66 / -33 and -70.4 / -33, noses inboard; that this is free deck is from the hook's own outline in the rig, not seen in DCS). The junkyard has no room (forward of it the hook's deck outline ends, aft of it is elevator 3); in the pocket by the island the deep place was shut in by the other. THE HANGAR KEEPS FOUR FOR NOW: with six tractors on its eight posts the rig's runs did not finish (a tractor that stands in a tow's way has no free post to move to; one of the eight is in seven tows' way): more posts and another solve are needed first. (7) OFF THE AFTER ELEVATORS A TOW KEEPS TO ITS OWN SIDE OF THE LANDING AREA where it can: a way that takes the tractor more than 3 m over the landing area's centre line (DCS's own landing strip: the hook's LA) is given up for one straight in along the spot's own line that does not: pulled forward onto that line as far as 60 m ahead of the spot, then pushed straight back (his green and orange). (8) A RING OF JOBS THAT WAIT FOR EACH OTHER (seen in the rig as soon as six tractors worked both after elevators: 5 behind 4, 6 behind 5, 4 behind 6) is broken: a tractor by itself, or one still on its way to its aircraft, is stood down (the aircraft is given out again). A ring of tows that all have their aircraft on the bar is only said. NOT DONE OF WHAT HE ASKED: a tow that takes an aircraft into the landing area and WAITS there on the bar when the way to its spot is in use (a tow still begins only when its whole way can be had, at once or behind another); the "pink" ways home for tractors that have delivered (they take the way-finder's way round what the others hold); six-pack aircraft pulled in from behind through the landing area; two more tractors in the hangar. RESULT IN THE RIG (default at paces 1, 2, 4, 8; without the player's jet and with the panel; active pause): all finish, 24 of 24 up, 16 parked 0.00 m, tractors 4.4 m apart at the least on the flight deck; 38 to 46 minutes of mission time (41 to 48 before these changes at the same paces; pace 1: 36 to 41). The time is the hangar's: an elevator's aircraft are brought to it one after the other, and the last six tows of the hangar take 13 to 18 minutes; on the flight deck an aircraft now stands 600 to 1040 s in all on raised platforms before its tow begins (1293 before). 221 tests pass.

(1) HOLDING IN THE LANDING AREA, THE AIRCRAFT ON THE BAR: a tow whose whole way cannot be had may begin BEHIND every job that holds part of the rest of it, if nothing of theirs meets its way at the same time before it is 11 s short of where it holds (the pull's end, or plan.holdS along it) and that place is in the landing area (DCS's own landing strip: the hook's LA): giveWay then stops it short of the first of its circles that another needs at the same time, and lets it go on (job.late; `free`: cs.lateFrom). Taken when the backed-in way cannot begin (2 to 3 of 16 tows in the rig: 113 to 115 m, about 80 s). It leaves the platform's front free; NOT yet chosen for that reason alone. (3) THE PINK WAYS: a tractor by itself pays 2.2 times for every cell of the landing area (the way-finder's FIND.la), so it goes round outboard where that is little longer and crosses by the shortest way where it must. (4) THE ELEVATORS' RELEASE: an aircraft is off its elevator's list once it is 14 m off its place there (it was: when parked), and the platform goes down when nothing still to come lies over the platform's own rectangle and 1.5 m (each tow as its own width; it was: anything held within 16 m of its middle). (6) WHAT KEEPS IT MOVING (each found as a standstill in the rig): a tractor called back or stood down that is in a tow's way steps aside to open deck near by (M.stepAside: off the landing area, off the fronts of the platforms, off the ways found for the aircraft that wait), after 25 s through what the others hold (they give way: job.force); a tractor boxed in has the tractors by themselves near it stood down; where two jobs stand in each other's way and one is a tractor by itself, that one is stood down and steps aside first; a ring of tows that all have aircraft on the bar: the one whose next 25 s are clear of where the others stand goes first; a swap is not turned back for 30 s; a parked tractor is in a tow's way only if the tow's own width touches it; a tractor does not drive to a place another stands idle within 5 m of. IN THE HANGAR. (7) A TRACTOR FOR EACH ELEVATOR IS NOW ON (CATCC_DEMO_NO_OWN switches it off): it starts at the nose of its elevator's first aircraft where the plan has that, and goes from each tow straight to the nose of the next. What made it slow before: (a) a tractor waiting at a nose, where the solver had it clear, "stood in the way" of the tow beside it by a 2.9 m disc and was sent 100 m to a post at the other end (now: its own shape, 0.6 m: M.tugHits); (b) one that really is in the way steps aside 6 to 30 m (M.hangarStandOk) before any far post; (c) no tractor is fetched to another post while one stands idle at a post with a solved way (one drove from post 1 to post 2 and back six times); (d) a tractor within 1.5 m of a post is at that post whichever way it faces (one 0.14 m off its post and facing another way had no way to its aircraft, and elevator 4 stood for good), and the way-finder's way back comes onto a post along the post's heading; (e) an elevator whose own tractor has found no way for 45 s takes any idle tractor. (8) A tractor's way back is held from the moment it is chosen and the way-finder's own way back is held against the other jobs (two hangar tractors had met 0.2 m apart); what a tow has swept is given up behind it. RESULT IN THE RIG (default at paces 1, 2, 4, 8): all finish, 24 of 24 up, 16 parked 0.00 m, tractors 3.3 m apart at the least (two side by side in the junkyard), an aircraft in tow 7.7 m from another tractor at the least: 33.6, 32.1, 35.4 and 46.1 minutes (before the night: 36 to 47; before his first run: 48). (b) With one planning step a frame a tractor that stood where its tow had ended, in the way of a tow that had stopped for it, was stood down every 41 s for an hour (every way out began through what that tow held): the third time in 150 s it sets off through what the others hold for the next 90 s, and they give way. (5) EARLIER THE SAME NIGHT, not yet written down: the sweep of a hangar tow also gives up the tractor's own start disc behind it; the own-side rule (a tow off an after elevator keeps to its side of the landing area) is for the FORWARD aircraft only, the one that goes into the landing area (for both: 31.6 / 32.1 / 35.0 / 29.1 minutes at paces 2 / 4 / 8 / 1; for neither: 29.6 / 33.6 / 32.8 / 27.7; for the forward one: 29.5 / 31.5 / 32.8 / 27.7); where an aircraft stands is held as three 5.5 m circles and not against tractors by themselves; another tractor is asked after 75 s only where the first found no way; the way of a tow that waits on a raised platform for a way in use is kept against tractors by themselves and shut for their way-finder (M.pending; CATCC_DEMO_NO_PENDING; with / without: 30.1 / 30.9 / 32.8 / 27.8 against 28.9 / 31.5 / 32.8 / 30.1: no clear difference, kept). TRIED AND TAKEN OUT AGAIN (the rig got worse: 31.7 / 31.4 / 32.8 / 28.2): in the hangar, the second aircraft of a trip coming first where the first has no tow yet (elevator 4 went up a minute sooner, elevator 1's third load left five minutes later), and a tractor whose own elevator has nothing left below taking another elevator's aircraft at once (its 2.5 minute tow across the hangar held up place 15, which place 12 and elevator 4's last trip wait for). RESULT IN THE RIG, eight variants, all finish, 24 of 24 up, 16 parked 0.00 m (12 with the player's jet on elevator 1): pace 2 28.3, pace 4 28.1, pace 8 33.6, pace 1 27.4, no player's jet with the panel 28.3, active pause 29.3, one planning step a frame 32.8, the player's jet on elevator 1 28.6 minutes. The hangar order: place 9 (elevator 2's first) cannot be pushed out until place 6 has left for elevator 1's third load, so elevator 2's first tow begins at 4:52; place 12 waits for place 15 to leave for elevator 3, so elevator 4's last two tows begin at 21:10 and 25:10. On deck: elevator 2's first trip meets elevator 1's second in the 50 m between them (four tows to the corral and the six-pack at once), and the way from behind into Sixpack 6 keeps elevator 2 up until that aircraft is parked (9:08 to 17:51 in that run). - **After the user's run of 2026-11-19 (07:31 to 08:01: the deck spotted in 31 minutes of mission time at 1 times speed; the rig had said 27.4 to 28.3 for that build): what a tow holds is asked for by TIME, tows hook on at once, and two rules in the hangar (rig results; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS).** The user, watching it: "the greatest time savings will come from 4d conflict resolution, because I still see a lot of tugs waiting to begin a 2nd tow off an elevator until the 1st tow has been completed, even though the paths of each would never intersect as long as the second tow was started once the first was sufficiently clear"; "the two El 2 tugs wait to connect to their aircraft until El 1 tows are complete. No reason they couldn't already be connected the whole time"; "only one of those El 2 tugs actually connects to its aircraft at 11:45, and does a pointless loop before it does. The other El 2 tug drives off to the junkyard and never comes back, leaving the aircraft for another tug to tow a full 4 minutes later"; "Tugs pulling into the sixpack are pulling the aircraft all the way through its parking location, then backing up to the final position"; "The final tow from El 2 to the sixpack does not leave much clearance between the right wingtip of the towed aircraft and the right wingtip of the aftmost aircraft already parked on the sixpack"; "The final tow out of the hangar still takes way too long". WHAT HIS LOG SHOWED AT 9:30: the second tow off elevator 1 gave way at the platform's edge for 94 s, until the first was parked: giveWay stopped a tow short of the first of its circles that touched ANY circle the other had ever held, and the tight circles two tows of one platform are held against each other by are never taken off the list behind the tow. At elevator 2 both tractors stood at the noses, each "in the way of a tow off elevator 2" because neither tow could begin; both were sent off after the 60 s, one was called back 13 s later, the other went to the junkyard. (1) giveWay BY TIME (CATCC_DEMO_NO_G4D: as it was): a circle the other has given up by its clock is not stopped short of; every tow (not only one that may hold in the landing area) stops short of the first circle the other needs AT THE SAME TIME, so it follows the one ahead; a tow that is pushing into its spot or unhooking is never stopped (M.fixed); no tow is stopped while it hooks on (nor in the instant before, if its tractor stood on the nose line: job.stub); "it stands in the first's way: it goes on first" is asked with the circles the two are held against each other by, and only if its own way is clear of where the first stands (M.wayClearOf; else both wait, 45 s at the most); a tractor by itself that is stood down in the way of a tow held with its aircraft on the bar sets off through what the others hold at once, not the third time. (2) HOOKED ON AT ONCE (late == 'nose'; CATCC_DEMO_NO_NOSE): a tow whose way is in use begins all the same if its tractor has a way to the nose: it hooks on and stands on the bar where the aircraft is until nothing a job that began before it needs at the same time lies on its way (M.noseYield: its pull and push with the times they would have if it set off now); every job that begins after it gives way to it. What it holds from the moment it begins: where its aircraft stands and a 3 m disc about its tractor where it hooks on. A tractor that stands on the nose line already is not asked for its 5 cm of way against the 9 m bands of the other tows, only that no other tow's own width comes to where it hooks on (M.noseStandOk). (3) WHAT A TOW HOLDS: the push line, the spot and the end of the pull have an END (about when the aircraft will stand: M.pushTime; taken off again when the push begins; CATCC_DEMO_NO_ENDS), so a tow can be planned across them for after; and ANY two tows are held against each other as the aircraft's own width along the tractor's track (the tight circles, 4.6 m) and 2 m (M.PAD), not as the 8 m bands (16 m track to track); push lines and spots stay 9 to 9.5 m (CATCC_DEMO_NO_ALLTIGHT). (4) THE SIX-PACK FROM BEHIND, NO SET-BACK (M.pullDirect; CATCC_DEMO_NO_DIRECT): the pull ends where the tractor stands when the main wheels are on their place, the longest line behind the spot first (30, 26, 22 m); taken if the aircraft then stands within 0.15 m to the side, 0.05 m along and 2 degrees (M.DIRECT). In the rig: 0.02 to 0.09 m to the side, 0.3 to 0.9 degrees; with 24 m of line 0.13 to 0.14 m and 1.3 to 1.4 degrees. No tow was set back in any run. Its way round the end of the row keeps 7 m more than a tractor's from the row's aircraft (3.6 m if there is no such way; CATCC_DEMO_NO_WIDEWALL). (5) IN THE HANGAR. (a) NOTHING IS DRAWN UP TO THE DOOR of an elevator while the elevator OPPOSITE it (3 and 4) is down and still has aircraft to bring for the trip it is taking on (M.doorWaits; five minutes at the most; CATCC_DEMO_NO_DOORWAIT): an aircraft at elevator 4's door with its tractor on the bar reaches 26 m across a hangar 43 m wide, and every aircraft of elevator 3 is drawn out through that place: elevator 4's third aircraft stood at its door from 6:53 until the platform came down at 10:50, and elevator 3's fourth waited for that from 8:14, its platform down: every minute elevator 4 stayed up on the flight deck was a minute lost for elevator 3 and all that follows it below. With the rule elevator 3's second trip is up at 10:39 instead of 13:17. (b) A hangar tractor whose own elevator has nothing left goes to the nose of the LAST aircraft of another elevator that has two and more to come (the one furthest from its elevator) and waits there; that tow is then its own (CATCC_DEMO_NO_LASTCLAIM): elevator 1's tractor waits at elevator 4's last aircraft from 14:16. It still began a minute after the one before stood on the platform: elevator 4's own tractor drives forward to its post through the way that aircraft is pushed along. RESULT IN THE RIG, eight variants, all finish, 24 of 24 up, 16 parked (12 with the player's jet on elevator 1): pace 2 27.5, pace 4 28.0, pace 8 31.7, pace 1 27.0, no player's jet with the panel 27.5, active pause 28.5, one planning step a frame 27.8, the player's jet on elevator 1 28.7 minutes (the build of the night before: 28.3 / 28.1 / 33.6 / 27.4 / 28.3 / 29.3 / 32.8 / 28.6). Two tractors 3.3 m apart at the least (side by side in the junkyard), an aircraft in tow 7.7 m from another tractor at the least; two aircraft "in tow" 9.3 m: the two of elevator 2 on their places, both hooked on. At pace 1: elevator 1's second trip, up at 9:17: both aircraft hooked on at 9:28 and pulling at 9:30, the platform down at 10:55; elevator 2's first trip down 5 min 10 after it came up (8 min 43 the night before); elevator 2 has its last aircraft up at 21:33 (26:31). WHAT THE DECK CHANGES ALONE DID TO THE WHOLE RUN: nothing, or made it longer (27.9 to 35.2 minutes over the builds of the day before (5) was in): the run ends with elevator 4's last trip, which waits below on the order 16, 15, 12, 8, and that order hangs on when elevator 3's second trip is down again. TRIED AND TAKEN OUT OR LEFT OFF. A tow gives way to a tractor that stands loose only where its own width touches it (CATCC_DEMO_LOOSE_TIGHT): the tight circles follow the tractor's track, and on a push the aircraft is not on it: an aircraft was pushed past a tractor 2.9 m from its centre line. Both aircraft of an after elevator kept to their side of the landing area (CATCC_DEMO_SIDE_ALL; the user: "if we can ensure that El 3 and El 4 tows never cross the LA centreline, then El 3 tugs never have to wait for El 4 tows"): 29.7 / 29.8 / 33.4 / 32.6 minutes at paces 2 / 4 / 8 / 1 against 27.6 / 28.5 / 30.5 / 27.1 without: the only way there is that does not cross is the pull forward and a straight push of 50 to 62 m; a curved way that stays on its side is not built. Every six-pack spot from behind first (CATCC_DEMO_BEHINDFIRST): 30.8 / 30.1 / 31.7 / 27.1. The second aircraft of a hangar trip begun by another tractor while the first is on its way to the platform: never begun: the first's remaining sweep is in its way until the first stands (the hangar's conflicts have no times). The time a circle is held behind a tow, 16 s instead of 22 (CATCC_DEMO_POST=16): 27.4 / 28.0 / 30.9 / 26.8 against 27.5 / 28.0 / 31.7 / 27.0: left at 22 (the aircraft's tail is 22 m behind the tractor's own point). TESTS: 221 pass; the whole-demo test now asks for one front-hitch tow on deck (two before), for a six-pack spot pulled straight to its place within 0.15 m and 2 degrees and none set back, for the sixteen parked within 0.15 m (those not at 0.00 m all six-pack pull-ins), for the opposite-door rule and the last-aircraft claim to have been used. OPEN. In the hangar a tow cannot follow another along the same corridor: elevator 4's last two (116 m and 90 m, 2.6 and 2.9 minutes) go one after the other; with times on what a hangar tow sweeps the last could set off about two minutes sooner. On deck the street between the six-pack row and elevators 1 and 2 is what the forward tows wait for each other in (a tow into Corral 2 along it, a back-in into Sixpack 1 from it, the end of a pull-in into Sixpack 3): what a tow holds is still circles about its tractor's track with times, not the aircraft's own swept shape. A tractor that is for an aircraft is still given another now and then while it stands at the nose place (assign).
- **The same day, after the user's second run (09:31 to 09:45 real time, time accelerated: the deck spotted in 28 minutes of mission time, as the rig had it; ten drivers): IN THE HANGAR A TOW FOLLOWS ANOTHER, and three ways a run could stand for good are closed (rig results; installed with DCS closed; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS).** (1) FOLLOWING (M.followable, M.followFrom, M.followOk, M.followTick, M.FOLLOW; CATCC_DEMO_NO_HFOLLOW: off). Until now a hangar tow could begin only when nothing of what it sweeps touched what any other tow still held, and it was planned against every other aircraft WHERE THAT STOOD AT THAT MOMENT: an aircraft on its way down the hangar to the same elevator was in the way of the next one's plan until it stood on the platform. Now: an aircraft that is under way (hooked on, pulling or pushing) onto a platform that is down is in the plan WHERE IT WILL STAND; the tow may begin if the first 20 m of what it sweeps are clear of all that one still holds (and 1.5 m); once under way it looks 10 m ahead along its own way twice a second and stops short of what the one ahead has not yet given up (a pull is braked; a push is stopped as it is short of a door: w.pushStop), and goes on when 16 m are clear; it no longer follows one whose tow has ended (that aircraft stands where the plan has it; its tractor waits there until this one has passed). The next aircraft of a trip is begun while the one before is on its way if a tractor other than the elevator's own waits at its nose (M.secondBy: the last-aircraft claim of the entry above; CATCC_DEMO_HSECOND: any idle tractor that may help: not tried). In the rig elevator 4's last trip: place 12 sets off 24 to 100 s sooner, behind place 15; place 8 sets off 21:00 instead of 23:54, behind place 12; the platform is up with both at 24:58 instead of 27:01. (2) A SEARCH FOR AN ELEVATOR'S NEXT AIRCRAFT THAT IS UNDER WAY IS FINISHED, NOT BEGUN ANEW, WHEN THE HANGAR CHANGES (CATCC_DEMO_NO_KEEPSEARCH): it goes a step a frame, and the hangar changes every 2 s while a tow there gives up what it has passed: at 8 times speed, a quarter of a second of mission time a frame, no search came to its end while anything moved below. This is most of what 8 times speed lost: 31.7 to 27.6 minutes. (3) THREE WAYS A RUN STOOD (all found with the player's jet on elevator 1, where the deck's timing is another). A tractor told to clear off an empty spot it waited on found its way home in use for two minutes; then the spot was "where it belongs" again, it stayed, and the aircraft whose tow it stood in the way of was left on elevator 4 for good: the two minutes now begin anew each time it is found in the way (shoo), and after 30 s with no way out and no place aside it sets off for its own place through what the others hold (CATCC_DEMO_NO_HOMEFORCE). A tractor by itself that was stood down out of a ring of three set off the same second by the same way and was stood down again, once a second for an hour and a quarter: it now steps aside first, and where a tow of the ring has its aircraft on the bar (or the second time in 150 s) it goes through what the others hold (CATCC_DEMO_NO_RINGFORCE). RESULT IN THE RIG, eight variants, all finish: pace 2 25.0, pace 4 25.4, pace 8 27.6, pace 1 25.1, no player's jet with the panel 25.0, active pause 26.0, one planning step a frame 25.3, the player's jet on elevator 1 28.3 minutes (the build of that morning: 27.5 / 28.0 / 31.7 / 27.0 / 27.5 / 28.5 / 27.8 / 28.7; of the night before: 28.3 / 28.1 / 33.6 / 27.4 / 28.3 / 29.3 / 32.8 / 28.6). On the flight deck as before: two tractors 3.3 m, an aircraft in tow 7.7 m from another tractor at the least. In the hangar (the rig measures it there now too): two aircraft in tow 10.0 m apart at the least, centre line to centre line; an aircraft in tow 5.2 m from a tractor that is not its own (5.3 m in a run where nothing followed). 221 tests pass; the whole-demo test asks for one tow that follows. THE USER'S SECOND RUN, performance: 849 s, 15542 frames: 18.3 fps on average, 15 to 21 by the second; frame time 54.6 ms on average, 6.5 to 223.2; 1% low 94.5 ms, 0.1% low 177.1 ms; the hook 2.41 ms a frame on average, its slowest 1% 74.9 ms, 0.1% 167.1 ms; 30 frames left out (the demo making its static aircraft). The hook's slow frames are most of the 1% low at accelerated time: OPEN. OPEN AS BEFORE: the street between the six-pack row and elevators 1 and 2; a tractor given another aircraft while it stands at a nose place; elevator 4's own tractor drives up the hangar after its last tow, through the way the last aircraft comes down.
- **The same day: THE HOOK'S LONG FRAMES (the user's second run, at about twice the speed: single frames in which the demo took 0.3 to 1.5 s: 1484, 765 and 552 ms the worst three; rig results; installed with DCS closed; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS).** MEASURED FIRST: the rig (catcc-dcs/test/demo-run.lua) now times each of its steps with the processor's clock (the runner keeps it as REAL_CLOCK before the test harness's own), lists the twelve slowest with what the log says in them, and with PROFILE=true samples which function is running every 2000 VM instructions, in the planning coroutines too (PROFILE_LINES: by line; PROFILE_STACK: by the chain of callers). What it showed, for 37 s of processor time in a 25 minute run: 20% in routeNearest and 20% in insideDeck (both the hook's: a tow driven in the head asks them at every tenth of a second of its way), a fifth in the way-finder, and single steps of 1.0 to 1.3 s where a hangar tractor looks for its way after a tow (hangarHome: up to nine way-finder runs across the hangar that find nothing, and the tows driven in the head after each that does) and 0.3 to 0.4 s where a deck tow is planned. (1) THE SAME ANSWERS, CHEAPER (crewtest_hook.lua: every run in the rig is the same run as before, to the second). routeNearest looks only at the legs between lo and hi, the first found by halving, with no function call in its loop (it looked at every leg of the route every time); routeAt finds its leg by halving; insideDeck has a grid of 2 m cells over the outline, filled in as it is asked: a cell the outline does not run through answers at once, the others by the test as it was; the way-finder stamps a disc with no function call a cell. Processor time of a run: 37 s to 22 to 27 s. (2) PLANNING SPREAD OVER FRAMES (M.sliced, M.pause, M.SLICE; CATCC_DEMO_NO_SLICE: as it was). A long piece of planning runs as a coroutine, a turn a frame, and gives up the frame before a planner call (`within`) or at the loops inside the heavy planners (M.pauseIn: each width of the hangar's way-finder in hangarFront and hangarApproach, each line tried in axisTow, each side of the six-pack's way from behind, each way of the hook's planSpot through Tow.pauseIn) once the demo has had its share of the frame: 12 ms by LuaSocket's clock (M.SLICE.ms). The rig and the tests have no such clock: nothing is spread there unless asked for by the count (CATCC_DEMO_SLICE_CALLS: planner calls a frame), so a run there stays the same run every time. Spread: a hangar tractor's way after a tow (hangarHome), each step of the search for an elevator's next aircraft (hsearch; what was asked before the tractor's way was looked for is asked again if frames passed: the tractor still idle, the aircraft still to come, the platform as it was, the tow's room free), a deck tow's plan (kept under the deck's state it was begun for) and the six-pack's way from behind. A planner call made from such a piece runs without pcall so that it can sleep inside; whoever gives a piece its turn finds the planners' surroundings, the depth of planner calls, the way-finder's switches and M.tried as they were (a piece is given its turn from inside the tow's own planner call: counted as a call inside a call it never slept, until that was put right). At the start the log says which: "planning is spread over frames: 12 ms of each frame, by the clock", or that there is no clock. RESULT IN THE RIG. Nothing spread (as the rig runs by default): the eight variants as in the entry above: 25.0 / 25.4 / 27.6 / 25.1 / 25.0 / 26.0 / 25.3 / 28.3 minutes; the slowest step 1.0 to 1.3 s. Spread, three planner calls a frame (the rig has 4 frames a second, so this is far harder on it than 12 ms at 18 frames a second): all eight finish: 25.7 / 28.4 / 30.1 / 25.4 / 25.7 / 26.7 / 26.2 / 30.1 minutes; the slowest step 105 to 178 ms (386 ms once); two tractors 3.3 m, an aircraft in tow 7.7 m from another tractor, in the hangar two aircraft in tow 9.0 m at the least. 221 tests pass (the deck tests read the hook's outline up to `local insideDeck`). NOT DONE: one way-finder run across the whole hangar that finds nothing is still one piece (30 to 50 ms in the rig); the give-way pass (8% of the processor time) and the tows' own driving are not spread. What DCS makes of the 12 ms is not known until a run: the next log has the frame costs.
- **The same day, after the user's review of the footage of his second run (rig results; installed with DCS closed; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS): the tugs twice as fast, six-pack spots from behind, tugs by themselves kept clear of whole aircraft, the right of way in the hangar.** The user: "at 2:36 (in my 2x run), the forward tug on el 1 is waiting for the tow to the corral to complete entirely before it pull off of el 1 to take its aircraft to the six pack via a left turn and reverse. Why? The ENTIRE area around the six pack is clear. It should be taking a right turn off the elevator and pulling the aircraft into its spot on the six pack from behind in a counter-clockwise turn"; "If we are parking aircraft in the sixpack it means the LA is closed"; "The final tow out of the hangar waits far too long. It waits for the penultimate tug to return all the way to its parking spot before towing ... Aircraft under tow have right of way, and it's up to tugs not actively towing to avoid them"; "At 06:02 ... a tug on el 2 is waiting for a pushback onto the corral to complete before pulling off the elevator ... There's also no reason for it to be doing a push back onto the six pack ... A tug drives through that aircraft just moments after, so it seems like maybe its pathfinding was stale? Again, tugs need their own route network for traversing the deck without a towed aircraft that always keeps them out of parking areas and the major tow paths"; "I'm comfortable increasing all tug speeds by 2x (including during forward turns) and 1.5x while pushing an aircraft backward and turning ... the ramp up and down to and from those top speeds (which should be gradual)"; "Don't leave any 'Still open' items this time." WHAT HIS LOG HAD: at 2:36 the six-pack aircraft's way was the street in front of elevator 1 (left turn, back in), which the corral tow also used: it gave way 69 s, until that one was parked. At 6:02 the after aircraft's tow off elevator 2 had been planned before the forward one's tractor came to its nose, through the place that tractor then stood on: the plan was stale, as he thought. (1) SPEEDS (crewtest_hook.lua TOW): speed 1.4 to 2.8 (turns too), fastTow 2.1 to 4.2, fastSolo 4.2 to 8.4, a push that turns 0.7 to 1.05, a straight push 1.05 to 2.1; accel 0.5 to 0.7 and brake 0.8 to 1.1 (my numbers). What is driven IN THE HEAD keeps the old pace, acceleration and braking (TOW.simSpeed, simAccel, simBrake): with the new ones in the head the one clear way to Stbd 1 was refused (a push whose bar comes within a degree of folding) and its aircraft was towed, after three minutes, through another's wheels. The times a tow's circles are held: 5 s before and 12 after (8 / 22), its end 10 (18); a tow gives way 6 s short (8). (2) A SIX-PACK SPOT FROM BEHIND WHENEVER THERE IS SUCH A WAY (140 m at the most); the backed-in way only when there has been none for a minute; a way from behind that is only in use is waited for hooked on. Each end of the row is tried with the wide berth and then with the plain one before the other end. Where the row and the corral are full the street leaves no way from behind for the hook's clearances (Sixpack 4 and 5 off elevator 2 late in the run): those are backed in. (CATCC_DEMO_NO_BEHINDFIRST.) (3) A TOW IS PLANNED CLEAR OF WHERE THE OTHER AIRCRAFT OF ITS PLATFORM HAS ITS TRACTOR HOOKED ON, whether one is there yet or not (plan.vt; CATCC_DEMO_NO_VTUG); both then hook on at once and neither is sent off. Not also of where that tractor waits before the platform is up: the road in front of the platform runs through that place. Where there is no such way (said in the log): as before. A tow that hooks on at once is not put off for where another tractor is at that moment, nor for the place the other aircraft's tractor is about to wait on. Once a tractor hooks on it holds a disc where it stands, not its way to the nose. (4) A TRACTOR BY ITSELF KEEPS CLEAR OF THE WHOLE AIRCRAFT (tail and tailplane 5.0 m, wings 5.9 m, nose 3.2 m about the centre line; it kept 3.4 m from the wheels and drove under noses, wings and tails); where there is no such way, once more clear of the wheels only, and the log says so (in the rig: none in seven of eight variants, one in the eighth). It pays 2.5 times for a cell within 9.5 m of where an aircraft stands or will stand on a deck spot and 1.5 times for the 26 m in front of a platform, not within 16 m of where it is going; the landing area costs it 1.2 (2.2). It no longer waits on empty deck spots (CATCC_DEMO_SPOT_STAGING): junkyard, pocket, or in front of its elevator. This is the cost field and the lanes of the roads, not a network of its own drawn by hand: SAID. (5) TRACTORS AND AIRCRAFT. A tractor that is for an aircraft that is up, or will be within half a minute, stays that aircraft's (assign). An aircraft that waits for another to be out of its way has its tractor at its nose meanwhile. The second aircraft of a platform has its tractor there with the first (DISPATCH.second 35 to 0; lead 6 to 12). A tractor that finds no way to the nose from where it stands drives to the place in front of the elevator first. Its way to a place it backs into is tried with 8 m more of the line it comes along, and from the other side ("no way there: it would not come out of its turn in line": 3 to 8 times a run before, none now). One tractor at a time drives out through what the others hold. (6) IN THE HANGAR. The tractor that has put an aircraft on a platform stays there while the trip's next aircraft is on its way with another tractor, or has one waiting at its nose (four minutes at the most; CATCC_DEMO_NO_HROW). A tow may be followed from the moment it hooks on; the follower needs 12 m clear (20). (7) THE LONG FRAMES, the rest of them: a deck tractor's way to its aircraft's nose is worked out once for the deck as it stands (it was worked out for each way the tow was tried, up to five times in a frame: 2.1 s) and spread over frames, as are a deck tractor's own ways (to a place, home after a tow); the way-finder gives up the frame every 1500 cells inside one search. The give-way pass is within the 56 to 125 ms the slowest step of a run now takes with planning spread. RESULT IN THE RIG, eight variants, all finish, 24 of 24 up, 16 parked (12 with the player's jet on elevator 1), none towed "not clear": pace 2 19.5, pace 4 17.7, pace 8 20.2, pace 1 17.7, no player's jet with the panel 19.5, active pause 20.5, one planning step a frame 19.1, the player's jet on elevator 1 17.7 minutes (before this entry: 25.0 / 25.4 / 27.6 / 25.1 / 25.0 / 26.0 / 25.3 / 28.3). With planning spread over frames (three planner calls a frame): 19.4 / 19.8 / 26.0 / 18.2 / 19.4 / 19.4 / 17.9 / 19.2; the slowest step 56 to 125 ms. Two tractors 3.0 m apart at the least (one driving out through what the others hold past the tractor of a tow that stood waiting: not touching; else 3.3 m, side by side in the junkyard); an aircraft in tow 5.7 m from another tractor at the least (that one ahead of its nose, not under it); in the hangar two aircraft in tow 8.6 m, in line. At pace 1: elevator 1's first trip: the corral tow begins the moment the platform is up, the six-pack tow (round the forward end and in from behind) 32 s later, when its tractor is there; the platform starts down 50 s after it came up (3 minutes before). 221 tests pass; those that measured speeds, wheel turns counted from quarter-second looks, the tractors' waiting on empty spots, a front-hitch tow and a tractor called back were changed to what is now so. FACTS LEFT AS THEY ARE: six deck tractors serve up to eight aircraft that come up within two minutes of each other (elevators 1 and 2 with 3 and 4): an aircraft then stands on a raised platform with no tractor free for it (229 s in all in the pace 1 run of 1062 s, 150 of them at elevator 4); the rig at pace 8 has two seconds of mission time a frame and is harder on the demo than DCS at 8 times speed.
- **The same day: EIGHT TRACTORS ON THE FLIGHT DECK, more tractors in the hangar tried, and the plan for the demo on its own thread (rig results; installed with DCS closed; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS).** The user: "add an 8th tug to the flight deck and evaluate whether any additional tugs in the hangar would be valuable with our new logic. The goal is to get complete deck spotting down to 15 minutes. Lastly, I want you to map out the plan for moving the logic onto its own thread like we discussed, assuming it is still reasonable to believe this will improve performance. You don't have to implement in this run, I'd rather get the 8th tug test in my hands sooner". READ AS: the flight deck's tractors from six to eight (there were six, not seven). (1) WHERE THE EIGHT STAND (crewtest_demo.lua DECK_HOMES8; CATCC_DEMO_SIX: the six of before): six in the junkyard, 3.2 m apart from 66.0 to 82.0 m aft (the first 0.87 m from the island's inboard face, the last 0.86 m from elevator 3's forward edge), and two in the pocket between the island and elevator 2, one behind the other. Tried and not kept: seven in the junkyard 2.64 m apart (0.84 m between two tractors' sides): a tractor backing in is then in what its neighbours hold: 204 times one was told to clear off in a run, two of four variants did not finish, the one that did took 24.4 minutes. (2) THE TWO OF THE POCKET (M.pairHome). The pocket is 6.2 m wide: a tractor cannot pass one that stands on the place in front. Which place is whose is settled each time one of them goes there: the first home takes the deep place, the other the place in front, and does not set off before the first stands (tug.homeHold). One that stands in front with nobody behind it backs straight into the deep place (soloWay: half a metre forward, then straight back; park.noArc: the way round by the line the place is backed into from begins too near to it). The one that is shut in gives what it is for to the one in front, if that one is for nothing or for something that is ready later (assign). Before these: the last aircraft of a run was the shut-in tractor's, the one in front had nothing to do, and the run never ended; and the one in front could not make room, so the other never came home. (3) A PLATFORM IS NOT KEPT UP BY A TRACTOR ON ITS OWN PLACE IN THE JUNKYARD, nor by the last 8 m of its way into it (the junkyard's last place is inside the metre the test keeps about a platform: elevator 3 stayed up for good for the tractor that stood there). (4) A TOW DOES NOT WAIT FOR A TRACTOR BY ITSELF THAT ITSELF STANDS GIVING WAY TO A THIRD (giveWay; CATCC_DEMO_NO_TOWPAST): the second tow off elevator 4 had started behind the first; the first's tractor, its aircraft parked, stood on its way home for 113 s for a tow off elevator 3, and the second tow stood 179 s on the platform for it. The wider rule (a tow before any tractor by itself, CATCC_DEMO_TOWFIRST) stays off. With six tractors neither (3) nor (4) changes a run: the four variants run again with CATCC_DEMO_SIX are 19.5 / 17.7 / 20.2 / 17.7 minutes, as before. (5) DRIVERS: catcc-t10 and catcc-t11 (groundcrew-patch.lua: catapult crews 3 and 4, ten members each then; twelve drivers for twelve tractors). They are in DCS only once director-test.ps1 -Spawn Spots -Crew Model has been run again (it writes into the DCS install, elevated): NOT RUN in this session (the session's permissions refused it). Until then DCS has ten drivers: the deck's eight tractors are seated first, two of the hangar's four drive without one. RESULT IN THE RIG, EIGHT DECK TRACTORS, all sixteen runs finish, 24 of 24 up, 16 parked (12 with the player's jet on elevator 1), none towed "not clear": pace 2 21.0, pace 4 18.9, pace 8 19.6, pace 1 19.1, no player's jet with the panel 21.0, active pause 22.0, one planning step a frame 22.6, the player's jet on elevator 1 18.0 minutes (six: 19.5 / 17.7 / 20.2 / 17.7 / 19.5 / 20.5 / 19.1 / 17.7). With planning spread over frames: 20.4 / 20.1 / 28.9 / 18.5 / 20.4 / 21.4 / 18.2 / 19.3 (six: 19.4 / 19.8 / 26.0 / 18.2 / 19.4 / 19.4 / 17.9 / 19.2); the slowest step 61 to 120 ms. Two tractors 3.2 m apart at the least (two standing side by side in the junkyard); an aircraft in tow 8.0 m from another tractor at the least (with planning spread 7.9 m, and 5.1 m once at pace 8); in the hangar an aircraft in tow 5.3 m from another tractor and two aircraft in tow 10.4 m at the least. 221 tests pass (the demo's tests ask for twelve tractors and twelve drivers). SO: EIGHT ARE NOT FASTER THAN SIX IN THE RIG (0.3 to 3.5 minutes slower in seven of eight variants, 0.6 faster at pace 8). An aircraft stands on a raised platform with no tractor for 390 to 609 s of a run in all (six: 466 to 670 s), and the time goes elsewhere: (a) the deep tractor of the pocket made 0, 2, 2 and 3 tows in the four paces: it is shut in whenever the other stands in front; (b) with a tractor for every aircraft, elevators 3 and 4 come up together and their four tows all begin within 20 s: Port 2 and Port 3 are backed into from the starboard side of the landing area, Stbd 1 and Patio 1 from the port side, so the four cross each other aft and go one after another: the last stood 154 s on elevator 4 (pace 1), and elevator 4 went down 2 minutes 56 s after its last tow began. With six, elevator 4's aircraft waited 75 s for tractors and so came after elevator 3's. MORE TRACTORS IN THE HANGAR (CATCC_DEMO_POSTS, pace 2, eight on deck): four 21.0 minutes; six 20.0; eight (the plan's four other posts taken too): did not finish: the tractors on those posts stand where the first tows need room (elevator 4's first aircraft: "no way for hangar tractor 4 to it: there is no room for the tractor to back round where it stands"), elevators 2 and 4 never got a first load; a second tractor for a trip's other aircraft (CATCC_DEMO_HSECOND) with four 27.4, with six 21.7. Why more do not help: at the end of the run with four, three of them stand idle (from 10:13, 12:09 and 14:01) while the last three aircraft wait in a chain: elevator 3's own tractor has 525 on its bar from 12:11 to 16:23 (2 minutes 44 s of that standing at the door, the platform up), and takes 637 only after; 514 cannot be drawn out before 637 is gone, 577 not before 514; elevator 4's last two are on it at 20:27. The limit there is the rule that an elevator's aircraft are its own tractor's, and that one aircraft waits at a door: not the number of tractors. WHAT STANDS BETWEEN 19 AND 15 MINUTES, by these runs: the four tows of elevators 3 and 4 crossing aft (about 3 minutes on elevator 4, which ends the run), and the chain of the last three aircraft in the hangar (elevator 4's last load is on 4 minutes 16 s after the platform is down). THE USER'S DCS SESSION OF 12:51 TO 12:55: three missions of a minute or less, the demo not started: "nothing of ours on the deck": fps 120.0 average (81 to 180), frame time 8.3 ms average (1.0 to 22.6), 1% low 16.1 ms, 0.1% low 20.4 ms, the hook's share 0.03 ms, nothing left out. No run of the demo with planning spread over frames is in the log yet. THE THREAD: catcc-handoff/WORKER-THREAD-PLAN.md (plan only, nothing built). The demo and the tow library run whole in a second Lua state on a worker thread of catcc_crew.dll, as they run whole in the rig; the hook sends a snapshot of what the demo reads each frame and applies the DCS calls the worker asks for; no DCS function is called from the worker. Checked for it: DCS's lua.dll is plain Lua 5.1 and exports all 49 functions asked for (luaL_newstate, lua_close, lua_next and the rest). What it buys, by the user's two runs: the long frames, and more planning per second; not the average frame rate (the hook's share was 1.71 and 2.41 ms of 50.6 and 54.6 ms).
