- **The same day: THE RIGHT OF WAY: A TOW BEFORE A TRACTOR BY ITSELF; a tow's own push line; the hangar's tractors begin hooked on (rig results; installed with DCS closed; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS).** The user, after his run of 14:09 (11 minutes at real time, the mission ended with the demo running): "There is a deadlock that developed around 12 minutes into the last run ... The tug parking the port-most aircraft on the fan tail took forwever, and the tug on el 3 waiting to park its aircraft on the starboard-most spot on the fan tail stood waitin for it (even though their paths don't intersect as long as the 1st tug takes a route the avoids its path after disconnecting. The starboard fantail to also blocked the 3rd tug waiting on el 3 to take its aircarft to the patio. Again, an unecessary wait, as the starboard fantail tow was already well out of the way at that point, and once disconnected, the tug without a tow should give way to the tug with a tow. Overall the run looked much better, but I want you to try to iron out these issues with robust logical fixes (rather than hardcoded hacks)." And while this was worked on: "Also, to speed up the first tows out of each elevator, spawn the hangar tugs attached to their first aircraft to be towed." HIS RUN'S PERFORMANCE (planning spread over frames by the clock, 12 ms: said in the log): fps 34.6 average (23 to 45), frame time 28.9 ms average (7.0 to 108.1), 1% low 48.0 ms, 0.1% low 66.3 ms; the hook's share 1.67 ms average, its slowest 1% 27.0 ms, 0.1% 57.3 ms; left out as test-only 58 frames (the longest 89 ms). No frame of 0.3 to 1.5 s any more. WHAT HIS LOG HAD. Deck tractor 4 towed 777 off elevator 4 to Port 5 (parked 14:16:20) and then stood at its nose, unhooked, for 30 s with no way home that was clear of what the tows at elevator 3 held. Deck tractor 1, with 725 for Stbd 1 on its bar at elevator 3, had begun behind it: it stood 38 s until 777 was parked, went on, and stood again 84 s for tractor 4 where that one stood; tractor 2, with 571 for Patio 1, stood behind tractor 1 for 77 s and again 34 s until 725 was parked; tractor 4, once it had a way, gave way to tractor 2: a ring. The ring was broken by standing tractor 4 down; it then found no place aside for 40 s. THE SAME IN THE RIG AT REAL TIME (the standard run): a tow to Patio 2 stood 100 s on elevator 3: 33 s for the tow before it and 59 s for that tow's tractor, which stood unhooked at the nose of Patio 3 waiting for a way clear of what the waiting tow held. Found with a new switch for the rig, CATCC_DEMO_YIELD_LOG (every 10 s: whom a standing job gives way to, and the two circles that decide it), and the scratch tool waits.py (seconds a tow stood for a tow, a tow for a tractor by itself, and the other ways round). THE RULES NOW (crewtest_demo.lua; each has its switch): (1) A TOW STOPS FOR ITS OWN PUSH LINE AND SPOT WHEN IT COMES TO THEM (M.needAt, c[11]; CATCC_DEMO_NO_NEEDAT). They are held from 15 s before the push begins so that nothing is planned into them; giveWay stopped the tow 6 s before THAT: with a pull of 17 s at the pace the tractors now drive, where it stands hooked on. So the second aircraft of a platform, and a tow to the spot beside one being pushed into, did not begin until the other was parked. (2) THE END OF A PULL THAT STAYS HELD FOR THE PUSH IS ITS LAST 24 m (M.TAIL: where the aircraft lies when the push begins), not its last 10 s (28 to 42 m since the speeds were doubled); and what a push has left behind is given up as it goes, in the tight lists too (CATCC_DEMO_NO_TAILM, CATCC_DEMO_NO_TAILGO). (3) THE RIGHT OF WAY (M.rowPair, M.escape, giveWay, `free`; CATCC_DEMO_NO_ROW: as it was). Between a tow (from the moment its tractor sets off for the aircraft until it has unhooked) and a tractor by itself: the tractor by itself gives way; the tow is never held, nor put off, for what such a tractor HOLDS (its way, the place it stands on); the tow stops only for the tractor ITSELF, where it is, if what the tow comes to within 10 s touches it. A tractor by itself does not stop for a tow INSIDE the way that tow has still to come to (stopping there is standing in it), nor for what a tow holds that itself stands for a tractor: there it stops only for the tow itself (its tractor, its aircraft on the bar) and for what the tow is on now or comes to within 6 s. (4) A TRACTOR BY ITSELF THAT STANDS IN A TOW'S WAY LEAVES AT ONCE (M.escape): one that has unhooked and has no way yet, one that stands giving way to another, one that stands idle. Its next way is asked for the same moment and it does not wait for its lane: the way round what the other jobs hold is taken however long, to where it was going, else to open deck aside. The tow that waits for it is, to it, only what stands there (standsFor), so it leaves through that tow's way; everything else that is held it keeps clear of. A PLACE ASIDE FOR IT: where there is none by the rules for a place to wait at (at the stern there is none: landing area, spots still to be filled, the fronts of elevators 3 and 4), any open deck clear of what the jobs in hand hold (M.asideLoose). TRIED FIRST AND TAKEN OUT: straight out through what the others hold: at real time one such tractor passed 2.3 m from a tractor that was backing onto its bar. (5) A TOW HOLDS ITS TRACTOR'S WAY ONTO THE BAR (from where its way to the aircraft ends to where it stands hooked on: 6 to 11 m, backwards) until it pulls; its way to the aircraft is given up when it pulls (it was held until the aircraft was parked); a tow that hooks on at once is a tow like any other to a tractor by itself (CATCC_DEMO_NO_HOOKTRACK). Found by the new rules' other timing: a tractor by itself drove across behind a tractor that was backing onto its bar, 0.5 m from it: nothing had ever held that stretch. (6) THE HANGAR'S TRACTORS BEGIN HOOKED ON TO THEIR FIRST AIRCRAFT (M.preHook; CATCC_DEMO_NO_SPAWNHOOK): at the start a tow of the hook's own is made for each tractor that stands at the nose of its elevator's first aircraft and driven in no time as far as 'hooked'; it rests until that tow begins, which takes it over and has only to push. THREE OF THE FOUR: elevators 1, 2 and 3. Elevator 4's tractor has no place at a nose in the hangar plan (a tractor standing there from the beginning would be in the way of elevator 3's first tows): it starts at its post as before. TRIED AND LEFT OFF (CATCC_DEMO_LOWERDRAW): drawing an elevator's aircraft towards its door while the platform is still on its way down (21 s): the first loads are on sooner (elevator 3's first at 0:49 for 1:01, elevator 4 up at 3:33 for 4:27), but more aircraft are then up at once than six deck tractors take, the whole took 15.8 to 17.9 minutes with it for 16.4 to 17.8 without, and with elevators 3 and 4 beginning together two aircraft in tow came within 1.4 m of each other in the hangar once. RESULT IN THE RIG AT REAL TIME, minutes (before this entry in brackets): his jet on a bow spot 16.7 (17.7), no player's jet with the panel 16.7 (17.7), his jet on elevator 1 17.1 (17.4); with planning spread over frames 17.3 (18.2), 17.3 (18.2), 17.6 (17.9). In the standard run a tow stood for a tractor by itself 24 s in all where it had stood 59 s at a time; at the stern, in the run that has the user's case (planning spread), the tow off elevator 3 stood 78 s for the tractor that had unhooked at Port 5; then 38 s; now it does not stand for it 30 s at any time (the 38 s: the tractor that had unhooked beside it at Port 4 held the place it had stood on, and the line it backed out along, until it was home: a tractor that has delivered gives those up once it is 8 m off: letGo, c[12]), and no ring formed in any of the sixteen runs. All sixteen finish (the accelerated ones are run to find stalls: none; at 8 times speed they take longer than before: 25.6 minutes for 20.2, with planning spread 38.2 for 26.0), 24 of 24 up, none towed "not clear"; two tractors 3.3 m apart at the least; an aircraft in tow 7.9 m from another tractor at the least at real time (5.8 m once at 8 times speed); in the hangar an aircraft in tow 5.2 m from another tractor and two aircraft in tow 6.3 m at the least. 221 tests pass (the start test asks for the three tractors hooked on). FACTS LEFT AS THEY ARE: tows still stand for tows whose swept ways cross (33 to 45 s at a time: the second aircraft of elevator 3 for the first while that one is pushed into the patio spot beside its own way); between the variants at real time the whole differs by more than a minute for small differences in timing, so one run does not show a rule's worth.
- **The same day, evening: THE USER'S RUN OF 18:01 WITH THE RIGHT-OF-WAY BUILD (read from the log; nothing changed in the build).** The user: "Just finsihed a run, the timing was roughly correct. Have a look and let me know if you see anything unexpected." TIMING: started 18:01:18, "the deck is spotted" at 18:19:12: 17 min 54 s at real time (every elevator move took its 19 / 21 s by the wall clock); the rig's run of the same build with planning spread: 17 min 23 s; every milestone within 10 to 50 s of the rig's. The last tow was parked at 13:15; the 4 min 39 s after it are the hangar bringing the four aircraft that stay on elevators 3 and 4, one after the other (525 from 11:42, 637 from 13:44, 514 from 14:38, 577 from 15:20; pushes of 45, 72, 58 and 117 m tail first; each waits for the one before to be out of its way): the rig has the same 4 min 53 s. So the whole is decided there, not on the deck. THE RIGHT OF WAY: no ring, no job stood down, nothing forced, none towed "not clear"; tows stood for tractors by themselves 60 s in all and never 30 s at a time; at the stern 777 was parked on Port 5 at 7:22, 725 on Stbd 1 at 9:31, 571 on Patio 1 at 10:12, and the tractor that had unhooked left the tow's way the same second (three times in the run). Tows stood for tows 193 s in all, 34 to 45 s four times. The three hangar tractors began hooked on; elevator 2's stood hooked on to 711 for 3 min 15 s (711 cannot be pushed until 771, of elevator 1's second load, has left: the plan's order), elevator 2 down and empty until 3:53. FOUND: A TOW LET GO AND STOPPED AGAIN EVERY SECOND FOR 20 s (18:10:29 to 18:10:48; 26 lines; the rig has it in every run, 13 to 36 lines, and the run of 14:09 had 19): the tow of 571 for Patio 1 stood at the end of its pull for 725, which was being pushed into Stbd 1. giveWay's swap ("stands in its way where it gave way: it goes on, and the other waits for it from now on") sent it on each pass; the next pass put the pushing tow first again, because a tow that is pushing into its spot is never the one that gives way (M.fixed), and stopped it. On screen: that tractor with its aircraft creeps and stops, a few centimetres at a time; in the rig at pace 2 it came 14 m further along its pull that way. Clearances were kept all the same (rig: an aircraft in tow 8.7 m from another tractor at the least). TRIED AND TAKEN OUT AGAIN (the build is the one that was run): no swap when the first is fixed. Twelve rig runs: the lines gone, all finish, the whole the same or 10 s shorter (16.7 / 17.4 / 16.7 / 17.4 / 17.2 / 17.7 min at real time), clearances the same; but the tow then stands 14 m further back for 32 s, and in five of the twelve the pushing tow's 3 s stop for it (the rule for two tows that stand in each other's way, which counts circles the pushing tow has long passed) shows as "WAIT FOR EACH OTHER IN A RING and none can be stood down", which the whole-demo test forbids. A right fix is two rules, not one: no swap with a tow that cannot stop, and "stands in its way" only for what the other has still to come to. Not done: the user asked for a look. PERFORMANCE of the run (1147 s, 18333 frames): fps 16.0 average (8 to 18 by the second); frame time 62.5 ms average (5.1 to 928.7); 1% low 90.5 ms, 0.1% low 158.7 ms; the hook's share 2.09 ms average, its slowest 1% 38.1 ms, 0.1% 77.0 ms; left out as test-only 22 frames (the longest 197 ms). Before the demo, the same mission: 105.4 fps (65 to 180). The frame rate fell to 14 the moment the demo's aircraft were made and was still 17 at the end with nothing moving and the hook at 0.4 ms a frame: about 58 ms of each frame is DCS's own with the demo's objects on the ship. The run of 14:09 had 29 ms a frame (34.6 fps) with the same objects, the run of 09:31 54.6 ms (18.3 fps): the log does not say where the camera was, so the difference is not explained. dcs.log: the display's audio device was unplugged and plugged in again at 18:11:05 to 18:11:13 and 18:16:08 to 18:16:46 (the monitor dropping out); the frames of 146 ms and 929 ms fall there. A CORRECTION to the entry before: the run of 14:09 was not at real time for its first minute: its four elevators came down in 9 s by the wall clock, not 21 (the mission's clock ran about 2.3 times as fast until about 1:00, then 1:1): its early times cannot be set beside this run's.
- **The same day, night: THE DECK SPOTTING DEMO ON ITS OWN THREAD (built; rig results; installed with DCS closed; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS).** The user: "Complete the multi-threaded implementation" (the plan: `WORKER-THREAD-PLAN.md`, whose section 0 now says what was built and where it departs). THE GATE (the plan's step 0), from his run of 18:01: the hook's slowest 1% of frames 38.1 ms, 0.1% 77.0 ms: over the 20 ms the plan set, so the thread is to buy the long frames as well as planning time. It cannot buy the frame rate: that run's 62.5 ms a frame was DCS's own (the hook 2.09 ms). WHAT IT IS. `catcc_crew.dll` (crewtest-19) makes one thread with a Lua state of its own and two queues of strings (worker_start / post / poll / wait / stop / state; in the worker's state CATCC_W.recv / send / clock / pending / arg; the module pins itself in memory once a worker has been made: Lua's loadlib frees a library when its state closes). `Scripts/CATCC/crewtest_worker.lua` (new) runs there: it puts stand-ins in place of everything of DCS's (the native module, DCS, Export, net, lfs, package.loadlib, require, loadfile) and loads `crewtest_hook.lua` and with it the demo a second time, WHOLE, as the test rig does outside DCS. Nothing of DCS's is called on that thread. The hook on DCS's main thread (Tow.Thr) no longer runs the demo: each frame it applies the worker's list of commands in order, answers its calls, and sends it the frame: the mission's clock, real time, active pause, and every value it reads that has changed. NO CALL SITE WAS CHANGED (the plan's bridge `B` at every call): the demo and the tow library reach DCS through five tables, and those are what is stood in for. In the hook: the log, `info` and `say` go to the main thread when it runs on the worker; its frame there runs only the catapult crews, the walk queue and legs, the ship's commands, the doors, the platforms, the tows and the demo. THE CALL RECORD first (the plan's step 1; one whole run of the rig, every call across the boundary counted): about 25 functions; in a frame of the rig (a quarter second) objarg 41 times, staticpos 14, shiparg 13, the mission's clock 8, place 5, anim 4, orderpeek 4, state 3.5, the mission's scripting state 2, shipcmds 1, the camera 1. From it each call's kind: a COMMAND (staticpos, place, objarg and shiparg written, forcepos, a catapult order held every frame, text for the screen): queued, applied on the main thread's next frame; a READ (the clocks, shiparg and objarg read, state, anim, orderpeek, shipcmds, deck, the camera, the player's aircraft, where the ship and the jet are): asked for once, then sent with every frame in which it changed (20 to 24 values in a run), let go after 600 steps unused; a CALL (everything else: making the statics, a scan, an elevator's command): sent and waited for. FOUND ON THE WAY, each by the rig: (a) the tow reads the nose wheel's steering argument back every step: 17,932 waits in a run: a model argument of an object of ours is now remembered on the worker as last written; (b) the first command for an object was waited for, so that what DCS answered was known: in a free run a tow that began stood every place still for five frames: it is now waited for only while the demo makes its objects (the main thread waits for the worker then anyway); in a step it is taken to have worked, the main thread sends back what DCS answered, and a failure fails every call after it; (c) the worker had no clock until its first frame: a demo begun two hours into a mission would have seen two hours pass in its first step: the clocks go with every command (rig: MT0=7200: the same run, two hours on). PLACES BETWEEN LISTS (the plan's 4.5, done on the main thread from the worker's last two lists, not sent with each pose): an object that moves is shown where it is one frame behind the mission's clock; with the worker's list on time nothing is done; with a list late it is moved on to there by what it moved between the last two steps (its place only; not over 12 m/s; 3 frames at the most), and what a step no longer places is put back where the worker last had it. PLANNING (the plan's 4.4 asked for 25 ms a step): 2 ms of each step, so that a step's places are not kept waiting, and the demo's tick is run again between the frames, 8 ms of planning at a time, while planning is unfinished and no frame waits: planning has what is left of a core. IN THE RIG (the real DLL and its thread; the harness is DCS's main thread; THREAD=true): each frame waits for the worker's step (CATCC_DEMO_LOCKSTEP), so a run is the same every time. All sixteen standard runs on the thread end at the same second as on the main thread (16.9 to 38.7 minutes as before), and at real time the demo's log is the same LINE FOR LINE (0 of 974 lines differ; the tractors end on the same places). With its commands applied a frame late, as in DCS ('late'): the same log. With one frame in five not sent and every list held back 0 to 3 frames (CATCC_DEMO_DISORDER, seeded): it finishes (17.2 min for 16.9), an aircraft in tow 8.7 m from another tractor at the least as in order, two tractors 3.3 m, in the hangar 5.5 m; a tractor's farthest move between two frames of the rig 5.3 m where its speed gives 1.8 m in order (a list three quarters of a second late at 8.4 m/s; DCS's frames are a tenth of the rig's). FREE-RUNNING, as in DCS (FREE=<ms>: a frame of the rig takes that long by the real clock, nothing waits for the worker, its planning goes by its own clock; no two such runs are the same): at real time (17 minutes each) the demo finished in 16.7 minutes with the user's jet on a bow spot and in 17.9 with it on elevator 1 (on the main thread, in the rig: 16.9 and 17.2 with planning in one frame, 17.4 and 17.7 with it spread over frames: no gain in spotting time is shown); at four times real time in 17.1. In each: an aircraft in tow 8.7 m from another tractor at the least, 5.2 m in the hangar, two tractors 3.3 m, no error; of some 4,000 lists of commands 51 to 65 came late and 300 to 530 places were moved on meanwhile; a tractor's farthest move between two frames 1.5 to 1.9 m (1.8 in order). Planning between the frames: 730 to 1,310 pieces, 9 to 17 s of processor time in a run. The worker's steps there: 4.0 to 7.9 ms on average with the waits for the main thread's answers in them (up to a quarter second each in this rig; a frame in DCS); the longest 1.5 to 1.8 s at real time; one step, the first after the start, waits for 13 answers; of the later ones one waits for 4 and thirteen for 2 (the lockstep run's count). The mission ended and begun again in the same process, the demo started again, the mission ended with it running: the worker stopped with each mission, a new one with the next demo, no error. THE MAIN THREAD'S SIDE, timed in the rig: 0.09 ms a frame reading and sending the frame, 0.06 ms applying the commands (the rig's stand-in calls with it); the worker waited for about 480 calls in a run, nearly all while the demo makes its objects. TESTS: the module's self-test (the thread and its queues: 100 starts and stops, 10,000 messages each way in order, a second start refused, a stop while busy not waited for, a crash in the body ending the thread and not the process); smoke.py through DCS's lua.dll (the same with a Lua state: an error in a step, an error that ends the script); 222 tests pass (one new: the thread against the main thread line for line, late, disorder, a late start, the missions' ends, active pause). vitest still reports its own "Timeout calling onTaskUpdate" after the whole-demo tests, as it did before this work (they block its worker for minutes). NOT BUILT: the plan's step 6, the binary buffer applied by the DLL: the main thread's side is 0.15 ms a frame in the rig; its cost in DCS is the 'demo' part of the hook's "frame cost" line. FACTS: (1) while the demo runs on its thread the hook's other tools on the main thread (the panel's elevator and bubble buttons, the catapult crews' commands) keep their own state: one of them used during the demo and the demo's own keeping of the same thing act against each other; (2) the recorder's lines for the demo's people are no longer in the log (the people are known to the worker's copy of the hook); (3) if the worker's thread ends by an error, what the demo had on the deck stays until the mission is restarted (said on screen). THE SWITCH: `demoThread = false` in `Config\CATCC-test.lua`: the demo on DCS's main thread, as before (install.ps1 keeps that line). The same happens by itself if the worker cannot be started (said in the log). WHAT THE USER WILL SEE: the demo starts and runs as before; the log says "demo thread: started" and "demo: on its own thread: its planning has 2 ms of each step and goes on between the frames, 8 ms at a time"; every performance line ends with the worker's side (its steps' times, how many lists came late, how many places were moved on meanwhile); the hook's share should be a fraction of a millisecond with no long frames of its own. TO CHECK IN DCS (none of it can be done from here): a whole run at 1x; time acceleration 2x and 4x; active pause on and off; the demo stopped mid-run and started again; the mission ended mid-run; a second mission in the same DCS session; DCS closed with the demo running.

CAUSE 1: A QUESTION WITH THE CAMERA'S PLACE IN IT. The demo's tick asks which carrier it is at and where it and the jet are (Tow.Deck.ctx): a line of script for the mission's scripting state with the camera's world coordinates in it. On the worker that was a read: asked for once by its text, then sent every frame. In DCS the camera's place is another number every frame (the ship steams), so the text was new every frame: each was waited for (one frame of DCS's for every step, and more when a frame came in during the wait and the place changed again: 1.35 waits a step), and each was then asked again by the main thread every frame for the 600 steps a read was kept: 650 to 830 calls into the mission's scripting state every frame, 0.07 ms each. In the rig the stand-in camera stood still. CAUSE 2: A COMMAND KEPT AGAINST DCS. The hook keeps the two catapult bubbles down for the demo; DCS puts its own command back, and the hook gives ours again four times a second ("given again (1000 times so far)" for each bubble in his log, as on the main thread before). On the worker each was a call that was waited for: some 2,500. In the rig the stand-in ship kept the command. THE FIXES. (1) Where the ship and the jet are is ONE VALUE THE MAIN THREAD WORKS OUT (its own Tow.Deck.ctx for the frame: `h.deckctx`), sent when it changes; the worker's Tow.Deck.ctx takes that. (2) A bubble's or an elevator's command given again to keep it (the native call's fourth argument) is a command, queued, not waited for. (3) A read nobody has asked for in 3 s is let go (600 steps before: a minute of them), and never more than 400 are kept. (4) THE WORKER SAYS IN THE LOG when one function is asked 150 different things in 20 s ("what it is asked changes all the time", with the last question), and for a minute does not keep that function's answers (each is still waited for, but the main thread is not left asking DCS all of them again every frame: tried in the rig with fix (1) switched off (CATCC_DEMO_NO_DECKCTX) and the threshold at 40 for the rig's four frames a second (CATCC_DEMO_CHURN): the line is in the log with the question, 4,631 waits remain, 22 values are read every frame, the main thread's side 0.06 ms a frame); every performance line now ends with the calls it waited for while the demo ran and the values read every frame, BY FUNCTION: a third cause would be named by the next run's log. (5) The worker's start (the hook and the demo loaded on its thread while the main one waits) is left out of the performance figures with the making of the aircraft. (6) A model argument that DCS would not let be read the first time is not readable later either (the worker gave back what it had written itself): his log has six lines "the jet's argument 2 read 0.011 where 0.000 was written: something else writes it too" at 00:12:30, when a hangar tow that was unhooking and the deck tow that had begun wrote the same nose wheel; the main thread never says that in the same situation (the run of 18:01: none), so there the read presumably gives no number (ASSUMED from that: the log does not say what it gives). With it, before the fixes, the rig showed his run: 12,621 waits (8,096 for the bubbles, 4,074 for the question with the camera in it), 671 values read every frame, the main thread's side 8.6 ms a frame with the rig's stand-ins. THE CHANGES (crewtest_demo.lua and the hook; each has its switch): A, OFF THE PLATFORM FIRST (CATCC_DEMO_NO_OFFFIRST): a hangar tractor that has put a trip's LAST aircraft on a platform goes on from the bar straight away from the aircraft, at twice its backing pace, until all of it is 1 m clear of the platform (M.offAt; the hook's 'moveup': w.offAt), and asks for its way on only there. The platform goes up when every tractor's own discs are 1 m clear of it (was: the point 15 m from the centre line). Not for a trip's first aircraft: that tractor stays at its aircraft's nose while the second comes (the right of way of 2026-11-19): tried for every aircraft first: it backed into the way the second was pushed in by, which came 2 minutes 15 later. Rig: the platform up 0 to 1 s after the tractor was off ("goes 6.0 to 7.0 m straight off the platform"). B, A PLACE BACKED INTO IS HELD AS A PASSING UNTIL THE TRACTOR IS IN (CATCC_DEMO_NO_PARKPASS): its quarter circle, its line and the end of its way are held until its backing is done (and 2 s); only the place and its tail for good. A tractor by itself may then follow another (`free`: behind) if it would stand no longer than 15 s behind it, and never one that itself follows a third: with no such limit the rig had one stand 42 s behind a tractor that stood behind a third, and its 73 m took 2.5 minutes. C1, THE END OF A PULL GIVEN UP WHICHEVER WAY THE PUSH TURNS (CATCC_DEMO_NO_TAILSWEEP): a circle kept of the pull is given up as soon as neither the tow as it is now nor what it still holds of its push is on it (was: only behind the tractor along the straight line from where the push began to the spot). **2026-11-21: dynamic spawn: no Lua step on the server; dynamic spawn templates are not slots.** The user asked whether a dynamic spawn could be put on a CATCC spot before the aircraft exists. A probe hook (catcc-dcs/deck-test/spawn-probe/CATCC-spawnprobe-hook.lua) looked for DCS's Scripts/GeneratorHelper.lua function createClientAircraftDataServer in every Lua state a hook reaches, and wrapped it if found: in the host's own dynamic spawn and in a second PC's (two runs, 2026-11-21) it was never there and never called; the server only logged "client[n] occupied unit 1000001". The joining client's DCS builds the aircraft (natively, from DCS.create_client_aircraft in its GUI state), so the server has nothing to rewrite; whether the CLIENT can choose the spot is the open question: the native builder (lua-MissionGenerator.dll) names parking / parking_id among its fields, read or written unknown. A second probe (CATCC-parkprobe-hook.lua) wraps DCS.create_client_aircraft in the GUI state, adds parking / parking_id for a free terminal DCS would not take first, and logs where the jet stands. NOT YET RUN. AND: the mission editor's dynamic spawn templates (a group's dynSpawnTemplate) were counted by the slot picker as slots (the user's run: "5 slots on carriers", all on parking 1, colliding on one spot). DCS still lists them as slots; under the rule that a slot is only ever an aircraft CATCC has spotted, the picker leaves them out and refuses taking one ("a dynamic spawn template, not a slot", in the chat). **2026-11-21: the client cannot choose its dynamic spawn's spot either.** The parking probe (catcc-dcs/deck-test/spawn-probe/CATCC-parkprobe-hook.lua) wrapped DCS.create_client_aircraft in the GUI state and added parking = parking_id = a free jet terminal far from the bow (31; an earlier try asked for 48, not a jet's kind). DCS took the request (it answered true) and spawned the Hornet on the forward-most bow spot each time (three spawns over two runs: 162.4 m forward, 9.3 m to starboard, to the decimetre). The fields are ignored: neither server nor client has a Lua step that picks the spot. What is left: move the jet after it spawns (ForcePosition, proven on a player jet), or make DCS's spawn points the CATCC spots (director-test.ps1 -Spawn Spots). Also seen: Airbase:getParking() gives a ship's terminals' vTerminalPos in the SHIP'S OWN frame (x -153.05, z 18.48 for one), not in world coordinates.

**2026-11-21: spawning a player INTO a spotted aircraft: the objective, and every route looked at.** The user rejected moving the jet after it spawns ("players spawning inside other players and immediately exploding"; "the player's camera will be jerked from one location to another. This can be unsettling for VR users") and set the objective: the player joins, sees the picker with the aircraft already spotted and selects one; he is put into a Client aircraft at that location "without any visible teleporting or risk of collision with other aircraft or static objects"; other players "only ever see a brief moment where the static aircraft that was 'holding' that spawn location disappears before being replaced with the client aircraft"; he can release the slot (the static replaces the Client aircraft) and release the reservation; "I do not care what kind of creative/hacky shenanigans go on in the background". WHAT THE DESK RESEARCH FOUND (nothing here run in DCS yet): (1) DCS hands a carrier's spawn spots out in a fixed order and skips a blocked one: "Spawn point can be blocked by players, AI, or static objects", a client gets "the next available spawnpoint", none free: "Your flight is delayed" (Hoggit wiki, DCS editor carrier spawns, written for 2.7.17); our three dynamic spawns all stood on spot 1 (Bow stbd 1: 162.4 m forward, 9.3 m to starboard), the first of the 40 deck spots director-test.ps1 -Spawn Spots installed. So the spot can be chosen by leaving it the only open one. DCS's slot screens ask DCS.checkMultiplayerSpawnConflict(takeOffType, ship or airdrome id, isAirdrome, unitId, type) whether a spot is free. (2) Flight.dll EXPORTS the air traffic control's own handling of spawn spots (woATC, wsBasicTerminal; the DLL's code is packed on disk, so what each does is known by its name only): wsBasicTerminal::acquire / free / is_free / is_free_for_me / set_acquire_order / get_client_id; woATC::setClientSpawnOnTerminal(unsigned), setClientSpawnOnTerminalByPoint(unsigned, Vec3f), setClientSpawnOnFreeTerminal, acquireFreeShipSpawnTerm_, getForcedTerminalByClient_, isPointObstructedForDynamicSpawn, haveFreeTerminalForDynamicSpawnClient, updateTermObstructions, getTerminalList, AddTerminalPoint, tryPlaceClientsInShipTerminals, and the network handlers procRequestTerminalForSpawn / procTerminalStatus (a spot is ASKED FOR and its state sent round: the choice looks like the server's). They can be called from the native module as it calls the others. (3) coalition.addGroup cannot make a Client aircraft (Hoggit wiki, DCS func addGroup): no slot can be scripted into being. (4) A dynamic spawn's request carries the aircraft's identity: type, livery, board number, fuel, payload (DCS.create_client_aircraft, GUI state), so the picker can ask for the very aircraft CATCC has spotted; the spot is the only thing it cannot name. (5) Airbase:getParking gives a ship's terminals in the SHIP'S frame: 31 with parking routes (Term_Index 1..31), the 9 spawn-only spots 32..40 with Term_Index -1, 8 of another kind (41..48). THE ROUTES: (a) dynamic spawn with every other spot blocked, by objects (a spotted aircraft blocks its own spot; an unobtrusive object on each empty one) or by the ATC's own acquire / order calls; (b) the same steering for mission slots; (c) the ATC told the spot directly (setClientSpawnOnTerminal...); (d) a spot's place rewritten, or a terminal added, before each spawn; (e) moving the jet after it spawns: rejected by the user. THE FIRST TEST: catcc-dcs/deck-test/spawn-probe/CATCC-steerprobe-hook.lua, on the server, in the mission 'Deck Spotting Test - dynamic spawn' only: an object on every one of the 40 spots (our tiny CATCC_AxleProbe, then DCS's tractor, then a static Hornet: which kind makes DCS call the carrier busy), then one spot opened (Port 3, the one with the most room, never spot 1), then the user's own dynamic spawn: on the open spot or not. Run in the Lua rig with stand-ins. NOT YET RUN IN DCS.

**2026-11-22: objects do not choose a dynamic spawn's spot; a mission slot on every spot; the static goes before the slot is taken.** The steer probe's runs: (1) DCS.checkMultiplayerSpawnConflict answers "a free spot" with a static Hornet on every one of the 40 spots (Airbase:getParking too: 48 available): it counts live aircraft only. (2) One static Hornet on spot 1: the dynamic spawn stood on spot 5 (Bow stbd 6), not in the static, but spots 2 to 4 (up to 59 m from it) were passed over as well. (3) Static Hornets on 39 spots, Port 3 open: DCS's dynamic spawn screen offered no F/A-18C at all (helicopters only). So for a DYNAMIC spawn a static blocks far more than its own spot: on a spotted deck dynamic spawn is all but shut, and objects cannot pick the spot. THEN mission slots: the mission copy 'Deck Spotting Test - slot per spot' has a Client Hornet on each of the 40 spots (parking 1..40). The user's run, the demo's 16 aircraft on deck (positions from the Tacview recording): the slot of spot 5 (Bow stbd 6) came up on spot 5; the slots of spot 15 (Point 4) and spot 33 (Stbd 2) both came up on spot 7 (Patio 2). The picker took the slot first and the static was only taken away once the slot was the player's (S.track, up to a second later), while DCS makes his aircraft the moment the slot is his: the likely cause (not proven): the aircraft's own static still stood on the spot. NOW the picker's Spawn takes the static away and reserves the aircraft first, and takes the slot 3 s later (SPAWN_PRE); a slot not taken after all: the static is made again. (Also seen: the first Spawn of that run, 10 s after Spot Deck, was refused "no aircraft spotted there": the demo's report was more than 5 s old while it started.) NOT SEEN IN DCS.
## 2026-11-23: the slot picker puts a player's aircraft on its own spot (spawn aiming)

- **The rule found (ATC probe, runs 1 to 9; `catcc-dcs/deck-test/spawn-probe/README.md` on branch `task/atc-probe`):** DCS gives a new aircraft the deck spawn spot with the lowest number in play (from 5 up, nothing standing on it), whatever the slot's parking number, and the aircraft appears at that number's route start. Numbers 1 to 4 are given only at mission start.
- **Aiming:** just before the picker takes a slot it puts every number before the slot's spot out of play (the native module's `aim`: number + 100 through `woATC::procInitTerminals`; the nine spawn-only spots' numbers, which no record reaches, are written directly in the terminal object) and puts them back (`restore`) once an aircraft stands on the spot, when one comes up elsewhere, or after 90 s. Mode `up` only: `low` (every lifted number 4) froze DCS once and missed twice with mission slots.
- **Pre-check:** before the slot is taken the picker asks DCS whether the spot is clear (`check`: `woATC::isAdrTermObstructedFor` and the terminal's own state), for up to 8 s after the static went; not clear: the Spawn is refused and the static comes back. DCS would otherwise put the aircraft on another spot, whatever stands there (the run of 2026-11-22: damage and deaths).
- **Not in a mission's first 60 s:** DCS froze twice at an aimed spawn made 16 and 23 s after the mission loaded, never at one from 29 s on. Five data points; the cause is not known.
- **The first four spawn routes are DCS's own (the user, 2026-11-23: "The first four spawns should be untouched ... 'Alert 7' locations"):** `spots.gen.ts` keeps DCS's routes 1 to 4 (to the four catapults) in front of the plan's 40, so deck spot n is DCS's spawn route n + 4 (`SPAWN_OFFSET`), every deck spot can be aimed at, and the hand-off lists are 44. The picker tells which routes file is installed by the ship's terminal count. A spot whose DCS number is below 5 is refused.
- **The demo's two aircraft that stay on an elevator** are named by the platform place their solved tow parks them on (they stood on each other's spots on elevators 2, 3 and 4, so the picker took the wrong static away).
- **Spot All** (the picker, beside Spot Deck; the user: "an alternative deck spot button that spots all statics in their positions"): every aircraft on its deck spot at once, none in the hangar, nothing towed.
- **Tractor drivers on a server:** DCS makes the ship's people only once a player is in an aircraft on it, so a demo started from the slot screen found none; it now looks again every 5 s until every tractor has its driver, and sends the catapult crews away then.
- **Still the probe's DLL** (`catcc_atcprobe.dll`, loaded by the picker for `aim` / `restore` / `check`); a small module of its own is the worker's recommendation, not built. Only the host aims: a remote client's Spawn, a dedicated server and two players at once are untested.

## Deck spot names: port/starboard first, numbered by nearness to the catapult (2026-11-23)
The user: port/starboard comes before bow/quarter ("port bow", "starboard quarter"); the four aft spots are the Fantail (FT1, FT2 ...); "If a grouping of slots has a logical sequence to the nearest catapult (or the catapult those locations would normally be dispatched to) number them by proximity"; the short ids change with the names.
- **The rule.** A spot's name is its area and its place in that area ("Stbd bow 1", "Port quarter 1", "Fantail 1"); its short id the area's two letters and the same number (SB1, PQ1, FT1; elevators E11 ... E42 as before). Within an area, 1 is the spot whose route to its catapult (the one DCS's spawn table sends it to: `spotCat` in `catcc-dcs/deck-test/spots.gen.ts`) is shortest, by the unrounded length of that route (`buildSpawnRoutes().len`). `spots.test.ts` checks it for every area.
- **Nothing moved.** Every spot keeps its number (DCS's route order, the missions' parking numbers and "Spot n-1" slot names), place, heading and catapult. `director-spots.lua` and `crewtest_routes.lua` are what they were with the names mapped; the demo's flights, boards, elevator destinations, tows and clearances are the same spots under the new names.
- **The fantail mixes two catapults.** Its starboard pair were "Stbd" (catapult 3) and its port pair "Port" 4 and 5 (catapult 4). Catapults stay per area (`AREA_CAT`), with the fantail's four given their own (`SPOT_CAT`: FT1, FT2 catapult 4; FT3, FT4 catapult 3). The fantail is numbered across both by its route length to each spot's own catapult. FT1 (110.94 m) and FT2 (111.01 m) round to the same 111 m; the unrounded lengths decide, so no tie rule was needed.
- **The mapping** (old id / old name -> new id / new name, spot number, route length to the catapult):

| Spot | Old | New | Catapult | Route |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | BS6 Bow stbd 6 | SB1 Stbd bow 1 | 1 | 67.5 m |
| 4 | BS5 Bow stbd 5 | SB2 Stbd bow 2 | 1 | 79.2 m |
| 3 | BS4 Bow stbd 4 | SB3 Stbd bow 3 | 1 | 92.2 m |
| 6 | BS3 Bow stbd 3 | SB4 Stbd bow 4 | 1 | 106.3 m |

| 14 | PT3 Point 3 | PT2 Point 2 | 1 | 54.4 m |
