  Unchanged, their order already by nearness: Sixpack 1-6 (35.1 / 53.2 / 60.7 / 70.2 / 79.7 / 89.1 m to catapult 2), Corral 1-2 (68.3 / 80.5 m, catapult 1), Patio 1-3 (105.2 / 114.3 / 125.7 m, catapult 3), El 1 (35.9 / 45.8), El 2 (93.2 / 104.3), El 3 (94.4 / 100.5), El 4 (44.6 / 56.6).
- **Where the names come from.** `deck-spots-dcs.csv` names every spot; `deck-spots.csv` (the drawing's) now carries the same name for each number, as does `deck-spots.lua`. The DCS file's `was` column keeps the drawing's names of 2026-11-16 in the old scheme (it records what the drawing called each spot before the respot). `nimitz-spots.py`'s fallback areas are the new ones, but it numbers the drawing's spots bow to stern: rerun, it would rewrite `deck-spots.csv`'s names (the DCS file's names still win).
- **What shows the change.** The F10 menu "Tow me to a parking spot" groups areas bow to stern, ten items a group: "Stbd bow, Port bow, Crotch", "Point, El 1", "Sixpack, Corral, El 2", "Port quarter, El 3, El 4, Patio", "Fantail" (the patio moved into the fourth group because the old "Port" area split in two). "Marshal me to a parking spot": "Starboard bow" / "Port bow" (still the first parking route of that area, the same spot as before). Dated comments and the user's words keep the names of their day, with "(now ...)" where that helps.

**2026-11-22: the tractor drivers turn their heads (built, OFF until `install.ps1 -DriverHead on`; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS; the whole account: catcc-dcs/deck-test/crew-test/DRIVER-HEAD.md).** The user: "can you investigate whether it's possible to get the tug drivers to turn their heads while they are driving? If so, while reversing they should turn their heads 90-110 degrees (whatever is realistic for a human) to the right. At other times it would be nice if their heads moved to look where they are about to turn or check the area around them."
  - *Possible, by DCS's own means.* The F/A-18C pilot figure has a look-round clip of its own, `F18_39_Look_LR.fbx` (201 frames, full left to full right, the whole upper body), and the deck crew can play it (it is in their list). From it `crew-test/model/driver_head.py` makes five still poses (head 100 and 50 degrees left, ahead, 50 and 100 right) and a blend space over them, `catcc_bs_drv_look`, with one axis: the parameter `catcc_head_yaw` (degrees, + to the driver's right). A driver sits in that blend space instead of `f18_0_init`; writing the parameter turns his head. Made at install on the user's PC from the user's DCS (DCS's animation data is not kept in our repository), into the test mod's `animations/typeV`.
  - *What a blend space file is, read from animator.dll (not packed; `dumpbin /disasm` on the file, nothing run):* `AnimationManager::_load_blend_spaces` takes the files under `<animations path>/<skeleton>/blend_space/`; each RETURNS a table and the blend space is named after its file; `Animation::load` reads `type` ("gesture" and "additive" are kinds of their own), `x_axis` / `y_axis` = `{ name, default_value }` (either may be absent), then `{ x, y, animation }` entries; the bounds are the entries' least and most. Beside it: `overrides/` and `masks/` (an override animation on some bones only: `mask_name`, `animations`), `graph/`, `states/`, `ik_config/`.
  - *The angle:* backing, 100 degrees to the right = torso 18.5 (all the source clip turns it) + neck and head 81.5 (a neck turns about 80). The clip's own end (112 = 18.5 + 93.5) is more neck than a person has and is not used. More than 100 needs a pose of our own with the shoulders round (not made).
  - *Where he looks (`Scripts/CATCC/crewtest_head.lua`):* backing: over his right shoulder, held through a stop shorter than 0.6 s; driving: towards the point of his way 9 m on (into a turn before the tractor begins it), on a straight a look to one side every 6 to 12 s; standing: a look round every 4 to 9 s. What is random comes from his name. He turns when the tractor begins to back, not before (looking first would mean the tow waits a second before backing: not changed).
  - *The turn itself is the native module's* (crewtest-21: `head(h, degrees [, briskness])`, `headtick()` once a frame): critically damped, 100 degrees in about 0.9 s; the hook only says where a head is to go, when that changes by 4 degrees or more (the whole demo in the rig: 3,374 calls for ten drivers in 16 minutes; on the demo's thread they are commands, not waited for). A step of the Lua for one driver: 0.4 microseconds under DCS's Lua.
  - *Routes not taken:* the stock clip played as it is (a sweep in time: a held look needs a loop range in the playing record, which only `Animation::apply` with a record of our own sets); an override with a bone mask (one pose at a time, not an angle; worth it for a figure that also walks); bone matrices through `CharacterModelCookie::set_node_matrix` / `set_direct_pose_control` / the IK solver (exported, wholly undocumented: several runs, each able to crash); a draw argument (nothing ties one to a bone); a look-at of the animator's (none exported).
  - *Guessed, for the run to show:* that DCS lists `blend_space/*.lua` from OUR mod's mounted animations path as it lists our clip (run 7) and the F/A-18C module's graph. If not: "Head: right 100" says NOT TURNED, the drivers sit as before, and the next route is an override or a graph file.
  - *Tests:* `catcc-dcs/test/driver-head.test.ts` (13: the look logic, the calls made of the module, the poses read back and read by DCS's own Assimp, the blend space file); the native self-test (10 more) and smoke test; in the hook's rig a tow with the head on (seated in the blend space; 92 to 100 degrees whenever the tractor has backed for a second, never over the shoulder while it drives; into the turns; the menu's items; both ways of being off), and the whole demo with the heads on, on DCS's thread and on its own: line for line the run without, but for the head calls.
  - *To try:* DCS closed, `native\build.cmd`, `install.ps1 -DriverHead on`; in the mission F10 > Tow > Driver seat > "Head: right 100 (as when backing)": every driver turns head and shoulders to his right in about a second and holds it; then the demo.

## Slot picker: the carrier in the view behind it (2026-11-23; built, rig results; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS)

The user: "Is it possible to get the game to show the F9 (external ship view) on mission start instead of the F10 map? Can the camera be positioned and angled such that the carrier is framed in the bottom half of the view, while our interface is shown in the top half?" (his picture: the picker at the top centre, the ship broadside from off its starboard side and above, bow to the right as in the picker's deck picture.)

- **What it does.** While the picker is up and the player is in no aircraft (no slot in multiplayer, no unit of his own), the camera is put beside the carrier the picker shows (`S.carriers[S.carrierIx]`), every frame, so it rides with the ship: off the starboard side, 33 degrees above the horizon as seen from the ship's middle, level (the ship's heading only: the horizon stays level while the ship rolls), the ship's centreline along the screen's width with the bow to the right, the hull centred in the part of the screen below the picker's window. Another carrier's tab moves it there. Given up (nothing put any more, no view command sent, the camera left where it is) when the player is in his aircraft (DCS's own cockpit view), when the picker is put away, when DCS's own slot list is asked for, and at the mission's end; it stays between the picker's Spawn and the slot being taken. The picker's window first stands at the screen's top centre (24 pixels down) instead of 60, 120.
- **The numbers** are one table at the top of `Scripts/CATCC/crewtest_slots.lua`, `VIEW` (`on = false` switches all of it off, the window's place included): `elev` 33, `side` 1 (starboard), `fill` 0.72 (the ship's length as a part of the screen's width), `fillV` 0.85 (the hull's box takes at most that part of the free height: the camera stands further off on a low, wide screen), `minFree`, `fov` 78 / `fovAxis` 'h' (taken when DCS gives no field of view), `setFov` (nil: the field of view is left alone), the hull (`aft` -164, `bow` 168, `beam` 40, `deck` 20: PriFly's deck outline), `cmds` { 13, 16 }, `settle` 30 frames, `tol` 5 m, `tolDeg` 2, `after` (nil; 15 sends the view back to the map when the view is given up outside an aircraft). The camera is worked out from the screen's size (`dxgui.GetScreenSize`), the field of view (`DCS.getCurrentFOV`, read every frame: a zoom moves the camera and the picture stays) and where the window's lower edge is at that moment (the window dragged: the ship follows): at 2560 x 1600 with 78 degrees it stands 288 m from the ship's middle, 167 m up and 242 m to starboard, looking down 24 degrees.
- **By which calls, and what of them is known.** (1) *The camera read and put:* `Export.LoGetCameraPosition` / `Export.LoSetCameraPosition`; both are in the hooks' `Export` table by DCS's own `API/Sim_ControlAPI.md`. Reading is seen in DCS (the deck tool's lock, the director camera). Putting is not: the panel's "Saved view" uses it and no run ever pressed it (there is no `Logs/catcc-camera.lua`). (2) *Off the map:* `Export.LoSetCommand(13)`. `LoSetCommand` is seen in DCS from this hook (view command 2007 turns the cockpit camera). 13 is `iCommandViewNavy`, the command the F9 key is bound to (`Config/Input/Aircrafts/common_keyboard_binding.lua`); the numbers are not in any Lua file of this install: they are each name's place in `bin/Input.dll`'s own list of command names (0 `iCommandNull`, 7 `iCommandViewCockpit`, 8 `iCommandViewAir`, 13 `iCommandViewNavy`, 15 `iCommandViewAWACS` (the map), 16 `iCommandViewFree` (F11), 51 `iCommandQuit`, 52 `iCommandBrakeGo`, 68 `iCommandPlaneGear`, 72 `iCommandPlaneFlaps`: the last four agree with the numbers long published for them). Not seen: that a spectator's view under the slot screen answers the command, which ship F9 goes to, and whether the ship view keeps a camera put from outside (it follows its own ship). (3) *The ship:* the mission script's `CATCC_CR.ctx` by the ship's name (the hook's `getCtx`, handed to the picker as `api.shipCtx`): seen in DCS (the picker's Spot Deck). (4) *The field of view:* `DCS.getCurrentFOV` (DCS's own options screen calls it and `DCS.setCurrentFOV`); taken as the angle across the screen's WIDTH, as the in-game panel takes it (`Config/View/View.lua`: `ExternalViewAngleDefault = 78`).
- **So it checks itself, in one run.** Every frame the camera is read before it is put again and set against where it was put the frame before. 30 frames after the view command: within 5 m and 2 degrees, "HOLDS"; not, the next command of `VIEW.cmds` is sent (16, F11's free camera, which follows nothing) and checked the same way; neither holds: one message on the screen, and the camera is left alone for the rest of the mission. `Logs/catcc-crewtest.log`, lines "slots: view:": which `Export` functions are there (also `LoForceCamera` / `LoCreateCameraRequest`, which `Scripts/Export.lua` describes as not released: looked for, not used), the screen, the field of view, the camera before, each view command and whether it raised an error, the picture worked out, the first put, HOLDS / does NOT hold with the distance and angle found, given up and why.
- **What the hook cannot tell:** whether the screen DRAWS that camera. If DCS keeps the map on the screen while the camera reads as put, the log says HOLDS and the screen shows the map: then no view command leaves the map for a spectator from Lua, and what is left is (a) the player presses F9 or F11 himself once (the camera is put from then on), (b) `VIEW.cmds` with another command first, (c) the camera request of `Scripts/Export.lua` (`LoForceCamera` with `name = "CameraNearNavy"` or `"CameraFree"`) if the log shows this install has it.
- **Rig** (`catcc-dcs/test/slots-run.lua` part 19, `crewtest.test.ts` "the slot picker"): DCS's camera stood in for by a view that keeps a put camera only in the views a test names; the ship's ends on one row below the window with the bow to the right, the camera with the ship as it moves, the window dragged, a zoom, another carrier's tab, a carrier that cannot be asked for, given up in the aircraft / for DCS's slot list and taken again, the ship view not holding (16 next), no view holding; the picture at 1920 x 1080, 2560 x 1440, 2560 x 1600, 3440 x 1440, 3840 x 2160 and 1600 x 1200 (the hull's box inside the width, between the window's lower edge and the screen's, centred there).

## The tractor drivers' legs and arms: feet at the pedals, hands on the steering wheel (2026-11-23; built, OFF until `install.ps1 -DriverPose on`; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS; the whole account: catcc-dcs/deck-test/crew-test/DRIVER-POSE.md)

The user, with a picture of a driver from his tractor's left and the wanted limbs drawn over it: "Is it possible retarget the deck tractor drivers leg and arm bone positions so they line up with the pedals/steering wheel better? Can you infer the correct positions from the image (green is left leg, red is right, pink is left arm, orange is right)?"

- **Possible, by keying the seated pose's bones again, not by moving bones while DCS runs.** `crew-test/model/driver_pose.py` solves each leg and arm of the pilot figure's seated pose (two bones and a hinge: hip, knee, ankle; shoulder, elbow, wrist) onto fixed places in the tractor's cab and writes still clips, as `driver_head.py` writes the head poses (which the user has seen play well from our mod, 2026-11-23). The fingers are there (20 bones a hand) and are left curled as the pilot's are round throttle and stick.
- **Where the wheel, pedals, floor and seat are is measured from the tractor's model** (`as32-31a.edm`, read with `edm.py`), not taken from the picture: the wheel's rim is centred 0.171 m ahead of the tractor's position, 0.581 left, 0.840 up, 0.165 m in radius, its top leaning 15.5 degrees forward; the accelerator is the small pedal 0.335 left, the brake the wide one 0.576 left; the floor 0.240 up. The picture gives how the limbs bend: left hand lower on the rim (9 o'clock) than the right (2.5), left foot flat on the floor left of the pedals, right foot on the accelerator, knees up, elbows down and out. All of it is one table, `POSE`, in the tractor's frame; a test holds its cab against the model and its seat against the hook's `TOW.seat`.
- **Departure from "keep the torso as it is": the torso is brought 18 degrees forward and the collar bones 18 degrees forward of sideways.** The figure's arms are 0.54 m from shoulder to wrist; from the pilot's reclined torso the rim is 0.71 m away: with straight arms the hands stop 9 cm (left) and 6 cm (right) short. With the lean both hands hold the rim in all five head poses with bent elbows (45 and 53 degrees facing ahead). The neck is turned back by what the spine came forward, so the head's direction in every pose is exactly what the head poses gave it. The other choices: the seat 5 cm forward with a 12 degree lean (changes the hook's seat for every driver), or no lean and hands short of the wheel (`lean` 0).
- **The switch is its own:** `install.ps1 -DriverPose on / off` (`driverPose` in `Config\CATCC-test.lua`), beside `-DriverHead`: the heads are seen and good, the limbs are not yet seen, and each can be taken out alone. With it the five head poses are made with the limbs (`driver_head.py --pose`: solved for each pose, since the shoulders move with the torso while the hands stay on the wheel) and a plain seated pose `catcc_drv_seat` is made. The hook's `Tow.sit` seats a driver whose head does not turn in `catcc_drv_seat` where the mod has it, else in `f18_0_init` as before; the demo seats its drivers through it.
- **Checked without DCS:** wrists and ankles within 0.1 cm of their places in every pose, bone lengths unchanged, no joint beyond a man's (knees 104 and 109 degrees, elbows 20 to 69 over the poses), the clips read by DCS's own Assimp with hands and feet where they were solved, the grips within 0.3 cm of the rim half way between two head poses, no bone's turns opposite between neighbouring poses. A drawing of the skeleton over the model's lines: `driver_pose.py --svg`.
- **Not known until DCS shows it:** what the skin does at shoulders and hips; whether the feet sit on or in the floor (`sole`, estimated) and the fingers close on the rim (`grip`, estimated); that the thighs, lower now, cross the cushion's front edge (the pelvis is put 0.65 up over a cushion 0.651 up: `TOW.seat.up`, unchanged).
- **Hands following the wheel as it turns: not built.** It takes a second axis on the blend space (15 clips), a second parameter in the native module and a DCS run to show that a two-axis blend space loads. The wheel turns 225 degrees each way; hands can stay on it for the first 30 to 45.

## Slot picker: the static goes at the player's FLY, not at Spawn (2026-11-23; built, rig results; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS)

- **Asked (the user):** "In an ideal world, the substitution would be immediate, so that other players on the deck do not see aircraft vanishing into thin air only to be replaced seconds later. Do you think we can tighten the delay between removing the static and spawning the actual aircraft?" The user chose to try the two tightening steps first (FLY held; the wait after the static shortened), before any overlap trick.
- **Where the gap came from (the runs of 2026-11-23):** the static went at Spawn, the slot was taken 3 s later (`SPAWN_PRE`), then DCS's briefing; DCS makes the aircraft only at the briefing's FLY (`net.spawn_player`, `BriefingDialog.lua`). The spot stood empty 9 to 11 s on ordinary spawns, 90 s once (a slot held in the briefing: no terminal was held for anyone in those 90 s, so DCS chooses the spot when it makes the aircraft, not when the slot is taken).
- **Now, on the host, with DCS's GUI state reached:** Spawn takes the slot at once with the static standing. In the GUI state `BriefingDialog.Fly_onChange` (and its button, which DCS binds to it once, when the briefing is first made) and `net.spawn_player` (every other way into the jet) are wrapped (`CATCC_FLY`): while the hook holds a 2 s lease on the player's own slot, they only note that they were asked, and the briefing stays up. The hook then takes the static away, waits until the mission no longer has a static within 3 m of the spot at flight deck height (with the demo on its own thread the static goes a frame or two after the command), calls the ATC's own pass over what stands on its spots (native `updobs`: `woATC::updateTermObstructions`, new in `atcprobe-11`), waits the ladder's step, runs the clear check and the aim as before, and makes DCS's own FLY. The first-minute rule (`AIM.notBefore`) now waits with the static still standing. A FLY that cannot be made (static not gone in 3 s, spot not clear) puts the static back and stays held, so the next FLY tries again. A slot the host takes through DCS's own slot list is held the same way when its spot's static stands.
- **The wait ladder (step 2, a test):** 1, 0.5, 0.25, 0 s after the static is seen gone. Each FLY that comes up on its spot makes the next one wait the next step; one that comes up elsewhere puts it back a step and stops the ladder for the mission. Each says in the chat how long its spot stood empty and how much of that was the wait, the check and aim, and DCS (with DCS's longest frame: the host stalls while it loads the aircraft). `Config/CATCC-slots.lua`: `settle = <s>` fixes the wait, `settleRefresh = false` leaves `updobs` out.
- **Not changed:** without the GUI state, and for a remote player's slot (his FLY is on his own machine), the static still goes when the slot is taken. A remote player's FLY would need the pilot mod on his machine to hold it and ask the server: not built.

## Held FLY: the first DCS run (2026-11-23, 23:22 to 23:29, hosted "slot per spot", the host the player)

- **Ten held FLYs, ten on their own spots**, none refused, no exception, no "came up elsewhere": Stbd bow, Sixpack, Port, elevator and stern spots (deck spots 2, 20, 32, 1, 31, 27, 38, 29, 34, 5). The wrap landed at 23:22:13 (`net.spawn_player` and `Fly_onChange` both). A FLY at 21 s of mission time was held with its static standing until 60 s.
- **The ladder went 1, 0.5, 0.25 and then 0 s for the last seven:** with no wait at all, the static seen gone 0.01 s after it was taken away (the demo on its own thread), the check and aim 0.00 s, DCS's FLY in the same frame, every aircraft on its spot. `updobs` ran every time ("the ATC refreshed: yes"); whether a 0 s wait also lands without it was not tried (`settleRefresh = false`). DCS's own `isAdrTermObstructedFor` still said obstructed for spot 5 after it (the bow spots, as before: the deck's own check passed it).
- **What is left of the gap is one host stall:** the spot stood empty 5.4 to 6.2 s on the 0 s spawns (8.4 s on the first of the mission), and each time DCS's longest frame was the whole of it: the host does not run a frame from its FLY until the aircraft exists. Nothing in our sequence remains in the gap.
- **Not known:** what a remote player on the deck sees. The static is destroyed in the frame of the FLY, and the host then sends nothing for about 6 s; whether the destroy reaches the other players before the stall (6 s of empty spot) or only with the new aircraft after it (a near-swap) can only be seen from a second machine.

## Held FLY: the wait fixed at 0 s; updobs tested by alternating (2026-11-23)

- **The wait is 0 s** (the user: "Fix it at 0s for now"): `FLY.settle = 0`, the ladder taken out. `Config/CATCC-slots.lua` `settle = <s>` still sets another.
- **Whether updobs is needed, one mission:** `FLY.refresh = 'alternate'` for now: the mission's odd held FLYs call `woATC::updateTermObstructions` the moment the static is seen gone, the even ones do not. Every held FLY logs DCS's own reading of the spot (native `check`: obstructed, wide, object, usable) as the static goes and again after updobs, and says in the chat whether updobs was on. Without it, a spot DCS still counts as blocked would send the aircraft to another spot ("came up on ANOTHER spot"). `FLY.refresh` is to be set to `true` or `false` by what the run shows.

## Held FLY: updobs is not needed (the run of 2026-11-23, 23:47 to 23:50)

- **Eleven held FLYs, all on their own spots, the wait 0 s:** spots 27, 26, 25, 32, 33, 37, 38, 23, 3, 2, 1. The five even ones ran without `updobs` (26, 32, 37, 23, 2) and landed as the others did.
- **DCS's own reading of the spot (native `check`) was the same before and after `updobs` every time**, and already what it would be 0.01 s after the static was taken away: obstructed false, usable true on the sixpack, corral, port, elevator and stern spots; obstructed true on the starboard bow spots 2 and 3 with or without it (the deck's own check passed them, as before, and they landed).
- **So `FLY.refresh = false`.** The call stays in the native module and can be switched back on (`true`, or `'alternate'`).
- The spot still stood empty 5.3 to 6.1 s: the host's stall while DCS makes the aircraft, as in the run before.

## First-spawn test: how early a mission's first spawn can be (2026-11-24; built, rig results; NOT YET RUN IN DCS)

- **Asked (the user):** "Let's try lower first spawn timings to see where the same limit is." The 60 s hold (`AIM.notBefore`) stands on five first aimed spawns of 2026-11-22: frozen at 16 and 23 s after the mission loaded, fine at 23 (taken at 29), 29 and 35 s. With the held FLY the static stays on the spot while it waits, so the hold costs only the player's wait.
- **The series (`FIRST` in `crewtest_slots.lua`, `on` for now):** each mission start runs the next case of 25, 20, 15, 10 s: the mission's first held FLY waits until that much mission time (a FLY after it goes at once, and the time it went is what is written). Later spawns of the mission wait for nothing (no spawn after the first has ever frozen). `Saved Games\DCS\Logs\catcc-firstspawn.txt` gets "case n started" (mission time, real time since the mission started, slot, spot), written and closed just before DCS's FLY, then "landed" / "elsewhere" / "none", or "stopped" if the mission ends between. A start that finds "started" as the last line writes "case n froze" and the series stops there; the hold is 60 s again; deleting the file starts over. The file cannot tell a freeze from DCS ended by hand in those seconds.

## First-spawn test, run 1: void (a second 60 s hold); messages of settled systems off the screen (2026-11-24)

- **Run 1 of the series did not test anything below 60 s.** The held FLY waited to its case's time (20, 15, 10 s) with the static standing, then took the static away; the clear check (`S.aimReady`) then applied `AIM.notBefore` (60 s) again, with the spot empty: 40 to 45 s of empty spot and "the slot is taken in N s" on the screen (the user saw it). Every first spawn of the four starts went at 60 s or later (case 1's FLY came at 65 s anyway), all on their spots. Fixed: the clear check of a held FLY applies no time rule of its own. The run's file is kept as `Logs\catcc-firstspawn.invalid-2026-11-24.txt`; the series starts again at case 1.
- **Messages of settled systems in the log only** (the user: "remove any debugging messages that are for systems we've already settled? Like the CATCC movement with chocks messages"):
  - pilot mod: the chocks' and the salute's comings and goings (in contact with PriFly; PriFly holds / let go of your chocks; chocks request kept; ready call sent; salute held / reported; ground crew chocks in; asking the ground crew). Still shown: no contact, contact lost, the installer to run again, the salute key that cannot be held;
  - crew test: the list of people at mission start (still shown: the memory scan that is about to freeze DCS for a few seconds);
  - deck spotting demo: its start line and its stop line, and "the deck is spotted" unless an aircraft could not be moved or a tow was not clear;
  - slot picker: the held FLY's timing line, unless the aircraft came up elsewhere or not at all.
  - Left as they were: the deck spotting and hangar marks tools' read-outs (answers to the user's own commands), the slot picker's refusals and waits, the first-spawn test's case line, the chock test's and route lines' menus.

### 2026-11-24 — First-spawn series, run 2: 25 to 10 s all landed; chock watch off the screen

- **Run 2 of the first-spawn series** (after the second 60 s hold was fixed): case 1 at 25 s, case 2 at 20 s, case 3 at 15 s and case 4 at 10 s of mission time (15.8 s of real time since the mission started) all landed on their spots, DCS 5.4–6.0 s from FLY to the aircraft. No freeze. The two freezes of 2026-11-22 (16 and 23 s) came before the held FLY. The series now goes on with 5 s and 0 s (0: no hold at all, the FLY goes when pressed); the file keeps cases 1–4, so the next start runs case 5. `AIM.notBefore` (60 s) stays in force for after the series until the user picks a value.
- **Cases 5 (5 s) and 6 (0 s, no hold) landed too** (case 6's FLY at 2.6 s of mission time; DCS 5.9 and 5.7 s). The series found no limit and is done: `FIRST.on = false`, and **`AIM.notBefore` is 0**: a mission's first spawn goes when FLY is pressed. Set `AIM.notBefore` higher again if a first spawn ever freezes DCS; `FIRST.on = true` runs a new series (delete `Logs/catcc-firstspawn.txt` first).
- **The chock test's watch on the Hornet's own chocks is in dcs.log only** (the user: the "moved N m with the Hornet's own chocks on" messages are not needed any more): chocks on, chocks off and movement while on. The PriFly hold ON reply no longer says movement is reported. The menu's replies stay on screen.

## The TD-1A tiedown model (2026-11-24; built, tested in the rig; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS)

- **Asked (the user):** a textured 3D model from references of the Navy's aircraft tiedown (Peck & Hale's drawing 3893 of the 14MTC/1512 assembly, a manual's figure of the tensioner and a hook in a padeye, photographs, the Gloria GR48-205 kit for shape). The request named it the "Universal Wheel Chock", but every reference shows the TD-1A chain tiedown, and the chock is already built (above). It must be "lightweight": surface detail in textures and normal maps. One animation argument sets the chain's length, so that the model can be put between a padeye and an aircraft's tiedown point and the length between them changed while running.
- **Built:** static type `CATCC_Tiedown` in the crew test mod (declared in `Database/db_catcc_crew.lua`). Model and textures from `crew-test/model/tiedown.py` (our own .edm writer; it uses the tow bar's and the chock's helpers): `Shapes/CATCC_Tiedown.edm` and three 1024 px DDS textures in `Shapes/textures` (colours BC1, normal map BC5, roughmet BC1). Pictures and an OBJ copy go to `model/out`.
- **Two arguments, not one.** The spare chain hangs from the pocket to the deck and lies there, and how it hangs depends on how steeply the chain rises. A model turned by the script cannot show that from its length alone. Argument 10 is the length from the padeye to the fitting / 4 m (0.476 to 3.228 m). Argument 11 is the rise from the deck / 90 degrees. The script heads the static level towards the fitting and sets: LEN = |f - p| / 4, ELEV = asin(rise / |f - p|) / 90 degrees. The origin is the centre of the tensioner hook's bend, which is where the padeye's bar sits, on the deck.
- **How the length works (as the real tiedown does):** the chain hook and the chain move out along the line by the length. The chain locks in the pocket by a whole link, and only every other link (one standing on edge), so the body, nut and pocket move along the threaded rod by what is left over a whole two links (0 to 0.064 m). Links on the hook's side of the pocket are hidden in pairs (`ArgVisibilityNode`s on argument 10). The spare chain hangs straight down from the pocket, up to 9 links and only as far as the deck allows (visibility on both arguments). The rest lies in one of four heaps on the deck, chosen by how much is spare. Each heap is a low mound textured with heaped links drawn from above.
- **Sizes:** chain of 86 links, 2.752 m (108 in), pitch 32 mm, 8 mm wire. Tensioner 0.330 to 0.394 m from the hook's bend to the pocket (Peck & Hale: 13.0 to 16.5 in). The link size was measured on the photographs against the padeye and gloves. The tensioner body is yellow (the most common in the photographs; `TENSIONER_PAINT` in the script also has 'red' and 'orange'). The nut, rod and hooks are bare steel; the chain is dark steel with rust. "HOOK END" and "PULL LEVER TO RELEASE" are stamped on the body's sides.
- **Weight:** the tensioner, both hooks and the first two links are 828 triangles. Each other link is 64: a link's shape is geometry, so it cannot go into a texture without alpha cut-outs, which our writer has not tried in DCS. At 1.5 m and 40 degrees about 3,300 triangles are drawn; at the full length about 6,200. Threads, knurling, letters, pitting, rust and the heaped links are in the textures.
- **Untried in DCS:** `ArgVisibilityNode` written by our writer (DCS's own models use it, and its layout is read from them), and a static's arguments 10 and 11 being set by the script. `tiedown.test.ts` reads the written file back and works out its node tree at 112 settings: the hook sits at the length along the rise, the pocket holds the right link, hanging links stay above the deck and the heap lies on it.

## The TD-1A tiedown, rebuilt from the user's review (2026-11-24; built, tested in the rig; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS)

Replaces the parts of "The TD-1A tiedown model" (above) that the user's review changed:

- **The tensioner is at the aircraft, not the padeye** (the user, with photographs of F/A-18s, an SH-60 and tensioners hanging at a fitting, and a sketch). Its latched hook goes into the aircraft's fitting, and the chain's hook into the padeye. The spare chain hangs from the tensioner in loops "looped back up close to the aircraft so it's not bunched up on the deck". The heaps on the deck are gone: the user read them as a pile of dirt.
- **The body is measured on a catalogue photograph** (the user: "excellent reference"; the red tensioners' photograph for detail). It has two cast side plates with a flared jaw round the pocket's pin, a release lever as a raised wedge along the top (two prongs and the "PULL LEVER TO RELEASE" trigger plate), "HOOK END" with its arrow and "FREE END" cast in the plates, three bolts, a large nut with four lugs on the threaded rod, a swivel, and a latched hook. Every outline is kept in the photograph's pixels in `tiedown.py`. The photograph is scaled so that its hook's bend to the pocket's pin is Peck & Hale's 13.0 in. The fitting's bar sits above the rod's line in that hook, so a loaded tensioner hangs 10 degrees off its rod, and the model hangs the same way.
- **The chain's size is from the same photograph:** 9/32 in chain, pitch 22 mm, wire 7.2 mm. That is 124 links (2.728 m) instead of 86 links of 32 mm. The links are 48 triangles each (6 sides round, 4 across).
- **Arguments:** unchanged in meaning. Argument 10 is the length / 4 m, now 0.461 to 3.189 m; argument 11 is the rise / 90 degrees. The origin is still the padeye: the centre of the chain hook's bend. The length is measured to the fitting's bar in the tensioner hook's bend.
- **Few draw calls:** the taut chain is six pieces of 2 to 64 links, shown and placed by the length, since any even count is a sum of them. The spare chain is up to five 24-link loops plus one shorter loop, each one piece, fanned about the vertical and hanging up to 0.28 m. At most 15 pieces are drawn. All 124 links are always drawn: about 6,700 triangles at every setting.
- **Limit:** the loops cannot know how high the tensioner is (each argument works alone). Below a pocket height of about 0.3 m (a short tiedown at a low angle), the lowest loop goes into the deck.

## The TD-1A tiedown: the padeye hook at the fitting too (2026-11-24; built, tested in the rig; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS)

- **Asked (the user):** the tensioner's hook (the catalogue photograph's latched hook) "looks ugly compared to the padeye side": make it look like the padeye side.
- **Built:** the tensioner's hook is now the padeye hook, with the same shape and eye ring and no latch. Its eye takes a ring on the swivel, as the padeye hook's eye takes the chain's first link. The hook hangs on the chain's line. The swivel, rod, nut and body hang from the joint of the eye and the ring, turned 7.8 degrees so that the pocket is on the chain's line.
- **What moved:** the padeye hook and its ring are longer than the photograph's hook, so the swivel, nut and body sit 3 cm further from the fitting. The fitting to the pocket is now 14.2 in at the shortest (Peck & Hale's 'B' is 13.0 to 16.5 in). Argument 10's range is now 0.492 to 3.220 m (still the length / 4 m). The fixed parts are about 1,000 triangles (before: 850), and about 6,900 triangles are drawn at every setting.

## The TD-1A tiedown: thin hinged lever, eight-lug ring, tapered body, finish on argument 12 (2026-11-24; built, tested in the rig; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS)

- **Asked (the user, with photographs of red tensioners carried and hung side on, Peck & Hale's drawing 4823, a bare-metal tensioner and the MB-1 manual figure):** "much better". The release lever is too thick; it hinges on the bolt by the jaw. The tensioning ring should be closer to circular with 8 protrusions. The body's sides taper toward the ring. Can the colour be set by an argument (yellow, red, bare metal)?
- **The release lever** is a thin plate (3.5 mm): two prongs with a slot, narrowing to a waist, then the triangular trigger plate widening to the ring's end, with "PULL & LIFT TO RELEASE" cast on it. It hinges on the first bolt by an ear down each side of the jaw, and lies 3 mm clear of the body at the hinge, lifting to 13 mm at the ring's end.
- **The tensioning ring** is round with eight lugs on its rim (32 points round, smooth-shaded) and "TIGHTEN" with an arrow stamped in the rim twice.
- **The side plates** follow the red tensioners' side view on the catalogue photograph's scale: straight on the lever's side, a narrow neck at the ring, a quick shoulder and a slow flare to the jaw on the other side. Seen from the lever's side they close in from the jaw (38 mm across) to the ring (20 mm).
- **Argument 12 is the finish:** 0 yellow, 0.1 red, 0.2 bare metal; each value holds to half way to the next. The painted parts (side plates, neck, lever and ears) are in the model once per finish, each with its own part of the textures, and an `ArgVisibilityNode` shows one. Red is chipped and worn more than yellow, as the photographs have it. Orange (one photograph) is not in: the 1024 px textures are full with three finishes.
- **Counts:** 28 parts on 35 nodes. The tensioner, hooks and first two links are 1,128 triangles, and about 7,000 are drawn at every setting, in 9 to 11 pieces.

## The TD-1A tiedown: the body from the manual's drawing, the slotted bar, wear that follows the shape (2026-11-24; built, tested in the rig; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS)

- **Asked (the user):** "I think you can do much better", with the manual's line drawing of the tensioner from the side ("basically a perfect 2d profile of the tensioner body") and the photograph of red tensioners from many angles. On the painted versions the paint seemed removed by a noise layer "instead of removing the paint based on where the shape would become worn the most". The threaded bar "is NOT a simple threaded rod": it is a slotted bar with an eyelet on one end, partially threaded.
- **The body is now measured on the drawing**, which replaces the catalogue photograph as the source of every outline (kept in the drawing's pixels in `tiedown.py`, read at 8 times its size from the saved file). Scale: the body, ring to jaw's end, is 0.200 m (twice the ring's diameter in every photograph; 9.5 chain pitches in the catalogue's). That makes it much slimmer than the catalogue trace: the neck is 41 mm deep and the jaw 70 mm (before: 46 and 93). The ring is 0.10 m across.
- **What the drawing and the red photograph show, and the model now has:**
  - Each cheek has a raised rim round its outline, a raised ring round the jaw's pin, a lug under each of the two bolts, and a raised tongue at the neck with a small bolt. "HOOK END", "FREE END" and an arrow are cast on the sunk face.
  - The two cheeks stand 66 mm apart at the jaw and close in to 28 mm at the ring. The jaw's pin and the hinge bolt cross between them.
  - The release lever is a 3.2 mm plate above the cheeks' top edge: a lip turned down to the neck at the ring, the trigger plate as wide as the jaw ("PULL & LIFT TO RELEASE" cast across it, two ribs running into it), then two prongs that run between the cheeks to the hinge bolt. The ears outside the cheeks of the last version are gone: the photograph shows the prongs inside.
  - The chain lock's ribbed housing shows between the prongs and rises over the jaw's top. The taut chain is held there, on top of the jaw, not at the pin. The spare chain goes round the jaw's end (two links) and hangs in loops from between the cheeks.
  - The tensioning ring is rounded towards both faces, with grooves between its eight lugs, a sunk channel round its hub, and "TIGHTEN" with an arrow cast round its face (the photograph has it there, not on the rim).
- **The bar** is flat, 29 mm wide and 10 mm thick, with a slot along it (a real opening), teeth cut in its two narrow edges beyond the eyelet, and an eyelet end. A link joins the eyelet to the hook's eye. The ring has a block of a hub on the hook's side. The round rod and the swivel collar are gone. The bar and body hang 10.7 degrees off the chain's line from the bar's eyelet, which puts the chain lock on the line.
- **Wear follows the shape.** Each painted face works out how far each point is from the nearest raised thing. What stands proud is rubbed bare: rims, the pin's ring, bolt lugs, letter tops, the lever's edges and ribs. The broad tongue keeps some paint. The open middle of a cheek is scuffed through a primer (pink under red, cream under yellow) to the metal, more the further it is from anything raised. Beside a rim or boss the paint is whole and dirt collects. The lever is worn most at its wide end, where it is pulled. Noise now only makes those edges ragged. On the bare finish the same places are rubbed bright.
- **Numbers:** argument 10 is still the length / 4 m, now 0.512 to 3.240 m; the fitting to the chain lock is 15.0 in at the shortest. 29 parts on 36 nodes. The tensioner, hooks and first two links are 1,372 triangles; 7,228 are drawn at every setting, in 9 to 12 pieces. The previews now light every view from the camera's side.

## Folded flag on the glareshield (2026-11-25 .. 26; seen in DCS; done)

- **Asked (the user, with an F-15E photograph):** "add a small folded triangular american flag inside the canopy, forward of the canopy rail, on top of the glareshield ... only to select aircraft through my testing UI for now".
- **The model (crew-test/model/flag.py, CATCC_Flag in the CATCC-Crew mod):** the triangle of a ceremonial fold standing on its long edge, point up, a face to each side, soft rounded edges; the blue union with white stars (rows at 45 degrees, as on a folded flag), plain blue round the edge. Legs 0.19 m (long edge 0.269, point 0.134 up), 0.04 m thick: a full 3 x 5 flag's fold (legs 0.229) would touch the Hornet's windscreen. A static linked to the ship stands on the deck, so its height over the deck, pitch and lean are model arguments 10, 11, 12.
- **Where (crewtest_flag.lua):** the F/A-18C's glareshield beside the HUD on the left, read from fa-18c.edm: 4.40 m forward of the origin, 0.929 up, 0.20 to port, pitched 12 degrees down forward (the glareshield's slope); the windscreen 0.175 m above. Carried with each aircraft's own pitch and roll (asked of the mission). Only the F/A-18C is known; other types are refused.
- **The panel's "Flag" page:** "Flag my jet" / "Flag selected" (the deck page's clicked aircraft) put a flag on or take it off, any number of aircraft; moves and turns (fore / aft, left / right, up / down, turn, pitch, lean) apply to every flag and last until DCS closes; "Report" logs them to become the defaults. A flagged aircraft that is towed gets its flag made anew where it stops; one that takes off or is gone loses it. The panel is 60 px wider for the tab.
- **The slot picker** does not count a flag (`catcc-flag-*`) as a static on the spot, as it does not count tie-down chains.
- **Rounder, stars whole (the user, 2026-11-25, on the preview: "Everything should be a little more rounded and the stars shouldn't get cut off going around the corners"):** the edge's radius in the plane 0.007 -> 0.016 m (the corners as round), the faces puffed 8 mm and rising smoothly from the edge (no crease where face meets edge). The edge no longer has a plain patch: each face's cloth is unrolled round it to the edge's middle line, and the star field is placed where the most stars fit whole short of that line; a star that would cross it is left out.
- **Smaller stars, rows near the long edge (the user, 2026-11-25, with the reference photograph: "The stars should be smaller, and more closely aligned with the long edge of the triangle (not perfectly but about 5-10 deg off")):** stars 0.7 of the scaled flag's (0.033 m across), the rows 7 degrees off the long edge. Whole-star test now measures round the corners as the soft edge unrolls there (the distance to the triangle, not to its sides' lines).
- **Rebuilt from the product photographs (the user, 2026-11-25, with three folded-flag photographs: "Way too many stars ... spaced way too closely, and not aligned correctly at all. Use the provided references to try again. Adjust the shape to better match too"):** shape and stars measured on the square-on photograph (960 x 450 px). Height 0.47 of the long edge (was 0.5), pointed corners lightly rounded (4 mm, the point 8 mm), every edge a fold of radius T / 2 (T 0.032 m), faces puffed 9 mm. Nine stars a face where the photograph has them (one under the point, a row of two plus one half round each leg's fold, a row of four along the long edge), each 0.08 of the long edge in radius. The texture follows the cloth over every fold to the fold's middle, where both faces read the same point of the plane, so a star going round a fold carries on unbroken. 3550 triangles.
- **Stars turned (the user, 2026-11-25: "rotated about 30 degrees counter clockwise", then "another 10 degrees ccw", then "rotate 5 degrees clockwise"):** each star 38 degrees anticlockwise (was 3); their places unchanged.
- **Embroidered stars, turned 0.5 more (the user, 2026-11-25, with a photograph of an embroidered flag: "rotate 0.5 degree ccw, also improve the texture of the stars so they look embroidered"):** each star a raised satin stitch in the normal map: its height the distance in from its outline (0.15 mm at the edge rising to 1 mm), so a ridge runs to every point and a valley to every inner corner, the two halves of each arm lit differently; stitches across from the outline to the ridges 1.4 mm apart; a sewn-in groove and shadow 1.2 mm round each star on the blue; the stars a little glossier than the cloth. Found doing it: the star outline had been drawn 36 degrees round from its own up (the user's turns made up for it), now right, so the same look is a turn of 2.5 degrees.
- **The embroidery as the close photograph has it (the user, 2026-11-25: "The embroidery meets at a point in the center of each star, and the threads run perpendicular to a line from the center of the star to each point"):** each star ten flat facets in the normal map, every arm's two halves, from the centre (1.6 mm up) to its point (0.2) and inner corner (0.25): a ridge out to every point, a crease in to every inner corner, all meeting at the centre. The satin-stitch threads lie across each arm, square to the centre-to-point line, 1.4 mm apart.
- **Seen in DCS: the user's place, 20 % larger (the user, 2026-11-26: "The model's position is good where I reported it, but I think it should be scaled up by about 20%"):** the default glareshield point is the user's Report (4.270 m forward, 0.879 up, 0.322 to port; turned 188, pitch 7, lean -45: the Report said -64, but the model's lean stopped at 45, so -45 is what was seen). The model's pitch and lean now reach 90 either way. Every size of the cloth 1.2 x (long edge 0.323 m, point 0.152 m up, 0.038 m thick); the stitches and groove stay thread-sized.
- **Shadow and levels of detail (the user, 2026-11-26: "The flag doesn't cast any shadow on the jet. Is it possible? Please also create LODs"):** no cause found in the files: its material's shadow flag is the one DCS's own props and the Hornet use (1), the user's settings draw object shadows (shadows 4, terrain object shadows default), and DCS's .lods files carry nothing about shadows. The one difference from DCS's own statics: ours stands on the deck and is lifted, pitched and leaned 2.7 m up to the glareshield by model arguments written from the panel, and the shadow may be drawn without them (at the deck, under the jet). So the model now sits in the user's pose with its arguments at 0 (2.68 m up, pitch 7, lean -45; the arguments move it from there): if the shadow ignores the arguments it now still falls from the glareshield. NOT YET SEEN IN DCS. Four levels of detail (CATCC_Flag.lods: 3400 triangles to 6 m, 884 to 20, 238 to 60, 24 to 200, nothing beyond). Seen in DCS (the user, 2026-11-26): the levels of detail fine; still no shadow on the jet, which the user takes as an engine limit and leaves there ("we can leave this project here").

## Brake riders: brown shirts in the cockpit of towed aircraft (2026-11-25; tests; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS)

- **Asked (the user):** "Please add brown shirts to the cockpit of aircraft being towed. They should spawn into the cockpit just before the tug arrives. They should remain in the cockpit until the tug is clear. They should look around while in the cockpit. The canopy should remain open. They should place their hands on the "towel rack" / grab handles around the canopy frame."
- **Who:** nine brown shirts, `catcc-b0` .. `b8` (crew data patch, mode `model`): three in each of catapult crews 2, 3 and 4, filling them to the twelve crew 1 is seen to work with. That makes nine riders for ten tractors. A tenth tow at the same moment goes without one, and the log says so once. In the rig's whole demo none went without. While not needed they wait 900 m aft of the ship's origin. The hook's role `rider` keeps them out of the marshalling crew.
- **When (crewtest_cockpit.lua):** in when the tractor is 20 m from the place it hooks on at. Out when the delivering tractor is 8 m further from the aircraft than it was at delivery, or after at most 60 s, or at once when the tow ends or is broken off. An aircraft handed from the hangar's tractor to the deck's keeps its rider.
- **Where:** in the Hornet's seat, at `fa-18c.edm`'s connector `seat_pilot` (3.640 m ahead, 0.425 up). Checked: the pilot figure there has its hand within 5 cm of the throttle connector. **The hands grip the fixed windscreen arch** (0.22 m either side of the centre line, under its inner edge at 4.066 m ahead and 1.159 up), not the canopy's own bow. Open, the canopy's frame is 1.0 to 2.5 m up and out of a seated man's reach. The legs stay the pilot's, on the pedals (the brakes). The torso comes 4 degrees forward.
- **Poses (crew-test/model/rider_pose.py, made at every install, no switch):** five head poses with the hands solved on the handles, plus the one facing ahead. A blend space `catcc_bs_rdr_look` sits on the drivers' `catcc_head_yaw`. Where he looks is crewtest_head.lua's standing-driver logic. Grips 0.0 cm from the handles in every pose and 0.3 cm at most halfway between two poses. Elbows 63 to 96 degrees, wrists 34 to 42.
- **Canopy:** argument 38 is set to 0.9 (the jet's own Door0 opening) when a rider climbs in, and left open.
- **Not done:** riders on the USB-installed PC. Its installer carries neither `model\` nor the crew patch, the same as for the drivers' poses.

Elbows 90 to 104 degrees, wrists 11 to 18. This replaces the previous entry's hands on the fixed windscreen arch. The `'arch'` setting is kept in the script, unused.

## Brake riders: one rider from the hangar to the deck spot (2026-11-26; tests; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS)

- **Asked (the user, after a DCS run):** "When an aircraft from the hangar is taken to an elevator, the tug disconnects and the brake rider is removed from the cockpit, then reappears in the cockpit before the flight deck tractor reconnects. I would prefer the brake rider to remain in the cockpit during elevator rides between tugs."
- **Now (crewtest_cockpit.lua stage `hold`; crewtest_demo.lua M.cockpitTick):** an aircraft on a platform's list between its two tractors keeps the rider it has, up the elevator, until the deck's tractor has it. Nobody is put in for a platform. The two aircraft that stay on a platform as their deck spots let him out once their hangar tractor is clear (or after 60 s).
- **Found with it:** a delivered aircraft's rider climbed out at once when its tractor was given its next job, before that tractor was clear. Now the tractor is followed into its next job, and the rider waits until it is 8 m further off than at delivery (or 60 s).
- **Rig (the whole demo, on its thread):** each of the 24 aircraft had exactly one rider. Every climb-out was "its tractor is clear" or "still near after 60 s". Two tows started before a rider was free, and the rider climbed in when one was.

## Demo: an aircraft short of its platform is not raised; chocks follow a reused object id (2026-11-26; tests; installed; NOT YET SEEN IN DCS)

- **Seen (the user's run, with screenshots):** the second aircraft for elevator 1 (711) was raised through the hangar's ceiling. Pairs of our wheel chocks stood on empty deck spots.
- **The aircraft (Logs/catcc-crewtest.log, 11:04:55):** its pull ended exactly where the run before had it (tractor at 42.8 / 8.2, jet at 32.6 / 8.4), but the push onto the platform was refused ("the bar would fold against the tractor"). The tow unhooked 24 m short of the platform, and the demo counted the aircraft as aboard because its tractor was leaving.
  - Now (crewtest_hook.lua Tow.tickOne): a demo tow whose push cannot begin holds on the bar and asks again every 2 s for up to 90 s (`TOW.pushRetry`, `TOW.pushWait`). Only then does it stop short as before (`w.pushShort`).
  - Now (crewtest_demo.lua): a hangar tow that ends short, or with the aircraft off the platform's area by more than 1 m, does not put the aircraft on the elevator. It stays in the hangar where it stands, as a STOPPED tow's aircraft does, and the elevator goes on without it. The log says so and so does a mission message.
  - Why the same push was refused one time and not the other is not known. The push is planned against what stands round it at that moment, which differed between the runs.
- **The chocks (crewtest_chocks.lua, "the other Hornets"):** the chocks of the run before (40 Hornets on deck) stayed on deck spots whose aircraft the next run started in the hangar. The demo's statics are made anew with the same object ids, so the "gone" check never fired. Now chocks put on an object are taken away and put again when that object id's name, ship or place (0.3 m, or its heading) changes.
